# Public surface change log

This is the append-only signoff trail for provisional Rust, UniFFI, Swift, and
Kotlin surface changes. Once merged, existing bytes are immutable; corrections
are new entries. The committed baselines live in [`docs/surface/`](surface/).
The governance-introduction PR is the one-time bootstrap: its base cannot run a
workflow that does not yet exist, so it seeds only the already-merged #67, #73,
and #77 trails below and relies on existing CI plus manual review. Every later
entry is validated against the actual PR context by the trusted base workflow.

## 2026-07-11 — Split indexed filter tags from event tag selection ([PR #67](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/67))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #64 showed that one constrained `TagName` type incorrectly represented both NIP-01 indexed filter keys and arbitrary event tag names.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust introduced `IndexedTagName` for `Filter.tags` while selector tag keys became unconstrained strings; FFI, Swift, and Kotlin mirrors changed in the same PR.
- **Persistence impact:** none; this changed query syntax and evaluation, not stored event or journal layout.
- **Diagnostics impact:** wire filters retain one-letter indexed keys; query selection can inspect arbitrary acquired tags without changing diagnostics records.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp-grammar`/`nmp-resolver` contract tests, FFI conversion tests, and Swift/Kotlin filter tests in PR #67.
- **Superseded path removed:** the shared constrained `TagName` type and its whitelist were deleted rather than retained as aliases.
- **Human signoff:** merged through PR #67 on 2026-07-11; the PR review and merge record is the approval trail.

## 2026-07-11 — Rethread FFI over the canonical Rust facade ([PR #73](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/73))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #52 proved that direct Rust and FFI callers independently assembled mechanisms and could inherit different acceptance guarantees.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-ffi` now delegates to `nmp::Engine`; observer cancellation became opaque and native wrappers were updated for the resulting error/write shapes.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema change; the facade owns the same memory/redb selection previously duplicated by FFI.
- **Diagnostics impact:** FFI diagnostics are a projection of the canonical facade rather than an independently assembled engine.
- **Updated falsifiers:** facade, FFI conversion/observer, Swift diagnostics, and Kotlin package tests recorded in PR #73.
- **Superseded path removed:** direct `nmp-ffi` mechanism assembly, redundant signed-event verification, and unreachable error variants were deleted.
- **Human signoff:** merged through PR #73 on 2026-07-11; the PR review and merge record is the approval trail.

## 2026-07-11 — Replace aggregate query coverage with scoped acquisition evidence ([PR #77](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/77))

- **Failure evidence:** issues #12/#49 showed that aggregate `Coverage` could overstate what the current source plan proved and conflated query evidence with diagnostic intervals.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** query batches now carry `AcquisitionEvidence`; FFI and both native SDKs mirror every source status/auth/shortfall variant while diagnostics retain `CoverageInterval`.
- **Persistence impact:** durable per-filter/per-relay intervals remain the stored fact; the query evidence is derived from the current plan.
- **Diagnostics impact:** diagnostics continue to expose optional exact intervals and compute coalesced wire facts from absorbed atom keys, distinct from query evidence.
- **Updated falsifiers:** engine plan-churn/coalescing/zero-source tests plus exhaustive Rust, Swift, and Kotlin evidence mapping tests in PR #77.
- **Superseded path removed:** `QueryCoverage`, aggregate query `Coverage`, stale facade vocabulary, and the non-optional empty Swift pre-first-batch evidence state were deleted.
- **Human signoff:** merged through PR #77 on 2026-07-11 after local, Atlas, and Nova review; the PR review and merge record is the approval trail.

## 2026-07-11 — Add stable receipt reattachment and truthful restart ambiguity ([PR #83](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/83))

- **Failure evidence:** issues #2/#3 showed that a process restart could retain an accepted write in the store while losing the Rust receipt observer, frozen signer work, and per-relay attempt ownership; an at-most-once send crossing that boundary also had no truthful public status distinct from ordinary `GaveUp`.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust exports `ReceiptId`/`ReceiptStream`, adds `Engine::publish_tracked` and `Engine::reattach_receipt`, and adds `WriteStatus::OutcomeUnknown`; UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin add the corresponding `OutcomeUnknown(relay)` status without collapsing it into `GaveUp`. Native stable-id reattachment APIs remain a later coordinated surface rather than being fabricated in this change.
- **Persistence impact:** Redb now retains versioned, exact-byte `(intent, relay, ordinal)` attempt facts and stable receipt ids; attempt keys are unambiguous, terminal transitions distinguish committed/idempotent-same from missing/conflicting facts, corrupt/unknown versions fail closed, and durable receipt ids are bounded below the disjoint unaccepted-correlation namespace.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema changes; restart and attempt facts are replayed through receipt streams, while existing diagnostics remain the read-only relay/query projection.
- **Updated falsifiers:** cross-backend attempt ordering/prefix/terminal/corruption/overflow tests, genuine Redb close/reopen recovery tests, exact frozen-signer and boot-before-first-command tests, two-observer future-transition proof, Rust/FFI parity, and exhaustive Swift/Kotlin `OutcomeUnknown` mapping tests.
- **Superseded path removed:** stream-local accepted-write identity, lossy boot reconstruction, blind at-most-once resend/`GaveUp` projection, panic-based attempt decoding, ambiguous relay-prefix keys, and false-success terminal persistence are replaced rather than retained as compatibility paths.
- **Human signoff:** PR #83 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session, with independent exact-head review plus the required CI and merge record serving as the approval trail.

## 2026-07-12 — Govern dependency-reexported Rust facade shapes ([PR #90](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/90))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #89 showed that the Rust facade snapshot recorded dependency-owned reexports opaquely, so changing a supported enum variant or record field could leave the Rust projection unchanged and produce a false governance verdict.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the Rust snapshot now resolves the shapes and root inherent APIs of explicit `nmp` reexports; no UniFFI, Swift, or Kotlin product surface changes in this governance correction.
- **Persistence impact:** none; this changes the generated public-surface evidence and its protected extractor, not stored events, receipts, attempts, or schema versions.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; diagnostics types are captured more accurately when reexported, but their runtime meaning and values are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** compiler-backed fixtures cover renamed dependencies, nested structs/enums/aliases, cycles, root methods and associated items, mixed visibility, lock drift, unrelated dependency APIs, numeric-ID rejection, deterministic fresh-clone regeneration, and snapshot size bounds.
- **Superseded path removed:** the opaque dependency-reexport evidence path is eliminated: pinned `cargo-public-api` remains the direct-facade prefix, while a locked, pinned rustdoc-JSON extractor now resolves every explicit dependency-owned reexport and fails closed on unresolved shapes.
- **Human signoff:** PR #90 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session, with independent exact-head review, the adversarial governance suite, deterministic regeneration, and repository CI serving as the approval trail.

## 2026-07-12 — Retain write lanes across persistence failures ([PR #91](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/91))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #85 showed that `start_attempt` persistence failure could leave a routed relay with no durable Started fact, no wire EVENT, and no owned nonterminal lane; for dynamically resolved `AuthorOutbox`/`ToInboxes` routes, restart with an empty or changed directory could also erase the exact failed relay.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds distinct nonterminal `WriteStatus::PersistenceBlocked(relay)` for a durably route-owned lane whose attempt could not start and `WriteStatus::RoutePersistenceBlocked(relay)` for a resolved URL whose route revision itself did not commit; UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin mirror both without collapsing either into `GaveUp`, `OutcomeUnknown`, or whole-intent `Failed`.
- **Persistence impact:** MemoryStore and RedbStore add typed append-only, canonically ordered resolved-route revisions keyed by `(intent, ordinal)`; the engine commits a complete revision before any corresponding attempt or EVENT, and boot recovers the union of every revision relay and attempt relay without subtracting later directory removals. A failed revision commit persists no URL and therefore makes no false crash-survival claim.
- **Diagnostics impact:** receipt streams gain the two nonterminal persistence-blocked facts; query/relay diagnostics schemas are unchanged, and no blocked lane is reported as terminal or as a wire send.
- **Updated falsifiers:** backend-parity and real-Redb-reopen route-revision tests; a SIGABRT-before-commit rollback/ordinal proof; one-lane, all-lane, mixed-success-plus-ACK, and repeated restart tests; dynamic AuthorOutbox empty-directory recovery; ToInboxes changed-directory/removal recovery with partial revision failure; exhaustive Rust/FFI/parity plus Swift/Kotlin status mappings.
- **Superseded path removed:** the ambiguous empty `pending_relays` sentinel and recovery that depended only on current directory resolution are replaced by separate Started, durable-unstarted, and volatile-route-blocked ownership sets backed by append-only route facts; no compatibility alias or blind retry path remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #91 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Distinguish absent receipts from retained unreadable evidence ([PR #97](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/97))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #88 showed that the prior optional/bare-false reattachment result made a never-issued receipt indistinguishable from a retained durable obligation whose receipt, attempt, or route evidence could not be decoded.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust replaces optional reattachment with `ReceiptReattachment::{Attached, NotFound, RetainedButUnreadable}`; UniFFI mirrors the three outcomes, `publish` returns the stable store-issued receipt id, and Swift/Kotlin expose a native typed reattachment result carrying a receipt stream only for `Attached`.
- **Persistence impact:** the canonical store receipt lookup is now fallible; Redb preserves undecodable receipt bytes through boot reconciliation and reports them as unreadable, while corrupt receipt/attempt/route evidence never registers an observer, publishes, or deletes the retained obligation. Terminal retained receipts remain readable and reattachable.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; receipt reattachment remains a write-observation surface and does not change query or relay diagnostics schemas.
- **Updated falsifiers:** unknown, live, terminal, multiple-observer, genuine-restart, corrupt-receipt, corrupt-attempt, and corrupt-route/lane tests plus exhaustive Rust/FFI/Swift/Kotlin three-variant mapping tests.
- **Superseded path removed:** optional/bare-false reattachment and the infallible Redb receipt decoder are replaced rather than retained as compatibility paths; corruption can no longer collapse into absence or panic during boot reconciliation.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #97 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Make pre-receipt correlation exhaustion fallible ([PR #100](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/100))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #86 showed that the upper-half allocator panicked while issuing its final valid correlation id, so a public publish boundary could crash instead of truthfully reporting that no receipt identity remained.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `EngineError::ReceiptCorrelationIdExhausted`; UniFFI mirrors the fieldless typed error, and Swift/Kotlin map it to native `NMPError` cases. Publish method signatures remain fallible as before, but this capacity boundary is now explicit instead of a panic.
- **Persistence impact:** none; durable store-issued receipt ids remain confined below `2^63`, while the volatile pre-acceptance allocator issues the final upper-half id `2^63` exactly once and then remains exhausted without wrap, reuse, collision, or stored state.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; no receipt or status stream exists on exhaustion, and query/relay diagnostics are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** a test-only boundary seed proves the last valid and first/repeated exhausted allocations without `2^63` iterations; runtime, Rust facade, FFI, Swift, and Kotlin mapping tests preserve the typed failure, while existing store boundary tests retain the lower-half proof.
- **Superseded path removed:** the decrement-and-`expect` allocator is replaced by an exhaustion-encoding state and typed publish failure; no sentinel id, compatibility alias, fabricated status, wrap, or reuse path remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #100 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Project unioned relay provenance as reactive row metadata ([PR #109](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/109))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #105 showed that `nmp-store` already persists a unioned, per-event-id relay-observation set (`Provenance::seen`), but live-query rows exposed only the bare event -- making strict relay-pinned cache reads (the #1/#63 prerequisite) impossible, and hiding a real state change: the same event later arriving from a second relay produced no update at all, since row equality was event-ID-only.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust introduces `Row { event, sources: BTreeSet<RelayUrl> }` as the canonical row value and adds `RowDelta::SourcesGrew { id, sources }` (never reusing `Added`, which would falsely claim "newly matches"), carrying the row's full current source set; UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin mirror both, with `FfiRow` gaining `sources` and `FfiRowDelta` gaining the matching variant.
- **Persistence impact:** none; the underlying relay-observation union was already fully implemented, tested, and durable in `nmp-store` (`EventStore::insert`'s dedup path, survives Redb reopen, correct through signature promotion) -- this PR only projects the already-persisted fact onto the reactive surface that previously discarded it.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; `AcquisitionEvidence`/relay diagnostics are unchanged and remain orthogonal to per-row provenance (query-plan-level facts vs. per-event-id facts).
- **Updated falsifiers:** a two-relay union proof (union across relays, no-op on identical redelivery, `SourcesGrew` never a second `Added`); the load-bearing lifecycle-trigger proof (an UNRELATED query's own subscribe/unsubscribe recompute must never spuriously emit `SourcesGrew` for a row whose provenance did not change); a genuine Redb close/reopen proving the projection survives restart; exhaustive Rust/FFI/Swift/Kotlin mapping tests proving `SourcesGrew` replaces a row's provenance in place, never duplicating it.
- **Superseded path removed:** `RowDelta::Added`'s bare-`nostr::Event` payload is replaced by `Row` (event + sources) at every call site in the workspace; no compatibility alias or parallel bare-event delivery path remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #109 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Make live-query identity an explicit selection + source + access demand descriptor ([PR #112](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/112))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #106 showed that live-query identity and coverage stayed effectively filter-shaped: routing inferred source behavior from filter shape alone, a nested `Derived` demand could inherit its outer's context by accident, and two equal selections under different intended authority had no way to avoid sharing evidence, coverage, or teardown identity merely because their wire filters looked alike (bug-class ledger #18).
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `Demand{selection, source, access, cache}`, `SourceAuthority{AuthorOutboxes, Public}`, `AccessContext{Public}`, `CacheMode{Agnostic, Strict}`, and `ContextualAtom` (the new atom/refcount/coverage identity); `Binding::Derived.inner` is now a full `Demand`, never a bare `Filter`, so an inner query owns its own context and never inherits the outer's. `Demand::from_filter` is the static, total default (any `authors` binding shape -> `AuthorOutboxes`, else `Public`), applied automatically by `LiveQuery::from_filter`. `Demand::new(selection, source, access)` is the explicit fallible constructor for a non-default combination (e.g. `Public` on an author-bearing selection -- the one new expressible behavior this PR adds), returning the new `DemandError` for the one unconstructible combination (`AuthorOutboxes` over a selection that does not bind `authors` at all); `Demand::atom_context()` is the single projection deciding atom/wire/coverage identity (`cache` deliberately excluded). `DemandOp::Open`/`Close` now carry a full `ContextualAtom`, not a bare `ConcreteFilter`, so `DemandDelta` reflects exactly what the atom-refcount table keys on. The UniFFI wire schema, Swift, and Kotlin are unchanged in this PR: `FfiDerived.inner` stays `FfiFilter`, and the new Rust-only types apply/strip the same static default transparently at the `nmp-ffi` conversion boundary.
- **Persistence impact:** `CoverageKey`'s identity widens to a full `ContextualAtom` (selection + source + access) and gains a schema-version tag folded into its hash; `RedbStore`'s durable row key gains an independent `"d2:"` version prefix, and `gc()` gains a legacy-row purge pass that deletes any coverage row lacking the current prefix rather than let a pre-#106, filter-only row linger or be silently credited under the new meaning.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; `AcquisitionEvidence`/`ShortfallFact`'s own public surface is unchanged (`ShortfallFact.atom` stays a bare `ConcreteFilter`, reporting which selection lacks a source, not a context distinction).
- **Updated falsifiers:** a static-default guardrail proving a `$myFollows`-shaped `Derived` authors binding still lowers to `AuthorOutboxes` even though it may resolve empty at runtime; a row-matching-stays-pure guardrail (`root_atoms()` untouched, existing feed/provenance tests green); anti-alias falsifiers at every layer (`ContextualAtom::hash`, `CoverageKey`, `SubId::for_wire`) proving an identical selection under different `SourceAuthority` never aliases evidence, coverage, or wire/attribution identity; a router-level falsifier proving an identical selection under different `SourceAuthority` routed to the SAME relay via two different lanes (outbox-solve vs. pinned/group-host) ships as two separate `WireReq`s with distinct sub-ids, never merged (`coalesce.rs` itself stays pure selection-only and unaware of context; equal-context-only coalescing is enforced by `Router::compile` partitioning its per-relay bag before calling the unchanged rule engine); `Demand::new` constructor-validation falsifiers (the one unconstructible combination errors; `Public` over an author-bearing selection is legal); a legacy-row-purge falsifier for the versioned coverage-key migration; the full existing `nmp-grammar`/`nmp-resolver`/`nmp-router`/`nmp-store`/`nmp-engine`/`nmp`/`nmp-ffi`/`nmp-bdd`/`nmp-demo`/`nmp-consumer-check`/`nmp-parity` suites stay green (the default `AuthorOutboxes + Public` path reproduces today's exact behavior).
- **Superseded path removed:** bare `ConcreteFilter`/`Filter` as the atom/refcount/coverage identity is replaced by `ContextualAtom`/`Demand` at every call site in the workspace, including `DemandOp`'s own payload; `SubId::for_filter` and the context-free `coverage_key(&ConcreteFilter)` are removed rather than kept as compatibility aliases; a coverage row computed under the pre-#106 unversioned key format is never reused under the new meaning.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #112 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review (atlas's landing review, re-running the hard guardrail proof floors) and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Expose a bech32 nostr-entity decoder over FFI ([PR #120](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/120))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #116 showed that a consumer (29er-next) had no way to turn a `nostr:npub1…`/`nprofile1…` mention into a queryable hex pubkey over FFI, forcing either a hand-rolled bech32 decoder in Swift (duplicating a protocol-level codec) or leaving `@name` mention resolution blocked.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `nmp_grammar::{NostrEntity, NostrEntityError, decode_nostr_entity}` (re-exported from `nmp`), a thin adapter over `nostr`'s own `Nip19::from_bech32`/`Nip21`; `nmp-ffi` adds a top-level `decode_nostr_entity` function (no `NmpEngine` instance required) plus `FfiNostrEntity` and two new `FfiError` variants (`InvalidNostrEntity`, `NostrEntitySecretKeyRejected`); Swift and Kotlin each add an ergonomic `NostrEntity`/`decodeNostrEntity` mirroring the identical five-case shape plus the matching `NMPError` cases.
- **Persistence impact:** none; a pure, stateless codec with no store/journal/schema interaction of any kind.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; unrelated to the query/write/diagnostics planes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust decode tests for all five entity kinds (npub/nprofile/note/nevent/naddr) plus the `nostr:`-prefixed-URI-equivalence, malformed-bech32, and secret-key-refusal cases; FFI mapping tests for the same set; exhaustive Swift and Kotlin construction/mapping tests mirroring every case, including the honest-modeling proof that `nevent`'s `author`/`kind` stay independently optional rather than force-fit.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is a net-new codec with no prior FFI-exposed decoder to alias or replace.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #120 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Implement explicit pinned read authority with agnostic and strict cache modes ([PR #133](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/133))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #107 (parent #1) showed that a query had no way to declare an explicit relay set that NMP would ask on the wire without silently widening it to author outboxes, directory facts, app relays, fallbacks, or indexers, and no way to declare whether a cached row's provenance had to actually intersect that set before being served.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `SourceAuthority::Pinned(BTreeSet<RelayUrl>)` (dropping `Copy` from the enum) and a `DemandError::PinnedRequiresNonemptyRelaySet` validation case; `nmp-router` adds `AtomClass::ExplicitPinned`, `Lane::ExplicitPinned`, `RouteKind::ExplicitPinned`, and routes these atoms through a dedicated `Router::compile` step that applies no additive lane; `nmp-engine::rows_and_evidence_for` reads each handle's own `CacheMode` (never the shared graph node's) and, under `Strict`, filters cached rows to those whose provenance intersects the pinned set. UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin gain the first real `Demand` projection since #106 deferred it (#113): `FfiDemand`/`FfiSourceAuthority`/`FfiAccessContext`/`FfiCacheMode` and `NmpEngine::observe_demand`, mirrored as `NMPDemand`/`NMPSourceAuthority`/etc. and a second `observe(_:)` overload on both native SDKs. Also folds in #118: `EngineCore::get_coverage`/`active_demand` now take/return the atom's true `ContextualAtom` context instead of reconstructing it via a removed `default_context_atom` helper, since `Pinned` is the first production path making non-default `Demand` construction reachable.
- **Persistence impact:** `fold_context` (the `ContextualAtom`/`CoverageKey` identity hash) gains a `Pinned` arm hashing the relay set's own canonicalized bytes (length-prefixed UTF8 over the `BTreeSet`'s already-sorted order, blake3) rather than a fixed discriminant, so two different pinned sets are genuinely distinct coverage/attribution identities while insertion order never perturbs the hash; no store schema change beyond this identity widening (the `"d2:"` versioned key from #106 already covers it).
- **Diagnostics impact:** none beyond the existing `SourceAuthority`-keyed evidence/coverage projection; `CacheMode` is deliberately excluded from `ContextualAtom`'s hashed identity and governs only the local row-projection read, never diagnostics.
- **Updated falsifiers:** router-level falsifiers proving `ExplicitPinned` atoms never contact directory/outbox/app/fallback/indexer relays even when every one of those lanes is configured to happily route the same filter, and that `classify()` maps `Pinned` to `ExplicitPinned` regardless of filter shape; grammar-level hash falsifiers for distinct/reordered pinned sets; engine-level falsifiers proving an Agnostic and a Strict handle sharing the identical `AcquisitionKey` (and therefore one wire subscription) still project different row sets, and that the identical filter pinned to two DIFFERENT relay sets yields fully independent handles, sub-ids, EOSE watermarks, and teardown; the #118 falsifier proving `get_coverage` finds coverage under a query's TRUE declared context and not under the static-default's wrong guess; FFI round-trip falsifiers for `Pinned`+`Strict` (including canonical-sort proof) and both unconstructible `Demand` combinations; exhaustive Swift/Kotlin conversion tests verified against real local `swift build`/`swift test` and `./gradlew test` runs (not simulated).
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp-engine`'s private `default_context_atom` reconstruction helper is deleted outright, not kept as a fallback; no compatibility alias exists for either the FFI-only `FfiFilter` entry point (which remains, applying `Demand::from_filter`'s static default) or the new `FfiDemand` one.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #133 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Compose NIP-51 kind 10009 into a read-only NIP-29 host browser ([PR #136](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/136))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #108 (advances #63, #45) showed that NMP lacked ownership of NIP-51 kind:10009 (remembered NIP-29 groups) and the typed composition from a remembered host to host-scoped NIP-29 reads, blocking 29er-next's group-host browser without hand-decoding list events or reimplementing the pinned-relay primitive #107 just landed.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** two new crates. `nmp-nip51` exclusively owns kind:10009 -- `decode_simple_groups_list`/`decode_simple_groups_list_from_raw_tags` decode public "group"/"r" tags (order-preserving, malformed items counted not fatal), `active_account_demand()` uses the existing `AuthorOutboxes + Public` default over a `Reactive(ActivePubkey)` selection, and `claims()` declares its one exclusive `KindClaim` on `Kind(10009)`. `nmp-nip29` composes nip51's typed output into `GroupRef`/`RememberedGroups` (a pure mapping, no second subscription) and provides `group_discovery_demand`/`group_content_demand`, both built on `SourceAuthority::Pinned({host})` (#107's primitive) rather than the pre-existing directory-fact `Lane::GroupHost` path, which stays untouched; nip29 registers no `KindClaim` of its own in this PR. UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin mirror `active_account_demand`/`group_discovery_demand`/`group_content_demand`/`decode_remembered_groups` as top-level free functions needing no engine instance (mirroring #116's `decode_nostr_entity` precedent), exposing only nip29's vocabulary (`FfiGroupRef`/`FfiRememberedGroups`) -- nip51 stays an internal transitive detail.
- **Persistence impact:** none; this is a read-only codec/composition layer over the existing kind:10009 read path -- no store schema change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none beyond what #107 already provides; the selected host's identity rides entirely on `Demand.source` (`SourceAuthority::Pinned`), so it flows through `ContextualAtom`/`AcquisitionEvidence`/diagnostics for free with no new mechanism.
- **Updated falsifiers:** a cross-crate integration proof against a real `EngineCore` that signed-out state yields zero kind:10009 atoms, signing in reconstructs exactly one, and rerooting to a different account REPLACES rather than accumulates; a protocol-level instance of #107's R1-vs-R2 anti-alias proof (identical group content on two different hosts yields distinct `atom_context()`); an ownership-audit test proving nip51's sole claim exclusively covers kind:10009 and nip29 registers nothing; a raw-tags-vs-event decode equivalence proof (the seam the FFI boundary relies on); FFI-boundary fallibility-restoration tests (an FFI-supplied host string can be malformed even though the direct-Rust singleton-relay-set constructors are infallible by construction); exhaustive Swift/Kotlin construction/decode tests verified against real local `swift build`/`swift test` and `./gradlew test` runs.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is net-new read-only capability with no prior kind:10009/NIP-29-host-browser surface to alias or replace.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #136 on 2026-07-12; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Add ReceiptObserver on_closed terminal callback ([PR #129](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/129))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #125 showed that `ReceiptObserver` declared only `on_status`, so when a receipt `Sender` was dropped (intent resolved or engine shutdown) the `nmp-ffi` drain loop ended with no terminal callback -- a Swift `AsyncStream`/Kotlin `Flow` receipt stream was never finished, leaking/hanging the continuation. `RowObserver` and `DiagnosticsObserver` already fired `on_closed`; this was a plain terminal-callback asymmetry.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `fn on_closed(&self)` to the `nmp-ffi` `ReceiptObserver` callback-interface trait, called exactly once after both receipt drain loops (`NmpEngine::publish` and `reattach_receipt`'s `Attached` arm), mirroring the row/diagnostics drains; the UniFFI component surface gains the `receiptobserver_on_closed` method. Swift `ReceiptBridge.onClosed` finishes the `AsyncStream` continuation and Kotlin `ReceiptBridge.onClosed` closes the channel, matching `RowBridge`/`DiagnosticsBridge`. The Rust `nmp` facade is unchanged (this trait is nmp-ffi-only). No method signature or type changed; only a terminal callback was added.
- **Persistence impact:** none; this is an in-process observer-lifecycle callback and touches no store, journal, or receipt-retention layout.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; the receipt stream is a write-observation surface and query/relay diagnostics schemas are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `ffi_receipt_stream_yields_exactly_one_on_closed_when_sender_dropped` drives the real FFI publish drain thread and proves the receipt observer terminates with exactly one `on_closed` (never zero -- a hang, never more than once) after the receipt `Sender` is dropped, mirroring the existing `RowObserver` `ffi_repeated_cancel_across_arc_owners_and_drop_yields_exactly_one_on_closed` proof.
- **Superseded path removed:** the terminal-less receipt drain loop is replaced rather than retained; there is no compatibility path where a receipt stream ends without `on_closed`.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #129 on 2026-07-12, after a converging Opus correctness review and an FFI-surface review from the #116/#120 specialist; exact-head adversarial review and required CI remain merge gates.
## 2026-07-12 — Reject discovered private/loopback/.onion relays + relay-admission ([PR #134](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/134))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #121 showed that discovered relay URLs — network-sourced, validly-signed NIP-65 kind:10002 relay lists — reached the transport pool with no admission policy, so a signed-but-hostile list could name a loopback/RFC-1918/link-local host (an SSRF pivot) or a `.onion` host (forced Tor); the signature gate closed only the forged-10002 half.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust `nmp-transport` exports a pure HOST-only `classify_relay_host`/`relay_host_key`/`RelayHostClass` and wires the previously-dead `PoolConfig::max_relays` (plus `Pool::admission_rejections`); `nmp-engine` exports `RelayAdmissionPolicy` and threads it through `EngineThread::spawn`/`EngineCore::new`; the `nmp::EngineConfig` facade and the UniFFI `NmpEngineConfig` record gain `allowed_local_relay_hosts` (`default = []`) and `max_relays` (`default = 0`), both non-breaking record defaults, and `DiagnosticsSnapshot`/`FfiDiagnosticsSnapshot` gain `discovered_private_relays_rejected` and `relays_rejected_over_cap`; no Swift or Kotlin product surface changes in this PR.
- **Persistence impact:** none; admission is an in-memory routing/directory gate, and neither the store schema, receipts, attempts, nor coverage rows change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** two new engine-global counters are exposed — `discovered_private_relays_rejected` (a per-lane tally: the write and read parse of one kind:10002 each count, so N rejected hosts bump it by up to 2N) and `relays_rejected_over_cap` (folded in at the runtime edge from the transport pool's own count); existing diagnostics fields are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp-transport` classifier falsifiers (per-user path `wss://nostr.wine/<npub>` admitted; loopback/RFC-1918/link-local/unspecified, IPv4-mapped and deprecated IPv4-compatible IPv6, decimal/hex IPv4 encodings, and `.onion`/`localhost` rejected; host-only port-insensitive key) and a pool relay-count-cap falsifier; `nmp-engine` core falsifiers driving a real signed kind:10002 through `on_relay_frame` (discovered private/`.onion` relays rejected, counted, and absent from `write_relays`/`read_relays` so they never reach `ensure_open`; an opted-in local host admitted; a public host at a path admitted); FFI conversion test and `nmp-parity` real-loopback facade parity (opting the loopback host in) kept green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the unenforced "declared dead" `PoolConfig::max_relays` field is now actually enforced rather than left inert; there was no prior admission path to retain as an alias — discovered relays previously flowed unfiltered into the directory, and that unconditional ingest is replaced by the gated `filter_discovered` path at the sole network-discovery choke point.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #134 on 2026-07-12, after an Opus security review confirmed the core gate sound (`ingest_relay_list_winner` is the sole live network-discovery path) and directed the FFI-default and IPv4-encoding hardening folded in here; exact-head review and required CI, including surface-governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Isolate @Observable SDK adapters to @MainActor ([PR #128](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/128))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #126 showed that the `@Observable` SwiftUI-sugar adapters `NMPQuerySnapshot` and `NMPDiagnosticsSnapshotObserver` were not `@MainActor`, so their unstructured `Task { … }` wrote observable state (`rows`/`evidence`/`snapshot`) off the main actor while SwiftUI reads it on the main actor -- a latent data race in the Swift projection.
- **Changed projections:** swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Swift-only. Both `public final class` adapters gain `@MainActor`, so `init`'s context-inheriting `Task` now resumes every observable-state write on the main actor; `consumeTask` becomes `@ObservationIgnored private nonisolated(unsafe)` so the nonisolated `deinit` can still cancel it. No Rust, FFI, or Kotlin surface moved; the public member signatures of both classes are unchanged apart from main-actor isolation. Consumer-facing tightening: `@MainActor` now requires these two classes be constructed on the main actor -- the intended usage for SwiftUI `@Observable` sugar (a `View`'s `init` is already `@MainActor`), but a source-level constraint for any off-main construction site.
- **Persistence impact:** none; this is a Swift concurrency-isolation annotation on convenience adapters and touches no stored event, receipt, attempt, route, or coverage layout.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; `NMPDiagnosticsSnapshotObserver` exposes the same `DiagnosticsSnapshot` value with identical meaning, now written on the main actor rather than off it.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `swift build` and `swift build --build-tests` over the regenerated `NMP.xcframework`/`NMPFFI` bindings compile clean with zero warnings, proving the `@MainActor` classes and the `nonisolated(unsafe)` deinit type-check; existing Swift SDK tests remain green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the off-main-actor mutation path (a plain `@Observable` class writing observable state from an unstructured `Task`) is eliminated rather than retained; no non-`@MainActor` construction path or `MainActor.run` shim remains. `isolated deinit` (Swift 6.1) was deliberately not used, keeping the adapters portable below Swift 6.1 while CI's Xcode is unpinned -- a future cleanup once Swift is pinned ≥ 6.1.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #128 on 2026-07-12 after passing Opus review with no blocking findings; exact-head review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-12 — Make EventStore ingest/read doors fallible; surface store-degraded diagnostic ([PR #135](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/135))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #122 showed that while the durable-WRITE `EventStore` doors were already fallible (`accept_write`/`promote_signed`/`compensate_write` + journal doors crossing FFI), the ingest/read doors (`insert`/`query`/`remove`/`expire_due`/`record_coverage`/`gc`) still `.expect()`-panicked on real redb I/O — so a disk-full during ordinary relay ingest (`RedbStore::insert` runs on every EVENT frame) panicked the host app rather than degrading.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust widens the six ingest/read `EventStore` doors to return `Result<_, PersistenceError>` (matching the pre-existing durable-write doors); `MemoryStore` implements them as never-`Err` (no I/O), `RedbStore` propagates real redb errors via `.map_err(persist_err)?` on every redb operation while serde/logic invariant violations on a corrupt persisted row remain `.expect()`-on-invariant by design. The only public Rust facade movement is a new `nmp::DiagnosticsSnapshot.store_degraded: Option<String>` field — the engine's minimal, read-only "the local cache went read-only" signal, set the first time an ingest/read door fails. `EventStore` itself is not part of the `nmp` public facade snapshot, so the trait widening does not move the recorded Rust surface; the UniFFI component snapshot is unchanged (`FfiDiagnosticsSnapshot` is NOT widened in this change — plumbing `store_degraded` across FFI/Swift/Kotlin is explicitly deferred, see below).
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema or on-disk layout change; the redb backend's transaction structure is identical, only its error handling changes from panic to propagated `PersistenceError`. `next_expiration`/`get_coverage` (small read peeks) are deliberately NOT widened in this change and still `.expect()` — flagged for follow-up.
- **Diagnostics impact:** `DiagnosticsSnapshot` gains a read-only `store_degraded` field (`None` while healthy, `Some(message)` once the local cache has degraded to read-only). Off the data path — never a routing input; emitted eagerly when a door first fails so an observer sees the degrade immediately.
- **Engine failure policy:** on an ingest/read door failure the engine reducer NEVER panics — it latches the first error message (read-only degrade), skips the affected reactive step (leaving already-delivered handle rows untouched rather than fabricating a phantom retraction), emits the `store_degraded` diagnostic, and keeps handling subsequent messages. Minimal by intent: a richer failure-mode framework (recovery, reopen, per-door policy, FFI plumbing) is deliberately out of scope.
- **Updated falsifiers:** a door-level falsifier proving `EventStore::insert` surfaces an injected ingest I/O failure as `Err(PersistenceError)` rather than panicking; an engine-level falsifier proving a relay EVENT frame whose `insert` fails degrades the engine to read-only (emits a `store_degraded` diagnostic, delivers no phantom rows) and leaves the reducer usable — both via a new `FailIngestStore` fault-injecting `EventStore` in `nmp-engine`'s `core_headless` suite. The full `nmp-store` cross-backend contract suite (diffed for both `MemoryStore` and `RedbStore`) and the `nmp-engine` suite stay green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the `.expect()`-on-I/O panic path in `RedbStore`'s six ingest/read doors is replaced by `PersistenceError` propagation at every real redb operation; no panic-on-I/O path is retained for these doors.
- **Human signoff:** PR #135 is prepared on `impl-122-fallible-store-doors` on 2026-07-12 under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; independent exact-head Opus review passed with no blocking findings (false-completeness fails closed — a failed `record_coverage` only under-claims, never fabricates a watermark; redb atomicity confirmed; `candidate_ids` fix and resolver threading confirmed correct), and required CI plus the merge record serve as the approval trail.

## 2026-07-12 — NIP-29 kind:9 group send routed to a pinned host authority ([PR #143](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/143))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #115 showed that NMP had no write-side counterpart to #107/#108's read-side pinned-host browsing -- a NIP-29 group send had no typed way to route to its group's exact host relay, forcing either a hand-rolled `WriteIntent` per app (re-deriving `h`/`previous` tag composition, host selection, and truncation independently) or blocking 29er-next's group chat on the write half entirely.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** hard-break vocabulary relocation: `Durability`, `WritePayload`, `WriteIntent`, `WriteRouting`, `NarrowOnly`, `PrivateRoute` move from `nmp-engine::outbox` to `nmp-grammar` (no compat alias; every workspace caller moved in the same PR), alongside a new `HostAuthority` and `WriteRouting::PinnedHost(HostAuthority)` variant -- `WriteStatus`/`Receipt`/`ReceiptSink` stay in `nmp-engine` (they reference `core::ReceiptId`; runtime evidence, not intent vocab). `resolve_routes` gains one infallible, directory-blind `PinnedHost` arm; `routing_snapshot`/`parse_routing_snapshot` both gain matching encode/decode arms so a pinned-host write survives a restart. New crate module `nmp-nip29/src/send.rs`: `GroupTimelineEvidence` (compose-time couriered `previous` evidence, kind-blind h-tag filter, newest-first, capped at `PREVIOUS_MAX = 10`) and `compose_group_send` (the one generic kind-blind group-send composer; no engine/store/router/resolver dependency added). `nmp-ffi` adds the opaque, take-once `FfiComposedWriteIntent`, the free function `group_send_intent` (mirrors #108's/#116's "no `NmpEngine` instance needed" shape; couriers delivered rows into `GroupTimelineEvidence` internally), and `NmpEngine::publish_composed`; `FfiWriteRouting` gains NO variant (the exhaustive `{AuthorOutbox, ToInboxes}` match staying exhaustive IS the app-tier enforcement -- an app can only reach a pinned-host write transitively through `group_send_intent`'s already-composed intent, never by naming a host itself). New `FfiError::ReservedGroupTag`/`IntentAlreadyConsumed` variants. Swift and Kotlin each mirror the app-facing surface: `GroupSendIntent` (opaque, take-once) + `groupSendIntent(...)` as a top-level free function (same "no engine instance needed" shape as #108's read-side projection), and `NMPEngine.publishComposed(_:)`/`publishComposed` wired through the identical receipt-stream bridge `publish(_:)` already uses, plus the matching `NMPError`/`NMPError` (Kotlin) cases. Neither native SDK exposes `HostAuthority`, routing, or the `h`/`previous` tags -- only the two-tier FFI enforcement (Fork 1) makes that withholding actually reach the app boundary.
- **Persistence impact:** the restart/reattach round-trip for a `PinnedHost`-routed pending write is the only persistence-layer change -- `routing_snapshot` encodes it as `pinned-host-hex:<hex>`, `parse_routing_snapshot` decodes it back; no other store schema change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none beyond what #107 already provides; a `PinnedHost` write's evidence rides entirely on the existing per-relay `WriteStatus`/`Routed({host})` receipt stream, no new diagnostic field.
- **Updated falsifiers:** kind-blindness (kind 9 vs 9999 produce identical tags/routing); a live two-relay falsifier proving a `PinnedHost` send reaches ONLY the pinned host even against an EMPTY `FixtureDirectory` (directory-blind), with the delivered row's tags/kind/content matching the composed template exactly (frozen-template) and reappearing via the production `group_content_demand` read (read-back loop); a live falsifier proving a host's admission veto surfaces as a typed `WriteStatus::Rejected(host, reason)`, never silence; `previous`-boundary falsifiers (reserved-tag rejection, wrong-group exclusion, empty-is-legal, 8-char prefixes newest-first capped at `PREVIOUS_MAX` -- scoped to minted-provenance, not freshness: courier-starvation/replay of a stale set is acceptable per NIP-29's own best-effort semantics); a restart-round-trip falsifier for the `PinnedHost` persistence arms (raised by an independent parallel review after the ruling text named only `resolve_routes`); an FFI take-once falsifier proving a second `publish_composed` call on the same handle returns `IntentAlreadyConsumed`; a purity audit (`cargo tree -p nmp-nip29 -e normal` has no `nmp-engine`/`nmp-store`/`nmp-router`/`nmp-resolver` entry). Swift and Kotlin each add construction/compile tests mirroring #108's precedent (kind-blind compose, reserved-tag rejection, courier-row plumbing) plus a take-once test driven against a real headless engine (no live relay, no signer attached -- the intent settles into the retained `Accepted`/`AwaitingCapability` steady state, and a second `publishComposed` throws `IntentAlreadyConsumed`); the live round-trip itself stays the Rust falsifier, never re-driven from either native SDK. Verified via real local `scripts/build-swift-xcframework.sh --sim-only` + `swift test` (43 tests) and `scripts/build-kotlin-jvm.sh` + `./gradlew test` (30 tests).
- **Superseded path removed:** the write-intent vocabulary's residence in `nmp-engine::outbox` is retired outright, not kept as a compatibility alias; every prior `nmp_engine::outbox::{Durability, WriteIntent, WritePayload, WriteRouting, NarrowOnly, PrivateRoute}` import path moved to `nmp_grammar` in this same PR.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #143 on 2026-07-12, built from a Fable design-gate ruling on issue #115 with an independent parallel review folded in; exact-head adversarial review and required CI, including surface-governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Add the optional live content engine ([PR #160](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/160))

- **Failure evidence:** issues #147 and #75 show that apps otherwise have to independently parse mixed Nostr content, normalize NIP-19/NIP-21 references, decode kind:0 and NIP-23 resources, and reconstruct reference acquisition; 29er-next issue #31 is a concrete downstream symptom because channel previews can only show raw bech32 text today.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds the optional `nmp-content` semantic/parser/resource crate and a public `nmp::admits_network_relay_hint` safety predicate while keeping `nmp::Core` independent of content; FFI projects the pure document, normalized-reference, demand-plan, resource, and hydration-policy values; Swift adds the optional `NMPContent` product with `NostrContentSession`/claims/snapshots over ordinary `NMPClient` queries; Kotlin adds the equivalent values and structured-concurrency `ContentSession` over ordinary `NmpClient` flows. No renderer, UI registry, or component host enters the engine.
- **Persistence impact:** none; content-derived canonical and helper demands use the existing query engine and one event store, with no second cache, resolver database, or winner-selection path.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema changes; content snapshots courier the canonical query's acquisition evidence plus helper-query evidence and distinguish loading, refreshing, resolved, withdrawn, shortfall, stopped, and collapsed states without claiming global absence or inventing a deletion reason.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust parser tests cover plaintext/Markdown, malformed-visible behavior, source ranges, duplicate-occurrence deduplication, Unicode/property inputs, truncation bounds, NIP-19 normalization, helper-demand construction, and rejection of loopback/private/link-local/`.onion` network relay hints; Swift's 51-test suite includes real profile acquisition through only `purplepag.es`/`relay.primal.net` and a real relay-hint-free `naddr` resolved via ordinary kind:10002 outbox discovery; Kotlin tests cover equivalent projection and structured cancellation; strict clippy is clean across all touched crates.
- **Superseded path removed:** none in NMP v2; this is a net-new optional surface. The draft's unsafe direct relay-hint-to-pinned-authority path was removed before publication, and the design deliberately does not restore the historical global component registry, parallel embed resolver, or closed kind-projection enum.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner requested this exact content-engine foundation for the component and Gallery work; draft PR #160 on 2026-07-13 is the review record, with exact-head review and required CI as merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Ship the first SwiftUI component family and live Gallery ([PR #163](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/163))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #154 (under #75) records that the previous Gallery exposed parser internals, omitted Avatar/Name primitives, and presented padding variants instead of genuinely different useful compositions; 29er-next issue #31 is the concrete preview failure where a NIP-27 mention remained raw bech32 text.
- **Changed projections:** swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Swift adds the optional `NMPUI` product above `NMPContent`: fallback-safe identity primitives, one `NostrContent` semantic renderer with arbitrary native inline/block nodes, an immutable locally scoped renderer set, three mention treatments, slotted event chrome, distinct portrait and Medium-style NIP-23 cards, three user-card layouts, and three controlled reaction families. Rust, UniFFI, and Kotlin surfaces are unchanged; NMP Core gains no UI dependency, renderer registry, component host, or lifecycle callback.
- **Persistence impact:** none; the UI owns no event store or protocol mirror and renders the existing `NostrContentSession` snapshots.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change; the Gallery's Live proof screen consumes the existing diagnostics stream to show the two configured indexers and additional author relays discovered by ordinary NMP routing.
- **Updated falsifiers:** 55 Swift tests pass, including the real profile/no-hint-article acquisition proofs and an app-only kind renderer acceptance test; `NMPUIGallery` builds and runs on iOS Simulator through XcodeBuildMCP; runtime inspection proves resolved channel-preview names, both materially different real article layouts, generic and locally overridden event chrome, discovered author relays, VoiceOver-visible labels, light/dark rendering, deterministic missing-metadata continuity, and reduced-motion-aware reaction presentation.
- **Superseded path removed:** the rejected parser-internals Gallery and its fake article padding variants are not retained; the public UI has no `NostrInlineContent`, `NostrBlockContent`, `ListBlock`, `CodeBlock`, global mutable registry, or leaf-owned fetching surface.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested this component family, real two-indexer Gallery, and channel-preview-capable renderer contract; draft PR #163 on 2026-07-13 is the review record, with exact-head review and required CI as merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Complete SwiftUI scripted-preview and Gallery conformance surfaces ([PR #171](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/171))

- **Failure evidence:** the post-merge audit on reopened issue #154 found that PR #163 proved the live happy path but did not implement its explicit network-free scripted-preview seam or repeatable Dynamic Type, RTL, reduced-motion, unknown-kind, long-content, and rapid-scroll gates; `NMPUITests` had only four non-rendering helper tests, so the issue's broader completion claim was not supported by current evidence.
- **Changed projections:** swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Swift-only. `Row` gains a public raw-value initializer explicitly documented as non-validating and non-inserting, enabling deterministic component fixtures and import adapters. `NostrContentSession` gains network-free `scripted(content:)`/`scripted(document:)`, `isLive`, and acquisition-mode-preserving `nestedSession`; its previously public `client` storage is retired so renderers cannot reach around the session abstraction. `NMPUI` adds public article image/title/summary/byline/reading-time, NIP-05, and avatar-group leaves plus explicit reaction motion preference; composed cards are rebuilt from those leaves and noninteractive article/mention surfaces no longer masquerade as disabled buttons. Rust, UniFFI, and Kotlin are unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** none; scripted sessions own immutable supplied presentation states only, cannot open a query, and never insert their fixture rows into NMP's canonical store.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; live Gallery proof continues to consume the existing diagnostics stream, while the new deterministic States tab is deliberately network-free and makes no evidence claim.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Swift tests prove a scripted resolved event, inert claims, nested scripted parity, article leaf fallback rules, avatar overflow, and reaction count formatting; the generated iOS Gallery UI-test target traverses the real catalog, deterministic loading/shortfall/cycle/unknown-kind environments, and a 72-row rapid-scroll list with two production references per row. The Gallery adds explicit Dynamic Type, RTL, reduced-motion, dark-appearance, long-Markdown, and visible-claim surfaces for simulator/accessibility inspection.
- **Superseded path removed:** `NostrContentSession.client` is removed rather than kept as an engine escape hatch; `NMPNestedEventContent` descends through the session's acquisition-mode-preserving constructor. Article title/summary/image/byline implementations no longer remain duplicated privately inside both card layouts.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner asked that the component family, Gallery, tests, and running result be fully delivered rather than narrowed to the already-merged happy path; reopened issue #154 and draft PR #171 are the review record, with exact-head required CI and simulator conformance evidence remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Parse relay events once and bound persistent verification ([PR #173](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/173))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #168 and the preserved 1,114-event corpus showed that relay ingest parsed EVENT JSON twice, serialized the event subtree between parses, created scoped verifier threads per burst, verified while holding `PoolInner`, and allowed queue drains to grow one unbounded engine transaction; review also found cached id/signature hits did not recompute the event id and shutdown could deadlock across the bounded sink/bridge acknowledgement cycle.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust transport replaces opaque text frames with typed `RelayFrame` values whose EVENT payload is shared through `Arc<Event>`, exports `DurableSendOutcome`, adds explicit queue/verifier/cache/engine-batch bounds to `PoolConfig`, and adds `DiagnosticsSnapshot.transport_degraded`; UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin add the matching optional transport-degradation diagnostic. Existing constructors keep `nil`/`null` defaults, and no platform owns verification or routing policy.
- **Persistence impact:** no on-disk schema change; accepted typed events still enter the existing governed `insert_batch` state machine and one crash-atomic redb transaction covering events, every index, provenance, coverage, and outbox effects. The engine bridge now caps each transaction at 128 frames by default.
- **Diagnostics impact:** verifier unavailability and transport acceptance failures no longer disappear at the runtime bridge; the latest optional message is emitted through Rust diagnostics and projected through UniFFI/Swift/Kotlin. Invalid signatures retain their separate relay-misbehavior count, while an internal worker outage leaves that count unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** typed one-parse classification; shared-allocation ownership; same-batch Connected-before-Frame generation planning; mutated payload under a cached id/signature; invalid/mismatched signatures; bounded cache and worker replacement; capped ordered engine batches; blocked-sink and unacked-bridge shutdown cancellation; EVENT-before-EOSE and real reconnect behavior; Rust-to-FFI diagnostic conversion; real-corpus 1/2/8/32/128/512/all parse, verification, known-redelivery, and typed resolver/redb matrices.
- **Superseded path removed:** transport's direct `serde_json` dependency, `Value -> event JSON -> Event -> original frame parse`, per-burst verifier threads, production first-seen event deep clone, unbounded bridge drain, blocking synchronous durable-failure loopback, and dropped health events are removed rather than retained behind compatibility aliases.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly required the broad nostrdb-derived redesign rather than preserving JSON-era hot paths; PR #173 is the exact-head implementation and adversarial-review record, with required CI and the remaining device room-open measurement as merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Add the NMP-owned NIP-02 follow action and SwiftUI button ([PR #184](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/184))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #180 (parent #75, safe-edit decision #50) records that an app-owned following Boolean plus an app-composed kind:3 replacement can display stale state and, more seriously, publish a newer partial contact list that silently erases contacts, relay hints, petnames, content, or unrelated tags.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds the optional `nmp-nip02` module, whose ordinary active-account kind:3 demand, source-scoped relationship/availability projection, exact tag-preserving editor, and `set_following` action own NIP-02 semantics without adding kind knowledge to core. `WritePayload` gains `UnsignedReplaceableEdit { unsigned, expected_base }`; store acceptance compares that exact base against the canonical replaceable winner atomically and emits `WriteStatus::ReplaceableConflict` without allocating a durable intent/receipt id or changing the canonical row when it differs. `nmp::Engine` exposes its active account under the existing lifecycle mutex so an action can pin the author and detect a switch. UniFFI adds the following snapshot/action callback vocabulary and `NmpEngine.observe_following`/`follow`/`unfollow`; malformed targets and every operational action failure are streamed state. Swift mirrors those values as `NMPFollowingObservation`, `NMPFollowAction`, and the main-actor `NMPFollowing` adapter; `NMPUI` adds the controlled `NMPFollowButtonBody` plus the connected `NMPFollowButton` in compact, prominent, and icon variants. The iOS-first NIP-02 action is not projected as a Kotlin convenience API in this vertical proof, but Kotlin's exhaustive generic `WriteStatus` mirror gains `ReplaceableConflict(expected, actual)` so the shared UniFFI write surface remains source-compatible and conflict-honest.
- **Persistence impact:** no on-disk schema or epoch change. The exact-base precondition is checked inside each store's existing atomic write-acceptance transaction and is not retained after acceptance; an accepted guarded edit uses the existing durable intent, pending-row, signer, route, attempt, and receipt persistence path unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; following readiness is a bounded projection of the ordinary query's existing per-source `AcquisitionEvidence`, and write progress remains the existing typed receipt stream. `Ready` is explicitly source-scoped, never global completeness. Reconciled absence projects `NoContactList`, and ordinary follow/unfollow refuses to create a first list.
- **Updated falsifiers:** pure editor tests preserve content, full tag order, relay hints, petnames, unrelated tags, and duplicate-target semantics; both memory and redb stores prove exact-base success, generic expected-`None`, regular-event misuse refusal, and concurrent-winner conflict with no journal residue; the engine proves the typed conflict before acceptance; signed-out/no-source/reconciled-no-list cases publish nothing. A real scripted indexer/outbox relay is driven through both direct Rust and the exact iOS FFI path, proving initial canonical state, follow through Accepted/Signed/Routed/Sent/Acked, reactive Following, duplicate no-op, preservation of another contact, unfollow through ACK, reactive NotFollowing, and identical missing-list refusal. Strict clippy is green; 63 Swift tests pass; the iOS Gallery builds/runs and its UI tests prove connected signed-out state plus disabled missing-list and explicit retry accessibility states.
- **Superseded path removed:** `NMPUserCard` no longer accepts an app-maintained `isFollowing` Boolean and follow callback; it accepts the optional live `NMPFollowing` resource. Raw FFI writes cannot mint `UnsignedReplaceableEdit`, and the ordinary NIP-02 editor no longer accepts an absent base, so there is no compatibility route that silently reconstructs or first-creates kind:3.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly required a simple follow action from NMP itself and required that the button not own the logic; issue #180 and draft PR #184 are the review record, with exact-head CI and simulator evidence as merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Add current NIP-46 remote signing and native one-click handoff ([PR #192](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/192))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #6 records that NMP apps could not use a remote signer through the current NIP-46 protocol, while the Olas reference app only discovered signer cards and left the connection action unfinished; review also found that detaching stale session A by pubkey could remove a newer healthy replacement session B for the same account.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds the current kind:24133/NIP-44 NIP-46 RPC client, bunker and invitation flows, signer registration/removal, exact response correlation and signed-event verification, AUTH/auth_url handling, bounded pending work, relay switching, crypto methods, and logout. Signer registration returns an opaque identity lease, so removal is scoped to the exact registration rather than only its pubkey. UniFFI projects the signer catalog, invitations, connection state, one owned connection handle, and deterministic `disconnect`; Swift and Kotlin mirror those values, discover installed Primal handoff targets, start the listener before launching the signer, and retain the connection until explicit close/drop. Android also reports Amber honestly as NIP-55-only and never offers it as a NIP-46 execution path.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema change. An accepted write that awaits its frozen author remains durable across shutdown and restart; attaching the matching NIP-46 signer re-arms that retained intent through the existing journal and receipt path. Remote signer secrets are not persisted by this PR and OS-backed vault restore remains documented as a gap.
- **Diagnostics impact:** per-connection connecting, authorization-required, available, retryable-failure, terminal-failure, and closed states cross FFI to native observers. Permanent lifecycle counters remain tracked by issue #51 rather than being claimed here.
- **Updated falsifiers:** real WebSocket mock-relay coverage proves AUTH, auth_url, signing, NIP-44 crypto, invitation-secret rejection, exact signed-event validation, and malformed-response refusal; a redb restart test proves offline acceptance, restart, real bunker reattachment, exact frozen-author signing, publish, and relay ACK; the registry race registers A, replaces it with B for the same pubkey, detaches A, and proves B remains usable; FFI proves close/drop is idempotent and terminates only its own stream; Swift and Kotlin tests prove truthful discovery, launch targeting, state projection, and owned close.
- **Superseded path removed:** pubkey-only signer removal is replaced by registration-scoped leases, and the engine-retained vector of remote connections is removed; each native connection handle now owns and deterministically disposes exactly one session. The Olas-style detected-but-inert signer card is replaced by a listener-first handoff path for NIP-46-capable Primal targets.
- **Human signoff:** Pablo explicitly requested current NIP-46 support plus Olas-informed local-signer discovery and one-click connection; PR #192 is the review record.

## 2026-07-13 — Make native group messages a typed NMP operation ([PR #196](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/196))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #156 records that the native NIP-29 composer still required Swift/Kotlin to choose an event timestamp and encode raw recipient `p` rows; the repository-owner follow-up expands the consequence to ordinary mentions and replies, where 29er-next#55 otherwise must hand-roll `nostr:npub…` content and reply `e`/`p` rows.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust gains an opt-in `nmp-nip29` `engine` feature with `compose_group_message`, `GroupReplyParent`, and `GroupMessageError`; the default feature set remains engine-free and the existing lower-level kind-blind/raw-tag composer remains the module-author seam. The typed operation accepts only host/group/content/recipients/reply and derives `previous` from the initial canonical snapshot of an ordinary `group_content_demand` forced to `Strict` cache mode for that exact pinned host, so no caller can mint or mutate the provenance input. UniFFI replaces the free `group_send_intent(author, created_at, kind, extra_tags, …)` surface with `NmpEngine::group_message_intent(content, recipient_pubkeys, reply_to, …)`, deriving the active author and Rust-side time and materializing ordered/deduplicated NIP-27 mentions, matching `p` rows, and the marked direct-parent `e` row before the existing `h`/`previous`/pinned-host composer. Swift and Kotlin mirror that operation as `NMPEngine.groupMessageIntent`, expose typed `GroupReplyParent`, retain the opaque take-once `GroupSendIntent`, and map signed-out composition to `NoActiveAccount`/`.noActiveAccount`; neither native API accepts recent rows or relay-source claims.
- **Persistence impact:** none; the semantic operation produces the same existing durable `PinnedHost` write intent, so pending intent, signer, route, attempt, receipt-retention, restart, and canonical event storage formats are unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; host routing and relay acknowledgement remain visible through the existing receipt/query evidence path. The one-frame `previous` snapshot is an ordinary demand with deterministic drop, not a second acquisition mechanism, and adds no diagnostics field.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust proves signed-out refusal, NMP-owned active author/kind/time, ordered mention and recipient deduplication, reply-author notification, exact reply row, pinned-host routing, and that the internal timeline demand is both exact-host pinned and `Strict`. FFI rejects malformed typed inputs, projects the exact semantic template, and proves compose-as-A/switch-to-B yields `Failed` as its first and sole receipt fact with no canonical row, retained receipt, or intent/receipt/meta journal residue; the engine's sole-path counting-signer proof independently asserts that neither account capability is invoked. Swift and Kotlin each publish through generated UniFFI, observe the canonical row through an ordinary NMP query, and assert byte-identical author/kind/content/tags plus NMP-owned current time. Kotlin additionally drives the generated API through real local RFC 6455 relays: one proof observes `OK` as `WriteStatus.Acked` and canonical read-back, while a same-group-id/two-host proof re-delivers distinct valid events from X and Y, then confirms a Y-pinned compose admits Y's engine-recorded event into `previous` and excludes X's. Both native suites retain the take-once publish proof; strict clippy is green, and the default `cargo tree -p nmp-nip29 -e normal` contains no engine/store/router/resolver dependency.
- **Superseded path removed:** the native free composer that accepted caller-selected author, timestamp, kind, and arbitrary tag rows is deleted rather than aliased; its now-unreachable `ReservedGroupTag` FFI/Swift/Kotlin error projection is removed in the same change. Direct Rust modules that deliberately need arbitrary kinds/tags keep only the pre-existing lower-level `compose_group_send` seam.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly required NMP to own typed group-message recipients, replies, and time so 29er-next never writes raw protocol rows; issue #156 and draft PR #196 are the review record, with exact-head adversarial review and required CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Add explicit plaintext local-account autologin ([PR #229](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/229))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #197 and 29er-next issue #56 record that a local account imported through `addAccount` vanished on every engine restart, so an app could not offer automatic login without either persisting the nsec itself or waiting for an unrelated protected-vault integration.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the Rust facade and signer authority remain unchanged; UniFFI adds the read-only `NmpEngine::active_account` projection over that existing authority. Swift adds `NMPInsecureFileAccountStore` and Kotlin adds `InsecureFileAccountStore`, each accepted optionally by its SDK engine constructor. On successful construction the SDK reads its own checkpoint, imports the signer through the ordinary FFI door, activates it in Rust, and exposes only the active public key to the app. Successful local-account import refreshes the checkpoint, and an explicit clear removes it without adding any public read-back API for the secret.
- **Persistence impact:** the opt-in provider writes one plaintext UTF-8 nsec to a separate app-selected file with best-effort private-directory permissions and mandatory owner-only file permissions on POSIX platforms. It never writes the secret to NMP's canonical event/outbox database, does not alter that database's schema, and makes no Keychain, Keystore, hardware-backed, or encrypted-storage claim.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; restoration failures surface as the existing typed invalid-secret or SDK I/O construction error, while the only new observation is the existing Rust-owned active public key.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust proves the active-account projection follows set/clear lifecycle authority and rejects reads after shutdown. Swift and Kotlin each import a fixed test key, shut down, reconstruct from the same checkpoint, observe only its public key, clear it, and prove a later reconstruction is signed out; corrupt checkpoint content fails closed, and the Swift proof also checks owner-only file permissions.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is a deliberately opt-in provider. It does not add an app-local mirror or compatibility route, and it does not claim to supersede the governed secure provider work tracked by issue #47.
- **Human signoff:** Pablo explicitly requested nsec preservation and automatic login without Keychain; PR #229 is the review record for the intentionally plaintext tradeoff.

## 2026-07-13 — Add governed destructive persistent-store reset ([PR #233](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/233))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #232 records a production 29er-next iPhone unable to construct NMP because its retained redb reported `Manual upgrade required. Expected file format version 3, but file is version 6`; reinstalling the app binary preserved the incompatible container, while deleting NMP persistence directly from Swift would violate store ownership.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `Engine::reset_persistent_store` and typed `EngineError::StoreResetFailed`; UniFFI exports the same idempotent operation and error; Swift and Kotlin add `NMPEngine.resetPersistentStore` plus their ergonomic error mirrors. The recovery door is static because it must remain callable when engine construction fails, and its contract requires every engine using the path to be shut down and dropped first.
- **Persistence impact:** reset removes the one requested canonical redb file, thereby clearing cached events, pending writes, receipts, coverage/evidence, and all related persisted engine state. A missing file is already reset and succeeds. Separately configured platform account checkpoints are outside this path and remain untouched.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; reset is an explicit, synchronous lifecycle operation performed while no engine is running, not a new diagnostic stream or recovery loop.
- **Updated falsifiers:** direct Rust and UniFFI tests prove closed-store deletion, idempotent retry, and fresh reopen; Swift and Kotlin tests additionally prove that the separate plaintext local-account checkpoint survives reset and restores the same active public key. The 29er-next consumer proof is tracked by issue #61.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is the first supported destructive recovery door. Native callers still have no app-local cache-invalidation or direct store-deletion path.
- **Human signoff:** Pablo explicitly requested a way to nuke the database and launch the production app on his iPhone while retaining the previously requested automatic login; PR #233 is the exact-head implementation and review record.

## 2026-07-13 — Enforce one global relay admission ceiling ([PR #235](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/235))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #20 and bug-class-ledger #4 recorded that `Router::compile` spent the full relay cap separately for each skeleton and then added indexer, app, fallback, and explicit-pinned lanes outside those solves, so the final executable plan could exceed the claimed whole-demand bound; the public transport door represented capacity/shutdown refusal with an invalid handle, and default `0` could restore unbounded worker growth.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust `nmp-router::RelayPlan` gains exact contextual `limited` coverage keys and diagnostic-only `refused_relays`; `nmp-transport::Pool::ensure_open` now returns `Result<RelayHandle, RelayOpenError>`, exports `RelayOpenError` and `DEFAULT_MAX_RELAYS`, and makes the finite default 10; `nmp::EngineConfig::default` is now explicit so its router and worker limits cannot drift; the UniFFI `NmpEngineConfig.max_relays` record default changes from the interim uncapped `0` to `10`, while an explicitly supplied zero is normalized to the same finite default. Swift and Kotlin source APIs do not change because this remains an optional record field with a generated default.
- **Persistence impact:** none; relay selection, admission, and worker allocation remain in-memory. Durable write lanes already persist their route and retry state before requesting a connection, so a transport-cap refusal stays owned as the existing `WaitingConnection` fact rather than creating a new schema or untracked wire attempt.
- **Diagnostics impact:** `DiagnosticsSnapshot.relays_rejected_over_cap` now combines deterministic router plan-time refusals with the transport backstop's monotonic refusal count. Every read atom whose otherwise-routable source was removed by the whole-demand cap additionally carries `ShortfallFact::LocalLimit`, including partial-source cases, so a selected subset cannot masquerade as complete acquisition.
