- **Updated falsifiers:** two disjoint skeletons share one deterministic cap; additive app and explicit-pinned lanes spend the same budget; zero budget yields an empty executable plan plus exact limit evidence; partially and fully refused atoms project `LocalLimit`; a cap-sized read plan can replace every relay without historical workers stranding the new demand; a nonterminal durable write lane retains a worker after read demand disappears; the public pool returns typed capacity and shutdown refusals; a connected relay outside the current read plan cannot mint replay or capability-probe work; the existing signed kind:10002 admission matrix continues to reject discovered private/local/link-local/`.onion` targets while preserving operator and explicit contextual authority.
- **Superseded path removed:** per-skeleton cap spending, the uncapped-zero default, the public invalid-handle refusal sentinel, and unconditional read capability probing for any connected URL are replaced outright. The already-landed #121 discovered-host admission policy and #123 dead-`RelayLimits` deletion are reused rather than duplicated or aliased.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly assigned issue #20 for end-to-end implementation and authorized exact-head merge after required CI; PR #235 is the review record, with surface governance and all required gates still mandatory.

## 2026-07-14 — Bound engine-owned threads and surface spawn pressure ([PR #448](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/448))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #442 records the hosted Swift resource-pressure failure from PR #411: relay/verifier and native bridge thread spawns could panic on OS `WouldBlock`, and relay close/reopen churn had no exact proof that retired OS threads returned to a finite engine-owned budget.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust makes pool/engine construction, initial query worker acquisition, NIP-02 observer/action workers, and NIP-46 initial relay acquisition fallible through typed `ThreadUnavailable { component, reason }` paths; the prepared NIP-02 action runner lets native code establish its observer before work begins. UniFFI mirrors the engine error and adds `ThreadUnavailable` to terminal follow-action failures; Swift and Kotlin map both shapes natively, while Swift's bunker connection becomes throwing and Kotlin's unchecked exception path is normalized through `nmpRethrowing`.

- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostic schema change; verifier replacement refusal continues to surface through existing verifier-unavailable relay health, while synchronous construction/initial-worker refusal returns the typed error before a query handle escapes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** deterministic injected spawn refusal covers every partial pool-construction stage and relay creation, NIP-02 observer/action worker refusal, native action-bridge refusal before any action side effect, and NIP-46 relay-worker refusal projected to the waiting caller; lifecycle census tests prove exact shutdown baselines; cap-sized churn records the active-plus-retiring peak; FFI mapping preserves safe component/reason text; Swift and Kotlin repeatedly construct and close complete native engines.
- **Superseded path removed:** host-parallelism-sized verifier sets, `.expect`-based engine-owned thread spawns, immediate retirement-budget release, invalid relay-handle sentinels, and panic-prone native observer bridge spawns are removed rather than retained behind aliases; issue #446 honestly tracks the distinct remaining per-call bridge-count bound.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #448 on 2026-07-14; exact-head adversarial review and fresh required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-14 — Bound native observer, action, and signer tasks ([PR #457](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/457))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #446 records that #442 made each native bridge spawn fallible but left the number of simultaneous observer, receipt, diagnostics, action, signer-result, and NIP-46 forwarding tasks proportional to caller-created streams and operations, so the process still had no finite concurrency bound.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds the finite zero-queue `nmp-executor`, `EngineConfig.max_native_tasks` with default 12, and distinct `ExecutorSaturated { component, capacity }` variants across engine, NIP-02 action, and NIP-46 boundaries. UniFFI adds the matching config/error projections plus an exact lifecycle census and idle barrier. Swift and Kotlin expose `maxNativeTasks` and native saturation errors while keeping the census/barrier internal to lifecycle falsifiers. Engine-associated native bridges and NIP-46 sessions share the engine executor; independently constructed direct-Rust NIP-46 sessions each own a separate finite executor.
- **Persistence impact:** none; executor admission, reservations, cancellation signals, task handles, and census state are in-process runtime ownership only. Accepted signer obligations remain durably owned and retryable when a later internal task cannot start.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no query or relay diagnostics schema change. Saturation is a synchronous call or terminal action fact, while the exact native-task census and event-driven idle barrier are lifecycle proof seams rather than scheduler-pressure telemetry.
- **Updated falsifiers:** executor tests prove fallible partial construction, reservation and task-spawn rollback, cap-sized typed refusal with no queue, forgotten-reservation invalidation, active shutdown of idle receivers, panic-safe join accounting, callback-initiated two-phase shutdown, and exact-zero recovery. Rust/FFI tests measure five simultaneous observer/action/receipt drains; a real NIP-46 relay test measures the six-task signing peak and an odd-capacity proof establishes two executor slots per forwardable session. Swift and Kotlin each prove typed cap-one refusal, cancellation, and exact-baseline teardown.
- **Superseded path removed:** unmanaged per-observer/action/signer `thread::spawn` paths, ownership transfer before thread admission, saturation collapsed into OS `ThreadUnavailable`, and NIP-46's unqualified relay/session fan-out are replaced outright; no compatibility alias or hidden accepted-work queue remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in draft PR #457 on 2026-07-14; independent exact-head review, the final log-only delta review, and fresh required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-14 — Add governed sign-only event signing ([PR #466](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/466))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #464 and 29er-next issue #71 record that an embedded NIP-07 browser could not request a signature without abusing the publish pipeline or moving signer authority into Swift.

- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `Engine::sign_event(SignEventRequest)` and a runtime-private `Handle::sign_only` command. The facade freezes the active account under the lifecycle lock, while the runtime supports both immediate and pending governed signer providers on the existing bounded native executor. UniFFI adds author-free `FfiSignEventRequest` and complete `FfiSignedEvent` records; Swift and Kotlin expose `NMPUnsignedEvent`, `NMPSignedEvent`, and `NMPEngine.signEvent`. The signer result must match the requested author, timestamp, kind, tags, and content and pass full event verification before it crosses the public boundary.

- **Diagnostics impact:** none; a pending remote signer consumes one existing bounded native-task slot and returns typed saturation or signer failure directly to the caller.
- **Updated falsifiers:** direct Rust proves signed-out refusal and exact active-account signing with a valid id/signature; the exact `Handle` surface assertion records the new reviewed verb; existing NMP engine and UniFFI suites remain green, and governed Rust/FFI/native snapshots are regenerated.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is the first sign-without-publish door. Native applications still receive no secret material and gain no app-local signer or protocol implementation path.
- **Human signoff:** Pablo explicitly requested NIP-07 signing for embedded chat links while retaining NMP ownership of signing; issue #464 and PR #466 are the implementation and review record.

## 2026-07-14 — Correct governed sign cancellation and native ownership ([PR #469](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/469))

- **Failure evidence:** post-merge audit of issue #464 and PR #466 found that synchronous facade signing held the lifecycle lock across pending signer work, invoked the signer before bounded admission, and exposed no caller cancellation; the first corrective head then charged two executor slots through FFI and let cancellation or shutdown invoke foreign completion outside the admitted worker, race a signer result, and retain a no-hook producer forever.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust now returns a cancellable `SignEventOperation` with typed `SignEventError`, freezes the author under the facade lifecycle lock but releases that lock before runtime admission and signer work, and makes pending signer results selectable against one shared `Open`/`Cancelled`/`Resolved` terminal state. Exactly one admitted worker validates and completes each accepted operation; cancellation only atomically claims, signals that worker, and releases optional adapter work. UniFFI replaces the synchronous result with `SignEventObserver`, `FfiSignEventFailure`, and `NmpSignEventHandle`; Swift exposes an `async throws` cancellation bridge and Kotlin a suspending cancellable bridge, both safe when completion wins before the native handle is installed.
- **Persistence impact:** none; governed signing still creates no write intent, canonical row, pending row, receipt, outbox lane, routing plan, relay attempt, or account-store mutation, and the persistent-store falsifier reopens to an empty canonical query and outbox.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no public diagnostics schema change; executor saturation is rejected synchronously before signer invocation, while the existing internal native-task census and idle barrier prove cancellation and shutdown join every admitted worker back to exact zero.
- **Updated falsifiers:** direct Rust covers cap-one exact-once invocation, capacity refusal before invocation, caller cancellation, shutdown, retained producers with and without adapter hooks, a hook that races by completing the signer, malicious signer output, exact verified bytes, and persistent zero residue. FFI covers cap one, malformed input, closed engine, missing signer, malicious output, caller cancellation, shutdown, exactly-once callback delivery, zero census, and a foreign callback reentering engine verbs and shutdown without deadlock; Swift and Kotlin cover completion before cancellation-handle installation in addition to exact returned fields and empty query state.
- **Superseded path removed:** the synchronous `Handle::sign_only` facade, lifecycle-lock-held signer wait, signer-before-admission ordering, second FFI observer reservation, generic signer-error collapse, independent cancellation booleans, cancellation-thread foreign callbacks, and non-selectable pending result wait are removed outright rather than kept as compatibility aliases.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator authorized the corrective governed-surface workflow in draft PR #469 on 2026-07-14; exact-head review, native gates, strict surface governance, and fresh required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-14 — Close external signer construction and Swift terminal races ([PR #469](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/469))

- **Failure evidence:** exact-head review of PR #469 at `205ea48` found two remaining merge blockers: the advertised external asynchronous signer constructor still required a caller-visible `crossbeam_channel::Receiver`, so an `nmp`-only consumer could not implement it, and Swift tracked cancellation and completion in independent booleans, so cancellation before native-handle installation could lose cancellation, let the callback claim the continuation, and invoke the deferred cancel zero times.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust replaces the receiver-taking `SignerOp::pending` and `pending_with_cancel` constructors with `pending_channel` and `pending_channel_with_cancel`, returning a cloneable NMP-owned `PendingSignerSender` whose typed `resolve` door claims exactly one result without exposing the internal channel implementation. Engine and NIP-46 waiters consume the opaque pending operation through cancellation-biased selection rather than decomposing it into public channel parts. The UniFFI component, Swift public signatures, and Kotlin public signatures do not widen; Swift's internal bridge now has one lock-protected `Open`/`Cancelled`/`Completed` terminal state that owns continuation resumption and late native-handle cancellation exactly once, while the already-correct Kotlin state machine is preserved unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** none; the completion door and terminal state are in-process operation ownership only, and sign-only still creates no write intent, stored event, pending row, receipt, route, relay attempt, or outbox mutation.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no public diagnostics schema change; cancellation, dropped receivers, duplicate resolution, and signer refusal remain typed operation results, while the existing native-task census continues to prove joined worker ownership.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the `nmp`-only consumer crate now implements a genuinely asynchronous external signer using only `nmp` plus the standard library and drives its typed rejection through a real engine. Rust tests prove cloned completion doors accept exactly one result, cancellation wins a simultaneous ready result, retained producers observe receiver drop, and NIP-46 executor accounting remains bounded. Swift deterministically forces cancellation and callback completion before handle installation and repeatedly races simultaneous cancellation against completion, asserting one terminal result and exactly one cancellation call when cancellation wins.
- **Superseded path removed:** public receiver-taking signer constructors, public pending-operation decomposition, direct engine dependence on signer channel parts, and Swift's independent `cancellationRequested`/`completed` booleans are removed outright; no compatibility alias or second completion path remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator authorized resolving the exact-head blockers and completing the corrective surface in PR #469 on 2026-07-14; fresh exact-head review, native gates, strict surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-13 — Add engine-owned one-shot NIP-11 acquisition and capability evidence ([PR #191](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/191))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #185 showed that NMP had no governed way to acquire relay presentation/capability metadata, so each UI consumer would otherwise issue its own NIP-11 HTTP requests and engine capability decisions could only probe blindly. The Applesauce/Welshman comparison also exposed the failure modes to avoid: an unbounded latest-value cache with no freshness contract, a cache whose refresh path can race or overwrite last-good evidence, and an HTTP client that can follow a public relay's redirect into a local service.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds async `Engine::relay_information(relay, policy)` plus typed document/snapshot/freshness/cache-policy/error values. UniFFI mirrors the one-shot as an async `NmpEngine::relay_information`; Swift adds `async throws relayInformation(for:policy:)` and Kotlin adds suspending `relayInformation(relay, policy)`. Executor and per-relay waiter saturation, OS-thread refusal, service closure, credentialed-relay refusal, HTTP failure, size refusal, and invalid documents remain distinct Rust values; UniFFI and both ergonomic wrappers preserve executor/thread/waiter admission as typed errors while other acquisition failures remain explicit unavailable errors. The document carries typed current fields, typed optional advisory limitation claims, exact raw JSON, and structured unknown fragments, so an upstream NIP-11 type cannot truncate the governed contract. A BLAKE3 revision cites the exact received representation. HTTP `ETag`, `Last-Modified`, `Cache-Control`, and `Expires` metadata are projected with saturating interpreted freshness. No `observeRelays`/NIP-11 stream is added: refresh remains an explicit one-shot policy.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema change. The first cache is deliberately process-local and engine-owned. The exact bound is `cached snapshots <= 256`, including the last-good snapshot of every refreshing entry; total entries are at most those 256 cached-bearing entries plus the shared executor's active uncached flights, and waiters are at most active flights times the per-flight waiter cap. Completion evicts only an idle least-recently-used cached entry. If all 256 cached entries are refreshing, a 257th uncached completion is delivered but not retained, preserving both strict cardinality and every refreshing entry's stale-on-error authority. Cross-process persistence remains an explicit known gap rather than an accidental promise.
- **Diagnostics impact:** per-relay diagnostics add the latest advertised `supported_nips`, its document revision/freshness/last-refresh-error provenance, an NIP-77 advertisement state (`unknown`/`advertised_supported`/`advertised_unsupported`), and an independently sourced behavioral state (`unknown`/`probing`/`behaviorally_proven`/`behaviorally_rejected`). NIP-11 resolution emits diagnostics immediately. Reducer evidence is retained only for relays in the current read plan and pruned when that plan changes; freshness is derived from the engine clock and the cited document's `fresh_until`, so it becomes stale at the deadline without another acquisition. Advertisement never mints the opaque `ProbedRelay` token`; only a real NEG response can produce behavioral proof.
- **Updated falsifiers:** concurrent same-relay refreshes share one exact-generation flight; dropping the last async waiter cancels only that generation and late completion cannot overwrite or drain its replacement; waiter and executor saturation are typed and retain no refused intent; service close resolves every admitted waiter; 256 refreshing cached entries retain every last-good snapshot while a 257th completion is delivered without retention and cannot become an unbounded reducer shadow cache; a hostile maximum `max-age` saturates without panic, stranded flight, or failed retry; fresh hits and stale-on-error remain exact, and no-cache, zero-fresh 304, and already-expired responses are stale at delivery. Redirect targets observe no connection, credentialed relay URLs produce a typed refusal before any TCP request or Basic `Authorization` header, an empty userinfo marker is normalized to a credential-free typed URL, a 503 produces exactly one request, a slow-drip body stops under the total deadline, a local DNS-protocol fixture proves the configured Hickory resolver path without system GAI, and a held DNS request is cancelled and joined at shutdown. The current-thread runtime has no Tokio worker pool. A real engine with live HTTP, retained engine handle, subscription, and cancellation token shuts down below five seconds and returns the native census to exact zero. The independent engine-loop 250 ms fallback and eventual evidence are generation-tested. macOS-host Swift tests and desktop-JVM Kotlin tests exercise public wrapper saturation; the generated XCFramework simulator slices compile, but iOS Simulator runtime qualification needs the missing test harness tracked by [#465](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/465). Android/AAR remains explicitly unqualified.
- **Superseded path removed:** unconditional immediate NIP-77 probing on every first connection is replaced by a cache-informed one-shot decision with an independent 250 ms engine-loop grace and behavioral fallback; the raw eight-thread NIP-11 pool, 64-job queue, unbounded waiter vector, non-cancellable system DNS, URL-userinfo-derived Basic authorization, automatic HTTP redirects, and synchronous native NIP-11 call surface are removed. There is no parallel app-owned cache, compatibility alias, polling loop, reconnect-triggered forced refresh, or reactive NIP-11 stream.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested the design issue, then requested “implement, test, PR” for #185 in this session; draft PR #191 is the exact-head review record, with required CI and exact-head review remaining merge gates.
## 2026-07-14 — Project truthful durable-write retry states across every SDK ([PR #411](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/411))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #96 records that durable write receipts were silent while an intent waited for relay connectivity or relay authentication, and that `Sent` lacked the persisted handoff attempt and timestamp needed to prove the claim; applications therefore could not distinguish an ordinary retryable wait from an ambiguous transport handoff, including after restart and receipt reattachment.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust `WriteStatus` gains `AwaitingRelay { relay }`, `AwaitingAuth { relay }`, `RetryEligible { relay, attempt, eligible_at }`, and `HandoffAmbiguous { relay, attempt, observed_at }`; `Sent` gains the required durable attempt ordinal and persisted `written_at` timestamp. The engine emits and replays those exact facts from its governed outbox state. UniFFI mirrors every case and field, and the Swift and Kotlin SDK enums map them exhaustively without collapsing them into query `SourceStatus` or inventing platform-local retry state.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema or epoch change; the existing persisted outbox attempts and `Written` handoff records are now projected truthfully. `Sent` is emitted only from a persisted `Written` record and always names that record's ordinal; an ephemeral handoff has no outbox record and cannot emit `Sent`. The pre-handoff and ambiguous states derive from the already-governed retry lane and survive restart through the existing journal replay path.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; these are per-intent write-receipt facts. Query acquisition `SourceStatus` and the diagnostics schema remain unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** engine tests prove offline and authentication waits, exact retry deadlines and attempts, persisted-Written-only `Sent`, ambiguous handoff timing, restart replay, and reattachment equivalence; parity drives every new direct-Rust case through the actual FFI converter; Swift and Kotlin mapping tests exhaustively assert every payload field. Full Rust tests, strict clippy, 74 Swift tests, and the Kotlin Gradle suite pass.
- **Superseded path removed:** the relay-only tuple form of `Sent`, the receipt-silent relay/authentication/retry/ambiguous paths, and the projection that could call an unpersisted or ambiguous handoff `Sent` are replaced outright; no optional-attempt representation, compatibility alias, or platform-local fallback state remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed the orchestrator to complete the open NMP work and retain exact-head review gates; draft PR #411 is the implementation and review record, with adversarial review and required CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-14 — Add controlled native relay identity primitives ([PR #476](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/476))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #198 records that the engine-owned NIP-11 value from #185 had no reusable native presentation boundary, so applications would independently reinterpret missing metadata, stale-last-good evidence, acquisition failure, and query-scoped relay runtime status or let a view silently acquire its own network/cache ownership.
- **Changed projections:** kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust and UniFFI are unchanged. Swift adds immutable relay presentation/information/runtime states plus controlled `NMPRelayIcon`, `NMPRelayName`, `NMPRelayDescription`, `NMPRelayRuntimeStatus`, and `NMPRelayListEntry` views to the optional `NMPUI` product. Kotlin adds the semantically matching `com.nmp.ui` types and composables in a separate optional desktop-JVM Compose child project. NMP's Kotlin core SDK remains Compose-free; neither Kotlin project claims Android/AAR qualification. Both UI projections expose advertised icon text but accept only an already-resolved native SwiftUI `Image` or Compose `Painter`.
- **Persistence impact:** none; both view families are immutable caller-controlled projections. They add no cache, store, engine handle, network client, polling loop, timer, image loader, or reconnect owner.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. The runtime badge consumes only optional query-scoped `SourceStatus`; it does not invent URL-global connected, authenticated, healthy, or reconnecting state. One-shot NIP-11 freshness and last-error evidence remain separate, and available stale-last-good content remains renderable while no-snapshot failure is explicitly unavailable.
- **Updated falsifiers:** 89 Swift package tests pass, including six relay UI tests covering trimmed metadata and deterministic host/description/initial fallbacks, loading, fresh versus stale-last-good, unavailable with a reason, nil/blank unavailable defaults, every projected `SourceStatus`/`AuthPhase`, construction of every controlled leaf/list component with a non-nil caller-supplied `Image`, exact pure list accessibility-label composition, and structural absence of engine/HTTP/timer/loader ownership. The 55-test Kotlin core suite is green and the separate Compose child adds six matching state/construction tests using a non-null desktop `ColorPainter`, plus pure label-model coverage and a successful `:ui:build`; structural checks prove Compose dependencies remain absent from the core SDK. These are pure state/construction assertions, not a rendered accessibility-tree qualification.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is the first relay identity component family. It composes the one-shot contract from #185 without adding a reactive relay-information stream or global relay-health model.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approved the exact issue #198 surface contract for review in draft PR #476 on 2026-07-14; strict PR-context governance and independent exact-head review remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-15 — Refuse reset of a live persistent store ([PR #497](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/497))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #489 (a follow-on to the #233 governed reset) showed `Engine::reset_persistent_store` deleted the store file with no enforcement that the store was closed. The "caller must shut down and drop every engine on this path first" precondition existed only as a Rust doc comment and degraded to nothing by the app-facing Swift (`Engine.swift`) and Kotlin (`Engine.kt`) surfaces, and redb takes no OS-level file lock, so resetting a live store was a reachable data-corruption path with no accidental safety net.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds a process-local canonical-path registry (`nmp-store::persistent_store_lifetime`) whose registration spans pre-open through fully-joined teardown, carried as an RAII guard moved with the owning store; `RedbStore::reset`/`Engine::reset_persistent_store` take the same registry lock as `open` and return the new typed `EngineError::StoreStillOpen` instead of deleting a live store. UniFFI mirrors it as `FfiError::StoreStillOpen`; Swift adds `.storeStillOpen` and Kotlin adds `StoreStillOpen`, exhaustively mapped with Display parity. A genuinely-closed store still resets and a missing path still succeeds; no public signature widens beyond the added error case.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema or epoch change. The guard is in-process open/reset exclusion only; it adds no on-disk state. Cross-process exclusion is explicitly NOT provided (redb takes no OS advisory/exclusive lock) and is recorded as a known gap; external symlink retargeting of the store path is likewise not an in-process guarantee.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; the refusal is a typed operation error, not a diagnostics-schema change.
- **Updated falsifiers:** store-layer tests drive both interleavings (reset-during-open refuses; open-during-reset creates fresh bytes after deletion), exact two-owner refcount release, registry-poison recovery, and existing/dangling symlink resolution; engine tests prove live refusal with bytes intact then shutdown-then-reset succeeds, closed reset, missing reset, drop-releases, and failed-open release; the FFI and Swift/Kotlin layers prove the typed refusal and file-intact behavior end to end; a raw-runtime test proves `EngineThread` holds the guard until join. Built on an isolated cargo target: `nmp-store`/`nmp`/`nmp-ffi`/`nmp-engine` tests green, strict clippy clean, snapshots regenerated (facade 29,788 lines, under ceiling).
- **Superseded path removed:** the unguarded `remove_file` reset and the documentation-only precondition are replaced by a typed refusal enforced by the registry; no doc-only precondition or bypass path remains. Cross-process locking remains a tracked follow-up, not a silent promise.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner delegated backlog orchestration to this session (2026-07-15); this fix was recovered intact from the prior orchestrator's local branch, independently opus-reviewed (APPROVE, no blockers), and passed isolated-target build/clippy/tests. Draft PR #497 is the review record; PR review and required CI, including strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

- **Failure evidence:** the #485 architecture sweep found FFI error erasure concentrated at the external-service seams. NIP-46 connection failures collapsed the `Nip46Error` variants via `.to_string()` (`crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs`), and NIP-11 relay-information errors were stringified, while sibling features (`FfiFollowActionFailure`, `FfiSignEventFailure`) preserve discriminants — so an SDK consumer could not branch on the failure kind.

Three provably-dead `Nip46Error` variants (`InvalidRelay`/`InvalidInvitation`/`SecretMismatch`, zero construction sites) are removed, shrinking the re-exported facade; `InvalidLaunchScheme` is retained (3 live sites). Swift and Kotlin project identical variant sets via the established error idiom; presentation `lastError` stays `String?` rendered from the typed value. The engine-attach converter distinguishes `EngineClosed` (→ `Disconnected`) from the signer-missing-key case rather than collapsing both.

- **Updated falsifiers:** the existing Kotlin `RelayInformationTest` is updated to assert the typed `.kind` rather than the old `.reason` string. `cargo fmt --check`, `clippy --workspace -D warnings`, and `cargo test --workspace` pass on an isolated target; surface regenerated (FFI component +382, facade −52, 29,734 lines under ceiling). Swift/Kotlin projection builds are verified by the swift-package/kotlin-package CI jobs.
- **Superseded path removed:** the `.to_string()` NIP-46 failure collapse, the stringified NIP-11 relay-information error at both seams, and the 3 dead `Nip46Error` variants are removed; no stringly-typed erasure remains at these two seams.

## 2026-07-15 — Cross-SDK parity: Kotlin checkpoint dir perms + NostrContentPolicy clamp ([PR #509](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/509))

- **Failure evidence:** the #507 discovery sweep found two Swift↔Kotlin divergences. (a) Swift hardens the insecure-checkpoint directory to owner-only `0o700` (`InsecureFileAccountStore.swift:35-40`) but Kotlin created it with the JVM default mode — group/world-readable on a multi-user host. (b) `NostrContentPolicy` silently clamps out-of-range bounds on Swift (`max(1,…)`/`max(0,…)`) but threw a bare `require(...)` `IllegalArgumentException` on Kotlin — same constructor, same input, opposite observable behavior.
- **Changed projections:** kotlin
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust/FFI/Swift unchanged. Kotlin `InsecureFileAccountStore` now sets `OWNER_READ|WRITE|EXECUTE` on the checkpoint directory after `createDirectories`, inside the existing `catch (UnsupportedOperationException)` idiom (graceful on non-POSIX filesystems), matching Swift's `0o700`. Kotlin `NostrContentPolicy` now clamps all four bounds (`coerceAtLeast(1/1/0/0)`, including the signed-`Long` `releaseGraceMilliseconds`) to match Swift's `max(...)`, via a private primary constructor + companion `invoke` that preserves the exact public signature and defaults; `@ConsistentCopyVisibility` makes the generated `copy()` private so it cannot bypass the clamp. No public signature changes — all existing call sites compile unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** none; the directory-permission change hardens the same on-disk checkpoint layout, no schema/format change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/build-kotlin-jvm.sh` + `./gradlew test` on JDK 17 build successfully with the full Kotlin suite green and no compiler warnings (the previously-emitted exposed-`copy()` warning is silenced by `@ConsistentCopyVisibility`). Existing `ContentSessionTest` and `InsecureFileAccountStoreTest` pass unchanged.
- **Superseded path removed:** the Kotlin `require(...)`-throw path on `NostrContentPolicy` and the unhardened checkpoint-directory creation are replaced; no locale/host-dependent divergence from Swift remains at these two points.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner delegated backlog orchestration to this session (2026-07-15); both fixes were independently opus-reviewed (clamp + copy-visibility gaps caught and fixed) and passed the JDK-17 Kotlin build/test suite. Draft PR #509 is the review record; PR review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-15 — Omit unsafe NIP-29 previous tags from typed group messages ([PR #516](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/516))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #515 records a real 29er-next iPhone write rejection from `nip29.f7z.io`. The typed composer consumed the first pinned `Strict` cache frame before any live relay result and treated historical provenance as current recency. Croissant accepts `previous` prefixes only from its rolling last 50 `h`-tagged events, so a stale cached row could produce `previous id ... wasn't found in this relay` and prevent an otherwise valid group message from being published.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the direct-Rust typed `compose_group_message` operation now passes empty `GroupTimelineEvidence` to the existing lower-level composer and therefore emits no optional `previous` tag. The lower-level explicit Rust `compose_group_send` seam and its validated caller-supplied evidence remain unchanged. UniFFI signatures and types are unchanged; its method/error documentation and checksum now describe event-time refresh and temporary omission. Swift and Kotlin retain the same `groupMessageIntent` signatures and opaque take-once intent, with documentation corrected to the actual behavior.
- **Persistence impact:** none; no store, row, receipt, intent, routing, attempt, or schema shape changes. Cached rows remain canonical query state but no longer influence the typed message template's optional timeline hint.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; pinned-host routing, write statuses, and query evidence remain unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust proves the typed operation emits no `previous`; the existing Kotlin two-host local-relay test still seeds relay-delivered canonical rows on both hosts, then proves neither cached prefix appears in the next Y-pinned wire event. Focused engine-feature Rust tests, FFI NIP-29 tests, strict NIP-29 clippy, governed snapshot regeneration, the Kotlin host build, and the exact Kotlin regression are green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the cache-only `trusted_previous`/`trusted_timeline_demand` path and its misleading exact-host/`Strict` proof are removed outright. There is no app-controlled suppression flag or Swift/Kotlin raw-tag workaround; trustworthy live-window composition remains an explicit known gap.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner diagnosed the iPhone failure in 29er-next Mosaico message `521bcf` and explicitly directed omission until NMP has a real fix in message `531c51`; issue #515 and draft PR #516 are the review record, with required CI and exact-head review remaining merge gates.

Uses the existing `ReentrantLock`/`withLock` idiom.

- **Diagnostics impact:** none.
