## 2026-07-15 — Windowing becomes a policy on the read noun: `observe(query, window)` ([#485](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/485), [PR #531](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/531), supersedes the #474 History* surface)

- **Failure evidence:** the #485 architecture review concluded the #474/#484 public shape — a parallel `History*` noun (`HistoryQuery`/`HistorySubscription`/`HistoryBatch`/`HistoryContinuation`/`HistoryLoadFact`/`HistoryLoadError`/`HistoryQueryError`/`HistoryAdvance`) — was a modeling error independent of cost. Delivery is not a free axis (unbounded live queries MUST deliver deltas — full-set redelivery is the confirmed O(rows²) P0; bounded windows CAN deliver snapshots because `max` makes each frame O(cap)), so `Unbounded + Snapshots` is an illegal state that two hard-coded nouns leave as tribal knowledge. The opaque `HistoryContinuation` carried zero information (a matching generation already implied the engine-computed boundary) and bumped on every idle rebalance, so a busy feed routinely handed the app a token that was already stale → spurious `StaleGeneration` retry-loops. Four of ten `HistoryLoadError` variants were unreachable through the supported API. Separately, #486: a windowed session leaked one live relay REQ per advance (tie-second + older-range) until teardown — `2*(max/page)+1` concurrent REQs per relay for a fully-scrolled feed, enough to trip a relay's per-connection subscription cap.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the `History*` public noun collapses into the existing read noun. Rust: `Engine::observe(query, window: Option<Window>)` (was `observe(query)` + a separate `observe_history`); one `Subscription` for both modes whose `recv`/`recv_timeout` return `Frame { deltas, window: Option<WindowContents { rows, load }>, evidence }`; growth is the monotonic, idempotent `Subscription::request_rows(at_least)` / cloneable `WindowHandle::request_rows` (replacing `load_older(continuation)`); new pinned enums `Window::Expandable { initial: NonZeroUsize, max: NonZeroUsize }`, `WindowLoad::{Idle,Requesting,Returned{added},AtBound{max}}`, `RequestRowsError::{Unwindowed,EngineClosed,StoreUnavailable,TransportUnavailable{reason}}` (all `#[non_exhaustive]`); `EngineError` gains `WindowInitialExceedsMax{initial,max}` + `WindowSelectionHasLimit`. Deleted: `observe_history`, `HistorySubscription`, `HistoryAdvance`, `HistoryBatch`, `HistoryContinuation`, `HistoryLoadError`, `HistoryLoadFact`, `HistoryQuery`, `HistoryQueryError`, and the public `RowsMsg` re-export. FFI mirrors this: `FfiFrame`/`FfiWindow`/`FfiWindowContents`/`FfiWindowLoad`/`FfiRequestRowsError` replace `FfiHistory*`/`NmpHistoryContinuation`/`NmpHistoryHandle`/`HistoryObserver`; `RowObserver` becomes `on_frame(FfiFrame)`+`on_closed`; `observe`/`observe_demand` take a required `window: Option<FfiWindow>` argument (between query and observer); `NmpQueryHandle::request_rows(at_least)`; `FfiError`'s six `History*` validation variants become `WindowZeroRows`/`WindowInitialExceedsMax{initial,max}`/`WindowSelectionHasLimit`. Swift adds `Window`/`WindowLoad`/`NMPRequestRowsError`, `NMPEngine.observe(_,window:)`, `NMPQuery.requestRows(atLeast:)`, `RowBatch.load: WindowLoad?`; Kotlin adds `Window`/`WindowLoad`/`NMPRequestRowsError`, `observe(filter/demand, window)` returning an `NMPQuery` with `requestRows(atLeast)`, `RowBatch.load: WindowLoad?`. Delivery mode is DERIVED from boundedness everywhere: unbounded frames carry deltas and `window == None/nil/null`; windowed frames carry the full authoritative row set in `window` and ship EMPTY wire deltas (a row never crosses UniFFI twice). `AtBound` is a delivered `WindowLoad` fact, never a thrown error.
- **Persistence impact:** none. The salvaged engine/store cursor scans (`query_newest_before`, `EventCursor`), tie-second acquisition, staging/commit/rollback, and live rebalance from #484 are kept byte-for-byte; only the public projection and the continuation/generation plumbing changed. `max` is an active-window bound, not a retention/GC signal (#459 unaffected).
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; acquisition evidence still rides on every frame and windowed acquisition still records coverage at EOSE.
- **Updated falsifiers:** same-second tie walk now driven by declarative `request_rows` across ≥3 pages (no gap/duplicate); new proofs that `request_rows` is monotonic/idempotent/clamped, that `AtBound` is a fact not an error, that an in-flight raise converges after commit, and that unbounded `request_rows` returns `Unwindowed`; #486 falsifier `deep_scroll_holds_bounded_live_subscriptions_per_relay` drives 40 advances and asserts the net open REQ set per relay stays ≤3 (peaks at 81 without the fix). FFI/Swift/Kotlin tests assert windowed frames replace state with empty deltas, unbounded frames still fold deltas, and typed error/`WindowLoad` parity across layers. Orchestrator-verified: full workspace builds, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean, all Rust suites green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the entire `History*` public noun, the opaque `HistoryContinuation`/`NmpHistoryContinuation` token, the `load_older(continuation)`/generation/`StaleGeneration` machinery, the wire `deltas` on bounded frames, and the collapsed `HistoryLoadError` variants are deleted outright — no compatibility alias, no `observe_history` shim, no host-built cursor. The #486 REQ leak is closed by retiring each prior advance's historical acquisitions at the next commit (keeping only the live-top demand plus the current advance's handles; `acquired_tie_seconds` retained as coverage evidence).
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #531 on 2026-07-15 (delegated per issue #485); the branch was produced by coordinated subagents and independently opus-reviewed, and the orchestrator re-verified fmt/clippy/full-test-suite green and regenerated the `docs/surface/` snapshots on an isolated target after rebasing onto current master. Exact-head adversarial review and required CI, including strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-15 — Access-scoped relay session identity (NIP-42 U1 foundation) ([#8](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/8), [PR #539](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/539))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #8 / bug-class ledger #18 — NIP-42 visibility is connection-scoped, but relay identity across planning, wire sub-ids, attribution, replay, evidence, coverage, and pool admission was keyed by URL alone. Public, account A, and account B reusing one URL-keyed session could credit one identity's acquired work to another, or replay-protect work under stale authority. This is the foundation unit (U1): it closes the session-identity core only — no AUTH reducer, no policy/signer capability surface, no app-facing AUTH API (those are later NIP-42 waves).
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust: `nmp_grammar::AccessContext` gains a second, closed variant `Nip42(nostr::PublicKey)` (a frozen expected pubkey; changing the engine's active account never redirects it) — `Public` stays the only unauthenticated context. New internal identity type `nmp_grammar::RelaySessionKey { relay, access }` becomes the key for router `RelayPlan.reqs`/`refused_sessions`, transport pool slots/admission/events, and every engine read/attribution/coverage/write-lane lookup; it is deliberately NOT re-exported through the `nmp` facade (it is engine-internal plumbing, absent from both governed baselines). Facade projection changes: `SourceEvidence` and `RelayDiagnosticsSnapshot` each gain an `access: AccessContext` field, and `DiagnosticsSnapshot::relays_rejected_over_cap` is renamed `sessions_rejected_over_cap` (the finite `DEFAULT_MAX_RELAYS` ceiling now counts physical sessions, not URL strings). FFI mirrors exactly: `FfiAccessContext` drops `Copy` and gains `Nip42 { public_key: String }` (hex); `FfiSourceEvidence`/`FfiRelayDiagnostics` gain `access: FfiAccessContext`; `FfiDiagnosticsSnapshot.relays_rejected_over_cap` → `sessions_rejected_over_cap`; `access_context_from_ffi` becomes fallible (hex parse). Swift/Kotlin hand-written wrappers under `Packages/` are updated in this PR: Swift `NMPAccessContext` gains a `nip42(publicKey:)` case; Kotlin `NMPAccessContext` becomes a sealed class (`Public` object + `Nip42(publicKey)`); `SourceEvidence`/`RelayDiagnostics` surface `access` on both platforms (Swift `RelayDiagnostics.id` folds access so two sessions on one URL don't collide). The cap rename has no wrapper impact (neither platform surfaced that field).
- **Persistence impact:** none. Coverage rows remain `(coverage_key, relay_url)`; because `coverage_key` already folds `AccessContext` via `fold_context` (Public = tag byte 0, Nip42 = tag byte 1 ++ 32-byte pubkey), the same URL under two contexts yields distinct rows with no schema change. No store, receipt, intent, or journal layout changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** per-session (not per-URL) diagnostics — three contexts on one relay produce three diagnostics rows carrying their `access`; the over-cap counter counts refused sessions. NIP-11 enrichment and negentropy/probe diagnostics remain Public-session-only.
- **Updated falsifiers:** new `nmp-router` `tests/session_context.rs` and `nmp-transport` `tests/session_context.rs` (three contexts on one URL → three plans/sub-ids/coverage keys; admission cap counts sessions, third context refused `AtCapacity`); new engine `relay_session_key_tests` (wrong-context EOSE cannot credit another session; disconnect of A preserves Public and B; protected REQs park until exact-generation AUTH-ready; protected neg frames cannot resolve the Public probe; intentional close never reopens). Engine external headless suites re-keyed to sessions. All touched-crate suites green on an isolated target (nmp-grammar/router/transport/engine/ffi/nip02/signer). An independent adversarial identity-isolation review (ledger #18) found all six isolation invariants HOLD with no defects.
- **Superseded path removed:** the URL-only relay-identity keys (`url_to_slot`, URL-keyed plan/attribution/evidence/coverage/admission lookups) are replaced outright by `RelaySessionKey`; the prior single-variant "reserved slot" `AccessContext` comment is removed. No compatibility alias and no URL-only fallback remain on the read/attribution/coverage/admission path (the surviving URL-keyed public pool methods `ensure_open`/`live_handle` are thin `Public`-session wrappers retained only for the NIP-46 signer transport).
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #539 on 2026-07-15 (delegated Wave-1 orchestration for issue #8). Produced by coordinated subagents and independently opus-reviewed for session isolation (all six ledger-#18 invariants SOUND). The orchestrator regenerated the governed facade + FFI snapshots and re-verified `fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`/full workspace test/Swift package/Kotlin package all green on an isolated target after rebasing onto current master. A real auth-required-relay proof and the AUTH reducer/policy surface remain later waves. Exact-head review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — NIP-42 AUTH reducer + epoch state machine (Wave 2) ([#8](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/8), [PR #541](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/541))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #8 — after Wave 1 (#539) closed the session-identity core, relay AUTH was still parsed-and-dropped: an auth-required content relay could park a durable write forever, and an authenticated write session had no machinery to actually authenticate. Wave 2 (U2) lands the AUTH reducer only — challenge epochs, the frozen kind:22242 template, and authenticated `Nip42(signing_pubkey)` write sessions — with no app-facing policy/registry surface yet (that is Wave 3).
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust facade (`nmp`): `AuthPhase` gains `AwaitingChallenge` and `AwaitingRelayAck`, and `SourceStatus::{AwaitingAuth, AuthDenied, Error}` are now populated (no shape change). The public auth-DIAGNOSTICS read-out (`AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot`/`AuthDiagnosticsPhase` types and `DiagnosticsSnapshot.auth_sessions`) is DEFERRED to Wave 3 — it lands together with its FFI projection and the policy API rather than shipping the facade half alone; the reducer's internal per-session auth-phase state machine is fully present, just not yet surfaced. Internally the engine reintroduces `Nip42(signing_pubkey)` write sessions at the nine U1 write sites that Wave 1 had reverted to `Public`; a protected write parks only on reducer-known non-Ready auth truth for the exact session (an unchallenged session sends optimistically and an `OK false auth-required` / `AUTH` frame then parks it), and the accepted-then-reparked attempt is never durably accepted unauthenticated. FFI (`nmp-ffi` component): `FfiAuthPhase` gains `AwaitingChallenge` + `AwaitingRelayAck` only — there is deliberately NO `FfiDiagnosticsSnapshot.auth_sessions` mirror this wave (the facade auth-diagnostics projection is Wave 3), so `check-sdk-parity.sh` has no cross-SDK gap. Swift (`Row.swift`, `NMPUI/RelayViews.swift`) and Kotlin (`Row.kt`, `ui/RelayViews.kt`) hand-written wrappers gain the two new `AuthPhase` cases + their display labels; generated UniFFI bindings are gitignored build artifacts regenerated at build time.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store/receipt/lane schema change. Ordinary kind:22242 writes are rejected pre-acceptance and boot-quarantined (`RetainedButUnreadable`); no new durable layout.
- **Diagnostics impact:** `SourceStatus` AUTH states (`AwaitingAuth`/`AuthDenied`/`Error`) are now reached in evidence. The dedicated per-session AUTH diagnostics read-out is deferred to Wave 3 (see impact). No diagnostics schema removal.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the `auth_core_headless` falsifier set (epoch supersession, AUTH-OK vs durable-ACK disjointness, 9-mutation frozen-template, timestamp monotonicity/future-window, counter exhaustion, capability-instance invalidation, teardown) plus two new missed-wake guards (`parked_auth_write_is_redriven_across_reconnect_not_wedged`, `boot_recovers_parked_auth_write_as_redrivable_not_wedged`); `nmp-parity` gains `auth_required_relay_parks_write_identically_direct_and_ffi` (the authenticated-write `AwaitingRelay` beat is now in the shared direct==FFI write oracle, byte-identical, with a real relay challenge). Independent opus adversarial review of all eight invariants: one MED missed-wakeup found (invariant 7) and fixed; re-verified no unresolved defects.
- **Superseded path removed:** Wave 1's `RelayAuthReady` seam is removed — its replay/wake/idempotence folds into `finish_auth_ok`; the U1 write-plane `Public` revert is replaced by real `Nip42` authenticated write sessions.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #541 on 2026-07-16 (delegated Wave-2 orchestration for issue #8). Produced by coordinated subagents; an independent opus reviewer attacked all eight AUTH-state-machine invariants at source level (verdict: no unresolved defects after the invariant-7 fix). The orchestrator regenerated the governed facade + FFI snapshots and re-verified `fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`/full workspace test (the two `relay_ingest_smoke` failures are the pre-existing macOS sandbox flake [#538], reproduced identically on pristine master)/Swift package/Kotlin package on an isolated target. Two latent items (u64::MAX epoch sentinel relies on the phase guard; baseline adapter must bind-on-request) are flagged for the Wave-3 registry. Exact-head review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — NIP-42 runtime capability binding + real-relay AUTH capstone (Wave 3) ([#8](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/8), [PR #542](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/542))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #8 — Waves 1–2 landed session identity and the AUTH reducer, but nothing could yet register an auth policy or prove an authenticated content-relay read end to end. Wave 3 (U4+U8+U10) lands the runtime capability registry, the shutdown self-wait fix, and a real-relay AUTH capstone. This reaches the milestone (authenticated read works E2E at the engine level); the app-facing facade/FFI policy API remains Wave 5.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** No new `nmp` facade API and no FFI/Swift/Kotlin type changes. The ONLY governed facade-snapshot change is a single line — `DiagnosticsSnapshot`'s `has_stripped_fields` flips `false→true` — because the engine-level `DiagnosticsSnapshot.auth_sessions` read-out (consumed by the capstone/falsifiers) is `#[doc(hidden)]`: exposing it as a documented public field would pull `AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot`/`AuthDiagnosticsPhase` shape-JSON into the snapshot and breach the 30,000-line facade ceiling, so the documented auth-diagnostics projection stays deferred to Wave 5 (exactly as U2 deferred it). Engine-level (non-facade) surface grew: `AuthPolicy`/`AuthPolicyOp`/`AuthPolicyRegistration`/`RuntimeConfig`/`Handle::{add,remove}_auth_policy` (recorded by the runtime_integration closed-Handle-surface test). Two new engine error variants map internally to the existing `EngineError::EngineClosed`. **Behavioral (no type change):** every durable write on a cold protected session now emits one deterministic `AwaitingAuth` receipt beat between `AwaitingRelay` and `Sent` (bounded AUTH discovery); the `nmp-parity` oracle is updated on both direct and FFI paths (byte-identical), and any SDK consumer that pattern-matches an exact write-receipt sequence should expect the extra beat.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store/receipt/lane schema change; the AUTH probe/park machinery and stickiness are in-memory only.
- **Diagnostics impact:** `SourceStatus` AUTH states are reached; the dedicated per-session AUTH diagnostics read-out remains `#[doc(hidden)]`/deferred to Wave 5. No diagnostics schema removal.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the AUTH capstone — a real strict-WebSocket loopback relay with a wrong-challenge oracle self-test — proves a depth-2 authenticated read (`follows_minus_mutes_resolves_over_a_real_relay`, `connections==1`/`pre_auth_reqs==0`), denial parking (`auth_policy_denial_keeps_real_relay_work_parked`), and reconnect-fresh-challenge (`reconnect_requires_a_fresh_real_relay_challenge`); a new `auth_sequence_counter_reserves_the_sentinel_and_exhaustion_fails_closed`; the reconnect/boot park-then-release guards strengthened to exactly-once re-drive; `nmp-parity` 7/7 incl. the AUTH-park beat on direct+FFI. Independent opus adversarial review of all 8 lifecycle/leak invariants: PASS ("unusually defense-in-depth… no correctness holes"); LOW/INFO findings only (a forever-blocking app cancel hook can wedge `EngineThread::join` — documented in known-gaps).
- **Superseded path removed:** an unauthenticated reconnect no longer re-drives a write a relay refused pre-auth — only the fresh generation's AUTH-OK does (matches issue #8's ratified text); the donor's on-every-message `required_relay_workers()` store read in `prune_unowned_relay_state` is gated on non-empty prune sets.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #542 on 2026-07-16 (delegated Wave-3 orchestration for issue #8). Produced by coordinated subagents; an independent opus reviewer attacked all eight lifecycle/leak invariants at source level plus a five-delta re-verification (verdict: PASS, no correctness holes). The orchestrator regenerated the governed snapshots (facade 29,957/30,000, FFI component byte-identical) and re-verified `fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`/full workspace test (the two `relay_ingest_smoke` failures are the pre-existing macOS flake [#538], reproduced identically on clean master)/Swift package/Kotlin package on an isolated target. Exact-head review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — NIP-42 app-facing facade auth surface (Wave 5) ([#8](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/8), closes [#495](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/495), [PR #543](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/543))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #8 — Waves 1–3 made an authenticated read work end to end at the ENGINE level, but the supported `nmp` facade could not express it: no way to register an `AuthPolicy`, no `remove_account` inverse (#495 — a registered local key was permanently resident for the process lifetime), and the per-session AUTH diagnostics read-out stayed `#[doc(hidden)]` because re-exporting its engine types would have breached the PROTECTED 30,000-line facade-snapshot ceiling (29,957 before this change). Wave 5 (U9) projects the frozen runtime surface onto the facade by OWNING the types instead of re-exporting them.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust only; the FFI component snapshot is byte-identical and Swift/Kotlin are untouched (the FFI/SDK projection is the next wave). Three facade additions, all defined IN `crates/nmp` (the `bc8fb97` "own public facade values" pattern — an `nmp`-defined item costs ~1 snapshot line, a dependency re-export expands to full rustdoc shape-JSON): **(1) Facade-owned diagnostics family** — `DiagnosticsSnapshot`/`RelayDiagnosticsSnapshot`/`FilterCoverageEntry` are no longer engine re-exports but `nmp`-owned mirrors (same names/field names/types — source-compatible, proved by `nmp-consumer-check`), converted once at the `DiagnosticsSubscription::recv` delivery boundary via exhaustive destructuring (a new engine field is a compile error, never a silently dropped fact), PLUS the newly documented `auth_sessions: Vec<AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot>` read-out and `AuthDiagnosticsPhase` (the engine's field stays `#[doc(hidden)]`). This deletes the ~785-line re-exported `DiagnosticsSnapshot` shape-JSON block: the snapshot NET SHRINKS 29,957 → 29,557 lines (−400) while ADDING the whole auth surface. **(2) Facade-owned AUTH-policy surface** (`nmp::auth`): `AuthPolicy` trait + `AuthPolicyRequest` (delegating getters over the engine request), `AuthPolicyDecision`/`AuthPolicyError`/`AuthPolicyResult` (small real mirrors FFI can construct), and `AuthPolicyOp`/`AuthPolicyPendingSender`/`AuthPolicyResolveError` as strict NEWTYPES over the engine operation — the pending channel, cancel handshake, and the `72eeb92` resolve/cancel linearization are inherited by delegation, never re-implemented; a private adapter bridges the facade trait to the engine trait. `Engine::add_auth_policy(PublicKey, impl AuthPolicy) -> AuthPolicyRegistration` / `Engine::remove_auth_policy(&AuthPolicyRegistration) -> bool`. **(3) The ratified account model (#8, closes #495, supersedes the closed #529):** `Engine::add_account` now returns `AccountRegistration` (BREAKING: previously `PublicKey`; call `.public_key()` on the registration) — the only value that may detach that exact installation via the new `Engine::remove_account(&AccountRegistration) -> bool`; there is deliberately NO pubkey-only removal. `EngineConfig` gains `max_auth_capabilities` (zero intentionally admits none) threading the Wave-3 `RuntimeConfig`; `EngineError` gains `AuthCapabilityRegistryFull { limit }` and `AuthCapabilityInstanceExhausted`, and internal FFI plumbing maps them through the existing signer-refusal lane without any FFI surface change.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store, receipt, intent, coverage, or journal layout changes; registrations and policies are in-memory capability state.
- **Diagnostics impact:** the per-session AUTH read-out (`auth_sessions`) becomes a DOCUMENTED facade fact — per exact session/generation/epoch phase, challenge hash, capability-bound flags, AUTH event id, and relay-ack facts — never claiming aggregate health, completeness, or authoritative emptiness. The engine-level field stays hidden mechanism. No diagnostics schema removal; every pre-existing field survives the mirror one-to-one.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp` gains mirror-completeness proofs (exhaustive engine→facade destructuring + an every-field/every-phase conversion test), newtype-delegation proofs (facade sender ↔ engine op share ONE channel; drop-fires-cancel-hook-once), and the registration falsifier set folded from #529: same-key replacement mints a NEW exact instance, a stale registration's removal is a `false` no-op that can never detach its replacement, removal is exact-instance idempotent, zero/shared `max_auth_capabilities` ceilings fail closed with the typed error, and every new verb fails `EngineClosed` after shutdown. `nmp-consumer-check` (depends on `nmp` alone) now names `AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot`/`AuthDiagnosticsPhase` exhaustively, implements an external async `AuthPolicy` with no channel crate, exercises the pending door's typed refusals, and round-trips `add_account`/`remove_account`/`add_auth_policy`/`remove_auth_policy`. `nmp-parity` re-verified on direct+FFI.
- **Superseded path removed:** `Engine::add_account -> PublicKey` is replaced by the registration-returning signature rather than kept as an alias (the #8-ratified model; #529's merged-then-superseded pubkey-keyed `remove_account` design was closed unmerged in its favor); the engine re-exports of `DiagnosticsSnapshot`/`RelayDiagnosticsSnapshot`/`FilterCoverageEntry` are removed from `nmp`'s root in favor of the facade-owned mirrors — no compatibility re-export remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #543 on 2026-07-16 (delegated Wave-5 orchestration for issue #8). Produced by the Wave-5 lead agent; an independent opus reviewer adversarially attacked mirror-conversion completeness, exact-instance registration semantics, and the strict-delegation constraint on the resolve/cancel machinery. The wave lead regenerated the governed snapshots (facade 29,557/30,000, FFI component byte-identical) and re-verified `fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`/full workspace test (the two `relay_ingest_smoke` failures are the pre-existing macOS flake [#538], reproduced identically on clean master) including `nmp-parity` and `nmp-consumer-check` on an isolated target. Exact-head review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — NIP-42 native SDK auth projection: FFI + Swift + Kotlin (Wave 6) ([#8](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/8), [PR #544](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/544))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #8 — Wave 5 (#543) made the supported Rust `nmp` facade express NIP-42 (an `AuthPolicy` trait + resolve/cancel completion machinery, exact `AccountRegistration`/`AuthPolicyRegistration` values, `max_auth_capabilities`, the two typed capability-refusal `EngineError`s, and a documented `auth_sessions` diagnostics read-out), but NO app SDK could reach any of it. The FFI boundary still returned a bare hex pubkey from `add_account` with no inverse, exposed no policy-callback surface, silently pinned `max_auth_capabilities` to the facade default (ignoring the config knob), folded `AuthCapabilityRegistryFull`/`AuthCapabilityInstanceExhausted` into the generic `InvalidSigner` reason lane, and dropped the entire `auth_sessions` projection — so Swift and Kotlin could neither register a policy, resolve/deny a challenge, observe per-session AUTH diagnostics, nor detach an account. Separately, the operational half of the closed #529 remained open on both platforms: removing an account left the plaintext `InsecureFileAccountStore` checkpoint intact, so the removed key resurrected on the next engine restart.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** No `nmp` facade change (the facade snapshot is byte-identical at 29,557/30,000); this wave is purely the FFI component + the two hand-written SDK wrappers. **FFI (`nmp-ffi`):** a new `auth` module projects the completion-only foreign policy surface — callback interface `FfiAuthPolicyCallback { evaluate(request, completion: Arc<FfiAuthPolicyCompletion>); on_cancelled(request) }`, the Rust-owned `FfiAuthPolicyCompletion` object whose `resolve(FfiAuthPolicyOutcome) -> Result<(), FfiAuthPolicyCompletionError{AlreadyCompleted|Cancelled|ReceiverGone}>` inherits Wave 5's resolve-vs-cancel linearization by delegation over `nmp::AuthPolicyPendingSender` (a `CompletionState`/`ResolvingOwner`/`Condvar` handshake: a queued answer wins a simultaneous terminal cancel, a cancel arriving mid-`Resolving` waits, and a resolver unwind disconnects the retained receiver — never a double-resolve, a lost resolve, or a `ReceiverGone` for a delivered answer), plus opaque `FfiAccountRegistration.public_key()` / `FfiAuthPolicyRegistration.expected_public_key()`. `NmpEngine.add_account` now returns `Arc<FfiAccountRegistration>` (BREAKING: was hex `String`); new `remove_account(registration) -> bool`, `add_auth_policy(expected_public_key, callback) -> Arc<FfiAuthPolicyRegistration>`, `remove_auth_policy(registration) -> bool`; `NmpEngineConfig` gains `max_auth_capabilities: u32` (`#[uniffi(default = 64)]`, zero admits none — the derived `Default` is replaced by a manual impl so a default-constructed config keeps the finite ceiling rather than zeroing it); `FfiError` gains real typed `AuthCapabilityRegistryFull { limit: u64 }` / `AuthCapabilityInstanceExhausted` (replacing the `InvalidSigner` routing); `FfiAuthPhase` gains `Ready`/`Denied`/`Error`; new record `FfiAuthDiagnostics` (relay, access, transport_generation, epoch_sequence, challenge_descriptor, phase, policy_bound, signer_bound, auth_event_id, send_handoff_accepted, relay_ok_accepted) and `FfiDiagnosticsSnapshot.auth_sessions: Vec<FfiAuthDiagnostics>` (the engine `AwaitingSend` phase folds to `AwaitingRelayAck` at the boundary). **Swift (`Packages/NMP`):** `NMPAuthPolicy` protocol (+ default no-op `onCancelled`), `NMPAuthPolicyRequest`/`NMPAuthPolicyOutcome`/`NMPAuthPolicyCompletion`/`NMPAuthPolicyCompletionError`, `NMPAccountRegistration`/`NMPAuthPolicyRegistration`, `NMPEngine.addAuthPolicy`/`removeAuthPolicy`; `addAccount` returns `NMPAccountRegistration` and rolls back the just-installed live signer if the checkpoint save throws; `AuthDiagnostics` + `DiagnosticsSnapshot.authSessions`; `AuthPhase` gains `.ready`/`.denied`/`.error`; `NMPError` gains `.authCapabilityRegistryFull(limit:)`/`.authCapabilityInstanceExhausted`; `NMPRelayRuntimePresentation` labels the three new phases. **Kotlin (`Packages/NMPKotlin`):** the equivalents, plus a `retain()`/`releaseCallbackScope()` deterministic-release handshake so an `evaluate` that neither resolves nor retains is observed as a release Rust-side (→ `Unavailable`) instead of depending on JVM GC timing; `NMPError` gains the two typed capability variants; `RelayViews` labels the three new phases.
- **Persistence impact:** none to canonical/store/receipt/journal layout. The one deliberate persistence-adjacent behavior change is the **#529 fold** (below): removing the checkpointed account now also clears its plaintext `InsecureFileAccountStore` file.
- **Diagnostics impact:** the facade's documented `auth_sessions` read-out is now projected end to end — FFI `FfiAuthDiagnostics`, Swift `AuthDiagnostics`, Kotlin `AuthDiagnostics` — per exact session/generation/epoch phase, BLAKE3 challenge descriptor, capability-bound flags, AUTH event id, and send/relay-ack facts; capability-instance ids and raw challenge bytes never cross the boundary. No diagnostics field removed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** FFI `auth::tests` port Wave 5's resolve/cancel race suite onto the completion object — `queued_resolution_wins_simultaneous_terminal_cancel_without_receiver_gone`, `cancel_arriving_during_resolving_waits_and_cannot_drop_receiver`, `resolver_unwind_after_ownership_disconnects_waiter_without_hang`, `retained_completion_is_pending_and_drop_cancels_once`, `returning_without_resolve_or_retain_is_unavailable`, `completion_reports_only_actual_terminal_errors`, `inline_completion_is_ready_and_exactly_once` (7/7) — plus facade registration falsifiers (`ffi_account_registration_is_explicit_repeatable_and_stale_safe`, `ffi_auth_policy_registration_is_explicit_repeatable_and_stale_safe`, `ffi_zero_auth_capacity_returns_typed_registry_refusal`, `ffi_config_manual_default_keeps_auth_capacity_finite`), `auth_capability_refusals_remain_typed_ffi_errors`, and the auth-diagnostics-projection assertions over every `FfiAuthPhase`. `nmp-parity` keeps the direct==FFI write oracle byte-identical (`ffi_status_name` gains the three non-evidence `invalid_*` arms; the FFI `add_account` sites now read `.public_key()`). Swift `AuthPolicyTests`/`EvidenceMappingTests` and Kotlin `AuthPolicyTest`/`EvidenceMappingTest` mirror the completion handshake and diagnostics mapping; both platforms add the #529 restart-resurrection + stale-registration guards. Orchestrator-verified on an isolated target: full workspace builds, `clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings` clean, `cargo fmt --check` clean, all Rust suites green including `nmp-ffi`, `nmp`, `nmp-parity`, and `nmp-consumer-check` (the two `relay_ingest_smoke` failures are the pre-existing macOS flake [#538], reproduced identically on clean master f7570f5); UniFFI Swift + Kotlin bindings regenerate cleanly and the hand-written wrappers match the regenerated shape; `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` passes.
- **Superseded path removed:** the FFI `add_account -> String` contract, the `max_auth_capabilities` config pin to the facade default, and the `InvalidSigner`-reason routing of the two capability refusals are replaced outright — no compatibility alias, no hex-pubkey removal overload, no shim. The closed-unmerged #529 (pubkey-keyed removal) stays superseded by Wave 5's registration model; only its checkpoint-clear obligation is folded in here, now keyed on the exact registration rather than a pubkey.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #544 on 2026-07-16 (delegated Wave-6 orchestration for issue #8, the final NIP-42 SDK wave). Produced by coordinated subagents on disjoint FFI/Swift/Kotlin paths; the wave lead independently attacked the resolve-vs-cancel linearization (no double-resolve, no lost resolve, no spurious `ReceiverGone`), completion/registration lifetime across engine shutdown (the weak-state cancel closure forbids an Arc cycle; on_cancelled runs outside the state lock so a reentrant resolve cannot deadlock; the known forever-blocking-cancel-hook join hazard [#538-adjacent, tracked in known-gaps] is app-owned and unchanged), the finite-executor bound on never-returning foreign callbacks, and the #529 checkpoint-clear (exact lock-guarded pubkey match, gated on engine-`true`, same-key replacement leaves a stale registration unable to clear its replacement's checkpoint) — verdict PASS, no correctness holes. The wave lead regenerated the governed snapshots (facade 29,557/30,000 byte-identical, FFI component grown by the auth vocabulary) and re-verified `fmt`/`clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`/full workspace test on an isolated target after branching from current origin/master. Exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance and the real Swift + Kotlin package builds, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Explicit per-write identity override ([PR #550](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/550))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #47 — single-identity coupling: an unsigned draft authored by any non-active registered identity could not publish without re-rooting the whole engine via `set_active_account`, so signing one write as a secondary account forced apps to swap the global read root and every reactive query with it.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_grammar::WriteIntent` gains `identity_override: Option<PublicKey>`. `None` preserves the fail-closed active-account contract verbatim; `Some(pk)` is explicit per-write consent to publish as `pk`, which must equal the draft's author (the engine never restamps a draft; a mismatch fails closed before acceptance), works regardless of the active account including fully logged out, and is pinned into the frozen acceptance so later `set_active_account` calls can never retarget the write; an override naming an identity with no registered signing capability parks durably as `AwaitingCapability` until that exact key's signer attaches. The FFI record and the Swift/Kotlin wrappers mirror the field with a source-compatible default of no override, so existing SDK callers compile and behave identically. `Engine::publish`/`publish_tracked` docs now state the default-versus-override contract, and the `set_active_account` doc truthfully distinguishes the keyless-active durable park from the foreign-author fail-closed refusal.
- **Persistence impact:** none — `expected_pubkey`/`signing_identity_ref` were already persisted per acceptance, so the pinned override rides the existing acceptance columns and restart replay without any schema change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; receipt streams carry the same `WriteStatus` vocabulary and no diagnostics schema or field changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** reducer-level override acceptance/pinning plus exact-message fail-closed proofs for the untouched default arm and for override/author mismatch on both unsigned and signed payloads (`core_headless`); registry-level secondary-identity signing with cryptographic row verification, the durable `AwaitingCapability` park that ignores a later account switch and resumes only under the exact override key, and logged-out override signing (`signer_registry_headless`); the facade proof that `active_account()` never moves while an override write signs (`crates/nmp` engine tests); and a genuine redb close/reopen replay proving the reattached receipt resumes pinned to the override identity and completes with the frozen body/id/pubkey (`durable_accepted_restart`).
- **Superseded path removed:** none behaviorally — the default `identity_override: None` arm is byte-for-byte the prior contract, so no compatibility shim exists or is needed. The one removal is syntactic: the previous three-field `WriteIntent` struct-literal shape no longer compiles for direct-Rust callers, and the migration is mechanical (add `identity_override: None`); FFI, Swift, and Kotlin callers are insulated by the source-compatible defaults.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #550 on 2026-07-16 (delegated orchestration for issue #47). Produced by coordinated subagents on disjoint Rust-core and FFI/SDK paths with an independent verifier running the workspace suites; exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Platform secure account-store providers: Keychain (Swift) + JVM KeyStore (Kotlin) ([#47](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/47), [PR #554](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/554))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #47 — Swift/Kotlin shipped only the deliberately-INSECURE plaintext `NMPInsecureFileAccountStore`; there was no standard secure-at-rest account/signer store, so an app either hand-rolled secure persistence or checkpointed a signing key in cleartext. Wave A widened `NMPEngine`'s init to accept any `NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint`; this lands the secure conforming providers.
- **Changed projections:** kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no Rust/FFI change. Swift adds `public final class NMPKeychainAccountStore: NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint` backed by the iOS/macOS **Keychain** (`SecItem`, `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` — not iCloud-synced, not device-migrated), overwrite-in-place on save, idempotent clear, errors carrying only an `OSStatus` (never the secret). Kotlin adds `NMPSecureKeyStoreAccountStore : NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint` backed by a password-protected JVM `KeyStore` (JCEKS `SecretKeyEntry`), atomic-write + 0600/0700 perms mirrored from the insecure store, fails closed on wrong password / corrupt file, zeroes secret byte arrays after use. Both are drop-in secure alternatives to the insecure store (identical `NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint` surface); apps still own import/removal/backup/consent. The providers are intentionally platform-specific (Keychain vs KeyStore); the cross-SDK parity gate is FFI-vocabulary-based and does not flag SDK-only providers, so no allowlist entry is needed. The real Android `AndroidKeyStore`/`EncryptedSharedPreferences` variant remains a documented seam behind the #40 AAR gap.
- **Persistence impact:** none to the engine store; these are app-selected local checkpoint providers, secure-at-rest replacements for the existing insecure file checkpoint.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Swift `KeychainAccountStoreTests` (save→restore round-trip, overwrite-in-place, idempotent clear, graceful `XCTSkip` when the sandbox Keychain is unavailable); Kotlin `SecureKeyStoreAccountStoreTest` (restart round-trip across a fresh store on the same file+password, POSIX-perm check, overwrite-replaces-secret, plus wrong-password and corrupt-file fail-closed cases).
- **Superseded path removed:** none — additive secure alternatives; the insecure store remains for dev/personal autologin.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #554 on 2026-07-16 (issue #47 signer-lifecycle). Two provider coders authored the platform files on disjoint paths; the orchestrator reconciled the Kotlin conformance to the public `NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint` interface and runs the real Swift + Kotlin package builds plus cross-SDK parity as merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Carry the frozen pubkey on `AwaitingCapability` (#47 Unit B) ([PR #556](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/556))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #47 Unit A (PR #550) proved the durable-park machinery is already general — any unsigned intent with no registered signer for its frozen author parks `WriteStatus::AwaitingCapability`, re-armed only by that exact key's `SignerAttached` or a matching `set_active_account`, and replayed verbatim across a redb close/reopen — but the fact itself carried no payload: an observer (or the FFI/SDK caller relaying it to app UI) could see "still parked" with no way to know, display, or act on WHICH identity the obligation was waiting for without separately threading the original intent's author alongside the receipt stream.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_engine::outbox::WriteStatus::AwaitingCapability` gains a `{ pubkey: PublicKey }` field — the exact identity frozen at acceptance (`AcceptWrite::expected_pubkey` / an `identity_override`, unchanged from Unit A) that no registered signer currently answers for. All three emission sites in `EngineCore` (`on_signer_completed`'s retryable-error arm, `on_signer_unavailable`, and the restart-replay `reattach_receipt` path) now carry `pending.signing_pubkey` / `receipt.expected_pubkey` on the fact instead of emitting a bare unit variant. `nmp-ffi`'s `FfiWriteStatus::AwaitingCapability` mirrors this as `{ pubkey: String }` (64-char hex, the module-wide convention every other pubkey field already follows) via `write_status_to_ffi`. Swift's `WriteStatus.awaitingCapability` and Kotlin's `WriteStatus.AwaitingCapability` change from a payload-less case/object to a case/data-class carrying `pubkey: String`, mapped from the regenerated FFI enum. This is a governed additive-shape change to an existing enum variant, not a new type: a UniFFI enum variant gaining an associated value is source-breaking for any existing Swift/Kotlin `switch`/`when` arm that matched the old payload-less case (the compiler forces every call site to update, exactly as this change did across `nmp-ffi`, `nmp-parity`, and the `nmp-engine` test suite) — no direct-Rust, FFI, or SDK caller can silently keep compiling against the old shape, but no caller can silently misread the new payload either.
- **Persistence impact:** none — `expected_pubkey` was already a persisted `OUTBOX_RECEIPTS`/`OUTBOX_INTENTS` column since Unit A (and #2/#3 before it); this change only starts reading that already-durable fact back onto the in-memory `WriteStatus` value at the three emission sites and at restart replay. No schema version bump, no new column, no journal layout change.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; this is a receipt-stream fact, not a diagnostics snapshot field, and no diagnostics schema changed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** three local-key (non-NIP-46) generality proofs in `signer_registry_headless.rs` now assert the exact parked pubkey rather than the bare variant — `attaching_matching_signer_rearms_awaiting_intent` (resumes only for the matching key), `accepted_b_intent_stays_pinned_after_switch_to_a_and_b_attach` (parked pubkey is B, immune to switching the active account to A), and `unregistered_override_parks_durably_and_never_retargets_on_account_switch` (falsifier d: parked pubkey is the override B, a switch to A's own registered signer produces silence, only B's signer resumes it). `durable_accepted_restart.rs`'s genuine redb close/reopen tests now assert the replayed `AwaitingCapability` carries the frozen author across the restart boundary, both for the plain single-identity path (`pending_row_and_frozen_signer_resume_after_reopen_then_cancel_compensates`) and the override path (`overridden_unsigned_intent_replays_and_resumes_pinned_to_override_after_reopen`, which also proves the post-restart pubkey survives an active-account switch and a wrong signer attach before the exact override key resumes it). `runtime_integration.rs`'s boot-recovery and multi-observer reattach tests, and `nip46_restart.rs`'s remote-signer restart test, assert the same payload. `core_headless.rs` strengthens `signer_unavailable_keeps_accepted_row_visible` and `retryable_signer_errors_retain_and_rearm_the_exact_write` to assert the emitted/retained fact names the exact author. `nmp-ffi`'s `every_write_status_variant_maps_without_terminal_rollup` and the three `facade.rs` FFI-boundary tests (`ffi_override_publish_for_unregistered_pubkey_parks_awaiting_capability`, and both `AwaitingCapability` observations in `ffi_reattach_replays_real_receipt_facts_through_a_fresh_observer`) assert the hex payload. `nmp-parity`'s `NormStatus::AwaitingCapability` now carries the hex pubkey so the direct/FFI oracle proves payload parity, not just variant-name parity, across both the acceptance-time and reattach-replay observations in the live durable-park proof.
- **Superseded path removed:** the payload-less `WriteStatus::AwaitingCapability` / `FfiWriteStatus::AwaitingCapability` / `WriteStatus.awaitingCapability` (Swift) / `WriteStatus.AwaitingCapability` (Kotlin) shapes no longer exist — every construction and pattern match across the workspace and both hand-written SDK mirrors was migrated in this same change; no compatibility alias or payload-less overload remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #556 on 2026-07-16 (delegated orchestration for issue #47 Unit B, following Unit A's #550). An independent opus reviewer adversarially attacked whether the parked pubkey is truly the frozen author and immune to retargeting, whether the new payload could ever silently drop a durable obligation, and whether the FFI/SDK payload mapping is lossless; its verdict is recorded in the PR discussion. The orchestrator ran `cargo test -p nmp-engine -p nmp-grammar -p nmp-ffi` on an isolated `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` and a full `cargo check --workspace --all-targets`; the governed Rust-facade and FFI-component snapshots and the real Swift/Kotlin package builds are re-verified by the repository owner before merge. Exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Project nmp-blossom through FFI, Swift, and Kotlin ([PR #560](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/560))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #555 (epic #216 T15-A-BLOSSOM): `nmp-blossom`'s #545/#551 upload/mirror/delete/list core was direct-Rust-only — a Swift or Kotlin app had no typed path to compose a kind:24242 authorization draft, get it signed, validate it fail-closed, or perform a self-verifying blob operation, and any hand-rolled bridge would have collapsed the crate's deliberately separated per-operation failure taxonomies into message strings at the boundary.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the Rust `nmp` facade is unchanged (nmp-blossom stays an opt-in protocol crate; `nmp-ffi` takes it as a direct dependency, the `nmp-nip29` precedent). New `crates/nmp-ffi/src/blossom.rs`: engine-less draft free functions (`blossom_upload_authorization_draft`/`blossom_delete_authorization_draft`/`blossom_list_authorization_draft`) returning `FfiBlossomAuthDraft` (canonical `unsigned_event_json` for external signers plus the raw `created_at_secs`/`kind`/`tags`/`content` tokens that feed the existing governed sign-only `NmpEngine::sign_event` unchanged — no new signing pathway); `FfiBlossomAuthorization` with two fail-closed constructors (`validate` from event JSON, `validate_signed_event` from the exact `FfiSignedEvent` the engine returns); the blocking `FfiBlossomClient` (`upload`/`mirror`/`delete`/`list`, per-call current-thread tokio runtime + per-call HTTP stack mirroring the engine's NIP-11 `http_runtime()` discipline); and per-operation error enums (`FfiBlossomAuthError`, `FfiBlossomUploadError`, `FfiBlossomMirrorError`, `FfiBlossomDeleteError`, `FfiBlossomListError` with nested `FfiBlossomServerUrlError`/`FfiBlossomSha256HexError`/`FfiBlossomDescriptorError`) mirroring the Rust taxonomies variant-for-variant — deliberately NOT widening the shared `FfiError`, and re-introducing boundary fallibility (server-URL/pubkey/sha256-hex parses) as typed variants. Swift adds `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/Blossom.swift` (idiomatic mirrors; `BlossomClient` async methods dispatch the blocking FFI calls onto a global queue via checked continuations) and Kotlin adds `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/Blossom.kt` (sealed error mirrors; suspend methods via `Dispatchers.IO`); neither SDK collapses any variant. Sign-only limitation carried honestly: `sign_event` freezes the author from the active account and the #550 identity override covers publish intents only, so a differing-author draft must be signed externally via its JSON (docs/known-gaps.md).
- **Persistence impact:** none; Blossom blob operations are HTTP against a caller-named server and touch no store, journal, receipt, or coverage layout, and drafts/authorizations are in-memory values.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; no diagnostics schema change — every failure surfaces as the operation's own typed error value, never a diagnostic counter or an empty-success.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Rust-side FFI boundary tests in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/blossom.rs`: wildcard-free variant-for-variant crossing proofs for every taxonomy (`every_auth_validation_variant_maps_to_a_distinct_ffi_variant`, `every_upload_error_variant_crosses_distinct`, `every_mirror_error_variant_crosses_distinct`, `every_delete_error_variant_crosses_distinct`, `every_list_error_variant_crosses_distinct`, `server_url_hex_and_descriptor_taxonomies_map_variant_for_variant`, `verb_mapping_round_trips_totally_in_both_directions`, `boundary_machinery_variants_are_constructible_and_distinct`); lossless descriptor crossing (`descriptor_round_trip_is_lossless`); draft integrity across both signing paths (`draft_json_and_raw_tokens_agree_on_the_exact_event`); fail-closed validation surviving the boundary (`validate_through_ffi_admits_the_real_event_and_refuses_a_tampered_one`, `validate_signed_event_record_admits_real_and_refuses_tampered`); config defaulting (`client_config_nones_resolve_to_crate_defaults`); and pre-I/O typed refusals through the real blocking client (`client_operations_refuse_malformed_inputs_typed_before_any_io`, `blocking_client_call_carries_typed_refusals_across_the_boundary`). `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` passes with zero new allowlist entries.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is the net-new projection of an existing crate — no prior FFI/Swift/Kotlin Blossom surface existed to alias or replace, and the direct-Rust `nmp-blossom` API is unchanged.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestrator approves this surface contract for review in PR #560 on 2026-07-16, per the issue-#555 unit plan under epic #216; exact-head adversarial review and required CI, including surface-governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Expose the gap-free NIP-77 live-handoff phase ([issue #563](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/563), [PR #570](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/570))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #563 — reconciliation-first could snapshot local/remote holdings before the ongoing REQ became active, so an event published in between could be delivered by neither path. Canonical dedup handles overlap but cannot recover an event never delivered. The runtime also reused one internal identity shape across NIP-01/NIP-77 even though NIP-77 defines a separate subscription-id namespace, and reconnect temporarily regressed to a plain REQ.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the acquisition reducer now sends a role-separated live `REQ {limit:0}`, waits for that exact EOSE, keeps it open through Negentropy and any missing-id fetch, and gives NEG/missing/fallback work distinct role-derived ids. Replacement overlap closes only after the successor is proven; timeout/NEG-ERR/malformed/store-failure paths use a distinct full-backlog REQ without closing live delivery; reconnect repeats the same sequence. `RelayDiagnosticsSnapshot` adds `nip77_handoff: &'static str` with `none | awaiting_live_eose | reconciling | backfilling | fallback_backlog | live`; `FfiRelayDiagnostics` mirrors it as `String`, Swift exposes `RelayDiagnostics.nip77Handoff`, and Kotlin exposes `RelayDiagnostics.nip77Handoff`. Existing advertisement and behavioral-probe facts remain separate and unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No event, coverage, outbox, journal, or schema layout changes. Coverage still credits only through the existing attribution door: `limit:0` EOSE is poisoned, NEG completion credits immediately only when no ids are missing, and missing-id coverage waits for that one-shot REQ's EOSE.
- **Diagnostics impact:** one additive per-session phase field, reactively emitted on candidate start, EOSE transition, NEG completion, and fallback start. It reports reducer-owned handoff truth and never changes routing.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-engine/tests/core_headless.rs` — `probed_relay_routes_broad_demand_to_negentropy_but_limited_demand_stays_on_req` proves `REQ(limit:0) → exact EOSE → NEG-OPEN`, separate ids, no live CLOSE, relay overdelivery without false coverage, and a newly published backdated event inside the old loss window; `neg_err_falls_back_without_closing_the_active_live_req`; `live_eose_timeout_uses_a_distinct_backlog_and_keeps_overlap_until_proven`; `reconnect_repeats_live_first_and_only_the_fresh_generation_eose_opens_neg`; `withdrawing_all_demand_closes_live_candidate_and_every_repair_owner`; `stale_negentropy_session_falls_back_to_req_after_the_liveness_deadline`; `next_deadline_is_min_over_expiry_and_neg_liveness`. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/runtime_integration.rs::neg_liveness_deadline_does_not_busy_spin` drives the exact order over a real WebSocket and proves deadline fallback. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/negentropy_live.rs::subscribe_widens_via_negentropy_and_surfaces_the_backfilled_post` runs against `nostr-relay-builder::LocalRelay`'s genuine server-side NIP-77 implementation, proves missing-id backfill/coverage, then publishes an ancient-timestamp event after reconciliation and proves the uninterrupted live REQ delivers it. `crates/nmp/src/diagnostics.rs::mirror_conversion_preserves_every_engine_fact_and_phase` and `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs::diagnostics_keeps_exact_intervals_distinct_from_query_evidence` prove the new phase crosses the facade and FFI without loss.
- **Superseded path removed:** the reconcile-first flow, its pre-NEG live CLOSE, the post-NEG `since: now` REQ reopen, same-role subscription identity, and reconnect's plain-REQ downgrade are removed outright. No compatibility alias or alternate ordering remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested this issue be implemented, verified with real relays, and landed on `master` on 2026-07-16; PR #570 carries that approved surface contract, with exact-head review and required CI, including surface governance, remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Per-handle query freshness from coverage ([issue #565](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/565), [PR #577](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/577))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #565 identified that every observation contributed live remote work even when the store already held sufficiently recent, source-scoped coverage, while callers had no strictly cache-only option. Treating event `created_at` as freshness would let a future-dated event manufacture a false recent check; evaluating `MaxAge` against the engine core's initial zero clock would incorrectly suppress stale data.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the existing `Demand` noun gains a per-handle `Freshness` policy: `Live` (the default cache-then-live behavior), `MaxAge { seconds: u64 }` (one opening-time coverage check), and `CacheOnly` (canonical cache projection with no remote work). Freshness remains outside atom/graph/wire/coverage identity, so equal descriptors share resolution, rows, and coverage while each handle independently contributes or suppresses wire work. `nmp-ffi` mirrors this as `FfiFreshness`; Swift adds `NMPFreshness.live` / `.maxAge(seconds: UInt64)` / `.cacheOnly`; Kotlin adds `NMPFreshness.Live` / `MaxAge(seconds: ULong)` / `CacheOnly`. Whole seconds cross every boundary exactly. Satisfied `MaxAge` handles retain their immutable opening plan for scoped evidence; unsatisfied handles become ordinary `Live` once and are not polled or re-evaluated.
- **Persistence impact:** none. The opening decision reads the existing `nmp-store` coverage rows; there is no new table, journal record, watermark shape, migration, shared coordinator, or background polling state.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. Query-scoped acquisition evidence uses the immutable plan that justified a satisfied `MaxAge`; `CacheOnly` carries an explicit empty evidence plan so it cannot borrow a live sibling's source evidence. Runtime completion attribution is capped at the engine wall clock at EOSE/NEG completion and the sent `until`; exact NEG send identity plus the captured completion timestamp prevents deferred missing-id backfill latency from inflating coverage. Event timestamps are never attribution inputs.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-engine/tests/freshness.rs` proves fresh cached rows and recent empty absence suppress wire, stale coverage becomes identical live work, all currently assigned relays must agree, `CacheOnly` neither opens wire nor borrows sibling evidence, pinned+strict scope composes, future event time cannot inflate freshness, satisfied history growth never preflights, and equal-policy handles drop independently; `subscribe_ticks_wall_clock_before_the_one_time_max_age_decision` drives the public runtime path and requires a real TCP connection after stale `MaxAge` becomes live; `correlated_completion_uses_exact_send_shape_and_completion_cap` proves exact NEG attribution and sent-`until` capping; `subscribe_widens_via_negentropy_and_surfaces_the_backfilled_post` remains green over a real NIP-77 relay. `demand_freshness_round_trips_all_whole_second_variants`, Swift `testFreshnessRoundTripsEveryWholeSecondVariant`, and Kotlin `freshnessRoundTripsEveryWholeSecondVariant` prove exact boundary parity.
- **Superseded path removed:** the unconditional per-handle live-wire behavior is replaced directly by the closed freshness policy on `Demand`; there is no compatibility alias, count budget, alternate acquisition coordinator, or second query API.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested implementation of #565 as the independent parallel track and resolved all four blocking design questions in the issue thread before coding; those resolutions define the surface carried by draft PR #577. Exact-head review and required CI, including the macOS Swift test and strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Durable pre-signature write cancellation ([issue #533](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/533), [PR #585](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/585))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #533 records that an accepted unsigned write could remain an observable, query-visible obligation indefinitely, but the app had no receipt-keyed operation to retract it; dropping a receipt observer was intentionally not ownership, and the internal failure-compensation path could not express deliberate cancellation as durable typed evidence.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `CancelWriteOutcome::Cancelled`, precise `CancelWriteError` variants, `Engine::cancel(ReceiptId)`, and `WriteStatus::Cancelled`; UniFFI mirrors the closed outcome/error/status vocabulary through `NmpEngine.cancel`; Swift exposes `NMPEngine.cancel(receiptId:)`, `WriteCancellationOutcome`, and `NMPWriteCancellationError`; Kotlin exposes the equivalent `NMPEngine.cancel(receiptId)`, `WriteCancellationOutcome`, and `NMPWriteCancellationError`. Success means the durable terminal fact committed, repeated cancellation is idempotent, and signed, compensated, abandoned, unknown, persistence-failed, and closed-engine paths cannot collapse into an impossible success value.
- **Persistence impact:** the existing v6 retained receipt JSON gains `Cancelled` and uses `Signed` for a successfully promoted ephemeral receipt; durable/at-most-once cancellation atomically retracts only the cancelling intent's still-pending ownership, reverses its suppression claims, restores any displaced predecessor through ordinary insertion, removes the open intent, and writes `Cancelled` in the same transaction. There is no schema-version or table-layout bump: current code reads all older v6 rows, while an older binary cannot decode newly written `Cancelled` rows (or the newly persisted ephemeral `Signed` state), so rollback after exercising this API requires rolling forward or discarding the development store.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema changes. Cancellation is receipt evidence and ordinary row reactivity; persistence refusal emits no false terminal receipt fact, and runtime signer-task census returns to its prior value only after committed cancellation.
- **Updated falsifiers:** cross-backend store tests prove atomic cancellation, predecessor restoration, suppression reversal, idempotence, crash-before-commit rollback, signed refusal, ephemeral cancellation, and restart replay; `core_headless` proves typed outcomes, late-signer inertness, direct persistence-failure retry, row reactivity, duplicate ownership, and terminal boundaries; `durable_accepted_restart` proves quarantined kind-22242 cancellation, signed-ephemeral replay/refusal, corrupt-receipt fallibility, and restart reattachment; facade/runtime tests prove observer drop is not cancellation and a never-resolving signer releases the sole native-task slot only after committed cancellation; FFI and direct-vs-FFI parity tests prove the same outcome and observed fact; executable Swift and Kotlin package tests publish an unsigned write, cancel it through the public SDK, observe `Accepted` then `Cancelled` with no `Signed`, repeat idempotently, and reattach the terminal fact.
- **Superseded path removed:** the generic failed-string projection for deliberate retraction and the app's inability to address an accepted obligation are replaced directly; there is no handle-drop cancellation, boolean success, status-parameterized destructive door, compatibility alias, or path that retracts a signed event.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested implementation, PR publication, and landing of issue #533 on 2026-07-16. Draft PR #585 carries the approved contract; independent adversarial review found and drove fixes for signer-task leaks, quarantined recovered writes, signed ephemeral replay, corrupt persistence, and impossible success/error states. Exact-head review and required CI, including macOS Swift execution and strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Bounded ordinary-row delivery ([issue #46](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/46), [PR #586](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/586))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #46 records that an ordinary unwindowed observation used an unbounded Rust `mpsc` queue, so a slow native callback retained every intermediate reducer batch even though the app ultimately needs only an exact transition from its last delivered state to current state. The already-bounded Swift/Kotlin consumer edges could not bound this producer-side backlog.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the engine's internal row-delivery edge now uses a one-slot mailbox that atomically composes skipped `RowDelta`s per event id. The next delivered batch is exact relative to the receiver's last return, carries the latest acquisition evidence, and is drained before disconnect; unchanged rows are not copied. The supported `nmp` and UniFFI method shapes are unchanged. Swift and Kotlin public API shapes are unchanged; their governed documentation now states that unbounded observations may conflate intermediate reducer emits while still reaching exact newest state. An unwindowed result's semantic cardinality remains intentionally unbounded.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No event, coverage, outbox, receipt, journal, or schema representation changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. The latest complete acquisition evidence shares the one pending transition and cannot accumulate independently.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp-engine::runtime::row_channel` proves that 10,000 skipped remove/add updates for one event collapse to one exact remove/add transition, add-then-remove cancels while the latest evidence is still delivered, repeated source growth retains the latest complete source set, impossible remove-then-source-growth fails closed without resurrecting a stale row, the pending transition is delivered before disconnect, and `RowsReceiver` is `Send` but deliberately not `Sync`. `nmp-ffi::facade::ffi_blocked_callback_receives_one_exact_rebased_frame_then_closes_once` blocks a real native callback, produces 64 durable local rows through the engine, cancels half, observes one exact 32-row rebased frame, then proves cancellation closes exactly once. Existing full `nmp-engine`, `nmp`, `nmp-ffi`, and `nmp-bdd` suites remain green; strict Clippy, formatting, diff checks, and SDK parity pass.
- **Superseded path removed:** the ordinary-row `std::sync::mpsc::Receiver<RowsMsg>` path is replaced outright by `RowsReceiver`; there is no compatibility queue or alternate unbounded delivery mode.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested that the comparison findings become tested NMP improvements and be landed. PR #586 carries the observation-delivery slice of #46; receipt observation, graph/wire ceilings, relay-advertised request limits, ingestion pressure, and scheduler/resource ceilings remain explicitly open and prevent this PR from closing the parent issue. Exact-head review and required CI, including surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Make nmp-content parser-only and move reference planning to nmp-grammar ([#567](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/567), [PR #569](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/569))

- **Failure evidence:** epic #561 and its ratified resolutions comment record the concrete boundary failure: parsing a reference in `nmp-content` also owned engine acquisition, kind:0/NIP-23 projection, hydration counters, and demand planning. That coupling made an app-selected literal/no-fetch reference renderer impossible at the component decision point and pulled the engine mechanism graph into a parser crate.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust makes `nmp-content` a pure document parser depending only on `nmp-grammar` among NMP crates; deletes its profile/article codecs, hydration counters, acquisition planning, and engine dependency; moves canonical, engine-free reference-target-to-demand lowering into `nmp_grammar::reference`; and gives relay-host safety classification one lower-level owner in `nmp_grammar::relay`, reused by transport admission. The `nmp` facade's now-misplaced relay-hint helper is removed. FFI removes the content-owned profile/article/hydration surface, moves reference targets and plans to `reference.rs`, replaces `contentReferenceDemandPlan` with the fallible `referenceDemandPlan`, and exposes explicitly discarded optional relay hints. Swift and Kotlin handwritten sources are unchanged in this Rust-first stacked unit; their deliberate migration away from the removed generated APIs is owned by the next ordered #561 child PRs, so this draft is their base rather than a standalone SDK-compatible merge.
- **Persistence impact:** none; reference lowering produces immutable `LiveQuery` values and never opens a handle, observes, writes, or changes store coverage.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; the removed hydration counters were speculative rendering budgets, not runtime diagnostics, and the ratified #561 resolution deletes them rather than relocating them.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `normal_dependency_tree_contains_no_engine_or_mechanism_crate`; `shared_public_nip19_fixtures_lower_to_exact_canonical_demands`; `event_hints_never_change_canonical_target_or_actual_kind_selection`; `relay_hints_are_canonical_deduplicated_safe_and_explicitly_bounded`; `malformed_identity_values_cannot_produce_acquisition_plans`; `invalid_optional_author_hint_is_ignored_without_losing_exact_event_plan`; `literal_local_private_onion_and_mapped_hosts_fail_closed`; `malformed_target_is_refused_before_it_can_become_a_demand`; `scripts/check-content-parser-boundary.sh`.
- **Superseded path removed:** the `nmp-content` engine dependency, `profile`, `article`, `hydration`, and acquisition-owning `reference` modules are deleted; the content-prefixed FFI planning function and all content profile/article/hydration exports are deleted; the facade relay-hint helper is deleted. There are no compatibility aliases.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly ratified the controlling #561 resolutions, requested this Unit 1 as the first child, and delegated its implementation for review in PR #569 on 2026-07-16; required CI and exact-head review remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Make Swift reference acquisition component-owned ([#573](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/573), [PR #578](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/578), [landing PR #569](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/569))

- **Failure evidence:** epic #561 and its ratified resolutions comment show that Swift `NMPContent` still converted every detected reference into session-owned acquisition: `NostrContentSession` and an unconditional claim modifier owned all query lifetimes, so an app could not select a literal, consent-gated, cache-aware, or explicit-relay loader at the component decision point. A shared mutable session also made equal visible occurrences share acquisition ownership even though each component must release only its own handle.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Swift removes the `NMP` engine dependency from the `NMPContent` target and leaves it as pure generated-document projection; deletes its session, claim, hydration-policy, kind:0, and NIP-23 APIs; adds immutable cycle/depth render context in `NMPUI`; adds a per-component `NMPVisibleReferenceObservation` plus the opt-in `observeWhileVisible` primitive that retains the latest canonical/helper snapshots while hidden and cancels only the handles it opened; and splits reference rendering into an app-selected profile component, a replaceable outer event loader, and a separate actual-row-kind plus purpose renderer table with generic fallback. Identity/article leaves now consume explicit app/protocol-owner presentation values rather than decoding event schemas. Rust/FFI are unchanged from stacked base PR #569; Kotlin is deliberately the next ordered #561 child and is unchanged here.
- **Persistence impact:** none; every selected component opens ordinary NMP observations against the existing canonical store, and neither the parser, render context, component table, nor retained last snapshot creates a second cache or changes any store schema.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. The Gallery reads existing engine `wireSubCount` facts during rapid visibility churn; the removed Swift hydration/active-reference counters are not relocated or replaced with a budget.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `testLiteralVisibleProfileAndEventComponentsOpenZeroHandles`; `testEqualVisibleComponentsOwnIndependentHandlesAndReleaseOnlyTheirOwn`; `testProfileReplacementIsLiveAndLastSnapshotSurvivesScrollAwayAndReturn`; `testCanonicalAndHelperEvidenceStayOnTheirExactHandles`; `testVisibilityThrashLeaksNoHandlesAndKeepsTheLastSnapshot`; `testActualKindAndPurposeDispatchIgnoreMisleadingHintAndReachFallback`; `testReplacingOuterLoaderPreservesActualKindRendererTable`; `testRenderContextStopsCyclesAndDepthWithoutMutableBudget`; `testLiveFactoryDisappearReturnsNativeTaskCensusToBaseline`; `testRapidVisibilityChurnReachesBothEnds`.
- **Superseded path removed:** `NostrContentSession`, `NostrContentClaim`, `NMPContentClient`, the unconditional `NMPReferenceClaimModifier`, Swift kind:0/NIP-23 decoders, shared hydration counters/policies, and session-taking `NostrContent` initializers are deleted outright; there are no compatibility aliases. Event dispatch no longer consults an authored kind hint, and production handle cancellation explicitly cancels both the Swift iteration task and its `NMPQuery`.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly ratified the controlling #561 resolutions, requested the Swift unit after parser-only PR #569, and delegated its implementation for review in draft PR #578 on 2026-07-16; PR #578 lands through final stack PR #569, with exact-head review and required macOS Swift/Gallery CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Remove Kotlin content-session acquisition ownership ([#580](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/580), [PR #582](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/582), [landing PR #578](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/578), [final PR #569](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/569))

After stacked parser boundary PR #569 removed the corresponding FFI session/schema surface, #578's Kotlin-package failure concretely exposed every stale import; restoring those symbols would preserve the architectural defect rather than fix the caller.

- **Updated falsifiers:** `parsingAndPlanningOpenNoNativeTasks`; `sharedNip19FixturesMatchKotlinTargetsAndDemandPlans`; `shared_nip19_fixtures_match_direct_rust_and_ffi_exactly`; `shared_corpus_is_versioned_unique_and_covers_every_required_outcome`; `only_public_entities_carry_actionable_expectations`; `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`. The five former Kotlin-local target/hint/malformed/secret fixture tests were deliberately removed by PR #584 because their expectations now come from the one versioned cross-platform corpus rather than a second platform-local oracle.
- **Superseded path removed:** `ContentSession.kt`, `ContentSessionTest.kt`, `NMPContentClient`, `NostrContentSession`, claims, session snapshots/states, hydration policies/count budgets, `NostrProfileMetadata`, `NostrArticle`, Kotlin kind:0/NIP-23 decoders, and the content-prefixed FFI planner path are deleted outright; no alias or renamed coordinator remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly ratified the controlling #561 resolutions, requested this Kotlin unit after Swift PR #578, and delegated its implementation for review in draft PR #582 on 2026-07-16; PR #582 lands through parent PR #578 and final PR #569, with exact-head review and required CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Prove shared NIP-19 reference-plan parity ([#583](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/583), [PR #584](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/584), [landing PR #582](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/582), [parent PR #578](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/578), [final PR #569](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/569))

The existing suites instead repeated a few constants and partial assertions per platform, so one projection could drift on target identity, untrusted author/kind hints, relay-hint safety/bounds, or malformed/secret refusal without another surface becoming red.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no production API or generated component shape changes. Test-only `nmp-bdd` now owns and schema-validates one versioned `fixtures/reference-plans.json` corpus; `nmp-parity` executes it through the direct Rust and FFI public entity-decoder, content-parser, target, and demand-plan surfaces. Swift `NMPUITests` and Kotlin SDK tests read the same JSON bytes and normalize their public decoder/parser/planner values into the same complete target/demand schema, including the independently merged #565 freshness axis. Kotlin `build.gradle.kts` adds only a test JSON dependency and the deterministic repository fixture path, which is why governance records the `kotlin` projection; no hand-written production SDK file changes.
- **Persistence impact:** none; fixtures and normalization exist only in test targets, and the exercised parser/planner calls construct immutable values without an engine, observation, store, or write.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; no evidence, shortfall, task-census, receipt, or diagnostics schema changes. Existing no-native-task coverage remains unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `shared_nip19_fixtures_match_direct_rust_and_ffi_exactly`; `testSharedNIP19FixturesMatchSwiftTargetsAndDemandPlans`; `sharedNip19FixturesMatchKotlinTargetsAndDemandPlans`; `shared_corpus_is_versioned_unique_and_covers_every_required_outcome`; `only_public_entities_carry_actionable_expectations`. Together they compare every target field and every canonical/helper demand axis—including exact `Freshness::Live` projection after #565—prove misleading `nevent` kind/author hints cannot become canonical match truth, prove URL-equivalent relay dedup plus local/onion/overflow refusal with an explicit discarded count, preserve NIP-21/bare equivalence, and keep malformed/secret inputs visible but non-actionable with distinct typed decoder errors.
