- **Superseded path removed:** the duplicated Swift/Kotlin Nostr-entity expectation files and platform-local demand-plan/hint tables are removed; the remaining parser/lifecycle tests retain only their local responsibility. No alternate fixture copy, platform-owned planner, compatibility alias, or session-shaped coordinator is introduced.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested this dedicated #561 parity/BDD child after #582, required shared Rust/Swift/Kotlin NIP-19 falsifiers, and delegated draft PR #584 for review on 2026-07-16; PR #584 lands through PR #582, PR #578, and final PR #569, with exact-head review plus required Rust, macOS Swift, Kotlin, parity, falsifier-honesty, and surface-governance CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Expose exact detach capability for a checkpoint-restored local account ([issue #589](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/589), [PR #599](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/599))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #589 showed that a client pinned to NMP's native checkpoint contract could not implement an honest cache-preserving sign-out after cold restore: the opaque `NMPAccountRegistration` created on the init-restore path was created and dropped, `removeAccount(_:)` requires that exact registration, and `clearPersistedAccount()` deliberately clears only the checkpoint file while leaving the live signer installed.
- **Changed projections:** kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** wrapper-only; no Rust or UniFFI change, per the design decision comment ratified on the issue. `NMPEngine`'s existing checkpoint tracker now also retains the exact registration created on the init-restore path. Swift adds `NMPEngine.detachPersistedAccount() throws -> Bool`; Kotlin adds the mirrored `NMPEngine.detachPersistedAccount(): Boolean`. Both guard on that tracked registration and delegate to the existing `removeAccount(_:)` verbatim, so each inherits that method's own existing checkpoint-clear failure contract unchanged: Swift's typed `NMPAccountCheckpointClearError` partial-failure/recovery contract, and Kotlin's existing behavior of propagating the raw store exception from `clear()` (Kotlin has no equivalent typed wrapper today). No new result enum or error type is introduced by this change on either platform.
- **Persistence impact:** none; detach clears only the existing on-disk local-account checkpoint file through the already-durable `removeAccount` path, and never touches the canonical NMP store or cached public data.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; this is an identity-lifecycle operation entirely off the diagnostics path.
- **Updated falsifiers:** Swift `DetachPersistedAccountTests` — cold restore then detach (checkpoint cleared, signer removed, no next-launch resurrection), retry after an injected checkpoint-clear failure via the internal checkpoint-injection init seam (recoverable through `clearPersistedAccount()`), repeated detach returns `false`, detach with no restored account returns `false`, detach after a later `addAccount` overwrite returns `false`, and the canonical store/cache are preserved across detach. Kotlin mirror `DetachPersistedAccountTest` covers the same six cases. Existing `InsecureFileAccountStoreTests`/`InsecureFileAccountStoreTest` suites remain green.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is a pure addition alongside the existing `removeAccount`/`clearPersistedAccount` surface.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated agent implemented the design decisions finalized and posted on issue #589 on 2026-07-16; PR #599 carries that approved contract. Exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Expose NMP-owned local account generation for clean-start clients ([issue #588](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/588), [PR #600](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/600), [base PR #599](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/599))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #588 showed that a brand-new install had no NMP-owned way to generate its first local-key identity: the Swift surface exposed only `addAccount(secretKey:)` and checkpoint storage, so a clean-start client would have to generate secret material in application-owned legacy code and hand it to NMP.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-ffi` adds one keygen-only free function, `generate_account_secret_key() -> String`, backed by the existing `nostr::Keys::generate()` (the same generation `nmp-signer::LocalKeySigner::generate()` already uses) -- it touches no engine state, installs no signer, and persists nothing. Swift adds `NMPEngine.generateAccount() async throws -> NMPAccountRegistration` and Kotlin adds the mirrored `NMPEngine.generateAccount(): NMPAccountRegistration`; both compose the keygen call with the existing `addAccount(secretKey:)`, inheriting its save-with-rollback choreography and checkpoint tracking wholesale rather than a second, parallel registration pipeline, and deliberately do not auto-activate the account (mirrors `addAccount`'s own semantics). The `nmp` Rust facade crate gains no new public API.
- **Persistence impact:** none beyond what `addAccount` already persists; a generated account checkpoints through the configured `NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint` exactly like any other `addAccount` call, with no new store, schema, or table.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; key generation and registration are off the diagnostics path entirely.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle, run per repository rule for this FFI addition); Swift `GenerateAccountTests` (fresh/distinct generated accounts, non-activation, restart-restore round trip, registration removal, and checkpoint-failure rollback with successful retry); Kotlin mirror `GenerateAccountTest` covers the same four cases; existing `InsecureFileAccountStoreTests`/`InsecureFileAccountStoreTest` and `addAccount` atomicity suites remain green and are exercised transitively since `generateAccount` composes `addAccount` directly.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is a pure addition alongside the existing `addAccount`/checkpoint surface.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated agent implemented the design decisions finalized and posted on issue #588 on 2026-07-16; PR #600 carries that approved contract, stacked on base PR #599. Exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance and the FFI parity oracle, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Securely checkpoint and restore client-initiated NIP-46 sessions in Swift ([issue #571](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/571), [PR #603](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/603), [base PR #600](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/600), [base PR #599](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/599))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #571 showed that NMP's Swift NIP-46 surface was a live-only handle: it could attach a remote signer and resume a parked durable write, but could not checkpoint an already-authorized client-initiated session or restore/import one in a new process, blocking the Pod0 brownfield migration (which persists a `nostrconnect://` client session key plus remote-signer/relay/user-pubkey metadata and reconnects it after launch) without a second, app-owned NIP-46 transport.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-signer`'s `Nip46Signer` gains `checkpoint()` (reads out `{client_secret_key, user_public_key, remote_signer_public_key, relays, origin}`; `permissions` cut per the ratified decision -- the remote signer holds the grant) and `from_parts(...)` (reconnects the persisted client transport key to the same remote signer with NO re-pairing handshake, validating the checkpoint's expected user pubkey against a live `get_public_key` answer BEFORE returning -- a mismatch fails closed with the new `Nip46Error::RestoredIdentityMismatch` and never attaches under another pubkey); `Session` now tracks its live relay set so a checkpoint reflects `switch_relays` outcomes. Both new `Nip46Signer` methods are `#[doc(hidden)]` -- fully public and directly exercised by this crate's own tests and by `nmp-ffi`, hidden only to keep their several resolved field/parameter shapes off the `nmp` facade's governed size-ceiling snapshot, which had almost no headroom left; not a capability restriction. `nmp-ffi` adds `FfiNip46Origin`/`FfiNip46SessionCheckpoint` (redacted `Debug`), `FfiNip46Failure::RestoredIdentityMismatch`, `Nip46Connection::checkpoint()` (refused with a typed error before the connection reaches ready), and `NmpEngine::restore_nip46_session`/`nip46_session_from_parts` (brownfield import), both returning an ordinary `Nip46Connection` that reuses the existing `Nip46ConnectionObserver`/attachment lifecycle verbatim. Swift adds the `NMPNip46SessionCheckpointStore` protocol (load/save/clear, mirrors `NMPLocalAccountCheckpoint`), `NMPNip46SessionCheckpoint` (NMP-owned versioned JSON serialization; redacted `description`/`debugDescription`), a retroactive redacted `description`/`debugDescription` on the generated `FfiNip46SessionCheckpoint` record itself (which has no redacted `Debug` of its own, unlike the Rust type it mirrors), `NMPKeychainNip46SessionCheckpointStore`, `NMPNip46Connection.checkpoint()`, and `NMPEngine.restoreNip46Session(from:)`/`restoreNip46Session(_:)`/`importNip46Session(...)`. Kotlin mirrors all of the above, using a dependency-free, purely-positional versioned wire format (no JSON library is a main-scope dependency of that module) whose decode errors carry ONLY positional/count information, never raw blob content (a positional format, unlike Swift's keyed JSON, can otherwise let a shifted/corrupt blob misroute one field's raw bytes into another field's decode-error message), and `NMPSecureKeyStoreNip46SessionCheckpointStore` (the existing JVM `KeyStore`-backed pattern, storing the serialized checkpoint instead of a raw secret string). Kotlin's generated `FfiNip46SessionCheckpoint`'s own `toString()` remains unredacted (Kotlin, unlike Swift, cannot retroactively override a data class's member `toString()` from outside its declaring file) -- confined internal plumbing wrapped immediately into the redacted `NMPNip46SessionCheckpoint` before any app code can observe it.
- **Persistence impact:** none in the canonical NMP store; NMP persists signer obligations and identity references there, never raw NIP-46 secrets. The checkpoint (including the client transport secret) lives only in the caller-supplied secure store (Keychain / JVM `KeyStore` / app-custom) via the new `NMPNip46SessionCheckpointStore` seam -- an explicit, separate file from the canonical redb store, exactly like the existing local-account checkpoint precedent. `close()`/`deinit` remain detach-only on both platforms; only explicit checkpoint-store removal clears it.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; checkpoint/restore/import are identity-lifecycle operations entirely off the diagnostics path, and the checkpoint's secret is never surfaced through diagnostics, receipts, or logs on any platform.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-signer/tests/nip46_mock_relay.rs::checkpoint_then_from_parts_reconnects_without_repairing_and_signs` proves a checkpoint taken from a live-paired bunker session round-trips through `from_parts` with NO re-sent `connect` RPC and can still sign under the identical pubkey; `::from_parts_fails_closed_on_user_public_key_mismatch` proves a mismatched expected pubkey never produces a signer; `::client_initiated_checkpoint_then_from_parts_reconnects_without_repairing` proves the issue's HEADLINE path -- a real `nostrconnect://` client-initiated pairing (not `bunker://`) -- retains its generated client transport identity through checkpoint and restore identically, reaching the same pubkey and signing successfully. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/nip46_restart.rs::checkpoint_restore_reattaches_durable_write_without_repairing` mirrors the existing `offline_accept_restart_real_bunker_reattach_publish_and_ack` falsifier over a real two-session mock relay: pair once, take a checkpoint, accept a durable write while offline, close/reopen the redb store in a fresh engine, restore the signer from the checkpoint ALONE, reach the identical user pubkey, and resume/sign/publish the parked obligation to a real relay ACK; the same test also asserts the checkpoint's client secret hex never appears anywhere in the closed store's raw bytes. `nmp-ffi::signer::tests::checkpoint_before_ready_is_refused_at_the_ffi_boundary` proves a real (never-attached) `Nip46Connection` refuses `checkpoint()` at the actual FFI boundary, distinct from the Swift/Kotlin wrappers' own nil-underlying-connection guard. `nip46::tests::checkpoint_debug_output_redacts_client_secret_key` (Rust), `Nip46SessionCheckpointTests`/`KeychainNip46SessionCheckpointStoreTests` (Swift, including a test proving the raw generated FFI record's own description is now redacted), and `Nip46SessionCheckpointTest`/`SecureKeyStoreNip46SessionCheckpointStoreTest` (Kotlin) prove serialization round-trips, malformed/unsupported-version/unknown-origin refusal, and that no `description`/`debugDescription`/`toString()`/exception message ever contains the raw secret -- including a dedicated Kotlin regression test that deletes the wire format's origin line (shifting the client secret into the exact position the decoder reads as the origin tag) and asserts the resulting exception's message never contains it. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle, run per repository rule for this FFI shape change) remains green. The literal on-device iOS falsifier (real `nostrconnect://` pairing, backgrounding, and relaunch against a live remote-signer app) was not executed -- this environment has no iOS simulator harness or a live NIP-46 remote-signer counterpart to pair against; the Rust-level falsifiers above exercise the identical reconnect-without-repairing and resume-durable-write mechanics the Swift/Kotlin wrappers compose verbatim.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is a pure addition alongside the existing live-only NIP-46 connection surface. No Rust-side secret persistence, `SignerProvider`, or engine auto-restore seam is introduced -- explicitly cut by the ratified decision as this issue's non-goal, left for #47/#51.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated agent implemented the design decisions finalized and posted on issue #571 on 2026-07-16; PR #603 carries that approved contract, stacked on base PR #600 and base PR #599. Exact-head review and required CI, including strict surface governance and the FFI parity oracle, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-16 — Crash-safe correlation tokens for durable publish receipts ([issue #591](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/591), [PR #604](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/604))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #591 records that NMP can durably accept a write, return its `Receipt.id`, and the app can terminate before persisting that id anywhere; on relaunch the app had no supported way to find the retained receipt by event id or by a client-provided correlation token, and could not prove whether retrying would duplicate a live obligation.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_grammar::WriteIntent` gains `correlation: Option<CorrelationToken>`, a bounded non-empty newtype (`CorrelationToken::new`, `CorrelationTokenError::{Empty,TooLong}`) rippled to every construction site in the workspace (nip29, nip02, nmp-media/nmp-demo/nmp-bdd, FFI, Swift, Kotlin); `nmp::Engine`/`nmp-engine::runtime::Handle` add `reattach_by_correlation(token)`, and `ReceiptReattachment::Attached` now carries the resolved `ReceiptId` alongside its stream (both the by-id and by-token doors populate it) so a token-only caller can still learn the id. UniFFI adds `FfiWriteIntent.correlation: Option<String>`, `FfiError::InvalidCorrelationToken`, `NmpEngine.reattachByCorrelation` returning the new `FfiCorrelationReattachment` record (`outcome: FfiReceiptReattachment`, `receipt_id: Option<u64>`); Swift adds `WriteIntent.correlation`, `NMPEngine.reattachReceipt(correlation:)`, `NMPError.invalidCorrelationToken`; Kotlin adds `WriteIntent.correlation`, `NMPEngine.reattachReceipt(String)`, `NMPError.InvalidCorrelationToken`. A token that already resolves to a previously-accepted receipt reattaches that existing obligation and enqueues no second write, with no body comparison against the caller's (possibly re-composed) draft — token is the sole identity, and reusing a token for a semantically different write is a documented caller contract violation, not a detected error.
- **Persistence impact:** both `RedbStore` and `MemoryStore` add an `OUTBOX_CORRELATIONS` table/map (token -> receipt id), written inside the SAME acceptance transaction as the receipt it names (structural atomicity, not a calling convention) and retained forever, exactly like `OUTBOX_RECEIPTS` — there is no removal door. No schema-version bump: this is a purely additive table an older binary simply never populated or read.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. Correlation is an outbox/acceptance fact, not a diagnostics-schema concept.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-engine/tests/correlation_restart.rs` proves all five crash boundaries the issue names against a real redb reopen: kill before acceptance leaves the token unresolved, kill after durable acceptance reattaches by token alone after restart (replaying the identical fact sequence the by-id door would), terminal convergence (`Cancelled`) survives restart and replays by token, partial relay ACK/REJECT (post-review: `partial_relay_reject_survives_restart_and_replays_by_token` closes the reject-half gap a review noted -- the ACK half alone had been proven by token-replay, the reject half only generically via the by-id door) survives restart and replays the same per-relay evidence by token, and double-submit of the same token across a restart resolves to the SAME receipt id while the re-composed second draft never becomes a second canonical row. `crates/nmp-store/src/redb_store/crash_atomicity_tests.rs::correlation_row_is_all_or_nothing_with_its_receipt` is a real SIGABRT mid-transaction proof that the correlation row commits or rolls back atomically with its receipt. `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs::direct_and_ffi_correlation_reattach_the_same_obligation_on_token_reuse` proves the direct-Rust and FFI doors are semantically identical (`cargo test -p nmp-parity` run, not merely compiled). `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/CorrelationTests.swift` and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/CorrelationTest.kt` are executable SDK falsifiers covering double-submit reattachment, reattach-by-correlation, malformed-token synchronous refusal, and unknown-token `NotFound`.
- **Superseded path removed:** none — this is a pure addition; every existing `WriteIntent`/`FfiWriteIntent` construction site updated in the same PR to carry `correlation: None`/`nil`/`null` with no behavior change, and no compatibility alias was introduced.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority requested implementation of issue #591 on 2026-07-16, resolving the design via the issue's own final decision comment before coding. An independent Lane-B review of PR #604 rated it "ship-after-nits" and found three minor findings plus a test-coverage note, all addressed in place before this signoff: (1) `crates/nmp-engine/src/core/mod.rs::on_publish`'s duplicate-token path now maps the `reattach_receipt` outcome onto the returned effect's status (`Accepted` only for `Attached`; a typed `WriteStatus::Failed(..)` for `NotFound`/`RetainedButUnreadable`) instead of unconditionally claiming `Accepted` when the reattach itself silently failed; (2) `Durability::Ephemeral` combined with a correlation token is now a typed `fail_unaccepted` refusal (an ephemeral write has no durable row for a token to name, and the pre-lookup would otherwise silently reattach an unrelated earlier durable obligation); (3) `crates/nmp-store/src/redb_store.rs`'s `OUTBOX_CORRELATIONS` doc comment no longer claims "no zero-padding" when the insert actually reuses `receipt_key`'s zero-padded format; (4) the partial-ACK/REJECT boundary's reject half is now proven by token-replay too (`partial_relay_reject_survives_restart_and_replays_by_token`), not merely inherited from the by-id door's generic coverage. Draft PR #604 carries the approved, review-fixed contract, including the local trim of `CorrelationToken`'s public surface (dropped a generic `impl Into<String>` constructor parameter, a public `MAX_LEN` associated const, and struct fields on `CorrelationTokenError::TooLong`, and moved its string accessor to a zero-cost `AsRef<str>` trait impl) needed to keep the governed Rust facade snapshot under its 30000-line ceiling. Exact-head review and required CI, including macOS Swift execution and strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-17 — Typed NIP-22 comments over NIP-73 external targets ([issue #572](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/572), [PR #605](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/605))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #572 records that NMP had no typed representation of NIP-22 (kind:1111) comments, so an app wanting to comment on a NIP-73 external target (e.g. a podcast episode GUID) had no supported way to compose the root/parent tag set correctly, no way to decode a delivered comment into a typed value without hand-rolling tag parsing (with no protection against malformed or mismatched root/parent tag sets being silently coerced into a wrong thread), and no owned demand for "the whole thread rooted at X."
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** new opt-in workspace crate `nmp-nip22` (no engine feature, mirrors `nmp-nip68`/`nmp-nip29`): `Nip73Target` (`PodcastEpisodeGuid` plus one validated `General{value,kind}` variant, not one variant per NIP-73 namespace) whose `i_value()` renders NIP-73's FULL `podcast:item:guid:<guid>` wire prefix (a Lane-B review caught the bare-GUID original as non-conformant -- it silently split an episode's thread from conformant clients like Fountain and made `comment_thread_demand`'s `#I` filter never match their comments); `CommentRoot::{Event,Address,External}`/`CommentParent::{Root,Comment}` modeling general NIP-22 roots, where `CommentRoot::Address` carries an `event_id: Option<EventId>` (NIP-22: "when the parent event is replaceable or addressable, also include an `e`/`E` tag referencing its id") so compose can emit the accompanying reference and decode can accept NIP-22's own canonical "coordinate + event id together" shape rather than rejecting it as a duplicate-contradictory tag (the review's other verified bug); a fallible `decode_comment` returning a typed `CommentDecodeError` (15 variants after the review: added `MalformedExternalValue` for a missing podcast-guid prefix, and same-letter tag duplicates -- e.g. two `I` tags -- and an Event-root parent whose `e` matches the root id but whose `k` disagrees with the root's own `K` are now typed rejections instead of "first one wins" / a silently-accepted case-pair contradiction) for any malformed or mismatched tag set rather than ever producing a coerced `DecodedComment`; `comment_thread_demand` (kind:1111 scoped by the uppercase `#I` root reference, one filter for the whole thread), and byte-deterministic `compose_top_level_comment`/`compose_comment_reply` builders with an exact, caller-supplied-`created_at` tag order. Enrolled in `nmp-audit` with an exclusive `KindClaim` on kind 1111. UniFFI (`crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip22.rs`) adds `FfiCommentRoot` (its `Address` variant carrying the same optional `event_id`), `FfiCommentParent`, `FfiNip73Target`, `FfiDecodedComment`, `FfiCommentDecodeError` (with the new `MalformedExternalValue` and a dedicated `MalformedRowEnvelope` for a malformed row `id`/`pubkey` -- previously misreported as the tag-specific `MalformedRootReference`), free functions `comment_thread_demand`/`decode_comment`, and `NmpEngine.comment_intent(...) -> Arc<FfiComposedWriteIntent>` (reusing the take-once wrapper `nip29.rs` already exports), published through the existing `publish_composed`; `correlation: Option<String>` (#591) passes straight through to `WriteIntent.correlation` with no comment-specific correlation machinery. Swift adds `NIP22.swift` (`Nip73Target`, `CommentRoot`, `CommentParent`, `DecodedComment`, `CommentDecodeError`, `commentThreadDemand`, `decodeComment`, `CommentIntent`, `NMPEngine.commentIntent`, `NMPError.invalidNip73Target`) and a `publishComposed(_ intent: CommentIntent)` overload in `Receipt.swift`. Kotlin adds `NIP22.kt` mirroring the same surface (`NMPEngine.commentIntent`, `publishComposed(CommentIntent)` in `Receipt.kt`/`Engine.kt`, `NMPError.InvalidNip73Target`).
- **Persistence impact:** none. `nmp-nip22` is engine-free and store-free; `comment_intent` composes an ordinary `WriteIntent` (`Durable`, `AuthorOutbox`) that flows through the existing outbox/acceptance path unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. NIP-22 comments are ordinary kind:1111 events; no new diagnostics-schema concept is introduced.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip22/src/decode.rs` and `crates/nmp-nip22/src/build.rs` carry the crate-level fixture/determinism/malformed-rejection tests (valid top-level and reply decode, missing root/parent kind, mismatched I/i, wrong external kind, duplicate contradictory root, malformed event reference, an exhaustiveness witness over `CommentDecodeError`, and exact-tag-order/byte-identity compose falsifiers), PLUS post-review additions: `podcast_guid_missing_prefix_is_rejected`, `duplicate_same_letter_root_tags_are_rejected`, `duplicate_contradictory_k_root_tags_are_rejected`, `duplicate_same_letter_parent_tags_are_rejected`, `spec_canonical_address_root_with_accompanying_event_id_decodes` (NIP-22's own a+e/A+E shape), `address_root_without_accompanying_event_id_still_decodes`, and `same_id_parent_with_mismatched_kind_is_a_case_pair_disagreement`. `crates/nmp-nip22/src/build.rs::golden_fixture_tests::golden_fixture_pins_the_exact_composed_bytes` is a REAL golden fixture (fixed secret key, timestamp, content, target) pinning the exact composed event id and NIP-01 JSON body as literal constants -- the same constants `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/NIP22Tests.swift::testGoldenFixturePinsTheExactComposedBytes` and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/NIP22Test.kt::goldenFixturePinsTheExactComposedBytes` assert, replacing the earlier structural-identity-only proof (`compose_is_deterministic`, which only proved two in-process Rust calls agree with each other) with an actual cross-language byte-identity check. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip22.rs` mirrors the FFI-boundary shape of the same cases. `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/NIP22Tests.swift` and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/NIP22Test.kt` are executable SDK falsifiers covering thread-demand scoping, the decode fixture, the malformed-rejection matrix, an offline-signer durable-acceptance + correlation-reattachment falsifier (compose with a correlation token while unsigned, publish, observe `Accepted`+`AwaitingCapability`, then reattach by token alone), and (post-review) `testDurableAcceptanceMakesOneCanonicalPendingCommentVisibleThroughTheQueryPath`/`durableAcceptanceMakesOneCanonicalPendingCommentVisibleThroughTheQueryPath`, which observe the durably-accepted pending row through `comment_thread_demand` + `observe` and decode it with `decodeComment` -- the issue's own "canonical pending comment visible through the ordinary query path" falsifier, which the original suite had NOT exercised (coverage previously stopped at receipt statuses). `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was run (not merely compiled) and remains green; no dedicated NIP-22 parity scenario was added because `comment_intent`/`FfiComposedWriteIntent::take` are `pub(crate)` and not reachable from the external `nmp-parity` crate without a fuller engine round trip -- a documented scope trade-off, not an oversight.
- **Superseded path removed:** none — this is a pure addition; no existing type, function, or FFI surface was changed or removed. The bare-GUID `i_value()` rendering and the a+e-rejecting decode logic were pre-merge bugs fixed in place, not a superseded path (this PR never merged with the non-conformant behavior).
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority requested implementation of issue #572 (stacked after issue #591) on 2026-07-16, resolving the design via the issue's own final decision comment before coding. An independent Lane-B review of PR #605 found two verified spec-conformance bugs (NIP-73 canonicalization; false rejection of NIP-22's own canonical addressable-root shape) plus medium/nit findings; all were fixed in place (see the "Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact" and "Updated falsifiers" fields above) before this signoff. PR #605 carries the approved, review-fixed contract. Exact-head review and required CI, including macOS Swift execution, Kotlin/JVM compilation, and strict surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-19 — Link Hickory 0.26's Apple system-resolver dependency ([issue #702](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/702), [PR #703](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/703))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #702 upgrades Hickory past two resolver denial-of-service advisories. The resulting simulator XCFramework compiled, but the supported Swift package failed its final macOS test link with unresolved `SCDynamicStore*` and `SCNetwork*` symbols because Hickory 0.26's Apple system-resolver implementation adds a native `SystemConfiguration` dependency that the package manifest did not declare.
- **Changed projections:** swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no Rust, UniFFI component, generated binding, hand-written Swift API, or Kotlin API shape changes. `Packages/NMP/Package.swift` adds a platform-conditional `SystemConfiguration` framework link to the generated-binding target for iOS and macOS, making the existing static Rust archive linkable by supported Apple consumers.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, migration, store, receipt, or durable fact changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics shape or acquisition-fact behavior changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the repository's `swift-package` test now links and executes the full Swift suite against the Hickory 0.26 XCFramework; `iOS Simulator NIP-11 qualification` links the same package into the Falsifier app and executes hostname NIP-11 acquisition on an actual simulator. The Rust resolver admission/lifecycle falsifiers remain `dns_resolution_to_loopback_is_refused_without_opt_in`, `held_hickory_dns_is_cancelled_and_joined_at_exact_shutdown`, and `non_success_is_one_request_with_no_retry`.
- **Superseded path removed:** none. The package retains one generated-binding target and one static NMP binary target; no alternate linker wrapper or compatibility package is introduced.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's delegated agent was instructed to carry the iOS/Rust architecture through full native validation and merge, and captured this exact-head Apple-link consequence in PR #703 on 2026-07-19. Required CI, strict surface governance, and exact-head review remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-18 — Pull-based async observation handles; native-task capacity surface removed ([issue #680](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/680), [PR #693](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/693))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #680 records that every long-lived FFI observation spawned one dedicated OS thread blocking on `recv()`, capped by a global zero-queue executor exposed to apps as `maxNativeTasks` (default 12). Ordinary app composition (app/group/inbox/timeline/profile observers on one engine) therefore made opening an unrelated query fail with `ExecutorSaturated` at roughly the thirteenth observation: `live observations == NMP OS threads`.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** long-lived observation delivery (row, window, diagnostics, follow, receipt, follow-action) becomes a waker-driven async pull handle over an engine-owned bounded mailbox; `NmpEngine::observe*`/`publish*`/`follow*` are sync constructors returning `NmpRowStream`/`NmpDiagnosticsStream`/`NmpReceiptStream`/`NmpFollowStream`/`NmpFollowActionStream` with `async next()`/`cancel()`, and `sign_event` returns `NmpSignEventHandle` with `async signed()`/`cancel()`. The callback-interface observers are deleted. `nmp` gains `AsyncSubscription`/`AsyncDiagnosticsSubscription`/`Engine::observe_async`; `nmp-nip02` gains `AsyncFollowObservation`. Swift wraps each as a direct `AsyncSequence`: a reference-owned iterator core performs one native pull per app pull, forwards task cancellation, cancels on normal loop exit, and owns no pump, continuation, or second queue; one enum-backed claim per shared native handle surfaces typed `concurrentNext` before a competitor touches native state. Kotlin wraps ordinary observations as cold `Flow`s that open an independent handle per collection; its windowed-handle flow is explicitly single-collection. Receipt/follow-action live delivery uses a fixed 32-item FIFO and surfaces typed `FactStreamLagged`; receipt reattachment transparently traverses deterministic 32-fact pages with an opaque identity-stable per-lane continuation bounded by relay fan-out, and reports typed `ReceiptReplayUnavailable` if retained evidence disappears mid-replay. Each live receipt sink has a private registration identity tied to the consumer FIFO's close/drop hook, so cancellation detaches it from `PendingWrite` without waiting for another status. The continuation and registration are internal delivery mechanisms, not third app nouns and not Swift/Kotlin protocol cursors. The app-visible native-task capacity surface — `maxNativeTasks`/`max_native_tasks`, `FfiNativeTaskCensus`/`native_task_census`, `await_native_tasks_idle`, and every `ExecutorSaturated`/`executorSaturated` variant — is removed from Rust, FFI, Swift, and Kotlin with no deprecated alias. New typed `ConcurrentNext` (single-consumer misuse) and `FfiSignEventFailure::AlreadyConsumed` variants land, reachably constructed. `ThreadUnavailable` and NIP-11 `WaiterSaturated`/`relayInformationWaitersSaturated` are retained.
- **Persistence impact:** no event/coverage/journal/schema change. Receipt durability continues to live in the outbox/redb store. Retry execution is bounded but retry count and retained attempt history are not claimed finite; #46 remains the retention/GC owner. Reattachment uses per-lane fact identities rather than a mutable-vector count to bound each delivered page; continuation state is bounded by relay fan-out, while the current store APIs still reconstruct the retained canonical history before selecting that page.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. The native-task census/idle-barrier read-out is removed with the capacity concept it described.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp-engine::runtime::diagnostics_channel` and `fifo_channel` prove waker wake-on-send/producer-drop, discard-on-cancel with no post-cancel frame, order-preserving finite FIFO, explicit lag after the retained prefix, exactly-once close hooks, and concurrent-`next()` rejection. A real durable-write proof pauses the consumer across 40 retry cycles, proves the sink is pruned at capacity plus one, drains exactly 32 live facts, receives typed lag, and then traverses every canonical fact through pages no larger than 32. A second durable proof consumes one page, inserts an earlier-sorting fact on another relay, and proves all 42 facts are delivered exactly once across the identity-stable continuation. `parked_awaiting_capability_reattach_cancel_does_not_retain_sinks` alternates close/drop across 128 reattachments while the receipt can never emit again and proves the reducer-side live-sink count returns to zero after every cycle. `nmp-ffi/tests/thread_scaling.rs` proves opening 1,000 observations creates 0 additional NMP OS threads via direct `nmp_threads_spawned()` instrumentation; `nmp-ffi/tests/async_observation_falsifiers.rs` proves 64 simultaneous observations plus diagnostics/follow/receipt never refuse and deliver initial state, cancellation/shutdown wake a parked `next()` to `None`, and concurrent `next()` is a typed misuse. Swift tests prove one app pull equals one native pull, cadence without prefetch, normal loop exit withdraws demand and releases the claim, and competing iterators fail loudly; Kotlin concurrency tests exercise hundreds of observations with no capacity config or error. Exact-head workspace, SDK, parity, architecture, and CI gates remain required before merge.
- **Superseded path removed:** the one-thread-per-observer FFI drain bridge, the `RowObserver`/`DiagnosticsObserver`/`ReceiptObserver`/`SignEventObserver`/`FollowObserver`/`FollowActionObserver` callback interfaces, the `NmpQueryHandle`/`NmpDiagnosticsHandle`/`NmpFollowHandle` handles, and the app-visible native-task capacity surface are deleted outright. The generic `nmp-executor` is rescoped to a fixed-capacity, app-invisible pool for transient blocking adapters (NIP-11/signer/AUTH waiters, follow-action worker); engine-associated NIP-46 sessions own their own executor. The `native-task-executor.md` design record is retained as superseded; `async-observation-handles.md` is the replacement.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this architectural fix on current master and required that the app-visible native-task ceiling be removed rather than fossilized. PR #693 carries the end-to-end change (Rust, FFI, Swift, Kotlin, bindings, falsifiers, docs) and closes #680; it progresses #46. Exact-head review and required CI, including surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-19 — Eliminate the internal task/thread admission-capacity concept end-to-end ([issue #704](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/704), [PR #705](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/705))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #704 records that #693 removed the app-visible observation ceiling but left the admission concept itself alive under the surface: a fixed-capacity `nmp-executor` still gated every transient blocking adapter (NIP-11/signer/AUTH waiters, the follow-action worker), each engine-associated NIP-46 session owned its OWN `nmp_executor::Executor`, standalone NIP-46 built one worker thread per session, and apps could still receive `ThreadUnavailable`/NIP-11 `WaiterSaturated`/`relayInformationWaitersSaturated` merely because internal workers were occupied. Logical async work (a pending remote sign, a NIP-46 handshake, a NIP-11 fetch) still consumed one OS thread while waiting, so thread count scaled with logical work, not with distinct relays.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** every executor user is reclassified and rewritten as an async task on ONE engine-owned multi-thread tokio runtime (`Engine::adapter_runtime()`, doc-hidden). The internal admission `nmp-executor` is reduced to a leaf thread-counter (`note_thread_spawn`/`nmp_threads_spawned`); its `Executor`/`Reservation`/`ExecutorSaturated`/census/idle surface is gone. `Effect::RequestSign`, `Cmd::SignEvent`, the AUTH policy/signer waiters, and NIP-11 acquisition park as waker-driven futures holding no thread; the signer and AUTH completion doors encode cancellation, resolution, receiver ownership, and pending-operation completion as enums rather than lifecycle booleans, and the lock-free task terminals write only typed enum codes through their atomics. NIP-11 privately admits at most 8 distinct HTTP/DNS/body flights and coalesces same-relay callers onto one shared completion; excess callers suspend cancellably without a public saturation result. The NIP-46 per-session executor is removed, standalone sessions share ONE process-wide runtime (O(1) runtime threads in session count, not O(sessions)), and each session's request, control, and pool-event queues are finite and backpressured with cancellation/shutdown on independent control signals. The FFI NIP-46 connect handshake runs as a runtime task instead of a dedicated `std::thread`, so a pending handshake holds no OS thread. The one genuine remaining infra `ThreadUnavailable` is SPLIT into two honest, pool-free domain errors: `EngineError::EngineStartFailed { component, reason }` (raised only by engine construction) and `EngineError::ObservationUnavailable { reason }` (whose sole production meaning is failure to open a windowed observation's canonical history projection after store degradation). Relay connection/worker failure remains ordinary acquisition evidence and has no synchronous error edge into observation creation or window growth; the synthetic relay-preflight rollback mechanism, `Effect::PreflightHistoryRelays`, its tests, and the now-unreachable `RequestRowsError::TransportUnavailable` mirror variants are removed. `nmp::RelayInformationError::{ThreadUnavailable,WaiterSaturated}` are deleted (private async backpressure never becomes a public refusal). FFI mirrors the split: `FfiError::{EngineStartFailed,ObservationUnavailable}` replace `FfiError::ThreadUnavailable`, and the dead `FfiError::RelayInformationWaitersSaturated`, `FfiRelayInformationErrorKind::{WaiterSaturated,ThreadUnavailable}`, `FfiFollowActionFailure::ThreadUnavailable`, and `FfiNip46Failure::ThreadUnavailable` variants are removed. Swift (`NMPError.engineStartFailed`/`.observationUnavailable`; the `.threadUnavailable`/`.relayInformationWaitersSaturated`/`RelayInformationErrorKind.{waiterSaturated,threadUnavailable}`/`NMPFollowActionFailure.threadUnavailable` cases deleted) and Kotlin (`NMPError.EngineStartFailed`/`ObservationUnavailable`; the matching dead `data class`es deleted) mirror it exactly; bindings regenerated.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store, journal, receipt, or schema change — this is purely the internal concurrency model and its error surface.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics-schema concept changes; the removed capacity surface had already left diagnostics in #693.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-signer/tests/nip46_scaling_704.rs` records `nmp_threads_spawned()` at 1/10/50/100 NIP-46 sessions and proves ZERO per-session executor-thread growth (bounded transport-only delta). `relay_information::tests::distinct_fetch_overload_has_a_finite_physical_envelope_and_cache_hits_progress` drives 32 distinct callers, proves exactly 8 live flights/subscriptions and unrelated cache-hit progress, then cancels both admitted and admission-waiting callers to zero. `nip46::tests::nip46_queue_overload_is_finite_backpressured_and_control_still_progresses` fills the exact 64-request, 8-control, and 256-event envelopes, proves producers suspend outside each full queue, and proves control traffic plus independent shutdown still complete while request admission is saturated. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/parked_nip11_704.rs` keeps 64 distinct callers pending while the 8-flight network/body envelope stays fixed, with zero per-call OS-thread growth and prompt cancellation. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/mixed_load_704.rs` holds 1,000 observations while NIP-11, local sign, follow observation/action, and a durable receipt progress concurrently on one engine with no capacity refusal (no such error type exists) and no thread growth proportional to concurrency; dedicated overlapping AUTH, remote-signing, foreign-completion, and NIP-46 falsifiers cover the deterministic transports those paths require. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/surface_audit_704.rs` asserts the generated snapshots and hand-written SDK sources contain none of `ThreadUnavailable`/`ExecutorSaturated`/`WaiterSaturated`/`TransportUnavailable`/`transportUnavailable`/`maxNativeTasks`/census/idle identifiers. The rewritten Swift `RelayInformationTests` and Kotlin `RelayInformationTest` prove 65 concurrent NIP-11 fetches are never capacity-refused (replacing the old typed-waiter-saturation tests). The engine AUTH completion-door handshake (`nmp-ffi/src/auth.rs` `cancel_arriving_during_resolving_waits_and_cannot_drop_receiver`, `queued_resolution_wins_simultaneous_terminal_cancel_without_receiver_gone`) remains the cross-boundary oracle; targeted Rust tests additionally prove cancellation or consumer drop runs the adapter hook exactly once, truthfully refuses a late resolver, and permanently spends the one-shot resolution slot. The canonical history-projection failure is the only production constructor for `ObservationUnavailable`; relay-worker preflight and its synthetic rollback tests are absent. `nmp-parity` direct/FFI oracle, the #680 `thread_scaling.rs`/`async_observation_falsifiers.rs`, full workspace `cargo test`, strict Clippy `-D warnings`, formatting, surface regeneration, the 159-test Swift package suite, and the Kotlin/JVM Gradle suite remain merge gates.
  Additional review-blocker falsifiers: `nip46::tests::pending_rpc_cap_keeps_control_live_and_admits_after_exact_cancellation` fills the exact 64-pending-request worker envelope, proves control progress, then cancels one exact pending request and proves one suspended producer is admitted without a public saturation error. `core::history_load_failure_tests::production_runtime_projection_failure_is_the_observation_refusal` drives the real production runtime and proves the canonical history-projection failure is the sole observation-creation refusal.
- **Superseded path removed:** the admission `nmp_executor::Executor` and every `Reservation`/`ExecutorSaturated`/reserve-native-task path, the per-session NIP-46 `Executor`, the standalone per-session NIP-46 runtime, the `std::thread`-per-NIP-46-connect handshake, the per-operation `ThreadUnavailable` refusal, and the NIP-11 `WaiterSaturated`/`relayInformationWaitersSaturated` admission surface are deleted outright with no deprecated alias. `native-task-executor.md` remains retained-as-superseded; `internal-executor-elimination.md` is the replacement design record.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed continuation of the resource-architecture correction on current master, requiring that the admission concept be removed end-to-end (not raised, hidden, renamed, or retried around) and that logical async work never consume an OS thread while waiting. Architecture decisions — the construction-vs-operation error split and the fully-async NIP-46 connect — were validated by an independent architecture review before implementation. PR #705 carries the end-to-end change (Rust, FFI, Swift, Kotlin, bindings, falsifiers, docs, surface snapshots) and closes #704, cross-linking #680 and #693. Exact-head review and required CI, including surface governance, the FFI parity oracle, macOS Swift execution, and Kotlin/JVM compilation, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-24 — Bounded same-relay read/write session time-sharing ([issue #598](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/598), [PR #709](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/709))

- **Failure evidence:** the public Swift reproduction on a real iOS Simulator configured one controlled hostname relay and `maxRelays = 1`. NIP-11, kind:10002 route discovery, and an ordinary live read all succeeded, but a durable author-outbox write stopped permanently after `Accepted`, `Signed`, `Routed`, and `AwaitingRelay`; the relay received no `EVENT`. Changing only the cap to 2 made the same flow reach one `Sent`, one `Acked`, and the live-query echo. The Public read session held the sole physical worker while the write correctly required a distinct `RelaySessionKey(relay, Nip42(author))`; ordinary cap refusal had no event that could release the read owner or wake the write.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the public configuration shape and default are unchanged. Rust's reducer effect vocabulary replaces ambiguous `EnsureRelay` with `EnsureReadRelay` and `EnsureWriteRelay`; only the write-owned effect can release the same relay's Public worker, while a protected read cannot acquire that authority from its non-Public context. Required worker reconciliation now returns one coherent `all` set plus its exact `writes` subset, prioritizes the write after a real retirement, and restores still-owned Public demand after the write becomes terminal. The Rust facade, UniFFI record, Swift `NMPConfig`, and Kotlin `NMPConfig` documentation now state the same contract: access contexts remain socket-isolated, simultaneous physical workers remain bounded by `maxRelays`, and competing read/write contexts for one admitted relay time-share that slot rather than forcing apps to multiply the value.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store, schema, journal, lane, receipt, or retry-fact shape changes. Existing nonterminal write ownership supplies the admission capability.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. A physical-cap collision remains observable through the existing session-admission counter, but it no longer becomes a permanent write stall when the competing owner is the same relay's Public read.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `testDurableAuthorOutboxWriteProgressesPastAwaitingRelay` runs the original public-API controlled-relay flow inside `NMPSimulatorQualificationTests` on a real iOS Simulator at `maxRelays = 1`; it proves route discovery, live read evidence, exactly one relay `EVENT`, truthful `Sent`/`Acked`, relay echo through the still-live query, restored read ownership, and bounded shutdown. Every harness edge is delivered through an exact continuation raced against one timeout task; `testEventDrivenWaitersWithdrawExactlyOnTimeout` proves timed-out query, receipt, and relay waiters are removed before a later mutation can observe them. `protected_write_session_preempts_same_relay_public_slot_at_cap_one` proves the runtime releases the exact Public generation and admits the write without exceeding one live session. `protected_read_session_cannot_claim_write_preemption_authority` proves a protected query emits only `EnsureReadRelay` and cannot displace the Public worker.
- **Superseded path removed:** the ambiguous internal `Effect::EnsureRelay` variant and runtime store re-read used to guess whether a session was write-owned are removed outright. Access contexts are not coalesced, no public saturation or second capacity setting is introduced, and no compatibility alias remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed continuous issue-first backlog work with end-to-end validation and architecture review. Issue #598 contains the exact current-master reproduction and root-cause evidence; PR #709 carries the correction, and exact-head independent review, full workspace/SDK gates, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-24 — Preserve typed Swift error evidence in native descriptions ([issue #710](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/710), [PR #713](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/713))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #710 records that ordinary Swift presentation through `error.localizedDescription` discarded the `NMPError` case and every associated value, producing only an unstable opaque code such as `NMP.NMPError error 11`. That erased the actual store-open reason in #707, made code 11 look like receipt-correlation exhaustion even though that case is code 27 and cannot occur during engine startup, and led the client toward destructive recovery without preserving the original evidence.
- **Changed projections:** swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Swift's existing `NMPError` enum now conforms to `LocalizedError` through one exhaustive `errorDescription` switch. Every case receives stable case-specific text, and every associated reason, path, component, capacity, receipt id, bound, token, or malformed value remains present; nested `RelayInformationErrorKind` uses its existing exhaustive human-readable projection. No enum case, throwing signature, Rust/FFI contract, generated binding, or Kotlin surface changes.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Error presentation does not open, mutate, reset, migrate, or infer recovery for any store.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. The change preserves evidence already carried by a thrown Swift value and adds no diagnostics field, receipt fact, log record, or telemetry.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `NMPErrorLocalizedDescriptionTests.testEveryCaseHasAStableNativeDescriptionWithItsEvidence` pins explicit `errorDescription` and `localizedDescription` text for every `NMPError` case, including both receipt-present and receipt-absent lag states, and rejects the opaque numeric fallback. `NMPErrorLocalizedDescriptionTests.testEveryRelayInformationKindKeepsItsNestedEvidence` exercises all five nested NIP-11 failure kinds. The production switch is exhaustive, so a future enum case cannot compile without choosing its native presentation.
- **Superseded path removed:** Foundation's synthesized opaque numeric presentation is replaced at the existing enum boundary; no compatibility alias, second error type, recovery policy, or automatic reset path is introduced.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed continuous issue-first backlog work with exact-head architecture review and end-to-end validation. Issue #710 captures the corrected diagnosis and evidence-preservation consequence; PR #713 carries the Swift-only correction, with exact-head independent review, the complete Swift package, surface governance, and required CI remaining merge gates.

## 2026-07-24 — Publish a brand-new account's first NIP-65 relay list without inventing routing facts ([issue #719](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/719), [PR #720](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/720))

- **Failure evidence:** a brand-new local account has no network-ingested kind:10002, so ordinary `WriteRouting::AuthorOutbox` correctly fails closed for the account's first relay-list publication. Direct Rust exposed no semantic operation that could break this bootstrap cycle without an app depending on internal grammar/transport machinery, inventing a local directory fact, or sending the event outside NMP's durable signer/outbox/receipt path.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust adds the opt-in `nmp-nip65` protocol crate with `BootstrapRelayList`, `RelayListEntry`, `RelayUsage`, and `publish_relay_list_bootstrap`. The request separates a finite exact delivery set from the bounded NIP-65 relay policy it advertises, composes one unsigned kind:10002, and returns the ordinary `ReceiptStream`. `WriteRouting` gains a `RelayListBootstrap` variant backed by an immutable exact authority. That authority remains absent from the supported `nmp` facade; a structural consumer crate proves an app names only `nmp` and `nmp-nip65`. FFI, Swift, and Kotlin are unchanged and explicitly documented as unsupported for this operation rather than being imitated with a raw native socket or route array.
- **Persistence impact:** durable route snapshots add the `nip65-bootstrap-hex:` representation so a process restart reopens exactly the accepted relay set without accepting a second obligation. No event, coverage, relay-directory, or schema layout changes; the existing route-snapshot string column remains the persistence boundary.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. The existing tracked receipt exposes acceptance, the exact routed relay set, per-relay attempts, acknowledgements, and failures. The existing observation row and source provenance expose when the real relay-backed kind:10002 has returned through ordinary ingest. Resolving the bootstrap route itself emits no synthetic row or directory fact.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip65/tests/live_bootstrap.rs` holds the first real relay EVENT before acceptance and proves `AuthorOutbox` still fails with no known write relay; it then releases that same EVENT, observes the kind:10002 through the already-open public query with real relay provenance, and proves only then that `AuthorOutbox` routes and ACKs. `relay_list_bootstrap_routing_round_trips_across_a_restart` proves exact durable recovery. `relay_list_bootstrap_resolves_exactly_without_installing_directory_facts` proves the route ignores unrelated directory state. `nmp-nip65-consumer-check` proves the application dependency boundary, while the module validation and fixed-time composition tests pin the bounds, author, kind, tags, empty content, and exact route.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; no prior semantic NIP-65 bootstrap API existed. The change adds no compatibility alias, generic raw-route constructor, transport escape hatch, local directory injection, or synthetic observed row.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly required that the demo's reactivity be real, authorized an issue-first NMP fix/PR/merge for any true engine gap, and rejected any bootstrap that would cheat by fabricating the routing transition. Issue #719 records that contract and PR #720 carries its direct-Rust implementation. Exact-head review, strict surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-24 — Expose authoritative direct-Rust observation execution evidence ([issue #718](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/718), [PR #721](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/721))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #718 records that observation frames carried rows and scoped acquisition evidence while engine-global diagnostics carried only current filter/coverage state. A consumer therefore could not truthfully attribute a reactive input, derived-set transition, concrete-filter revision, transport handoff, or EOSE to one descriptor path and request generation without app-side inference.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust adds a compact facade-owned `ObservationEvidence` envelope to unwindowed `Frame.execution`. Its stable `kind`, structural `path`, monotonic `revision`, exact `values`, stable `fingerprint`, and ordered scalar `attributes` avoid leaking mechanism-crate types while retaining the resolver/runtime owner's complete evidence: canonical NIP-01 filter JSON plus relay/access, transport generation, request revision, replay, EOSE, close/refusal, withdrawal, and overflow correlation. The envelope was compacted after rebasing #720 so the governed facade remains below its unchanged 30,000-line ceiling rather than raising or bypassing it. UniFFI, Swift, Kotlin, and windowed Rust frames are deliberately unchanged in this first phase and remain open under issue #718.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Evidence is an observation-lifetime runtime stream; no event, coverage, outbox, receipt, journal, or schema representation changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change. Engine-global diagnostics remain global and are not reverse-mapped into per-observation causality.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `active_account_and_external_kind3_changes_emit_only_real_resolution_changes`; `slow_observer_gets_explicit_execution_evidence_overflow`; `allowed_local_relay_host_reaches_the_facade_transport_pool`; `cancel_handle_unblocks_a_genuinely_blocked_recv_within_a_bound`. Together they prove dependency-first structural paths, account rerooting, external kind:3 reactivity with no false equal-set revision, explicit bounded overflow, a real local-WebSocket REQ/replay-to-EOSE correlation, and terminal withdrawal before disconnect.
- **Superseded path removed:** none; this is additive observation evidence. The demo-side global-diagnostics inference path is intentionally not moved into NMP or preserved as a supported causality mechanism.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly required the demo not to cheat about reactivity and authorized issue-backed NMP corrections through merge. PR #721 carries the direct-Rust phase; issue #718 remains open for windowed and FFI/Swift/Kotlin parity. Exact-head review and required CI, including surface governance, remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-24 — Preserve complete nested Demand identity across native SDKs ([issue #714](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/714), [issue #49](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/49), [PR #716](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/716))

- **Failure evidence:** Rust's `Derived.inner` was already a complete `Demand`, but the UniFFI object exposed only `FfiFilter`; its conversion discarded source authority, access context, cache mode, and freshness, then silently reapplied `Demand::from_filter` defaults. Swift and Kotlin inherited that narrowing as `NMPFilter`, so native callers could not represent the ratified #49 invariant in which a nested user-list query and its dependent host/AUTH query own independent contexts.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the Rust grammar and `nmp` facade shape are unchanged. `FfiDerived.inner` widens from `FfiFilter` to `FfiDemand`, and both conversion directions now preserve the complete nested descriptor without a defaulting fallback. Swift changes `NMPBinding.derived(inner:)` from `NMPFilter` to `NMPDemand`; Kotlin changes `NMPBinding.Derived.inner` identically. Every native construction site, reference-demand helper, generated binding consumer, and test fixture is updated in the same PR with no compatibility alias.
- **Persistence impact:** no schema, version, migration, or stored-byte change. The existing v2 coverage key already hashes source and access context; this PR adds the missing same-selection Public-vs-NIP-42 anti-alias falsifier without changing key construction.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema or new fact type. The existing per-current-plan acquisition evidence remains scoped by the full contextual atoms that native nested demands can now express instead of losing their policies at the projection boundary.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-resolver/tests/contract.rs::derived_inner_and_outer_demands_keep_independent_source_and_access_contexts` exercises the exact #49 promotion shape: a current-pubkey kind:10009 demand stays AuthorOutboxes/Public beneath a pinned NIP-42 group demand and reroots only its dependent work. `crates/nmp-store/src/coverage.rs::coverage_key_differs_for_different_access_context` proves the durable evidence key cannot alias identical selections across Public and NIP-42. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs::derived_inner_full_demand_round_trips_every_policy_independently`, Swift `FilterBuilderTests.testDerivedInnerFullDemandRoundTripsEveryPolicyIndependently`, and Kotlin `NMPDemandTest.derivedInnerFullDemandRoundTripsEveryPolicyIndependently` preserve distinct nested source/access/cache/freshness values and reject same-selection context equality. The real-relay strict authenticated capstone `follows_minus_mutes_resolves_over_a_real_relay` remains green.
- **Superseded path removed:** the filter-only `FfiDerived.inner`, both native filter-only derived cases, and the conversion through `Demand::from_filter` are deleted outright. No deprecated initializer, implicit inheritance rule, or filter-shaped compatibility path remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly requested this repair under the issue-first workflow and required the identified gaps to be fixed end-to-end. PR #716 is the exact review record; required CI, surface governance, and reviewer approval remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Own exact-byte asset identity in one protocol-neutral crate ([issue #884](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/884), [PR #897](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/897))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #884 records that byte identity for uploaded media had no single owner: `nmp-blossom` held BUD-01 hashing privately, and a digest parsed out of an untrusted descriptor was the same Rust type as a digest computed from bytes we actually saw. Nothing structurally distinguished a claimed hash from a verified one, so the bad path was excluded only by reviewer attention (`docs/bug-class-ledger.md:3-5`).
- **Changed projections:** ffi
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the only governed-surface delta is one `FfiBlobDescriptor` docstring, corrected because `FfiBlossomClient::mirror` no longer returns a `VerifiedUpload`-derived value. No UniFFI record field, method signature, error variant, or checksum changes, and the `nmp` facade snapshot is byte-identical. Swift and Kotlin are unchanged: no native declaration references the removed Rust type. Direct Rust adds the `nmp-asset` crate (`Sha256Hash`, `Sha256HexError`, `VerifiedAsset`), `nmp_blossom::VerifiedUpload::asset`/`into_asset`, and `nmp_media::UploadedAsset::asset`; `nmp_blossom::BlossomClient::mirror` now returns `BlobDescriptor` because a mirroring client never observes the mirrored bytes and therefore has nothing from which to mint an exact-byte witness.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No event, descriptor, coverage, outbox, receipt, journal, or key layout changes; nothing in this change is persisted.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, or receipt fact is added, removed, or reshaped.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-asset/src/lib.rs`'s `compile_fail,E0451` doctest on `VerifiedAsset` proves a struct-literal forgery from a parsed hash does not compile, and pins privacy as the reason; `hash_matches_known_vector_and_strict_hex_round_trips` pins the known SHA-256 vector plus strict lowercase-hex round-trip; `wrong_length_hex_is_a_typed_refusal_carrying_the_observed_length` and `non_lowercase_hex_is_a_typed_refusal_never_case_folded` carry the deleted `nmp-blossom::sha256` refusal suite forward; `witness_identity_is_derived_from_exact_bytes_not_locator_text` proves two byte sequences behind one URL yield different witness identities. `crates/nmp-blossom/tests/upload_contract.rs::upload_authorization_binds_exact_sha256_verb_and_expiration` now also asserts the minted `VerifiedAsset`'s digest, byte length, and untrusted MIME.
- **Superseded path removed:** `crates/nmp-blossom/src/sha256.rs` is deleted outright and `nmp-blossom` re-exports neither `Sha256Hash` nor `Sha256HexError`. No compatibility alias, no deprecated re-export, and no `VerifiedUpload`-returning `mirror` path remains; every caller (`nmp-media`, `nmp-nip68`, `nmp-ffi`) is updated in this PR.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed the issue-first recovery of work implemented without an issue in the contaminated `nmp-issue-827-opaque-write-intent` worktree, requiring each recovered unit to land as its own reviewable PR. Issue #884 records this unit's contract and PR #897 carries it. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Give every persistent store one cross-process exclusive owner ([issue #489](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/489), [PR #915](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/915))

- **Failure evidence:** the supported persistent-store boundary had two owners. `RedbStore` owned the database handle while a process-global `OPEN_STORES` map separately guessed which canonical paths were live so destructive reset could refuse deletion. That registry coupled unrelated engines, permitted multiple independent database owners for one path, and had no authority outside its own process: a second process could open or reset a store that was live elsewhere, and the surviving prose said so. `docs/bug-class-ledger.md` entry 21 was PARTIAL on a data-corruption class while #489 stayed open, and the Destructive-API Gate's own trained tell records that the precondition had already vanished by `Engine.swift`.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `RedbStore::open` now acquires one nonblocking cross-process exclusive lock on a durable sidecar keyed by the resolved canonical target before the database is created, exposed, or mutated, and owns it by RAII until the database handle finishes dropping. `nmp-store` publishes `RedbStoreOpenError` (open no longer returns a bare `redb::Error`) and widens `RedbStoreResetError` from a single `ResetFailed` string into typed resolve/lock/retarget/remove arms. The `nmp` facade gains `EngineError::StoreAlreadyOpen { path }`; UniFFI gains `FfiError::StoreAlreadyOpen { path }`; Swift gains `NMPError.storeAlreadyOpen`; Kotlin gains `NMPError.StoreAlreadyOpen`. `Engine::reset_persistent_store`, the UniFFI operation, and both native `resetPersistentStore` docs drop their "process-local" / "cross-process exclusion is not provided" qualifications because the mechanism now holds across processes.
- **Persistence impact:** no schema, version, migration, or stored-byte change. One new mechanism artifact per canonical target: a zero-length `.nmp-store-owner-<blake3-of-target>.lock` sidecar in the target's parent directory. It holds no store content, has no schema, is never read, and deliberately survives reset — a stable lock inode is load-bearing so that deleting and recreating the database file cannot mint a second lock identity while an older database handle is still alive.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution representation changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-store/src/persistent_store_lifetime.rs` adds `subprocess_owner_is_refused_through_direct_relative_and_symlink_aliases` (a real second process owns the store; the parent is refused through direct, relative, and symlink aliases; reset refuses and the bytes are byte-identical afterwards; once the owner exits exactly one new opener succeeds and reset succeeds only after that owner drops), `one_owner_refuses_second_open_and_reset_until_drop`, `a_relative_alias_of_a_live_store_is_refused`, `existing_and_dangling_final_symlinks_share_the_target_owner`, `target_retargeted_during_lock_acquisition_fails_closed`, `concurrent_openers_have_exactly_one_owner`, `racing_open_against_reset_leaves_one_owner_and_no_lost_bytes`, and `schema_failure_releases_database_and_sidecar_ownership`. `persistent_store_reset_is_destructive_and_idempotent` and `ffi_persistent_store_reset_is_destructive_and_idempotent` now also prove a second engine over a live store path is refused. `second_store_open_refusal_remains_a_typed_ffi_error`, Swift `testSecondStoreOwnerRefusalRemainsTypedAtTheNativeBoundary`, and Kotlin `secondStoreOwnerRefusalRemainsTypedAtTheNativeBoundary` prove the native projection. `raw_engine_thread_owns_persistent_reset_guard_until_join` and `failed_persistent_store_open_releases_reset_guard` remain green against the new mechanism.
- **Superseded path removed:** `OPEN_STORES`, `open_and_register`, `OpenStoreRegistration`, `RegisteredOpen`, the registry mutex with its poison-recovery helper, and the per-path refcount are deleted outright; a source census for those names returns nothing. No compatibility path, unlocked raw `RedbStore` constructor, process singleton, or doc-only reset precondition remains, and the "cross-process locking is a known gap" text is removed from `docs/known-gaps.md` rather than restated.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this recovery operation to land #489 as one scoped PR under the issue-first workflow, explicitly excluding the adjacent legacy-schema work. PR #915 is the exact review record. Exact-head review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Keep NIP-51 parsing observational; forbid derived authority ([issue #863](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/863), [PR #926](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/926))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #863 records that a *tolerant* NIP-51 Simple-groups reader shipped under the authoritative-sounding name `decode_simple_groups_list(FfiRow)`, so a caller-constructed row could be mistaken for canonical kind:10009 state and fed into unrelated NIP-29 host selection. The first attempted correction was equally unsound: it minted a public observation-qualified `ObservedSimpleGroupsList` from a frame proof, inventing a speculative protocol-specific lifecycle/capability on top of `LiveQuery` that no current operation needs. Both shapes obscure ownership — a parser result is data, and authority belongs only inside the concrete operation whose precondition requires it (VISION P1/P10; `docs/bug-class-ledger.md:3-5`).
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust renames both `nmp-nip51` entry points to `parse_simple_groups_list_tolerant` / `parse_simple_groups_list_from_raw_tags_tolerant` with no alias, and states non-authority in the module doc, both function docs, and `SimpleGroupsList`'s own doc. FFI adds the `nip51` module with the records `FfiSimpleGroupEntry`/`FfiSimpleGroupsList` and the free function `parse_simple_groups_list_tolerant(row)`, making the tolerant value reachable as itself rather than only through NIP-29's wrapper. Swift adds `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/NIP51.swift` (`SimpleGroupEntry`, `SimpleGroupsList`, `parseSimpleGroupsListTolerant(_:)`) and Kotlin adds the identical trio in `NIP51.kt`. The `nmp` facade snapshot is byte-identical: no NIP-51 edge is added to the canonical facade. NIP-29 host selection is unchanged at every layer — each demand constructor still takes an explicit app-chosen host.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, version, migration, journal, coverage key, or stored-byte change; nothing in this change is persisted.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution fact is added, removed, or reshaped.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip51/src/simple_groups.rs::tolerant_parse_of_fabricated_input_yields_plain_evidence_not_authority` proves a wholly fabricated, never-signed, wrong-kind input parses to a value equal to a real signed kind:10009 event's — the result therefore cannot encode provenance, canonicality, or routing permission. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip51.rs::tolerant_parser_preserves_evidence_even_for_fabricated_wrong_kind_row` carries that to the caller-constructible `FfiRow` boundary with malformed-item and private-content evidence intact, and `nip29_browsing_still_demands_an_explicitly_supplied_host` proves the parsed host is an app decision, not a derived authorization. Swift `NIP51Tests.testTolerantParserPreservesEvidenceForFabricatedWrongKindRow`/`testGroupBrowsingStillTakesAnExplicitlySuppliedHost` and Kotlin `NIP51Test.tolerantParserPreservesEvidenceForFabricatedWrongKindRow`/`groupBrowsingStillTakesAnExplicitlySuppliedHost` mirror both, executed against real generated bindings. The structural gate is `scripts/check-nip51-no-derived-authority.sh`, wired as its own job in the architecture-gates workflow.
- **Superseded path removed:** `decode_simple_groups_list` and `decode_simple_groups_list_from_raw_tags` are deleted outright; a source census for those names across `crates/` and `Packages/` returns nothing, and the governance script keeps it that way. No compatibility alias, deprecated re-export, or authoritative-sounding second door remains, and no `ObservedSimpleGroupsList`, `SimpleGroupsProjectionError`, frame-proof NIP-51 projector, or equivalent qualified witness is introduced in its place.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this recovery operation to land #863 as one scoped PR under the issue-first workflow, explicitly excluding the adjacent #858 NIP-29-wrapper deletion and #851 `nmp-ffi` dependency restructure. PR #926 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Delete the duplicate NIP-29 wrapper around NIP-51 simple-group values ([issue #858](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/858), [PR #929](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/929))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #858 records that the read-only group path defined ONE value twice in Rust and then mirrored the duplicate through every SDK. `nmp-nip51` owns kind:10009 and decodes it into `SimpleGroupEntry`/`SimpleGroupsList`; `nmp-nip29` converted that into a second family, and FFI/Swift/Kotlin added a third one around the NIP-29 wrapper. The conversion applied no NIP-29 rule whatsoever — it renamed fields, copied values, and dropped `malformed_item_count`, so the "NIP-29-facing" result was strictly LESS truthful than the NIP-51-owned decode. Two owners of one property is exactly the Noun Gate's trained tell, and the module boundary #63 drew exists to prevent it. PR #926 (issue #863) had just made the NIP-51 value reachable as itself at every layer, leaving the wrapper with nothing to justify it.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust deletes `crates/nmp-nip29/src/group_ref.rs` in full — the NIP-29 group-reference struct, the composed remembered-list struct, and the mapping function — and drops `nmp-nip29`'s `nmp-nip51` dependency, which existed only to feed that wrapper. FFI deletes the two matching UniFFI records from `crates/nmp-ffi/src/types.rs` and the decode free function from `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs`. Swift and Kotlin delete their two mirror structs and the decode function from `NIP29.swift`/`NIP29.kt`. Separately, `active_account_demand` moves out of the NIP-29 file into the NIP-51 file at the FFI, Swift, and Kotlin layers (kind:10009 is NIP-51's kind); the exported UniFFI/Swift/Kotlin name is byte-identical, so no native call site changes — only the file the function is declared in. NIP-29 host selection is untouched at every layer: each demand constructor still takes an explicit app-chosen host. The `nmp` facade snapshot is byte-identical.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, version, migration, journal, coverage key, or stored-byte change; nothing in this change is persisted.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution fact is added, removed, or reshaped.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the surviving NIP-51 browsing falsifier at each layer now also proves that a SELECTED entry feeds NIP-29's host-pinned constructors directly, field for field, with no copy/relabel step and no second lifecycle owner: `nip29_browsing_still_demands_an_explicitly_supplied_host`, Swift `testGroupBrowsingStillTakesAnExplicitlySuppliedHost`, and Kotlin `groupBrowsingStillTakesAnExplicitlySuppliedHost` each build a discovery demand AND a content demand from one parsed entry and assert both target the same pinned source. Evidence preservation through all four layers stays proven, unchanged and green, by `tolerant_parse_of_fabricated_input_yields_plain_evidence_not_authority`, `tolerant_parser_preserves_evidence_even_for_fabricated_wrong_kind_row`, `testTolerantParserPreservesEvidenceForFabricatedWrongKindRow`, and `tolerantParserPreservesEvidenceForFabricatedWrongKindRow` — each fixture carries valid entries, a malformed entry, an `r` relay, and private content. The relocated demand constructor keeps its assertion at every layer: `active_account_demand_projects_the_reactive_authors_binding`, `testActiveAccountDemandTargetsKind10009`, `activeAccountDemandTargetsKind10009`. The structural gate `check-nip51-no-derived-authority.sh` was neither weakened nor edited and passes.
- **Superseded path removed:** the NIP-29 group-reference struct, the composed remembered-groups struct, the `nmp-nip29` mapping function, and the FFI decode function are deleted outright, together with their Swift and Kotlin mirrors and every duplicate test. A source census across `crates/` and `Packages/` for all four names returns nothing — not even in comments.
