No compatibility alias, deprecated re-export, generic "group model" crate, or cross-protocol aggregate replaces them, and exactly one Rust definition and one SDK projection family now represent a decoded kind:10009 Simple-groups list.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this recovery operation to land #858 as one scoped PR under the issue-first workflow, explicitly excluding the #851 `nmp-ffi` dependency restructure queued after it. PR #929 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Enforce a per-relay subscription budget from NIP-11 `max_subscriptions` ([issue #931](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/931), [PR #941](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/941))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #931 records that NIP-11 `limitation.max_subscriptions` was parsed in `crates/nmp-engine/src/relay_information.rs` and read by nothing: no code in `nmp-engine/src/core/` or `nmp-router/src/` consulted it, so planning could open any number of concurrent subscriptions against a relay that had published the number it would hold. The thresholds were once a runtime type, `RelayLimits`, deleted in #123 as "a never-enforced router contract", leaving `MAX_SUBS_PER_RELAY = 20` alive only as a test expectation. Measured against live relays on 2026-07-27: 200 at relay.damus.io, 50 at nostr.wine and purplepag.es, 20 at nos.lol, relay.primal.net and offchain.pub, and NO document at all from relay.nostr.band or relay.snort.social, while `max_limit` is 500-1000 everywhere. `max_subid_length` was parsed and unread on the same path, so a relay advertising fewer than the 64 characters every NMP wire id carries would have rejected every REQ with nothing noticing.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the governed delta is four new public fields on `nmp::RelayDiagnosticsSnapshot` (`subscription_budget`, `subscriptions_refused`, `subid_length_limit`, `subid_length_rejects_our_ids`) and one on `nmp::DiagnosticsSnapshot` (`sessions_refused_by_subscription_budget`). No existing field, method signature, or error variant changes. The UniFFI component snapshot is byte-identical, so Swift and Kotlin are untouched: `FfiRelayDiagnostics` gains nothing and no native declaration changes. Direct Rust adds `nmp_router::{CompileBudget, AdvertisedRelayLimits, BudgetShortfall, WIRE_SUB_ID_CHARS}`; `Router::compile`'s third parameter widens from `usize` to `impl Into<CompileBudget>` with `impl From<usize>`, so every existing caller compiles unchanged and keeps meaning exactly the relay ceiling it always meant. `nmp_engine::relay_information::RelayInformationCapabilityEvidence` gains `max_subscriptions` and `max_subid_length`.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, version, migration, journal, coverage-key, or stored-byte change. The budget is derived per compile from retained NIP-11 evidence and nothing about it is persisted; in particular it feeds no identity — wire ids remain allocated tokens, so a re-published document can never move an established subscription.
- **Diagnostics impact:** per-session diagnostics now reports the relay's own advertised concurrent-subscription budget (`None` means the relay advertised nothing and is therefore unbudgeted, never a fabricated default), how many subscriptions that budget removed, the advertised subscription-id length, and whether that length is shorter than the ids NMP sends. Engine-global diagnostics counts sessions refused outright by a zero budget separately from those refused by the whole-demand relay ceiling, because one says the operator's plan was too wide and the other says this relay will hold nothing open. Refused demand additionally reaches the app as `ShortfallFact::LocalLimit` on the affected query, and `plan_is_fresh_for` refuses to call a refused atom fresh.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-router/tests/subscription_budget.rs` adds twelve, including `an_unadvertised_relay_carries_every_subscription` (fail-open), `an_advertised_budget_refuses_the_excess_and_says_so`, `a_bound_budget_does_not_churn_what_it_already_serves` (incumbents outrank newcomers, so a saturated relay emits no ops), `a_collapsed_catalog_of_three_hundred_stays_inside_a_budget_of_twenty`, `a_budget_of_zero_refuses_the_whole_session`, and `advertised_limits_never_move_an_established_wire_id`. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/core_headless/subscription_budget.rs` adds six for the document hop: `an_advertisement_resolved_after_planning_binds_immediately`, `refused_demand_reaches_the_app_as_an_explicit_local_limit`, `a_republished_document_replans_without_disturbing_incumbents`, `document_churn_that_does_not_move_the_budget_does_not_replan`, and the subscription-id-length diagnosis. `features/routing/relay-subscription-limits.feature` adds ten acceptance scenarios against real in-process relays that now serve their NIP-11 document over plain HTTP on their own address, driving the engine's real acquisition path; `crates/nmp-test-support/src/relays.rs::a_scripted_relay_serves_its_nip11_document_over_plain_http` is the harness's own falsifier for that, including the 404 a relay publishing nothing returns.
- **Superseded path removed:** none in this PR. `RelayLimits` was already deleted in #123 and is deliberately not revived: the budget flows through the existing `RelayPlan::limited` / `refused_sessions` reporting seam rather than a parallel limits type, and there is exactly one carrier (`CompileBudget`) for both the operator's relay ceiling and each relay's advertised limits. No second door, alias, or advisory-only path is left behind, and `docs/internals/subscriptions/identity-grouping-and-limits.md` §6 is rewritten from "parsed, and enforced nowhere" to what shipped rather than restating the gap.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner ruled on 2026-07-27 that this bound is enforced rather than advisory, and required it to flow through the existing `limited` / `refused_sessions` seam and to land only after the merge-rule collapse (#930), which it does. PR #941 is the exact review record. The fail-open ruling for relays that publish no document was additionally reviewed adversarially by Fable, which reached the same verdict independently. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-26 — Keep NIP-19 locator decoding free of profile and acquisition policy ([issue #879](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/879), [PR #905](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/905))

- **Failure evidence:** core `npub` decoding collapsed a bare public key into a profile target, then the generic grammar planner silently chose kind:0, `AuthorOutboxes`, canonical/helper observation roles, and relay-hint promotion policy. A locator therefore fixed event schema, acquisition authority, relay admission, and observation count before an optional component or app had selected a purpose.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust deletes the generic reference-planning module and its now-unused relay-host classifier, and lets `nmp-content::ReferenceOccurrence` preserve the exact five-variant `NostrEntity` value directly. UniFFI deletes `FfiReferenceTarget`, `FfiReferenceDemandPlan`, and `reference_demand_plan`; content occurrences now reuse the existing exact `FfiNostrEntity`. Swift `NMPContent` and Kotlin content projection preserve distinct pubkey/profile/event-id/event/coordinate variants and authored hints. Swift `NMPUI` deletes its plan wrapper, requires the live observation factory to receive an explicit app locator-to-ordinary-`NMPDemand` resolver, and gives each selected component one independently cancellable ordinary observation. Kotlin deliberately exposes no content acquisition helper until a real optional protocol/component owner exists. The Gallery owns its profile/event/address mapping explicitly and deliberately ignores authored hints, so this reference consumer may reach fewer relays than the removed generic planner.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Locator parsing, FFI projection, and component-selected ordinary demands change no store table, schema version, journal, receipt, checkpoint, or durable byte.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. Decoding creates no engine work or evidence. Any later observation carries the existing scoped evidence of the exact ordinary demand the app/component chose; no canonical/helper rollup or new diagnostics fact is introduced. The removed planner also capped promoted authored relay hints at eight; exact parsing intentionally has no replacement cardinality bound because the purpose owner now decides whether to promote hints and must supply its own URL validation, safety filtering, bounds, and scoped evidence. Destination admission still independently refuses local/private/`.onion` hosts unless explicitly allowed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `shared_nip19_fixtures_preserve_exact_locators_across_rust_and_ffi`, `bare_pubkey_and_authored_profile_remain_different_variants`, `testSharedNIP19FixturesPreserveExactSwiftLocators`, `sharedNip19FixturesPreserveExactKotlinLocators`, `testLiteralVisibleProfileAndEventComponentsOpenZeroHandles`, `testAuthoredRelayHintsDoNotCreateHiddenObservations`, `parserPreservesBarePubkeyAndEventId`, and `scripts/check-content-parser-boundary.sh` prove exact five-variant parity, malformed/secret refusal, zero parser work, exact untrusted-hint delivery to the app seam, one selected ordinary handle, and no acquisition vocabulary or mechanism dependency in the locator path. The boundary script now runs as the `Pure content/locator boundary` job in the architecture-gates workflow.
- **Superseded path removed:** `crates/nmp-grammar/src/reference.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/src/reference.rs`, `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMPUI/ReferenceDemand.swift`, `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/Reference.kt`, and `fixtures/reference-plans.json` are deleted outright. The reference planner's now-unreferenced `classify_relay_host` adapter is deleted too; destination classification remains owned by `nmp-network-policy` and enforced at dial admission. No compatibility planner, automatic profile fetch, global resolver registry, or second observation lifecycle remains.
- **Human signoff:** no separate owner ruling was sought for this cut; issue #879 is the tracked #824 dependency and captures the reason for moving acquisition policy out of locator decoding. PR #905 carries the exact cross-platform change and independent review record. Required CI and surface governance remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Return NIP-22 comments through the ordinary write lifecycle ([issue #822](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/822), [PR #907](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/907))

- **Failure evidence:** NIP-22 composition needs no engine state, but the supported FFI, Swift, and Kotlin surfaces put it on the generic engine, wrapped the ordinary write in a protocol-specific take-once noun, and added a second publication overload. A pure schema composer therefore changed the root engine, receipt facade, error/lifecycle vocabulary, and both native SDK engines even though the canonical `WriteIntent` → `publish` → `Receipt` path already preserves every NIP-22 invariant.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust retains `nmp_nip22::comment_intent` as the one owner of NIP-22 payload, durable author-outbox routing, identity, and correlation policy. FFI moves `comment_intent` off `NmpEngine` into an engine-free exported function returning the existing `FfiWriteIntent`, projecting the protocol owner's complete ordinary intent rather than re-stating its policy. Swift and Kotlin delete `CommentIntent`, move `commentIntent` from `NMPEngine` to a top-level protocol function returning ordinary `WriteIntent`, delete only the NIP-22 `publishComposed` overload, and publish through the existing generic engine door. An ordinary intent is a reusable value, so publishing the same uncorrelated NIP-22 intent twice is now permitted and creates two ordinary write attempts instead of returning `IntentAlreadyConsumed`; supplying the same correlation token retains #591 idempotency and reattaches to the same receipt id. NIP-29's distinct live composed carrier remains untouched under #838.
- **Persistence impact:** none. The same ordinary durable intent reaches the unchanged acceptance transaction, canonical pending row, outbox, receipt, retry, cancellation, and correlation-reattachment machinery. No store, journal, key, schema, or migration changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt fact, or diagnostics schema changes; only construction and publication spelling contract to the existing lifecycle.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip22.rs` `comment_intent_composes_the_ordinary_exact_write_intent` pins the FFI value's author, time, kind, tags, content, durability, routing, identity, and correlation; `composed_comment_uses_the_generic_publish_door` drives it through ordinary generic publish and proves its correlation token reattaches to that exact receipt. `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` `direct_and_public_ffi_nip22_comment_intents_are_exactly_identical` proves direct Rust and FFI produce the same canonical event id, exact unsigned JSON, route, durability, identity, and correlation; `direct_and_ffi_correlation_reattach_the_same_obligation_on_token_reuse` proves same-token publication reattaches rather than enqueuing a second obligation. Swift `NIP22Tests` and Kotlin `NIP22Test` exercise top-level composition, generic publication, durable acceptance, token reattachment, and canonical pending-row visibility through the ordinary query/decode path; reverse-projection tests cover every generic write field.
- **Superseded path removed:** `NmpEngine.comment_intent`, Swift/Kotlin engine `commentIntent`, native `CommentIntent`, the NIP-22 `publishComposed` overloads, and every NIP-22 dependency on `FfiComposedWriteIntent` are deleted outright. No alias, deprecation, forwarding method, compatibility adapter, second receipt path, or NIP-22 take-once error remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly asked for the most important Android-enabling work and directed continuous issue-first execution on #831–#834. PR #907 carries this #824 hard cut because the monolithic protocol surface is the concrete #831 packaging blocker; exact-head independent review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Remove raw recipient routing from the generic write noun ([issue #839](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/839), [PR #913](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/913))

- **Failure evidence:** `WriteIntent` accepted a recipient array beside an independently constructed event body. Nothing proved that the event schema owned those recipients, that addressing tags agreed with them, or that fan-out was bounded by a protocol contract. Core therefore exposed a generic routing authority that no production protocol operation owned.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust deletes `WriteRouting::ToInboxes`; the persistence codec and engine resolver delete their matching arms. FFI deletes `FfiWriteRouting::ToInboxes` and its conversion. Swift deletes `WriteRouting.toInboxes`; Kotlin deletes `WriteRouting.ToInboxes`. `AuthorOutbox` remains the sole app-constructible route, while closed protocol operations retain their withheld pinned, narrow, and relay-list bootstrap routes. The existing public-surface audit now rejects all three removed spellings in generated FFI plus hand-written Swift/Kotlin sources.
- **Persistence impact:** the obsolete `to-inboxes:` route snapshot codec is removed. There are no compatibility constraints; a retained obligation carrying that raw route fails closed as an unreadable routing snapshot rather than being defaulted, widened, or reinterpreted. No event, receipt, attempt, correlation, schema, table, or key format changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt fact, or diagnostics schema changes. The removed route can no longer create a routing or wire fact.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/surface_audit_704.rs` `public_surface_has_no_removed_vocabulary` scans generated Rust/FFI surfaces and hand-written Swift/Kotlin sources for `ToInboxes`, `toInboxes`, and `to_inboxes`; the type/API removal makes the bad construction path unnameable. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/core_headless/write_delivery.rs` `author_route_removal_cannot_erase_durable_lane_and_new_revision_failure_is_volatile` preserves the unrelated dynamic route-revision durability proof through the supported `AuthorOutbox` path and moves no application write. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/durable_accepted_restart.rs` `removed_to_inboxes_snapshot_is_retained_unreadable_and_never_reinterpreted` injects the exact legacy `to-inboxes:` snapshot into a real durable store, restarts, proves that reattachment is `RetainedButUnreadable`, and proves that signer completion emits no wire attempt while the obligation remains retained. Regenerated facade/component snapshots carry the exact deletion.
- **Superseded path removed:** the direct-Rust route, FFI enum/conversion, persistence codec, engine resolver, Swift/Kotlin projections, and three path-specific engine tests are deleted outright. No alias, deprecated forwarding case, renamed recipient array, default recipient inference, or raw route carrier remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner explicitly asked for the most important Android-enabling work and directed continuous issue-first execution on #831–#834. PR #913 carries this #824 hard cut because raw protocol/routing surface is part of the concrete #831 component-packaging blocker; exact-head independent review and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Restore exact NIP-29 ownership and give C7 its own kind-9 schema ([issue #838](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/838), [PR #942](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/942))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #838 records three owners collapsed into `nmp-nip29`: `compose_group_message` hardcoded C7 kind 9 while materializing client-owned NIP-27 mentions/notification `p` rows and the wrong reply `e` schema; `group_content_demand` declared `[9,30315]` to be the group's fixed content catalog even though NIP-29 permits foreign event kinds; and `GroupTimelineEvidence` let callers mint transplantable `previous` authority from raw tuples with no host/group/author capability. The universal write plane then retained a `PinnedHost(HostAuthority)` route whose only production constructor was that invalid composer.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust replaces the raw NIP-29 composer/evidence family with pure `contextualize_group_event(host, group_id, complete_draft) -> GroupPublication`: it preserves every foreign field/tag, appends only `h`, retains the selected host beside the event, rejects caller-supplied `h`/`previous`, and never emits `previous`. New engine-free crate `nmp-nipc7` exclusively owns kind 9 and NIP-18 `q` reply construction, with no NIP-29, mention, notification, routing, signer, or engine policy. NIP-29's fixed content demand is deleted. UniFFI deletes `group_content_demand`, `FfiGroupReplyParent`, `NmpEngine::group_message_intent`, and `FfiError::NoActiveAccount`; Swift and Kotlin delete their exact mirrors (`groupContentDemand`, `GroupReplyParent`, `GroupSendIntent`, `groupMessageIntent`, and the group-only error). Because #822 already contracts NIP-22 to the ordinary `FfiWriteIntent` and generic publish lifecycle, the combined tree also deletes `FfiComposedWriteIntent`, `publish_composed`, and `IntentAlreadyConsumed` entirely; no inert native NIP-29 replacement noun or second write lifecycle remains. With `PinnedHost` removed and supported native routing limited to `AuthorOutbox`, no supported general-purpose or NIP-29 operation can currently route an arbitrary write to one selected relay; the unrelated closed NIP-65 bootstrap remains scoped to its validated kind:10002 operation. Engine/native NIP-29 publication is therefore explicitly unbuilt in `README.md` and `docs/known-gaps.md`, while NIP-29 reads remain available.
- **Persistence impact:** the obsolete `pinned-host-hex:` route snapshot encoder/decoder and `WriteRouting::PinnedHost` are removed with their only supported producer. A retained old pending write containing that route now fails closed as unreadable during recovery; no compatibility decoder or migration preserves the deleted authority. Store tables, the schema marker, canonical events, coverage keys, receipt rows, and every other route representation are unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics, evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution shape changes. The deleted route can no longer produce new receipt facts; recovery of an old retained route follows the existing unreadable-persistence failure path rather than guessing another destination.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/publication.rs` `foreign_kind_and_schema_survive_except_for_appended_h` proves a foreign kind:20 draft is unchanged except for appended `h`; `c7_q_reply_survives_without_nip29_interpreting_it` proves contextualization preserves a foreign `q`; `caller_cannot_mint_group_or_previous_authority` and `publication_never_synthesizes_previous` close both reserved-tag paths. `crates/nmp-nipc7/src/lib.rs` `reply_uses_q_and_no_e_p_h_or_previous_rows` pins the exact C7 reply row independently. `crates/nmp-engine/tests/durable_accepted_restart.rs` `removed_pinned_host_snapshot_is_retained_unreadable_and_never_reinterpreted` injects a real legacy `pinned-host-hex:` snapshot, restarts, proves `RetainedButUnreadable`, and proves signer completion emits no wire while retaining the obligation. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, wired as the `NIP-29 exact context ownership` architecture job, forbids reintroduction of chat/content policy, caller-mintable `previous`, the removed native surface, or dead single-host write authority. Fresh UniFFI bindings compile and the real Swift/Kotlin package suites exercise the reduced surface.
- **Superseded path removed:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/message.rs`, `crates/nmp-nip29/src/send.rs`, the engine feature/dependency, the live pinned-host write/read-back test, the pinned-route persistence test, and all FFI/Swift/Kotlin group composer/content helpers are deleted outright. `HostAuthority`, `WriteRouting::PinnedHost`, its resolver/persistence arms, and the NIP-29-only engine dev dependency are also deleted. No compatibility alias, deprecated overload, fixed content-kind replacement, raw relay escape hatch, or old-reply shim remains.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed autonomous continuation of the issue-backed Android enablement work and challenged the agents not to wait for redundant permission. Issue #838 is the captured architecture contract and draft PR #942 is the exact review record. The PR remains draft; independent exact-head review, strict surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Split the optional NIP-46 signer provider from core ([issue #877](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/877), [PR #945](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/945))

- **Failure evidence:** the protocol-neutral signer capability and its concrete NIP-46 implementation had one package owner. Selecting signing therefore pulled NIP-46 protocol policy, relay transport, Tokio, URL, and JSON dependencies into every Rust consumer and both native SDK cores, even when an application used only a local signer. A core-only Android artifact could not exclude that family, so #824's optional-artifact contract and #831's componentized AAR qualification remained unproved.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-signer` now owns only dependency-light capability, operation, and exact immutable value types; `nmp-local-signer` owns local key custody and crypto; `nmp-nip46` owns the concrete remote-signer protocol and transport. The canonical `nmp` facade and `nmp-engine` consume only the neutral signer capability. Core UniFFI exposes an opaque `FfiSignerMailbox` and registration token; the separate `nmp-nip46-ffi` component consumes that mailbox and contributes an ordinary signer without receiving an engine handle or adding a foreign-language signing callback. Swift moves its NIP-46 surface into the separately selected `Packages/NMPNip46` package; Kotlin moves it into the conditional `Packages/NMPKotlin/nip46` module. Provider builders compile core and provider in one Cargo feature-resolution unit and refresh both artifacts, giving the external UniFFI mailbox one exact Rust type identity while preserving an independent core-only build.
- **Persistence impact:** no canonical-store schema, journal, coverage key, receipt, or local-account checkpoint representation changes. Existing NIP-46 session checkpoint values and secure-store wrappers move with the provider component; their wire formats and lifecycle behavior are unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, attribution, or task-census representation changes; signer registration and provider attachment remain identity-lifecycle operations outside diagnostics.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/check-nip46-provider-boundary.sh` proves the core dependency/source projections contain no concrete NIP-46 provider and that the provider requests exact crypto dependencies rather than rust-nostr's NIP-46 umbrella. `scripts/test-nip46-provider-removal.sh` deletes both provider crates in a clean copy and proves core FFI, the local signer, the neutral signer crate, and the facade signer surface still build and test. `scripts/check-nip46-artifact-inventory.sh` reads native link maps and generated bindings to prove zero NIP-46 names in core and positive provider inventory. `crates/nmp/tests/signer_surface.rs` proves a consumer depending only on `nmp` can implement the neutral signer surface. The NIP-46 provider workflow builds and tests clean core-only and selected-provider Swift/Kotlin paths, including a real core-plus-provider link; issue #946 records why Gate 6 cannot currently accept its leading-dot path as a checkable claim.
- **Superseded path removed:** NIP-46 implementation/catalog ownership inside `nmp-signer`, the NIP-46 dependency edge from the canonical facade, and the Swift/Kotlin core NIP-46 APIs are deleted outright. No compatibility alias, default-feature umbrella, runtime provider registry, duplicate signing operation family, or foreign callback bridge replaces them.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed continued issue-first Android enablement work and issue #877 records this blocker as its own boundary-only unit, explicitly preserving the separate signer lifecycle contracts in #606, #770, #783, #784, and #871. PR #945 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Own NIP-22 in the nmp facade and close nmp-ffi's mechanism edges ([issue #851](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/851), [PR #935](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/935))

- **Failure evidence:** `nmp-ffi` is the ONE staticlib/cdylib every Swift and Kotlin app links, and it took direct normal dependencies on `nmp-grammar` and `nmp-nip22` while also consuming `nmp`. The native product boundary could therefore bind a mechanism/protocol value the canonical facade never projected or governed, and "direct imports and facade re-exports stay aligned" was a convention a reviewer had to remember rather than a mechanism. NIP-22 in particular had two owners: `nmp-ffi` reached the comment vocabulary through its own edge, so a direct-Rust app and the native boundary resolved the same values by two independent routes.
- **Changed projections:** ffi
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp` gains a NON-DEFAULT `nip22` cargo feature and an `nmp::nip22` module re-exporting the comment vocabulary and its intent composer, so the governed default-feature Rust facade snapshot is byte-identical and the `rust` projection is unchanged. `nmp-ffi` enables that feature instead of taking a second edge, and every query-grammar and correlation value it previously reached through `nmp-grammar` now resolves through `nmp`; `nmp-grammar` drops to a dev-dependency and `nmp-nip22` is removed from the manifest entirely. The FFI component delta is exactly six docstrings that now name the facade module as the mirrored owner, plus the three exported NIP-22 function checksums that move with them — UniFFI computes a function checksum over the metadata buffer that includes its docstring, so a docstring edit shifts it. No UniFFI record field, argument, return type, method signature, or error variant changes, and bindings are always generated from the same build as the library they load. Swift and Kotlin sources are untouched; cross-SDK parity is green.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, version, migration, journal, coverage key, receipt row, or stored-byte change; nothing in this change is persisted.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution fact is added, removed, or reshaped.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh` is the structural mechanism, wired as its own architecture-gates job. It fails when any of the three mechanism crates returns to the FFI crate's normal dependency section, when the facade edge stops enabling the comment feature, when production source reaches the NIP-22 mechanism crate directly, when a mechanism path outside an exact residual list appears, and when a residual allowance stops being reached. All seven directions were falsified against temporarily mutated trees before the gate was trusted. The demotion to a dev-dependency is itself compiler-enforced: a non-test build of `nmp-ffi` can no longer resolve those crate paths at all, and `cargo tree -p nmp-ffi -e normal --depth 1` shows none of the three edges. `crates/nmp-consumer-check` composes a comment intent from a manifest naming `nmp` alone, so facade ownership of that vocabulary is a compile-time proof rather than a claim. The existing NIP-22 suite in the FFI crate and both parity suites stay green across the rewire.
- **Superseded path removed:** the NIP-22 mechanism dependency is deleted from `nmp-ffi` outright and the query-grammar dependency is demoted to test scope — no alias, no re-export shim, no second owner, no compatibility path. A source census for the NIP-22 mechanism crate across the FFI crate returns nothing outside prose, and the gate keeps it that way. The residual allowance list the earlier form of this PR carried is reduced to a single test-scope entry: the four NIP-46 entries were made stale by PR #945 moving that projection into its own component, and the reference-planning entry by PR #913 deleting the module that reached it; the gate's staleness assertion forced all five off the list rather than letting them persist as unused permission.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed continued issue-first Android enablement work and released the hold that queued this change behind the #824 hard-cut sequence, asking explicitly that it be rebased once onto the final tree and qualified. Issue #851 is the captured contract and issue #934 the ceiling blocker it no longer needs. PR #935 is the exact review record; exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-27 — Reject mismatched native provider components before mailbox exchange ([issue #952](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/952), [PR #953](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/953))

- **Failure evidence:** the separately selected NIP-46 native library lowered the core library's external `Arc<FfiSignerMailbox>` into its own statically linked copy of `nmp-ffi`. UniFFI checksummed only the provider namespace, so core and provider libraries from different builds could both load and reach the first incompatible Rust object operation instead of refusing version skew. The matched builders were a convention, not a runtime boundary.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** core FFI exports a plain deterministic `nmp-core-component-v1-*` identity over governed core/fixture inputs, Cargo lockfile, compiler, build flags, and an exact resolved unit graph (package roots/edges, transitive features, profiles, and targets) that `build.rs` derives through Cargo's no-build mode. The exactness comes from fixing the build shape, not inferring arbitrary parent Cargo roots: `scripts/build-component-release.sh` admits only the core-only or matched core/provider root set, mutually excludes concurrent builds of one package set with a kernel-held lock that survives a killed shell while Cargo/rustc descendants remain, records the exact target/release marker, holds a fresh authorization only inside its exact-root `cargo build --frozen` subprocess, and revokes it before returning. It extracts the provider library's compiled UniFFI metadata, requires positive metadata for both the core mailbox and provider proof, audits every callable namespace without a namespace exemption, permits only the exact outward-only core source `nmp_ffi::NmpEngine::signer_mailbox`, and requires exactly one other mailbox-carrying entry with a compatibility-proof input; only then does it copy the required native libraries/bindgen executables into a fresh artifact snapshot and release the lock. `FfiSignerMailbox` has private fields and a crate-private Rust constructor, so even a linked crate claiming the core namespace cannot mint an object for the one permitted return path; only the real core `NmpEngine` can vend it. UniFFI derives that exact audit label and its no-mangle symbol from the same module/type/function tuple, so forging it collides at link time; the audit independently requires exactly one source. This checks the same compiled export authority bindgen consumes rather than guessing which source syntax, claimed namespace, or linked crate can create an export. The Swift/Kotlin builders package only that snapshot and never the reusable Cargo target. `build.rs` canonicalizes the declared component root and actual `OUT_DIR`, requires the exact `<target>/release/build/nmp-ffi-*/out` layout, and checks the marker/authorization before hashing the fixed resolution. Thus ad-hoc release, workspace, all-targets, test, clippy, built-in bench, nested-target, `..`-escape, and custom-profile shapes cannot replace a package input; debug/IDE builds remain available but are not packaging inputs. The authorization is a working-discipline guard against accidental substitution, not a secret against a caller deliberately forging the marker or reading/replaying the in-flight token; it is never returned or printed, and the embedded runtime identity remains the final authority. Absolute checkout paths and workspace-bearing flags are canonicalized; external path patches are refused as non-reproducible inputs. Provider source is deliberately excluded from the core-owned object identity: provider changes do not move the core fingerprint, while the proof-bearing constructor remains on the separate provider surface whose missing snapshot is tracked by #954. Provider FFI compares its embedded requirement with the loaded core identity and mints an opaque `FfiNip46CoreCompatibility` proof only on equality; `NmpNip46Provider` now requires that proof beside the mailbox. Swift and Kotlin read the core identity first, run provider verification, and only inside the successful continuation request the mailbox. Skew maps to `NMPError.nativeComponentMismatch` / `NMPError.NativeComponentMismatch` with expected and actual identities. Core-only applications retain no provider dependency, registry, or runtime check.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No canonical-store schema, journal, checkpoint, route, write, receipt, correlation, key, or migration representation changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, attribution, relay, signer-session, or task-census representation changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/check-nip46-component-identity.sh` inspects real native archives and requires the core-only/matched package-set identities to be disjoint while the matched core/provider identity sets are equal. `scripts/test-component-identity-build.sh` runs the managed core and matched builders, proves stale lock-file bytes alone do not block a build, proves a live kernel-held lock refuses a concurrent builder specifically, then proves build authorizations have been revoked: core, pair, workspace, all-targets, test, clippy, and bench release-class shapes all refuse against the reusable targets while the sealed snapshots remain intact. `scripts/test-build-kotlin-jvm.sh` and `scripts/test-build-swift-xcframework.sh` prove both SDK builders consume fresh snapshots from exact roots/targets under the frozen plan; `scripts/check-nip46-provider-boundary.sh` binds the managed helper, authorization revocation, sealed outputs, self-derived graph, compiled-metadata audit, provider verifier, proof-bearing constructor, crate-private mailbox construction, matched builders, and both SDK pre-mailbox calls while refusing any declared-graph or obsolete broad-enablement input. `FfiSignerMailbox` carries a compile-fail doctest proving an external Rust crate cannot call its constructor; making that constructor public makes the doctest and provider-boundary gate red. `release_out_dir_must_be_the_exact_managed_target_and_profile` proves outside, nested, custom-profile, and wrong-crate output layouts cannot inherit release authorization. `unit_graph_identity_ignores_absolute_workspace_paths` and `build_flags_ignore_absolute_workspace_paths` prove two checkout roots canonicalize identically; `unit_graph_identity_preserves_resolved_feature_changes` proves a resolved feature change remains fingerprint input. `unit_graph_must_match_cargo_observed_provider_resolution` proves the fixed-root graph agrees with Cargo's provider feature; `unit_graph_refuses_external_path_overrides` refuses user-level path patches, and `all_literal_rust_includes_stay_inside_hashed_inputs` enumerates every current `include_str!` / `include_bytes!` input in the governed core trees. `crates/nmp-nip46-ffi/metadata-audit.rs` extracts every namespace's compiled library metadata consumed by UniFFI bindgen, grants no namespace exemption, recursively resolves records, enums, custom types, optionals, sequences, and maps, requires core-mailbox and provider-proof positive controls, permits exactly one outward-only core source (`nmp_ffi::NmpEngine::signer_mailbox`), and permits exactly one mailbox-carrying input callable: the proof-bearing `NmpNip46Provider::new` constructor. `compiled_record_carrier_cannot_hide_an_unproven_mailbox_entry`, `compiled_return_position_is_part_of_the_mailbox_boundary`, `foreign_namespace_mailbox_entry_is_not_hidden_from_audit`, `forged_core_namespace_mailbox_input_is_not_exempted`, `exact_core_mailbox_source_cannot_accept_a_mailbox_input`, and `exact_core_mailbox_source_cannot_throw_a_mailbox` prove transitive carriers, outward entries, linked sidecar namespaces, forged core namespaces, and the exact return-only core allowance are covered; `duplicate_core_mailbox_source_is_rejected` preserves the exact-one backstop behind UniFFI's link-time symbol collision. `exact_compiled_constructor_is_the_only_mailbox_entry`, `missing_compiled_mailbox_entry_is_rejected`, `exact_compiled_constructor_name_is_required`, `missing_mailbox_metadata_positive_control_is_rejected`, `missing_compatibility_metadata_positive_control_is_rejected`, and `missing_core_mailbox_source_positive_control_is_rejected` separately falsify exact cardinality/name and all three load-bearing positive controls; `valid_compiled_constructor_passes_the_full_audit` is the matched control. `exact_core_identity_mints_compatibility_and_mismatch_is_typed` proves exact equality mints the proof and mismatch remains typed. Swift `RemoteSignerTests.testMismatchedCoreIsTypedBeforeTheMailboxBodyRuns` and Kotlin `RemoteSignerTest.mismatchedCoreIsTypedBeforeTheMailboxBodyRuns` call the real verifier with a false identity and prove the mailbox-producing continuation never executes; existing invitation tests are the matched positive control. The regenerated core component surface snapshot carries the identity export and proof type; #954 records the missing provider-namespace snapshot as a protected-governance follow-up before independent publication.
- **Superseded path removed:** unchecked `NmpNip46Provider(mailbox)` construction is deleted. The generated constructor requires compatibility proof plus mailbox; no unchecked overload, best-effort continuation, adjacent-binary selection, compatibility alias, foreign callback, or second engine replaces it.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner authorized autonomous merging of the Android-enabling sequence. Issue #952 records the unchecked-pointer consequence discovered during #945 review, and PR #953 is the runtime/native guard slice. The issue intentionally remains open: #831 still owns binding this identity into AAR provenance/Gradle metadata and emulator-qualifying a deliberately mismatched artifact pair through the supported Kotlin surface. Exact-head independent review and required CI remain merge gates for this slice.

## 2026-07-27 — Fold nmp-engine into the nmp facade crate ([issue #827](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/827), [PR #958](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/958))

- **Failure evidence:** `nmp-engine` and `nmp` were one product split across two crates. Every crate in the M1/M2/M3 graph flowed into the engine and nothing depended on it except the facade, which selectively re-exported a few dozen engine items as the supported Rust surface. The split therefore published a second surface nobody was meant to consume, forced every in-workspace harness to take a second dependency edge to reach the reducer and the runtime, and left the componentized Android artifact work (#824, #831) with a second unit to qualify. It also made the recovery of #827's contaminated worktree unfinishable: nine remaining recovery issues (#808, #841, #845, #848, #850, #854, #872, #874, #875) all live inside files the split kept in the wrong crate.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the `nmp-engine` package is deleted and its `core`, `runtime`, `outbox`, `negentropy` and `ingest_attribution` modules move verbatim into `nmp` as PRIVATE modules; its one-shot NIP-11 acquisition service moves to `crates/nmp/src/relay_information_service.rs` because the facade already owns a `relay_information` module holding the corresponding values. No public name is added or removed: the selective re-export list that defined the product surface is unchanged except that each entry now reads `crate::core::…` or `crate::runtime::…` instead of `nmp_engine::core::…`. The governed Rust snapshot shrinks from 28817 to 26338 lines purely because the sixteen re-exported engine types are now defined in `nmp` and render inline rather than as a re-export plus a resolved dependency-owned entry. The FFI component snapshot is byte-identical and Swift and Kotlin sources are untouched, so `ffi`, `swift` and `kotlin` are unchanged projections. The mechanism stays unreachable as an app contract: a `#[doc(hidden)] pub mod mechanism` door re-exports the five private modules for this crate's own tests, its benchmark examples, and the in-workspace BDD and remote-signer restart harnesses only, and rustdoc omits it and everything beneath it.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No canonical-store schema, epoch, migration, journal, coverage key, receipt row, correlation token, or local-account checkpoint representation changes. The restart, corruption and retention falsifiers that read real on-disk `RedbStore` files move with the crate and pass unchanged against files written by the pre-fold binary layout.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, attribution, or task-census fact is added, removed, or reshaped. `crates/nmp/src/core/query.rs` and every file under `crates/nmp-router/` are byte-identical to the base revision, so the per-relay NIP-11 planning budget is still computed at recompile time from the already-pruned advertisement map and is still shared, unaltered, with the shadow plan that decides plan freshness.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless.rs` and the eleven submodules `support.rs` reaches through `#[path]` attributes are the falsifier for this move's one silent hazard — those submodules are not declared by directory convention, so a partial move would drop tests from the binary with no compile error. The listed test count and the full sorted test-name set were captured before the move and asserted identical after: 119 tests, zero name difference. `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/subscription_budget.rs` and `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/derived_tag_fanout.rs`, the two newest reducer suites, are carried forward and green. `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` regenerates deterministically and matches the committed snapshots; that the Rust snapshot shrank rather than grew is the evidence no mechanism module leaked into the facade. `crates/nmp-consumer-check` and `crates/nmp-nip65-consumer-check` still build from manifests naming the facade alone. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-nip46-provider-boundary.sh` and `scripts/check-content-parser-boundary.sh` are repointed at the folded paths with their assertions intact, and the forbidden-crate list they share with `crates/nmp-content/tests/dependency_boundary.rs` loses only the entry naming a crate that no longer exists.
- **Superseded path removed:** the `nmp-engine` package, its manifest, its library root, and its workspace member and dependency entries are deleted outright. No facade crate re-exports it, no compatibility alias or shim module stands in for it, and no crate retains a dependency edge to it. The two harnesses that reached it directly now depend on the facade instead.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this fold as the gating work for the componentized Android artifact (#824, #831) and as the final piece of the #827 contaminated-worktree recovery, holding five frozen pull requests behind it, and asked explicitly that it land as a relocation with the semantic issues inside the moved files left as follow-ups. PR #958 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-28 — Give reducer facts one runtime delivery owner ([issue #845](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/845), [PR #966](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/966))

- **Failure evidence:** rows, windowed history, and receipts each had two reducer-to-app delivery representations: reducer-owned callback objects plus typed effects. Production installed no-op row/history callbacks and ignored receipt effects, so correctness depended on keeping duplicate mutation paths aligned and complete evidence had already drifted between them.
- **Changed projections:** correction
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the hidden Rust mechanism deletes the callback traits and callback-bearing reducer command/state fields. `EngineCore` now emits each row, history, and receipt fact exactly once as a typed `Effect`; the runtime alone owns bounded/conflated mailboxes, live receipt registrations, receiver-close detachment, and replay-to-live attachment. Supported Rust, UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin application signatures and value projections are unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Receipt, attempt, route, correlation, event, coverage, and history representations are unchanged; replay still derives solely from canonical retained facts.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. The delivery owner changed, not the diagnostics or acquisition-evidence vocabulary.
- **Updated falsifiers:** reducer tests assert only typed effects; runtime tests cover one fact per mutation, exact live registration count after publish/reattach/drop, bounded lag plus cursor replay, terminal batch ordering, and shutdown wake-up. Redb restart/correlation tests continue to prove retained receipt truth, and row/history tests continue to prove complete evidence, window growth, and slow-consumer conflation.
- **Superseded path removed:** callback traits, callback fields, null callbacks, receipt callback vectors, and the deliberately ignored receipt-effect branch are deleted without aliases or a second observer registry. Older append-only entries that name a live receipt callback describe their historical revision; this entry supersedes that mechanism.
- **Human signoff:** issue #845 is the tracked architecture contract and PR #966 is the ready-for-review record; exact-head review, required CI, and surface governance remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Replace WriteRouting with Auto and Explicit, and make publishing to chosen relays first-class ([issue #972](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/972), [PR #1006](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1006))

- **Failure evidence:** the app-facing routing vocabulary was three variants of which exactly one crossed the FFI boundary. `WriteRouting` was `AuthorOutbox | PrivateNarrow(PrivateRoute) | RelayListBootstrap(RelayListBootstrapAuthority)`, while `FfiWriteRouting` had the single variant `AuthorOutbox` — so no Swift or Kotlin app could say "publish this event to this relay", and `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip22.rs` destructured a composed intent against a hard-coded routing variant with `unreachable!()` on the exported path (#951). #838 had removed the last single-host route on the premise that routing an arbitrary write to one selected relay is a capability with no legitimate general demand. The repository owner reversed that premise outright: "it's not just nip29 that should say 'publish event x to relay y', an app might offer a 'publish this event to relay: [_user_input_here_]' -- there are many many reasons to publish to a specific relay instead of outbox", naming a user-typed relay, a wiki crate's preferred relay kind, a DM crate's pair of parties, and republishing someone else's already-signed event to your own archive relay. `docs/internals/routing/auto-and-explicit.md` and `docs/internals/routing/removed-routes.md` are the settled record.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `WriteRouting` becomes exactly `Auto` and `Explicit(Vec<RelayUrl>)`. `AuthorOutbox`, `PrivateNarrow`, `RelayListBootstrap`, `PrivateRoute`, `NarrowOnly`, and `RelayListBootstrapAuthority` are deleted from `nmp-grammar` with their re-exports; the facade's withholding note about `NarrowOnly`/`PrivateRoute` goes with them. `FfiWriteRouting` projects BOTH variants — `Auto` and `Explicit { relays: Vec<String> }` — turning it from an `Enum` with only unit variants into one with a payload, which changes its generated Kotlin shape from `enum class` (`FfiWriteRouting.AUTHOR_OUTBOX`) to `sealed class` (`FfiWriteRouting.Auto` / `FfiWriteRouting.Explicit(relays)`) and its Swift shape to `.auto` / `.explicit(relays:)`. Relay URLs are parsed at the FFI boundary, so a malformed one is a typed synchronous `FfiError::InvalidRelayUrl` before any engine call. Swift `WriteRouting` becomes `.auto` / `.explicit(relays:)` and Kotlin `WriteRouting` becomes `Auto` / `Explicit(relays)`; both are ordinary app-constructible values on every surface. `nmp-nip65`'s bootstrap publication mints an ordinary `Explicit` instead of its dedicated route, and `nmp-ffi`'s NIP-22 projection now converts routing totally through `convert::write_routing_to_ffi` rather than pattern-matching a hard-coded variant, so a protocol module that changes which route it mints crosses the boundary faithfully instead of panicking (the routing axis of #951; the payload axis of that issue is untouched and remains open). The governed Rust facade snapshot shrinks from 26338 to 26223 lines against the 30000-line ceiling.
- **Persistence impact:** the durable routing vocabulary changes. `routing_snapshot`/`parse_routing_snapshot` write and read `auto` and `explicit-hex:<hex>,<hex>` and nothing else; `author-outbox`, `private-narrow-hex:`, and `nip65-bootstrap-hex:` are removed encoders and removed decoders. A pending write journalled under any of them is retained exactly as written and never resolved, through the existing `routing_valid` path — the same retained-but-unreadable behaviour a genuinely undecodable string already gets, and deliberately not a guess: reading `author-outbox` as `Auto` would republish an old obligation to relays nobody chose for it. An `explicit-hex:` row naming no relays is likewise unreadable, because an empty explicit route is refused before acceptance and no legitimate row can carry one. No store table, schema marker, canonical event, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. `WriteStatus::Routed` still reports the resolved relay set, and the resolution that produces it still runs on the SEND path — first attempt, boot recovery, queue drain — so a parked write is resolved against the directory as it stands when it finally goes out. An `Explicit` route never consults the directory at all, so its `Routed` set is exactly what the caller named, every time. `WriteStatus::Failed`'s doc no longer describes an unroutable "private" route: fail-closed is a routing property, and describing a group host or an archive relay as private was the category error the removed variant carried.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `cargo test -p nmp-parity` gains `direct_and_ffi_publish_to_one_explicitly_named_relay_identically` (one write, one caller-named relay, no seeded relay list and no discovery anywhere, identical ordered receipts direct and across FFI) and `direct_and_ffi_refuse_an_empty_explicit_route_at_the_door` (both surfaces refuse identically, with `Failed` the first and only status). `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/write_delivery.rs` replaces the old empty-private-route test with `an_explicit_route_with_no_relays_is_refused_before_acceptance` — one terminal status, no sign request, no relay contacted, nothing accepted — and adds its deliberate asymmetry `an_unreachable_explicit_relay_is_accepted_because_the_door_cannot_know`. `crates/nmp/src/core/write_tests.rs` proves verbatim resolution against a directory holding a DIFFERENT write relay for the same author, and that `Auto` journals a bare label while `Explicit` journals its relays in order. `crates/nmp/tests/durable_accepted_restart.rs` proves an explicit relay set reopens verbatim across a real redb close/reopen without consulting the directory. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs` proves an explicit route crosses the boundary verbatim and inverts exactly, that a malformed relay URL refuses the whole intent rather than silently narrowing it, and that emptiness is deliberately NOT caught at that boundary because it is a routing rule enforced once at the engine door for every surface. Swift `testExplicitRoutingCarriesTheAppsExactRelayListBothWays` and Kotlin `explicitRoutingCarriesTheAppsExactRelayListBothWays` prove the same round trip through the real generated bindings. Seven `@designed` BDD scenarios in `features/routing/auto-and-explicit.feature` and `features/routing/removed-routes.feature` are un-tagged and pass against the real engine and real scripted relays, including the archive-republish case proving routing is independent of authorship.
- **Superseded path removed:** the three shipped routing variants, their three carrier types, their journal encoders and decoders, and the single-variant FFI enum are deleted outright — no alias, no `From` impl, no legacy-routing module, no compatibility decoder. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` loses its `HostAuthority|PinnedHost` clause: it enforced #838's conclusion, and the premise of that conclusion is what the owner reversed, so a grep guarding a capability that should exist is sediment rather than a tripwire. What replaces it is a positive pin — the deleted routing vocabulary must never return to `crates/nmp-grammar/src`, `crates/nmp/src`, `crates/nmp-ffi/src`, or either SDK's sources — and the three newly retired journal spellings join the existing dead-spelling clause, so they cannot come back even as a test asserting they stay unreadable.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner reversed #838's premise directly in the 2026-07-28/29 design session and ruled on the empty route ("reject it immediately") and on the newtype question ("bare. It is not only overengineering; it's wrong for many other reasons"). `docs/internals/routing/auto-and-explicit.md` and `docs/internals/routing/removed-routes.md`, merged on master, are the settled record this change implements. PR #1006 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Replace `WritePayload::Unsigned` with an `EventBuilder` that demands only the kind ([issue #973](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/973), [PR #1005](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1005))

- **Failure evidence:** `WritePayload::Unsigned(UnsignedEvent)` forced every caller to know its own pubkey and read a clock before NMP would accept a draft it was about to sign anyway. `nostr::UnsignedEvent::new` requires `pubkey` and `created_at`, and the reducer then refused to change either — with `identity_override: Some(pk)`, `pk` had to EQUAL the draft's author; with `None`, the author had to equal the active account. So both halves of "just publish this as me / as this signer" were blocked by one requirement, universally, for every kind and every NIP. The comparison was the right rule enforced against the wrong input.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_grammar::EventBuilder` is added and re-exported from `nmp`: `{ kind: Kind, tags: Vec<Tag>, content: String, created_at: Option<Timestamp> }`, with `new(kind)` as the only constructor and consuming `content`/`tag`/`created_at` combinators. `WritePayload` becomes `{ Event(EventBuilder), ReplaceableEdit { builder, expected_base }, Signed(Event) }`. It is a value, not an object, and carries no author field at all, so the author/identity mismatch class is unrepresentable rather than fail-closed. At FFI it lands as a UniFFI Record with `#[uniffi(default)]` fields, `FfiEventBuilder`, which is a labeled-argument initializer in both Swift and Kotlin (`FfiEventBuilder(kind: 1, content: "hello")`); `FfiWritePayload` becomes `{ Event { builder }, Signed { … } }` and the builder record carries four fields where `Unsigned` carried five. Swift `WritePayload.event(kind:tags:content:createdAt:)` and Kotlin `WritePayload.Event` replace their `.unsigned` cases. `nmp::nip22::comment_intent` and `compose_top_level_comment`/`compose_comment_reply` drop their `author` and `created_at` parameters; `nmp_nip02::compose_follow_change` drops `author` and `now`; `nmp_nip65`'s bootstrap composer stops taking a clock and names its account through the identity instead. `ComposeFollowError::{BaseHasWrongAuthor, TimestampExhausted}` and their `FfiFollowActionFailure` mirrors are deleted.
- **Persistence impact:** `AcceptWrite` gains `monotonic_stamp: bool`. When set — a replaceable edit whose builder stated no `created_at` — the acceptance transaction moves the frozen `created_at` forward to `max(clock, winner.created_at + 1)` against the very row its `replaceable_base` comparison just held, re-deriving the frozen id inside that same transaction. No stored schema, epoch, journal, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes; the stamp is computed before the row is written, not applied to an existing one.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution fact is added, removed, or reshaped. `WriteStatus::ReplaceableConflict` remains the one conflict door, now also covering the foreign-authored base that `BaseHasWrongAuthor` used to catch at compose time.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp_grammar::write::tests::a_kind_alone_is_a_complete_builder` pins that the kind is the only constructor argument and that there is no field or combinator for an author. `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/write_state.rs::a_builder_publishes_as_the_active_account_and_refuses_when_there_is_none` and `identity_selects_on_a_builder_and_may_only_restate_on_a_signed_event` replace the two #47 mismatch falsifiers: the builder case now proves selection and the signed case keeps the fail-closed contradiction check verbatim. `crates/nmp/tests/signer_registry_headless.rs::a_builder_composed_before_a_switch_publishes_as_the_account_active_at_acceptance` proves an account switch between compose and publish resolves at acceptance and reaches exactly one signer. `nmp_nip02::edit::tests::a_foreign_base_composes_and_is_left_to_the_precondition` proves a foreign base is no longer a compose-time error. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle) proves both doors compose the identical builder and that neither invents a timestamp. `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` covers the byte-stable auth/signer-op doors across the payload change.
- **Superseded path removed:** `WritePayload::Unsigned`, `WritePayload::UnsignedReplaceableEdit` and `FfiWritePayload::Unsigned` are deleted outright — no alias, no deprecation, no second spelling. The NIP-22 golden fixture that pinned byte-identical composed output across Rust, Swift and Kotlin is deleted with them: reproducible bytes were rejected as a requirement, because if they were genuinely required they could not be one NIP's concern, and they are enforced nowhere and wanted nowhere. `nostr::UnsignedEvent` stays on the facade's re-export list only because `Handle::sign_event` (#464) takes one; it is no longer any payload's argument.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1005 carries this slice of epic #968 under the repository owner's design rulings recorded verbatim in `docs/internals/writes/event-builder.md` and `docs/internals/writes/payload-and-replaceable-edits.md` ("I dont think unsigned should exist nor draft; I think 'event builder' is a better name for what this is?", "it should demand the kind", "we don't need that shit at all"). Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Replace `identity_override` with `Identity { Active, Explicit(PublicKey) }` ([issue #974](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/974), [PR #1009](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1009))

- **Failure evidence:** `WriteIntent.identity_override: Option<PublicKey>` was an `Option` whose `None` meant something specific — "resolve the active account at acceptance and pin it" — rather than "absent". That is a positive instruction that can succeed, fail closed, and be pinned, and it shows up in receipts and diagnostics where a blank says nothing. The FFI mirror compounded it: `FfiWriteIntent.identity_override: Option<String>` accepted 64-char hex OR a bech32 `npub` *by design*, on the argument that "an identity is the one input an app most plausibly holds in display form". Holding a value in display form is a fact about the app's UI, so the place display forms are decoded is the app's own boundary, not NMP's write plane. One field taking two encodings is how "what does NMP accept here" stops having one answer and every pubkey-shaped input becomes an argument (`docs/internals/conventions/bech32-boundary.md`).
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_grammar::Identity` is added and re-exported from `nmp`: `enum Identity { Active, Explicit(PublicKey) }`, `Copy`, with `Active` as its `Default`. `WriteIntent.identity_override: Option<PublicKey>` becomes `WriteIntent.identity: Identity`. At FFI it lands as the mirrored two-variant `FfiIdentity { Active, Explicit { pubkey: String } }` and `FfiWriteIntent.identity: FfiIdentity`; `pubkey` is 64-char hex and nothing else, so a well-formed `npub` is now a synchronous `FfiError::InvalidPublicKey` where it used to be accepted. `convert::parse_identity_override`'s bech32 fallback is deleted; `convert::identity_to_ffi` is added so a protocol module's chosen identity projects back out totally. Swift gains `public enum Identity { case active, explicit(pubkey:) }` and `WriteIntent.identity: Identity = .active`; Kotlin gains `sealed class Identity { Active, Explicit(pubkey) }` and `WriteIntent.identity: Identity = Identity.Active`. **Deviation from `docs/internals/writes/identity.md` §3, reported rather than papered over:** the design says the FFI field carries `#[uniffi(default = ...)]` the way `identity_override` did, but UniFFI 0.29's record-field defaults parse only literals (`None`/`Some(lit)`/`[]`/int/float/bool/string), so an enum-valued default is not expressible at that boundary at all. `FfiWriteIntent.identity` is therefore a stated field in the raw generated bindings; the ergonomic native tiers default it in their own language, which is where app code actually writes it. `nmp_nip65`'s bootstrap composer names its account as `Identity::Explicit(author)` and keeps parking on `AwaitingCapability` for a non-active account.
- **Persistence impact:** none. `AcceptWrite`'s `expected_pubkey`/`signing_identity_ref` are unchanged and still carry the RESOLVED key, which is what the pin has always been; no stored schema, epoch, journal, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes. The input's spelling moved, the frozen fact did not.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. `WriteStatus::AwaitingCapability { pubkey }` still names the exact frozen identity; only its doc changes, to say that pubkey is the key `Explicit` named or the account that was active at publish time under `Active`, rather than "the override, or the active account when no override was given".
- **Updated falsifiers:** `nmp_grammar::write::tests::identity_is_two_words_and_active_is_the_default` pins the vocabulary and that `Active` is what a caller gets by default. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs` gains `a_bech32_npub_identity_is_refused_however_well_formed` (a real `npub`/hex fixture pair, refused with the same typed error every other malformed pubkey gets) alongside `an_explicit_identity_round_trips_as_the_parsed_pubkey`, `active_crosses_as_active_not_as_an_absence`, and `a_malformed_identity_is_a_typed_error_not_a_panic`. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle) runs `explicit_identity_publish_signs_as_that_key_identically_direct_and_ffi` over the new shape on both surfaces. `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` covers the byte-stable auth/signer-op doors across the field change. Swift `testWriteIntentDefaultInitMeansTheActiveAccount` and Kotlin `writeIntentDefaultMeansTheActiveAccount` prove there is no third "unset" state on either platform. The two #973 falsifiers `a_builder_publishes_as_the_active_account_and_refuses_when_there_is_none` and `identity_selects_on_a_builder_and_may_only_restate_on_a_signed_event` keep exactly what they proved, in the new vocabulary. Eighteen `@designed` BDD scenarios — all ten of `features/identity/write-identity.feature` and all eight of `features/identity/awaiting-signer.feature` — are un-tagged and pass against the real engine and real scripted relays. Those scenarios (and only those) run on a real on-disk `RedbStore` rather than the catalog's usual `MemoryStore`, because a `MemoryStore` cannot be reopened and four of them stop the engine thread and reconstruct a fresh one over the same store; the rest of the catalog is left on the in-memory store it has always used, so its wall clock is unchanged.
- **Superseded path removed:** `WriteIntent.identity_override`, `FfiWriteIntent.identity_override`, `Swift WriteIntent.identityOverride`, `Kotlin WriteIntent.identityOverride`, and `convert::parse_identity_override`'s bech32 acceptance are deleted outright — no alias, no `From` impl, no deprecation, no second spelling. `rg identity_override` over `crates/`, `Packages/`, `apps/` and `skills/` is empty. Bech32 no longer appears anywhere on an identity input path below the app boundary; `decode_nostr_entity` remains the one decode door, and the BDD scenario that pastes an npub proves an app decodes there and hands NMP a key.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1009 carries this slice of epic #968 under the repository owner's design rulings recorded verbatim in `docs/internals/writes/identity.md` ("it should default to using the identity active with an optional signer; whether it's on the call to creating the event builder or a .with().. I don't have a strong preference one way or the other; whatever is more ergonomic" and "B. But .explicit shouldn't be an npub... npub (any bech32) is outward-facing decoration; internally it should be a pubkey"). Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Publish into a NIP-29 group through a `Group` that mints both its read demand and its write ([issue #977](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/977), [PR #1011](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1011))

- **Failure evidence:** `contextualize_group_event` returned `GroupPublication { host, event }` and NOTHING in the workspace consumed it — no route could reach a selected host, so the only facade-constructible route for a group write was the author's outbox, which is semantically wrong for a group. The consequence was live in a shipping app: 29er-next's reaction path ignored `hostRelay` and published via the author route, so group reactions silently never reached the group host. The repository owner's ruling is that an app must not have to say "publish to group x, relay y" at all: it holds a group object, and `group.publish(event_builder_stuff)` takes care of adding the `h` and publishing to the correct relay.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_nip29::Group` is added — `new(host, group_id)`, `host()`, `id()`, `demand(Filter)`, `write_intent(EventBuilder)`, `signed_write_intent(Event)`, `validate_context(&Event)` — with `GroupContextError { CallerSuppliedContext, CallerSuppliedTimeline, MissingContext { expected }, MismatchedContext { found, expected }, AmbiguousContext { expected } }`. It is a pure value over `nostr` + `nmp-grammar`: it mints a read `Demand` (host pinned via `SourceAuthority::Pinned`, scoped by `#h`) and a complete `WriteIntent` carrying one appended `["h", group_id]` row and `WriteRouting::Explicit([host])`. `nmp` gains a dependency on `nmp-nip29`, re-exports it as `nmp::nip29`, and adds the extension trait `GroupOperations` (implemented for `nip29::Group`, re-exported so `group.publish(&engine, builder)` compiles with no import) plus `GroupPublishError { Context, Engine }` and the `GroupReceipts` alias. The trait's eleven methods are `publish`, `publish_signed`, and NIP-29's own named operations `join_request`, `leave_request`, `add_user`, `remove_user`, `edit_metadata`, `delete_event`, `create_group`, `delete_group`, `create_invite`. Every one of them takes `&Engine` and composes through `nmp-nip29`, then hands the minted intent to the ONE publish door; none of them accepts a relay, a routing value, or an `h` value. #989's nine composers change return type from `nostr::EventBuilder` to `nmp_grammar::EventBuilder` — the workspace builder the publish door actually takes, whose kind/tags/content are readable, unlike nostr's. There is deliberately no group read verb: reads go through `engine.observe(LiveQuery(group.demand(filter)), None)`. No FFI, Swift or Kotlin projection of group publication is added by this change; `groupDiscoveryDemand(host)` is unchanged and is still the only native NIP-29 surface.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, version, migration, journal, coverage key, receipt row, correlation representation, or stored-byte change. A group write is an ordinary durable write over the existing `Explicit` routing vocabulary, which #972 already made durable; nothing group-shaped is serialized.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No evidence, coverage, receipt, or attribution fact is added, removed, or reshaped. A group publication produces exactly the ordinary receipt lifecycle — acceptance, signing, `Routed`, `Sent`, ACK or the host's own `Rejected` message — with no second receipt path and no group-shaped status.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/group.rs` carries `draft_kind_and_schema_survive_except_for_appended_h` and `publication_never_synthesizes_previous` verbatim from the deleted seam, plus the pre-signed validation set (missing/mismatched/ambiguous `h`, byte-identical routing of a correctly contextualized event) and the two-groups-never-bleed proof. `features/groups/` is un-tagged in full and now runs in CI: 57 scenarios (68 with outline rows) across nine feature files — `publishing-through-the-group`, `group-is-an-identity`, `kind-blindness`, `reads-through-the-one-door`, `pre-signed-publication`, `context-tag-is-signed`, `app-supplied-context-refused`, `what-the-app-never-does`, and `nip29-operations` (which closes #989). They are driven against the real `nmp::Engine` over scripted in-process relays, and they read delivery off the RELAY's own record of the bytes it was handed as well as off the receipt, so a `must-never` claim never rests on the engine's self-report alone. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` is the structural half, and one scenario runs it — including against a deliberately mutated tree that privileges kind 9, which must fail it.
- **Superseded path removed:** `contextualize_group_event`, `GroupPublication`, and `crates/nmp-nip29/src/publication.rs` are deleted outright — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. Their duties moved inside `Group`, which mints the `h` row AND the route as one closed value rather than returning a contextualized event nothing could deliver. Both spellings are now BANNED by the ownership gate across `crates/`, `Packages/` and `skills/`, so neither can return even as a negative assertion, and the two skill references that documented the old free function are rewritten to the `Group` door. The gate's three requirements that named the deleted seam are repointed at the new one: it now requires `pub struct Group`, `pub fn write_intent(`, and `pub fn signed_write_intent(` in `crates/nmp-nip29/src/group.rs`, requires both carried-over falsifier names in that same file, forbids `fn observe`/`fn subscribe`/`fn stream` in either half of the group door (a group mints a `Demand`; `Engine::observe` is the one read door), and requires the engine binding to route through `engine.publish(intent)`. The `HostAuthority|PinnedHost` ban stays exactly as #972 left it: `GroupHostAuthority` is dead and the capability lives in the general `Explicit` route, not in a NIP-29-specific authority newtype.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1011 carries the NIP-29 slice of epic #968 under the repository owner's design rulings recorded verbatim in `docs/internals/nip29/group-publication.md` ("it should create a 'group' object like we've seen before and group.publish(event_builder_stuff) would take care of adding the h and publishing to the correct relay", "obviously it needs to have the h tag before its signed", "that OBVIOUSLY belongs in the nmp-nip29 crate!"), and under the settled shape decision recorded on issue #977: composition stays in `nmp-nip29` and the `&engine` methods live in `nmp` as an extension trait, because the dependency runs `nmp -> nmp-nip29` and never the other way. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Retire superseded replaceable outbox writes ([issue #901](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/901), [PR #995](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/995))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #901 records that two locally accepted events at the same NIP-01 replaceable/addressable coordinate could remain as separate open delivery obligations while offline. Reconnecting then drained both even though the newer event had already made the older unattempted obligation useless. The consequence was stale network work and an app-visible receipt that never said why the old write would no longer be sent.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the existing Rust `WriteStatus` gains terminal `Superseded`, and `CancelWriteError` gains the reachable `AlreadySuperseded { receipt_id }` refusal. UniFFI adds matching `FfiWriteStatus::Superseded` and `FfiCancelWriteError::AlreadySuperseded`; Swift adds `WriteStatus.superseded` and `CancelWriteError.alreadySuperseded`; Kotlin adds `WriteStatus.Superseded` and `CancelWriteError.AlreadySuperseded`. Store-to-engine `RetiredIntent` is internal acceptance evidence, not a third app-facing workload noun.
- **Persistence impact:** the existing receipt journal can now retain `ReceiptState::Superseded`; no table, key, schema epoch, or migration changes. Acceptance of the newer winner atomically removes only older intent, lane, route-revision, deadline, and displaced-draft ownership that has no attempt row, attempt-detail row, or nonzero lane ordinal. Correlations and receipts remain retained, and an older obligation with evidence of a wire attempt remains open because a relay may already have observed it.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics snapshot, evidence, coverage, or attribution shape changes. Receipt observation gains the precise terminal `Superseded` fact, including reattachment after restart, instead of leaving the older receipt open or collapsing it into a generic failure.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `features/writes/replaceable-outbox.feature` proves the offline/reconnect behavior for kinds 0, 3, 10001, and 30001 with its `d` coordinate. `newer_replaceable_write_retires_offline_work_before_any_attempt`, `superseded_receipt_and_pruned_outbox_survive_redb_reopen`, `a_started_attempt_preserves_the_older_replaceable_obligation`, `addressable_writes_with_different_identifiers_do_not_retire_each_other`, `retired_pending_predecessor_never_resurrects_when_newer_is_cancelled`, and `supersession_only_retires_unattempted_duplicate_coowners` pin store parity, restart, attempt, coordinate, compensation, and co-owner boundaries. `superseding_a_replaceable_write_cancels_its_pending_signer` proves runtime cleanup. Swift and Kotlin evidence-mapping tests pin the native variants, and `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` reports zero missing concepts.
- **Superseded path removed:** the acceptance path that retained every older unattempted owner at a replaced address is removed from both memory and redb stores. No compatibility branch, alias, background cleanup pass, or doc-only coalescing convention remains: the governed acceptance transaction itself retires eligible ownership and reports exactly which receipts the engine must terminate.
- **Human signoff:** Pablo explicitly directed the replaceable-outbox behavior and required red-first BDD in an isolated worktree. Issue #901 is the captured contract and PR #995 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Make `Auto` routing a durable strategy re-executed at every send attempt ([issue #975](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/975), [PR #1012](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1012))
