- **Failure evidence:** a verified defect on master, not a hypothetical. `AuthorOutbox` resolution errored whenever the directory knew no write relays for the author (`crates/nmp/src/core/write.rs:2599-2600`), and a routing error at `on_signed` removed the pending write, emitted `WriteStatus::Failed`, and returned (`:2229-2243`). Composed, those two mean **publishing anything before the author's first relay-list fetch completes — first run, cold start, offline — killed the write permanently**, with the event already signed, journalled and durable. The tell that it was a defect rather than a policy is the asymmetry: boot recovery treated the identical routing error as an empty answer and moved on (`:876`), so a crash-survivor outlived the exact condition that killed a fresh write. Separately, `WriteStatus::Routed(BTreeSet<RelayUrl>)` could not express the difference between "we have not decided where this goes" and "we know exactly where it goes and it has not gone yet", so an app's spinner could not tell a misconfigured indexer set from a slow relay. `docs/internals/routing/resolution-lifecycle.md`, `docs/internals/routing/knowledge-and-settlement.md`, and `docs/internals/routing/outbox.md` are the settled record.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `WriteStatus::Routed(BTreeSet<RelayUrl>)` becomes `Routed { relays: BTreeSet<RelayUrl>, complete: bool }`, and a new retained `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute { detail: String }` is added. `complete` flips on settled RESOLUTION — zero remaining unknowns — never on delivery, so `complete: true` with nothing delivered and `complete: false` with some relays already acked are both ordinary states. `FfiWriteStatus` mirrors both (`Routed { relays, complete }`, `AwaitingRoute { detail }`), Swift gains `.awaitingRoute(detail:)` and `.routed(relays:complete:)`, Kotlin gains `AwaitingRoute(detail)` and `Routed(relays, complete)`. In `nmp-router`, `RelayDirectory::knows_write_relays(&self, &PubkeyHex) -> bool` is DELETED and replaced by `relay_list_knowledge(&self, &PubkeyHex) -> RelayListKnowledge` (`Known` / `KnownAbsent` / `Unknown`) plus a `settle_relay_list_absent(&mut self, PubkeyHex)` door; `RelayListKnowledge` is a new exported type. The governed Rust facade snapshot grows from 26620 to 26624 lines against the 30000-line ceiling.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes, and the durable routing vocabulary is untouched — `routing_snapshot` still writes `auto` or `explicit-hex:` and nothing else, because nothing about resolution logic is ever serialized. The route-revision log (`record_route_revision`/`recover_route_revisions`) keeps its existing append-only shape; re-execution appends deltas to it through the same `commit_route_revision` projection door every lane mutation already uses. Settled absence is deliberately NOT persisted: it lives in `LiveDirectory` for the life of the process, has no store door at all, and a restart re-probes — absence is the one fact expected to change, so persisting it would bet against the author's own future publication and force an invented TTL.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema change in this PR. The receipt stream carries the new evidence: `AwaitingRoute { detail }` is retained and replayed verbatim on reattachment, so a route parked for a month is still visible with its reason across restarts, and `Routed { complete }` separates the routing axis from the delivery axis the per-relay facts already carry. The operator-facing `stalled_writes` section of `DiagnosticsSnapshot` is deliberately left to #756, which owns bounded write-scheduler evidence. Route unknowns now union into `sync_discovery`'s needed set (`needed = f(wire_demand) ∪ route_unknowns`), so parked writes appear in ordinary discovery diagnostics like any other demand rather than through a parallel path.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle) covers both new shapes, including `complete` as PAYLOAD rather than a tag, so a boundary that dropped the flag fails rather than passing a tag-only comparison. `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` covers the byte-stable auth/signer-op doors across the status change. `crates/nmp-router/src/facts.rs` gains `settled_absence_is_its_own_value_and_a_cache_not_a_tombstone` (settlement is a positive value distinct from ignorance, a later real relay list overwrites it, and a settlement pass never downgrades an author who is already `Known`). `crates/nmp/tests/signer_registry_headless.rs::an_auto_write_on_a_cold_directory_parks_instead_of_failing` replaces a fixture whose only surviving `Failed` came from this very defect. `crates/nmp-nip65/tests/live_bootstrap.rs` now proves the pre-echo ordinary write PARKS naming what it waits for and completes on its own once the kind:10002 returns through the network, where it previously asserted a permanent `Failed`.
- **Superseded path removed:** `RelayDirectory::knows_write_relays` and its `LiveDirectory` override are deleted outright — no alias, no default forwarding to the new three-valued answer, no boolean convenience wrapper. `WriteStatus::Routed`'s tuple form is deleted with it. The terminal-failure arm at `on_signed` is deleted rather than kept behind a condition: under this design "no relays known yet" is not an error, it is an `Auto` with unknowns, which is the normal INITIAL state of the queue rewriter, so the arm is unrepresentable rather than unreachable. `resolve_routes` loses its `Result` entirely for the same reason.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner's rulings are recorded verbatim in the merged design documents this change implements — "Perhaps that's what routing actually is, just a way to turn an item that has Auto in the queue to something that has Explicit on the queue", "an outbox can end too … then the outbox item is consumed", "once we know 'this event goes in relay 1, 2 and 3' it's been routed; it might have not been published", and "the moment we receive EOSE from the indexer relays we use we know, one way or another, whether we have a 10002 or not". PR #1012 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.
## 2026-07-29 — Expose the durable-write scheduler's stalled obligations in diagnostics ([issue #756](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/756), [PR #1025](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1025))

- **Failure evidence:** `DiagnosticsSnapshot` carried relay, query and AUTH facts and no write-plane evidence at all, so the only way to learn that a durable obligation could not progress was to be holding its receipt. Nobody holds the receipt for a message composed on a train three weeks ago. #975 made that population larger rather than smaller: a write that cannot resolve now PARKS in `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute` instead of being terminally failed and dropped, so parked writes are a new steady state that nothing engine-global could describe. The repository owner's ruling on why the surface must exist is recorded verbatim in `docs/internals/routing/preview-and-observability.md` §4.2: "This way of popping up a 'we were trying to publish to relay X and it didn't work' or 'we were trying to route this event but it didn't work' needs ato exist because there are many ways we'll find ourselves there."
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `DiagnosticsSnapshot` gains `stalled_writes: Vec<StalledWrite>` and `stalled_write_totals: StalledWriteTotals`, on the engine type and on the facade mirror alike. `StalledWrite { id: String, stage: StalledWriteStage, detail: String, stalled_since: Timestamp }`; `StalledWriteStage { Unroutable, Unsignable, Undeliverable }`; `StalledWriteTotals { unroutable, unsignable, undeliverable, omitted_details, detail_limit }`, all `u64`. UniFFI mirrors all three as `FfiStalledWrite` / `FfiStalledWriteStage` / `FfiStalledWriteTotals` with `stalled_since: u64` (`Timestamp::as_secs`, no narrowing anywhere); Swift gains `StalledWrite` / `StalledWriteStage` / `StalledWriteTotals` and the two snapshot fields; Kotlin gains the same three plus the two fields, `ULong` throughout. `id` is deliberately a domain-separated BLAKE3 descriptor over `(IntentId, frozen EventId)` and NOT a `ReceiptId`: it has no parser, no conversion door, and no counterpart on any surface that could be handed back to `reattach_receipt`, so this stays a bounded view of ACTIVE obligations and never becomes by accident the receipt-discovery door whose completeness, retention and access policy nothing has decided (#756's identity question; #903 owns the reverse link and has not landed). `stalled_since` is the acceptance INSTANT rather than an age: a snapshot is re-emitted only when engine state changes, so a duration baked into one would be stale exactly while nothing is happening — which is the entire population this section exists to describe. Its one known imprecision is documented at the field on every surface rather than hidden: it is when the OBLIGATION was accepted, which coincides with the stall for `Unroutable` and `Unsignable` (both attempted immediately) but is EARLIER for `Undeliverable`, so an app subtracting over-reports how long delivery has been failing. The park instant itself has no durable home, and holding it in memory instead is exactly what makes a restart reset it to the recovering process's clock — so this field takes the durable over-estimate rather than a number that lies after every reopen. Persisting the park instant is tracked as issue #1024. `detail` for `Unroutable` is the receipt's OWN park reason projected verbatim, never re-worded, so an operator comparing a diagnostics row with the receipt beside it never has to decide whether two sentences are the same fact. Nothing here is feature-gated: the three facade types are re-exported from `nmp`'s root outside any `cfg`, both snapshots are generated with DEFAULT features, and the whole change adds zero `#[cfg(...)]` and touches no `Cargo.toml` — so every line of new surface is surface the governed snapshots can see. The governed Rust facade snapshot grows from 27656 to 27726 lines against the 30000-line ceiling.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes, and no store door is added, widened, or called at snapshot time. `stalled_since` is the already-durable `AcceptWrite::accepted_at` that `RecoveredIntent::accepted_at` already replayed; the reducer now keeps it on its own pending row so the live projection and the one a later boot rebuilds are the same instant rather than two clocks that can disagree.
- **Diagnostics impact:** this IS the diagnostics change. The section is projected from the reducer state that transactionally owns the canonical durable facts — never a parallel retry or history map, never a `recover_*` history call, and with zero store reads inside `diagnostics_snapshot`, so `write_relay_workers`' no-store-read property is untouched. The detail window is bounded at 64 rows; the totals are exact over EVERY stalled obligation including the omitted ones, and `omitted_details` is an exact count rather than a boolean "there is more". Ordering is a documented display priority (stage, then acceptance instant, then descriptor) that no scheduler reads, so moving a row into or out of the window changes no total, and reading changes no retry order, wake deadline, receipt retention, or transport/signer lifetime. One new emission point: `EngineCore::handle` pushes a snapshot when a write-plane turn changed the stalled census, because a section nobody is ever pushed is a section nobody can read; the guard is a `(ReceiptId, stage)` census with no formatted detail and no descriptor, so an ordinary healthy publish still costs no rebuild at any beat of its lifecycle.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/stalled_writes.rs` adds seven. The unroutable row carries the receipt's OWN recorded park reason rather than a separately-derived sentence that could drift from it. An unsignable row stays pinned to the FROZEN author across an active-account switch and never names whoever is active now. A routed write is undeliverable exactly while no destination is connected, and leaves and re-enters the list on connect/disconnect with nothing having asked it to. Two hundred parked writes produce exactly 64 detail rows, an exact 200 census, an exact 136 omission, and byte-identical ordering across repeated reads of the same state. Two obligations over byte-identical events get distinct descriptors. A redb reopen reproduces the same descriptor AND the same acceptance instant from durable facts alone. A hundred reads move no wake deadline and change no answer. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs` proves every field and all three enum variants cross whole, with a `stalled_since` past the 32-bit boundary and `detail_limit: u64::MAX`. `crates/nmp/src/diagnostics.rs`'s exhaustive-destructure mirror test covers the new fields and all three stages, so a future engine fact cannot be silently dropped by the facade. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (the direct/FFI oracle) compares the detail rows in delivery ORDER plus all five census numbers on both surfaces. Three `@designed` BDD scenarios are un-tagged and pass against the real engine and real scripted relays: `features/diagnostics/stalled-writes.feature`'s "The relay that does not exist" (the repository owner's own `wss://non-existent.com` case — routed perfectly and instantly, undeliverable forever) and "Reading the list changes nothing" (a hundred reads make no delivery attempt, change no write's state, and record nothing durable), plus `features/diagnostics/delivery-attribution.feature`'s "A relay's refusal is reported in the relay's own words".
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp_test_support::RelayConfig::reject_writes` changes from `bool` to `Option<String>` — the flag is deleted, not kept beside the message, because a scripted relay that refuses in a sentence nobody wrote is exactly what the un-tagged scenario exists to forbid. No alias, no second spelling. Nothing else is replaced: the change is additive on every app-facing surface, and it deliberately adds no cancel, retry, or prune verb to diagnostics — the one abandonment door remains `Engine::cancel_write` on a typed receipt.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1025 carries the operator-facing half of epic #968 under the repository owner's rulings recorded verbatim in `docs/internals/routing/preview-and-observability.md` — the acceptance-time-impossibility paragraph quoted above, and §4.3's "there is no give-up policy. Visibility replaces it." Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Resolve `Auto` outbox to author write relays + app relays + p-tagged recipients' read relays ([issue #976](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/976), [PR #1037](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1037))

- **Failure evidence:** two verified defects on top of the derivation #975 landed. First, the operator fallback was armed by ANY contributing author below the 2-relay minimum, the author included — so every note published from a single-relay setup went to the operator's fallback relays, which is a different policy from the one that was ruled on. `docs/internals/routing/outbox.md` §6.2 is explicit that the write-side minimum "is about the RECIPIENT's coverage rather than the author's fan-out", and that a write already fans out to every write relay its author has, with no per-author solving to do. Second, a resolution that named no destination at all parked with the same reason a cold directory produces — "no relay list known yet for X" — so an operator whose relay lists had SETTLED ABSENT was told to wait for something that was never going to arrive. Both are behaviour a signature cannot express, which is why this entry exists against an unchanged snapshot.
- **Changed projections:** correction
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** NONE, on every surface. `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` and `docs/surface/nmp-ffi-component.txt` both regenerate BYTE-IDENTICAL to master at 27726 and 11638 lines, and no Swift or Kotlin byte changes. What changes is a STRING that #1025 made a stated contract on all five surfaces: `StalledWrite.detail` for `StalledWriteStage::Unroutable` is documented as the receipt's own `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute` park reason projected verbatim, so the wording this PR writes is the wording an app, an operator, Swift and Kotlin all read. A resolution that names nothing now reports every exhausted source and keeps the two shapes of nothing apart — `no destination could be determined: no relay list exists for <hex>; no app relays are configured; no fallback relays are configured` when discovery has settled, versus `no relay list known yet for <hex>` while it is still running. One is worth waiting for and the other never will be, and an operator told to wait for a fact that will never arrive has been told nothing. `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute`'s own doc previously gave only the second form as its example and now states both and the difference between them. Nothing here is feature-gated: this PR adds no `#[cfg(...)]` and touches no `Cargo.toml`.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes, and no store door is added, widened, or called. An earlier revision of this branch carried a process-local `PendingWrite::route_stalled_since`; it is deleted rather than kept beside #1025's field, because #1025 sources `stalled_since` from the durable acceptance instant and a second in-memory clock would reset on every reopen — which is exactly the lie that field would have told.
- **Diagnostics impact:** #1025 owns the `stalled_writes` section, its bounded window, its totals and its `StalledWrite`/`StalledWriteStage`/`StalledWriteTotals` shape; this PR adds no field and no type to it. What it contributes is the two halves that make `StalledWriteStage::Unroutable` mean something: the derivation that decides a write has nowhere to go, and the `detail` text that says why. `#1025`'s own entry deferred that text to whatever the routing arm wrote, and this is it. The receipt-side fact is unchanged in type — `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute { detail }` — and richer in content.
- **Updated falsifiers:** the 48 `@designed` scenarios of `features/routing/outbox-default-fan-out.feature`, `outbox-app-relays.feature`, `outbox-recipients-and-settlement.feature`, `outbox-fallback-coverage.feature` and `outbox-no-route.feature` are un-tagged and pass against the real engine and real scripted relays, plus `idempotent-resends.feature`'s "Two inputs resolving to the same relay make one destination, not two". `outbox-fallback-coverage.feature` pins the corrected trigger in both directions — a recipient with one inbox arms the top-up, an author with one write relay does not, and one short recipient beside a well-covered one still arms it. `outbox-no-route.feature` pins the refusal: the reason names the author's settled-absent relay list and the empty operator sets, it survives a restart, and the same write appears in `stalled_writes` as `Unroutable` carrying that reason verbatim beside the receipt's. `crates/nmp-nip65/tests/live_bootstrap.rs`'s park assertion stops pinning one phrasing of the reason and asserts what it is named for — that the park identifies the author whose relay list is missing — which is also the only claim stable across the two forms, since that test's controlled relay is its own indexer and its EOSE races the park.
- **Superseded path removed:** the author's own relay count no longer feeds the fallback trigger at all, and the coverage judgement is taken only from p-tagged recipients and only from SETTLED answers — an unknown recipient can no longer widen a route on ignorance and then never narrow it, because a relay already in an intent's route log is never withdrawn. `RouteAnswer`'s per-author contribution returns `Option<usize>` rather than a `usize` that collapsed "settled at zero" and "not looked up" into the same number; the collapsed form is deleted, not kept beside it. This branch's own `nmp::StalledWrite { receipt: ReceiptId, reason, stalled_since }` and its `DiagnosticsSnapshot::stalled_writes` field are deleted outright in favour of #1025's — no alias, no second field, no conversion door. Its `receipt: ReceiptId` was the specific thing worth deleting: making the row round-trippable would have turned a bounded view of active obligations into an undecided receipt-discovery door.
- **Human signoff:** the ruling this implements is recorded in `docs/internals/routing/outbox.md` §6.2 — "Pablo ruled yes: the write-side outbox resolver adopts the same set with the same suppression rule" — together with the scope answer on the issue itself, "yes, this stuff is needed too". The refusal's shape follows the owner's ruling quoted in `outbox-no-route.feature`: "This way of popping up a 'we were trying to publish to relay X and it didn't work' or 'we were trying to route this event but it didn't work' needs to exist because there are many ways we'll find ourselves there". PR #1037 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-29 — Correct the post-implementation EventBuilder and NIP-29 Group truth record ([issue #1016](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1016), [PR #1021](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1021))

- **Failure evidence:** the post-#1005 EventBuilder design record had replaced the rejected-alternative reasoning that explains why author and time resolution belong at the universal write boundary, while the post-#1011 Group record still read as an unbuilt proposal despite the direct-Rust door being live. The attempted correction also falsely downgraded `nmp-media`: its composer returns an `UnsignedEvent` whose public body fields copy into the public-field `EventBuilder`, so the existing publication pipeline remains valid. Separately, the Falsifier iOS qualification target still constructed the deleted Swift `.unsigned` payload, so the base branch's simulator workflow failed at compile time before running its runtime qualification.
- **Changed projections:** correction
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no public Rust, FFI, Swift SDK, or Kotlin SDK projection changes. The EventBuilder and Group design records now preserve their dated reasoning and append current-tree implementation anchors; README, supported-surface, and known-gap claims distinguish the built direct-Rust Group door from the still-absent native Group write projection. The Falsifier app's Swift qualification caller uses the shipped `.event(kind:tags:content:createdAt:)` case. Kotlin is unchanged, and the existing `nmp-media` Rust publication pipeline remains intact rather than being deleted or mislabeled.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, journal spelling, retained row, event bytes, route, receipt, correlation, coverage, or replay behavior changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** documentation now reports the existing EventBuilder, direct-Rust Group, native Group gap, and media pipeline accurately. No runtime diagnostic type, evidence fact, receipt status, or observability behavior changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `apps/Falsifier/Tests/FalsifierTests/NMPSimulatorQualificationTests.swift` compiles its write setup through `.event`, so the iOS simulator qualification can reach its NIP-11 runtime assertions again. `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-falsifier-honesty.sh`, and `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` remain the mechanical guards for the deleted `.unsigned` compatibility path, native parity claims, concrete falsifier anchors, and the Group ownership boundary.
- **Superseded path removed:** no public path is replaced in this correction. The stale Falsifier `.unsigned` call is removed rather than wrapped or aliased; the already-deleted production `.unsigned` surface stays absent. The false `nmp-media` downgrade is removed, preserving the one existing builder-backed publication pipeline.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner directed this implementation takeover through the Mosaico `/nmp` channel and identified issue #1016's interrupted handoff as the source of truth to complete. PR #1021 is the exact correction record; exact-head review, strict surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-30 — Move protocol discovery behind neutral routing facts ([issue #870](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/870), [PR #1059](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1059))

- **Failure evidence:** generic routing owned NIP-65 vocabulary, mutable protocol parsing, discovery kinds, indexer relays, and separate read/write mutators. That made protocol policy a router capability, let one direction become authoritative without the other, and made a generic content plan capable of acquiring a protocol-kind/indexer lane. Request completion had a second, independent defect at the same boundary: EOSE and NIP-77 could advance settlement after the exact event/coverage commit failed, contradicting #816's request-scoped coverage distinctions. The result could claim both route knowledge and request completeness that no committed fact justified.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust generic routing now consumes the read-only `RoutingFacts` trait and the exact `AuthorRouteState::Unknown | Present(AuthorRoutes) | Absent` state; `AuthorRoutes` replaces outbound and inbound sets together, and decoded `PublicKey` is used internally. Neutral lanes are `AuthorOutbound`, `Hint`, `Provenance`, `OperatorApp`, `OperatorFallback`, and `Exact`. Generic request evidence gains one `RequestSettled` fact with EOSE and NIP-77 evidence paths, emitted only after exact attributed coverage persistence succeeds. The engine-free `nmp-nip65` crate owns relay-list demand, canonical current-row selection, marker parsing, removal, bootstrap composition, and all-source settlement; only the optional Rust `nmp/nip65` feature privately assembles coordinator output into the fact writer. Rust `EngineConfig.indexer_relays` remains the explicit input to that optional assembly. The core FFI config deletes `indexer_relays`; Swift `NMPConfig.indexerRelays` and Kotlin `NMPConfig.indexerRelays` are deleted with every caller, so native core packaging exposes only operator `appRelays`/`fallbackRelays` and cannot claim an assembled protocol provider. The changed native diagnostics documentation describes the neutral lane strings rather than NIP-65/indexer lanes.
- **Persistence impact:** no store schema, epoch, journal spelling, event representation, coverage key, receipt row, or correlation representation changes. The change is behavioral at existing atomic doors: exact event attribution and coverage must commit before settlement evidence exists; a failed commit retires the affected completion evidence rather than recording a watermark. Author routing facts are private process state, and `Absent` remains deliberately memory-only. Removing the authoritative current NIP-65 row atomically clears the winner, reveals a same-batch predecessor when one exists, or publishes `Absent` after all exact sources settle; no stale route fact is persisted through restart.
- **Diagnostics impact:** relay plan lane strings now name neutral provenance rather than protocol ownership. `discovered_private_relays_rejected` is a monotonic rejection-occurrence tally, not a distinct-host or per-direction count: the optional NIP-65 provider increments it once when its admission callback rejects one syntactically valid relay-list tag, before an unmarked tag can project into both directions; rejected selector evidence counts once when that exact `(selection, evidence)` first becomes current. Query acquisition evidence preserves exact EOSE versus NIP-77 settlement evidence while sharing one generic settled-request fact. No aggregate completeness rollup is added.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `failed_event_commit_prevents_its_exact_request_from_recording_coverage` and `failed_missing_id_event_commit_poisons_the_original_neg_completion` pin #816's commit-failure distinction for ordinary EOSE and NIP-77. `authoritative_winner_removal_clears_winner_and_settles_absent` pins removal of the current row. `every_zero_destination_author_state_remains_a_provider_need` pins the truthful provider-work model across Unknown, Present-empty, and Absent until a positive route exists. `provider_rejections_increment_diagnostics_once_per_relay_list_tag` pins the optional provider callback's rejection counter, including the one-count-before-two-directions rule for an unmarked tag. `core_native_config_cannot_assemble_an_author_route_provider` pins the native feature-off boundary. Each load-bearing condition was inverted against its named test and restored before the full workspace qualification.
- **Superseded path removed:** `RelayDirectory`, `LiveDirectory`, `LiveDirectoryBuilder`, `FixtureDirectory`, `DiscoveryKinds`, `RelayListKnowledge`, the `Nip65Write`, `Nip65Read`, `IndexerDiscovery`, and `AppRelay` lanes, public half-mutators, core kind:10002 parsing, native `indexerRelays`, and the monolithic live-bootstrap integration path are deleted outright. No alias, compatibility decoder, generic indexer lane, or protocol-kind routing branch remains. The ledger's earlier statement that `WriteRouting::Explicit` can inject raw relays into shared routing facts is corrected: exact routes scope only their own operation and never mutate later routing.
- **Human signoff:** issue #870 is the architecture authority. Its durable correction at [issue comment 5128021420](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/870#issuecomment-5128021420) separates author-knowledge state from provider-work need: every zero-destination contributor remains a need until positive route knowledge can unpark the write, preserving the intended #1032 handoff. PR #1059 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, strict surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.
## 2026-07-30 — Govern every active UniFFI component with its own snapshot ([issue #954](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/954), [PR #1057](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1057))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #954 records that `nmp-nip46-ffi` is already a separately selectable provider namespace while the committed governance baseline extracts only the legacy aggregate core component. An accidental provider ABI/API change can therefore pass the versioned surface review even though #831 will publish that provider independently. The old snapshot layout also has no closed record of which artifact owns a namespace, which native roots are expected to wrap it, or which identities must remain reserved after retirement.
- **Changed projections:** ffi
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no supported Rust, FFI, Swift, or Kotlin declaration changes in this PR. The governed FFI representation changes: the legacy aggregate core snapshot moves to the sole `nmp-core/uniffi.txt` path, and the existing `nmp_nip46_ffi` namespace gains its first independent snapshot. A closed `component.toml` record binds each active namespace to its Cargo package, manifest, library stem, FFI sources, and stable Swift/Kotlin manifest/source roots; the catalog checks package/library names against that manifest while permitting a later valid governed package/build-owner move. The compiled extractor refuses namespaces absent from the active catalog and requires the requested namespace exactly once. Per-component parity means vocabulary from core can no longer mask a missing NIP-46 wrapper or vice versa. The current bootstrap has exactly two active self-owning records, `nmp-core` and `nmp-nip46`. It does not add the future #952 shared-interface or #824 independently packaged protocol records, and it makes no Android-publication claim for #831.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema or epoch, journal encoding, retained row, component compatibility identity, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. The catalog and snapshots are repository governance artifacts only.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No runtime diagnostics, evidence, coverage, attribution, receipt, or acquisition fact changes. This PR changes how public component surfaces are reviewed, not what an engine reports.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/test-surface-governance.sh` takes the workspace root explicitly and mutation-proves the exact bootstrap plus steady-state signal removal, configured evidence-path recognition outside the projection-only catalog tool, closed descriptor schema, governed package/build-owner moves with stable Swift/Kotlin roots, manifest package/library agreement, owner/co-location graph, missing roots, duplicate namespace/package/library/key, a gitlink source root, per-platform omissions, Swift-only/Kotlin-only/shared-manifest evidence, component-removal parity survival, the all-required Android table, descriptor/snapshot/catalog ceilings, NUL/CRLF snapshot refusal, immutable derived retirement tombstones, legacy-path resurrection refusal, reserved identity reuse refusal, live-child owner-retirement refusal, canonical component-scoped parity exceptions with stale-exception visibility, protected-program refusal, exact PR/projection/signoff evidence, and unstaged/staged/untracked dirty-checkout refusal. `nmp-component-interface-snapshot` has five unit mutations for a real multi-namespace inventory decision: selected, missing, undeclared-extra, wrong, and duplicate namespaces. In ordinary regeneration, `nmp-core` and `nmp-nip46` are built in isolated per-owner targets twice—catalog order and reverse order—and the checker requires the two complete generated trees to be byte-identical before comparing them with the committed head. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/surface_audit_704.rs` discovers each active descriptor at runtime and scans every corresponding component snapshot instead of naming the bootstrap records. The locked catalog crate remains compile-checked with zero Rust unit tests; extractor behavior is covered by its five unit tests, the shell matrix, and the two real regeneration orders.
- **Superseded path removed:** `docs/surface/nmp-ffi-component.txt` is deleted and permanently refused; `docs/surface/components/nmp-core/uniffi.txt` is its sole successor, with no compatibility read path or aggregate alias. The line-based `scripts/check-sdk-parity-allowlist.txt` is deleted and replaced outright by the closed-schema, component-keyed TOML exception catalog. No bootstrap flag is accepted after the exact legacy-to-two-record transition.
- **Human signoff:** the repository owner authorized the owner-controlled protected-governance bootstrap recorded by issue #954 and the documented two-phase procedure; PR #1057 is the exact review record created under that authorization. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still verify the protected-program diff and unrelated green checks before the separately documented #90 owner/admin guarded merge; this draft is not reviewer approval and is not merge authorization.

## 2026-07-30 — Make exact write AUTH denial durable and terminal ([issue #1032](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1032), [PR #1114](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1114))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1032 records that a true NIP-42 write denial could leave an accepted durable lane parked forever, including across restart, while public retry eligibility omitted the actual non-AUTH cause. The prior representation also could not distinguish an app policy refusal or signer rejection from a relay-owned AUTH refusal without source-jargon in a free-text sentence. A real-websocket run exposed the load-bearing ordering race: a ready policy denial could complete after the challenge but before the unauthenticated EVENT's `OK false auth-required`, skipping a lane that was still `InFlight`.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `AuthDenialSource::Policy | Signer | Relay`, `RetryCause::Interrupted | AckTimeout | ConnectionLost | RelayRateLimited | RelayError`, and `WriteStatus::AuthDenied { relay, pubkey, source, reason }`; `RetryEligible` gains required `cause` and optional exact relay `detail`. `AuthRequired` is deliberately absent from `RetryCause`: it remains `AwaitingAuth` and resumes only after exact-session AUTH succeeds. UniFFI adds exact mirrors, and Swift/Kotlin add exhaustive native enum cases and field mappings. No compatibility spelling, optional source, generic rejection fallback, or platform-local retry category remains.
- **Persistence impact:** the Redb schema epoch advances from 10 to 11 because `LaneState::Terminal` now owns typed `LaneTerminalOutcome`, including `AuthDenied(AuthDenial)`. `EventStore::deny_lane_auth` checks the exact expected lane revision before considering idempotent success, accepts only `WaitingAuth`, removes any deadline atomically, and can terminate at ordinal zero without fabricating an EVENT attempt. Policy `Deny`, signer `Rejected`, and exact-correlated kind:22242 `OK false` commit this terminal fact before receipt emission; failed commit emits none. Older schema bytes are refused at open under the repository's epoch-discard convention, with no migration or compatibility decoder.
- **Diagnostics impact:** write receipts now retain and replay the exact denial actor, identity, and reason. Retry receipts retain and replay their persisted non-AUTH cause and optional relay detail. Existing per-session AUTH diagnostics keep their read-side phase semantics: subscription `CLOSED auth-required`/`restricted` may describe that subscription session but has no write-terminalization authority. No aggregate success or new diagnostics rollup is added.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `auth_denial_is_a_durable_terminal_lane_fact_and_revision_precedes_idempotence` runs the exact contract over MemoryStore and Redb, including stale-revision refusal and reopen replay. `auth_denial_is_not_observable_after_process_death_before_commit` proves Redb commit atomicity. Engine tests pin exact policy/signer/relay inputs, commit-before-emit failure, Error/Unavailable and subscription-CLOSED contrasts, same-URL identity isolation, independent receipt lanes, and the challenge-before-OK race. Direct/FFI parity and exhaustive Swift/Kotlin mapping tests retain every source/cause/detail field. The executable BDD scenario uses the public `Engine::add_auth_policy` facade against a real local NIP-42 websocket relay, reconstructs over the same Redb store, reattaches by stable id, and independently observes through the relay's raw socket that no further EVENT is attempted.
- **Superseded path removed:** terminal lanes no longer store `AttemptOutcome` directly; the replaced shape is deleted in the same change. Receipt-silent policy/signer/relay denial, replay without source/reason, truthless retry eligibility, and the race that left a challenged in-flight EVENT outside denial reach are removed outright. `AuthRequired` is not retained as a deprecated or hidden retry cause.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1032 and its pre-implementation architectural-verdict comment are the correction authority. PR #1114 is the exact draft review record created for that owner-directed contract; exact-head review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-30 — Enforce every nested Demand's own cache and freshness policy ([issue #1106](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1106), [PR #1139](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1139))

- **Failure evidence:** the recursive resolver preserved each `Derived.inner` Demand value, but runtime acquisition collapsed cache and freshness to the root Demand. A strict nested projection could therefore admit cached rows observed only from a disallowed relay, apply `limit` before provenance refusal, inherit the root wire policy, and report evidence from the wrong scope.
- **Changed projections:** correction
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** no declaration or conversion changes. Runtime semantics now freeze and enforce cache and freshness independently at every Demand boundary in stable root-first structural order. SDK parity remains green; no native boundary tests are required.
- **Persistence impact:** no schema, epoch, or row-format change. Existing durable relay-observation and coverage rows are read through the corrected scope-specific policy. The Redb restart falsifier proves nested `MaxAge` coverage survives and remains scoped after reopening.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostic type changes. Evidence is computed for each Demand scope against that scope's acquisition plan, then merged by relay and access context; nested shortfalls are no longer hidden by root evidence.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `derived_inner_strict_cache_filters_provenance_before_limit`, `derived_inner_agnostic_cache_accepts_rows_from_any_provenance_before_limit`, `derived_inner_cache_policies_do_not_cross_contaminate_reactive_recompute`, `nested_cache_only_opens_no_inner_wire_under_live_outer`, `nested_live_opens_wire_under_cache_only_outer`, `nested_max_age_uses_inner_scoped_coverage_only`, `nested_strict_pins_do_not_contaminate_public_root_cache_projection`, `nested_max_age_scoped_coverage_survives_redb_restart`, and `public_engine_nested_strict_cache_uses_independent_relay_witnesses_before_limit` cover provenance-before-limit ordering, allowed A+B provenance, agnostic acceptance, reactive isolation, independent wire decisions, nested coverage, root projection isolation, durable restart behavior, and the supported public `Engine` facade with real signed rows.
- **Superseded path removed:** the whole-subtree acquisition decision and context-blind derived cache query are removed. There is no compatibility alias, NIP-29 workaround, relational redesign, or dependency on the separate composite-LiveQuery work in #1108.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1106 is the implementation authority and PR #1139 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-07-30 — Introduce the binary durable-delivery schema ([issue #1027](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1027), [PR #1150](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1150))

- **Failure evidence:** durable publication recovery read string/JSON Redb rows, so boot and reconciliation repeatedly validated UTF-8, allocated decoded objects, and parsed relay URLs before making a delivery decision. Calling that execution state “outbox” also collided with NIP-65 outbox route selection. Issue #1027 records both consequences and the required fresh-schema cut.
- **Changed projections:** ffi
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** internal Rust execution modules, store doors, types, and fixtures move from `outbox` to `delivery`. The governed FFI snapshot changes only the receipt-stream documentation from “persisted outbox/redb store” to “persisted durable-delivery Redb store”; no FFI declaration, Swift type, or Kotlin type changes.
- **Persistence impact:** schema epoch 12 opens a fresh `delivery_*_v1` namespace with an explicit codec-v1 marker, deterministic bounded binary keys and values, and an atomically allocated stable relay dictionary. There is no legacy reader, migration, dual write, row transformer, or automatic wipe. A store carrying legacy `outbox_intents` is refused without mutation and must be reset through the existing explicit offline destructive door.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics schema, evidence, coverage, or receipt-semantic change. Current documentation and fixture wording now distinguishes durable delivery execution from outbox route selection.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-store/tests/delivery_schema_contract.rs` proves the fresh namespace and typed legacy refusal. `relay_vector_is_human_explained_and_canonical`, `malformed_lengths_versions_and_trailing_bytes_fail_closed`, and `overlong_noncanonical_and_invalid_utf8_values_fail_before_adoption` pin the codec. `full_semantic_trace_matches_memory_and_redb_after_every_operation_and_reopen` compares exact normalized MemoryStore/RedbStore state after every operation and reopen, while `crates/nmp-store/src/redb_store/crash_atomicity_tests.rs` covers process-death transaction boundaries.
- **Superseded path removed:** legacy Redb outbox tables/modules, execution types, store doors, fixtures, and the execution-side feature-file spelling are deleted. No compatibility alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, legacy decoder, hidden reset, or dual-write branch remains. NIP-65 outbox routing is deliberately unchanged.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1027 and Indigo’s sole-owner assignment are the implementation authority, and PR #1150 is the exact review record. Exact-head independent review, surface governance, and required CI remain merge gates.

## 2026-07-31 — Retain delta rows until foreign completion ([issue #762](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/762), [PR #1161](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1161))

- **Failure evidence:** UniFFI 0.29.5 reports a Rust async result as READY before generated Kotlin calls the separate completion function that retrieves it. Coroutine cancellation in that interval freed the native future after `AsyncSubscription::next()` had destructively advanced the delta baseline, so a collector that continued pulling could permanently omit that frame and diverge from the engine's row state.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** direct Rust `AsyncSubscription` is unchanged. The row-stream FFI replaces destructive `next()` with synchronous `begin_next()` returning a private `NmpRowPull`; its start-once `receive()` awaits one result, `commit()` acknowledges foreign retrieval, and `abort()` restores an uncommitted delta. Kotlin creates the ticket before entering generated async code, commits before folding/emitting, aborts every non-commit path, and closes the generated ticket deterministically. Swift uses the same ticket inside its existing `AsyncSequence` iterator and commits before mapping or cadence control. Windowed row snapshots cross through the same transport for parity but remain self-contained and are not replayed.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, event encoding, receipt, route, coverage, or retained durable fact changes. The ticket retains process-local delivery state only.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostic type, counter, evidence fact, or aggregate is added. Stream cancellation still discards pending delivery and closes terminally.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `row_pull_cancellation.rs` proves READY ownership blocks a second ticket, abort replays the exact frame, commit prevents replay, 128 cancellation cycles remain bounded by one retained frame plus one composed successor, receive is start-once, commit refusals are typed, window snapshots are not replayed, and commit/abort/cancel races never resurrect or duplicate a delta. `RowPullCancellationTest.kt` drives generated UniFFI through the exact queued READY/free seam and proves free occurs before one SDK abort with no commit or escaped frame. `PullIteratorCoreTests.swift` proves the Swift ticket commits before frame mapping. The retained-frame restoration line and Kotlin abort call were each removed in turn to obtain the expected focused red result before restoration.
- **Superseded path removed:** exported `NmpRowStream::next()` and every row-stream caller are deleted in the same change; no alias, wrapper spelling, deprecation, or compatibility path remains. Snapshot and finite fact stream families keep their ordinary generated `next()` because they do not own delta baselines.
- **Human signoff:** issue #762 and its owner-authored implementation-shape comments are the correction authority, and PR #1161 is the exact review record for the rebased head. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-07-31 — Make observation opening transactional on canonical projection failure ([issue #809](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/809), [PR #1153](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1153))

- **Failure evidence:** ordinary observation opening could panic when its first canonical store projection failed, while windowed opening reported the unrelated `ThreadUnavailable` condition. Both paths could leave their newly allocated resolver and session ownership live even though no usable observation was returned.
- **Changed projections:** correction
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the lower-tier public Rust `runtime::EngineThreadError` gains reachable `ObservationUnavailable { reason }`, constructed only when an ordinary or windowed observation cannot establish its initial canonical projection. The high-level `EngineError::ObservationUnavailable` declaration is unchanged but now represents both observation forms. FFI, Swift, and Kotlin declarations are unchanged.
- **Persistence impact:** no schema, epoch, or row-format change. A failed open removes only ownership allocated by that attempt; monotonic internal resolver identifiers remain consumed so stale identifiers can never alias later owners.
- **Diagnostics impact:** refusal preserves the existing canonical projection degradation diagnostic and no longer substitutes a thread-availability claim. Refusal creates no row/history channel, demand, plan, pending execution, active execution, live wire, or registered observation owner.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `observation_open_failures_are_typed_leak_free_and_leave_runtime_usable` covers ordinary and history failures both before and after handle allocation, exact error mapping, zero ownership census, healthy empty observations, and continued engine use. `ordinary_projection_refusal_cannot_perturb_a_cap_sized_existing_plan` and `history_projection_refusal_cannot_perturb_a_cap_sized_existing_window` pin transactionality under compile-cap pressure. `shutdown_queued_during_each_refusal_keeps_the_typed_reply_and_never_panics` deterministically queues shutdown behind blocked canonical reads and proves the refusal reply wins without a panic. The governed QUERIES-OPENING scenarios trace the public ordinary, windowed, and shutdown-distinction contracts to those executable tests.
- **Superseded path removed:** effect-list scanning, the ordinary `.expect` panic, the windowed `ThreadUnavailable` substitution, and failed-open resolver/session leaks are deleted rather than retained behind aliases or compatibility paths.
- **Human signoff:** issue #809 is the implementation authority and PR #1153 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-07-31 — Compose independent Demand branches inside one live-query lifecycle ([issue #1108](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1108), [PR #1172](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1172))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1108 records that several correct reads need independent, fully scoped `Demand` branches whose results form one semantic query, and that NMP had no way to express that through one ordinary `LiveQuery`/`Engine::observe` lifecycle. Flattening host-scoped branches into one `SourceAuthority::Pinned` set produces a confidently wrong cross-product, and returning a list of demands makes every app rebuild coherent row merging, per-branch evidence, global bounds, atomic reactive transitions, and refcount-safe teardown. #1033 is the first blocked consumer.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `LiveQuery` moves from `nmp-resolver` to `nmp-grammar` and stops being a newtype over one `Demand`: it is now a canonical, nonempty branch set plus an optional aggregate result limit, built by `LiveQuery::single`, `LiveQuery::from_filter` and `LiveQuery::union`, read through `branches()`/`aggregate_result_limit()`, bounded by `LiveQuery::MAX_BRANCHES`, and refused by `LiveQueryError`. `nmp_resolver::Engine::subscribe` takes an atomic `Demand`. `Frame::evidence` and `HistoryBatch::evidence` become one `AcquisitionEvidence` per canonical branch; `ObservationEvidence` gains `branch`. `EngineError::WindowAggregateResultLimit` refuses a window that would compete with an aggregate bound. FFI adds `FfiLiveQuery`, `max_query_branches`, `NmpEngine::observe_query`, four `FfiError` refusal variants and `FfiFrame::evidence: Vec<FfiAcquisitionEvidence>`. Swift adds `NMPLiveQuery` and an `observe(_ query: NMPLiveQuery, window:)` overload; Kotlin adds `NMPLiveQuery` and matching `observe` overloads. Both SDKs' single-demand overloads now build a one-branch live query.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. Branch composition is a declaration and projection concern; each branch's atoms, wire requests and coverage rows keep the identity they already had.
- **Diagnostics impact:** a frame reports per-branch acquisition evidence in canonical branch order instead of one merged value, and no aggregate is added anywhere: two branches that resolved the same value keep separate entries, and one branch's shortfall is never masked by a sibling's proof. Execution facts carry the branch that produced them, so two branches resolving identical values at identical paths remain distinguishable. Engine-global diagnostics are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/live_query_union.rs` proves branch source authority is never flattened, equal branches keep independent evidence entries, an unplannable branch reports its own shortfall, rows union by event id with merged provenance in one coherent frame, the aggregate bound is applied after the union, a reactive change moves every branch in one frame, cancelling a union keeps work a sibling observation still owns, an over-cap union refuses the whole declaration, and a window bounds the union globally. Each was run red under its exact named mechanism disablement — flattened pinned sets, merged evidence, per-branch frames, per-branch bounds, unconditional withdrawal, per-branch window targets, and over-cap truncation — and green after restoration. `nmp-grammar`'s `live_query` unit tests pin canonical value identity, hashing, and every construction refusal. `nmp-parity` compares per-branch evidence entry-by-entry across the direct and FFI surfaces.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp_resolver::LiveQuery`, the tuple spelling `LiveQuery(demand)`, `HistoryQuery::initial_demand`/`tie_second_demand`/`older_demand`, `NmpEngine::observe_demand`, and the single-value `Frame`/`HistoryBatch`/`FfiFrame` evidence field are deleted in the same change. No alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or second observation door remains, and no composite observer, composite subscription, or parallel frame family is introduced.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1108 is the implementation authority and PR #1172 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-07-31 — Remove the per-family separate-native-artifact machinery and NIP-46 ([issue #824](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/824), [PR #1171](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1171))

- **Failure evidence:** the per-family separate-native-artifact mandate is removed by owner ruling — NMP does not owe consumers prebuilt per-configuration native artifacts, so families are selected by Cargo feature into one native library. Everything on master that existed only to make two separately linked artifacts safe together was therefore dead: a core build fingerprint, an opaque provider mailbox, a managed release builder holding a per-build authorization, and a second UniFFI namespace. The boundary also structurally forced `nmp-nip46` to own a private relay pool, a second Tokio runtime, and its own relay workers, because a separately linked artifact must not carry a second copy of the engine (#1169).
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-ffi` loses the entire `signer` module — `CORE_COMPONENT_IDENTITY`, the exported `nmp_core_component_identity()`, `FfiSignerMailbox`, and `FfiSignerRegistration` — plus `NmpEngine::signer_mailbox` and the `nip46-provider-component` feature. The `nmp-nip46-ffi` namespace and its whole surface are gone. Swift loses the `NMPComponentCore` product, `nmpProviderCoreComponentIdentity()`/`signerProviderMailbox()`, and the `NMPError.nativeComponentMismatch(component:expectedCoreIdentity:actualCoreIdentity:)` case; Kotlin loses `NMPProviderComponentApi`, `nmpProviderCoreComponentIdentity()`, `NMPEngine.signerProviderMailbox()` and `NMPError.NativeComponentMismatch`. That error existed only to refuse a skewed pair of separately linked libraries, which cannot occur with one library. The `NMPNip46` SwiftPM package and the `:nip46` Gradle module are deleted outright. The canonical `nmp` Rust facade is unchanged: `add_signer`/`remove_signer`/`SignerRegistration` remain the one generic capability door, so an app can still supply any remote signer.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No schema, epoch, or row format changes. NIP-46 session checkpoints were app-owned Swift/Kotlin storage outside the engine's event/delivery store, so no NMP-persisted state is orphaned or migrated.
- **Diagnostics impact:** no diagnostics field changes. The deleted NIP-46 connection-event channel and its streamed `failed(reason)`/`Failed` states were never diagnostics snapshot fields; `EngineStartFailed` and `ObservationUnavailable` keep their exact meanings. Removing the crate also removes its per-session relay workers from the engine's thread census.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `scripts/test-build-swift-xcframework.sh` and `scripts/test-build-kotlin-jvm.sh` now drive both native builders against an ordinary `cargo build --release` release directory and prove the caller's target directory, relative-path resolution, and missing library/bindgen diagnostics; the paired-provider case is deleted with the paired builder. `scripts/test-macos-ci-throughput.sh` keeps its baseline plus twelve mutations, with the provider-workflow mutation replaced by a duplicated-native-build mutation and the step-count expectation moved from eight to six. `tools/surface-component-catalog`'s `bootstrap` transition arm and its three fixtures are deleted because the arm hardcoded `nmp-core` + `nmp-nip46` as the required active record set; the retirement is proven instead by the tool's own `active -> retired` transition, which regenerates the exact `nmp-nip46` tombstone and refuses resurrection of every removed path. `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `check-nip29-ownership.sh` and `check-macos-ci-throughput.sh` pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** `crates/nmp-nip46-ffi`, `crates/nmp-nip46`, `crates/nmp-ffi/build.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/component_identity.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/component_identity.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs`, `Packages/NMPNip46`, `Packages/NMPKotlin/nip46`, both `ProviderComponent` SPI files, eleven component/NIP-46 shell scripts, and `.github/workflows/nip46-provider.yml` are deleted in this change. So are the now-memberless `protocol-provider`/`protocol-projection` roles in `scripts/dependency-direction-policy.json`, and the builders' single-arm component knobs (`NMP_FFI_CRATE`, `NMP_FFI_LIB_STEM`, `NMP_UNIFFI_BINDGEN_BIN`, the `NMP_SWIFT_*`/`NMP_KOTLIN_*` overrides and `NMP_FFI_CARGO_PACKAGES`), whose only callers were the deleted provider builders; the unreachable external-Swift-module import-injection branch goes with them. The two CLAUDE.md §1-violating crate aliases at `crates/nmp-nip46-ffi/src/signer.rs:17` and `:22-29` die with the file. No alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or retained feature flag remains, and the `nmp-nip46` tombstone permanently refuses reuse of its namespace, library identity, and source paths.
- **Human signoff:** issue #824's owner ruling is the removal authority, #1169 is the authority for the NIP-46 shape that replaces it later, and PR #1171 is the exact review record for this head. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-07-31 — Replace the single-host NIP-29 door with a relay-scope object and composable discovery predicates ([issue #1033](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1033), [PR #1173](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1173))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1033 records that a NIP-29 group can be hosted by several relays at once, and that the shipped door could not express it: `Group` took exactly one host and `group_discovery_demand(host)` pinned exactly one relay, so a multi-relay group was reachable only by dropping to the raw `Demand` grammar. The discovery constructor was also the wrong shape — a canned `kinds:[39000]` query whose only contribution was knowing one kind number, while the interesting NIP-29 questions ("groups whose member list includes me", "groups where someone I follow is an admin") are relational and already expressible through `Binding::Derived`. NIP-29 authority is per-relay, so resolving membership evidence at relay A while listing groups at relay B is a confidently wrong answer rather than a slow one.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::nip29` becomes a real facade module instead of `pub use nmp_nip29 as nip29`. It adds `on(hosts) -> Result<RelayScope, RelayScopeError>` (fallible: an app-supplied relay set can be empty), `RelayScope::group`/`groups_where`, the composable `GroupPredicate` with `member_list_includes`/`admin_list_includes` and `union`/`intersect`/`minus`, and `Group` with `read`, `validate_context`, `publish`, `publish_signed` and NIP-29's nine named operations as INHERENT methods. Unsigned group writes take an exact decoded `PublicKey` author (#878); a signed event derives its author from itself and accepts no second selector. Reads return one ordinary `LiveQuery` — `single` for one host, `union` of complete singleton-host branches for many — consumed by the one `Engine::observe` door. `nmp-nip29` stays engine-free and mints atomic values only: `groups_where_at`, `member_list_includes_at`, `admin_list_includes_at`, `group_demand_at`, `contextualize`, `validate_context`, plus `GROUP_METADATA_KIND`/`GROUP_ADMINS_KIND`/`GROUP_MEMBERS_KIND`; it never imports or returns a `WriteIntent`. FFI adds `FfiRelayScope`, `FfiGroup`, `FfiGroupPredicate`, `NmpGroupReceiptStream`, `live_query_to_ffi` and the group-context refusal variants folded into the single existing `FfiError`. Swift adds `NMPRelayScope`/`NMPGroup`/`NMPGroupPredicate`/`NMPGroupWriteStatus`; Kotlin mirrors them exactly, with no SDK-parity allowlist entry.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. Divergent relay-signed metadata is preserved exactly as before: the addressable coordinate already includes the author pubkey, so two relays' own kind:39000 records for one group id remain separate rows with their own `sources` sets.
- **Diagnostics impact:** a multi-relay NIP-29 read is one observation with one complete branch per host, so acquisition evidence arrives per branch in canonical host order rather than merged — one host's shortfall is never masked by another host's proof. Every NIP-29-owned demand carries `SourceAuthority::Pinned({host})` and `CacheMode::Strict`, so per-source evidence and the local row projection agree on which relay answered. Engine-global diagnostics are unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/nip29/mod.rs`'s `scope_stamps_exact_hosts_on_every_nested_nip29_demand` proves a depth-2 graph over a two-host scope pins BOTH host-scoping axes at every NIP-29-owned level; it was run red by removing exactly one inner pin (`pinned_public_at` -> `Demand::from_filter`), failing with the exact depth and `Public` versus `Pinned({host-1})`, and again by dropping `CacheMode::Strict`, failing with `Agnostic` versus `Strict`. `crates/nmp-nip29/src/discovery.rs`'s `every_nip29_owned_level_scopes_both_the_wire_and_the_cache_to_the_exact_host` is its unit twin. `crates/nmp/tests/group_publication_door.rs` proves at the wire that a two-host listing never lets one host's member evidence answer for another host's group, that a group write routes `Explicit` to the whole scope and never to the author outbox, and that pre-signed bytes reach every host unchanged. `crates/nmp-nip29/src/context.rs` proves a read selection already constraining `#h` is refused rather than overwritten, and `crates/nmp/src/nip29/predicate.rs` proves a caller-owned inner demand keeps its own `AuthorOutboxes` authority. `features/groups/relay-scope-and-discovery.feature` runs twelve governed scenarios; all 235 BDD scenarios pass.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp_nip29::group_discovery_demand`, its `pinned_demand` helper and `crates/nmp-nip29/src/demand.rs`; `Group::new(host, group_id)`, `Group::host()`, and the lower crate's `write_intent`/`signed_write_intent`; the `GroupOperations` extension trait and `crates/nmp/src/group.rs`; and the FFI/Swift/Kotlin `groupDiscoveryDemand` projections. All are deleted in this change with no alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or single-host convenience overload — a singleton is `nip29::on([host])?.group(id)`. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-nip51-no-derived-authority.sh` and `scripts/check-routing-vocabulary.sh` are retargeted at the new door in the same change.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1033 and its authority-correction comments are the implementation authority, and PR #1173 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-08-02 — Make the native live-query declaration the canonical branch set it is in Rust ([issue #1189](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1189), [PR #1206](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1206))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1189 records that #1108's cross-surface requirement — private union storage, factories that can refuse, and equality over the canonical set rather than caller insertion order — did not reach Swift or Kotlin. Both landed the declaration as an ordered list with public memberwise/data construction and synthesised structural equality, so the same two branches typed in the other order were a different value with a different hash while being one value in Rust, and a repeated branch survived in the native value until the boundary collapsed it, leaving the app's branch count and the delivered per-branch evidence count disagreeing. No Swift or Kotlin test named the type at all, which is why it shipped.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** FFI adds one exported free function, `live_query_union(branches: Vec<FfiLiveQuery>, aggregate_result_limit: Option<u32>) -> Result<FfiLiveQuery, FfiError>`, which is `nmp::LiveQuery::union` at the boundary: it validates, flattens, sorts and deduplicates through the same construction the engine uses, and returns the same four typed refusals. `FfiLiveQuery` itself is unchanged, as is every observe verb. `NMPLiveQuery` on both SDKs now holds its branches in private storage behind read-only `branches`/`aggregateResultLimit` accessors and is built only by a `single` factory and a throwing/`NMPError`-raising `union` factory that construct through that door, so equality and hashing are the canonical set on both platforms. Kotlin's type is an ordinary class with explicit `equals`/`hashCode`/`toString` over that set, and its lift from an FFI value is internal. Both engines' single-demand observe overloads go through `single`. The canonical Rust facade is unchanged: `LiveQuery::single`/`from_filter`/`union`/`branches`/`aggregate_result_limit`/`MAX_BRANCHES` and `LiveQueryError` keep their exact shapes and meanings.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. This is a declaration-construction change on two hand-written SDK surfaces plus one boundary function; the branches an observation opens are byte-identical to what Rust already computed for the same input.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. Per-branch acquisition evidence keeps its canonical branch order and one-entry-per-branch shape. What changes is that a native app can now rely on the correspondence: `branches[i]` names the branch each delivered batch's `evidence[i]` reports on, because a duplicate can no longer inflate the app's branch list past the branch set actually observed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/NMPLiveQueryTests.swift` and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/NMPLiveQueryTest.kt` are the first native tests to exercise this type: declaration order changes neither identity nor hash nor branch order, a branch declared twice yields one branch, nested input flattens to the flat declaration's value, the aggregate bound is part of the value, the app's branch count equals the evidence count delivered by a real observation, and each of the four unobservable declarations raises its own typed error with exact counts. Their first three assertions were run against the previous shape and failed on both platforms — Kotlin reporting one branch expected against two, and two branches against one evidence entry; Swift reporting the same two counts plus two unequal hash values — and pass after the change. `live_query_union`'s own tests in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/types.rs` pin permutation, duplicate collapse, bound survival and every refusal at the boundary. `cargo test -p nmp-parity`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `swift test` and the Kotlin `test` task all pass on this head, with no SDK-parity allowlist entry added.
- **Superseded path removed:** the public memberwise Swift initializer and the Kotlin data-class constructor for the native live-query declaration are deleted in this change, together with the publicly mutable Swift branch collection and the public Kotlin lift from an FFI value. No alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or second construction door remains: `single` and `union` are the only ways an app can make one, and neither can produce an empty, duplicated, or noncanonical declaration.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1189 is the implementation authority and PR #1206 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-08-03 — Read NIP-29's relay-signed group records through one reactive door ([issue #1233](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1233), [issue #1245](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1245), [PR #1246](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1246))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1233 records that `nmp-nip29` owned the three kinds NIP-29 defines for describing a group — 39000 metadata, 39001 admins, 39002 members — and exposed them as predicates only. `member_list_includes_at`, `admin_list_includes_at` and `groups_where_at` all answer *does this list include X*; none answers *who is in this group*. Both real consumer applications therefore hand-walked `p` tags off raw rows, four readings between them, and the readings disagree: mosaico carries two copies of the parse and the second drops the role field the first keeps, recording a role-less admin as an ordinary member, while 29er-next reconstructs all three kinds from `tags: [[String]]`. Issue #1245 records the second half: `Group::read` stamps `#h` and the three records key on `d`, so `read(kinds:[39001,39002])` built a filter no such event can ever match — including at a shipped call site in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs` whose variable is named `membership`. That read returned `Ok`, opened a subscription, and stayed empty forever, indistinguishable from a group whose relay published no roster, so it failed silently by construction.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::nip29` adds `RelayScope::observe(&engine, predicate, records) -> Result<GroupObservation, GroupObserveError>`, the free `nip29::group(hosts, id) -> Result<Group, RelayScopeError>` sugar, and `Group::observe(&engine, records)`. `GroupObservation` is the retained handle — `next`, `next_within`, `latest`, `cancel`, `cancel_handle`, and a `Drop` that withdraws the demand — mirroring `nmp_nip02`'s `FollowObservation` exactly. It delivers `Vec<GroupSnapshot>`; a group-scoped observation delivers exactly one. `GroupSnapshot` carries `id`, `metadata`, `admins`, `members`, `availability`, `per_host`, `disagreements`, plus `at(&host)` and `differs(record)`. New supporting values: `GroupAvailability`, `HostRecords`, `GroupWaitError`, and `GroupPredicate::AnyOf` with its `any_of(ids)` constructor. `nmp-nip29` stays engine-free (`nostr` + `nmp-grammar`) and gains the per-event typed projection beside the schema it parses: `GroupRecord` with `record_kind`/`of_kind`/`ALL`, `GroupMetadata`, `ListedRecord`, `ListedSubject`, `group_metadata_at`, `listed_record_at`, `join_key_of`, and `group_records_at(host, records, predicate)`. `GroupContextError` gains `RecordsAreNotContextScoped { kinds }`. FFI adds `FfiGroupRecord`, `FfiGroupAvailability`, `FfiListedSubject`, `FfiListedRecord`, `FfiGroupMetadata`, `FfiHostRecords`, `FfiGroupSnapshot`, the `NmpGroupRecordsStream` object, `FfiRelayScope::observe_records`, `FfiGroup::observe_records`, the `any_of` free function, and two `FfiError` variants (`GroupRecordsNotContextScoped { kinds }`, `GroupNoRecordSelected`). Swift adds `NMPGroupRecord`, `NMPGroupAvailability`, `NMPListedSubject`, `NMPListedRecord`, `NMPGroupMetadata`, `NMPHostRecords`, `NMPGroupSnapshot`, `NMPGroupRecordsObservation` and `NMPGroupObservation` (both `AsyncSequence`s), `NMPRelayScope.observeRecords(engine:matching:records:)`, `NMPGroup.observeRecords(engine:records:)`, `NMPGroupPredicate.anyOf`, and the two matching `NMPError` cases. Kotlin mirrors all of it as `Flow`-returning `observeRecords` overloads and the same data classes, with no SDK-parity allowlist entry added.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. The records observation is a projection over the ordinary `Engine::observe_async` subscription and the ordinary `RowDelta` stream; the branches it opens are the same per-host pinned, `CacheMode::Strict` demands `nmp-nip29` already minted, differing only in that the kind set is now the record selection the app named rather than a hardcoded all-three.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. `GroupSnapshot::availability` is derived per host from the frame's own `AcquisitionEvidence` — the same ladder `nmp_nip02`'s follow availability reads — and hoisted onto the snapshot as the minimum over every host in the scope, so a host that has reported nothing yet keeps the snapshot at `Acquiring` rather than being silently excluded. Because the read selects only the records the app named, a directory screen asking for metadata alone no longer puts two unread list kinds on the wire at every host.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/group_records_reader.rs` proves the aggregation rules end to end over real sockets against real in-process relays: the lists union across hosts with per-entry attribution, the metadata is one host's whole record and never a field-wise merge, a role the relay wrote survives and one it did not stays absent, `differs` answers both ways, the group-scoped door delivers a renderable state before any record arrives, and a composed predicate delivers one snapshot per matching group. Three were run red then green — seeding the union from only the first host that answered gave `left: {49f1…, e99a…}, right: {39b5…, 49f1…, e99a…}`; filling a field the winning metadata left empty from another host's record gave `left: Some("an about row only the OLDER record carries"), right: None`; defaulting a missing role to `"member"` gave `left: Some("member"), right: None` on both the integration test and `nmp-nip29`'s own unit twin. `crates/nmp-nip29/src/context.rs`'s `a_read_selection_naming_the_relay_signed_records_is_refused_not_answered` and `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`'s `a_roster_read_through_the_content_door_is_refused_not_silently_empty` prove #1245's refusal, run red by removing the guard and scoping the roster read by `#h` anyway (`left: None, right: Some(Context(RecordsAreNotContextScoped { kinds: {39001, 39002} }))`), with `ordinary_group_content_still_reads_through_the_content_door` proving the refusal is about the tag axis and not a kind catalogue. `crates/nmp/tests/group_publication_door.rs`'s cross-host leak falsifier now asserts on the delivered snapshot rather than the raw rows. `features/groups/reading-the-group-records.feature` and `features/groups/roster-records-are-not-group-content.feature` govern twelve new scenarios. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-nip29 -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-parity`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, the Swift package build and its 163 tests, and the Gradle compile and test tasks in Packages/NMPKotlin, all pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp::nip29::RelayScope::groups_where`, `nmp_nip29::groups_where_at` (replaced by `group_records_at`, which takes a record selection instead of hardcoding all three kinds), `RelayScope::listing_branches`, and `FfiRelayScope::groups_where` are deleted in this change with no alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or query-minting convenience left behind; `groups_where`/`groupsWhere` are tombstoned in `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`. `scripts/check-nip29-read-door.sh` and `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` are retargeted from banning the word `observe` on a group surface to checking the property they were aimed at — that a group value opens the engine's own subscription and owns no read lifecycle of its own — because the blanket ban excluded a typed projection over the one door along with the parallel lifecycle it meant to forbid. `crates/nmp/tests/nip29_owns_no_publication_visibility_rule.rs` is retargeted the same way: it banned the substring `.sources`, which also banned `AcquisitionEvidence`'s per-relay facts and the per-relay attribution NIP-29 owns, and now refuses a *verdict* on a row's relay set — emptiness, membership, set narrowing, filtering — while permitting a plain read; both defect shapes were verified red against it. `crates/nmp/src/nip29/records.rs` accordingly narrows nothing and drops nothing by provenance.
- **Human signoff:** issues #1233 and #1245 are the implementation authority and PR #1246 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
