## 2026-08-04 — Decide relay admission by whose declaration named the relay, not by what the address is ([issue #1251](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1251), [PR #1254](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1254))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1251 records that `parse_relay_list` refused relays by address and dropped each refused row silently. A parser holds one event and no identity, so it cannot tell an author's own relay list from a stranger's; it applied one address rule to both. A user whose kind:10002 named ONLY loopback / RFC-1918 / `.onion` hosts — the default local-development shape — projected as `AuthorRouteState::Present` with an empty outbound set, byte-identical to a user who declared no relays at all. The app told that user they have no relays configured while the repair, `EngineConfig::allowed_local_relay_hosts`, was invisible from outside. The same incoherence ran the other way at the socket: an operator's `app_relays: ["ws://localhost:7777"]` was heeded by routing and then refused by the dial guard unless the same host was declared a second time in a different setting. `docs/known-gaps.md` on `agent/1237-publish-queue` recorded the gap as needing the ruling extended rather than a variant invented.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-network-policy` gains `Declarer { Ourselves, SomeoneElse }` and `OnionReachability { Unreachable, Reachable }`, `HostClass` gains `Onion`, `DestinationPolicy::new` takes the reachability declaration, `admit_host` takes a `Declarer`, and `DestinationRefusal` gains `OnionUnreachable`. `nmp::mechanism::core` replaces `RelayAdmissionPolicy::admits_discovered(url) -> bool` with `admits(url, Declarer) -> Result<(), RelayRefusal>` and re-exports `Declarer` and `OnionReachability` rather than minting a second NMP-side spelling of the same two-valued answer. `nmp-nip65::parse_relay_list` loses its admission closure entirely, as do `Nip65Coordinator::observe`/`observe_current_delta`. `nmp-router::AuthorRoutes` gains `with_refused`/`refused()`/`every_declared_relay_was_refused()`. `nmp-transport::Pool::ensure_open`/`ensure_session` take the caller's `Declarer`. `nmp::EngineConfig` and the UniFFI `NmpEngineConfig` gain `tor_reachable` (`default = false`); `nmp_blossom::BlossomClientConfig` and `FfiBlossomClientConfig` gain the same field. Swift `NMPConfig`/`BlossomClientConfig` gain `torReachable: Bool = false` and Kotlin `NMPConfig`/`BlossomClientConfig` gain `torReachable: Boolean = false`, threaded through `toFfi()` by name. Additive with defaults on every record, so existing foreign call sites are source-compatible; no SDK-parity allowlist entry.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. `AuthorRoutes` is session-owned memory with no persistence door, so the added refusal set is never written; a restart re-learns the relay list and re-decides admission against the identities that process holds.
- **Diagnostics impact:** `DiagnosticsSnapshot::discovered_private_relays_rejected` keeps its name and its one-count-per-refusal discipline, but now counts refusals decided WITH provenance, so an operator running a local relay no longer inflates it by learning their own relay list. The zero-destination write detail changes: an author whose every declared relay was refused now reports "declared relays but every one was refused (…)" naming the exact URLs and the two declarations that would re-admit them, instead of "Present outbound routes for … are empty", which is the sentence that produced the user-visible defect. No counter is added or removed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/nip65.rs` carries the ten governed scenarios' proofs: `a_recipients_localhost_relay_row_is_skipped`, `our_own_localhost_relay_row_is_heeded_however_it_arrived`, `the_identical_list_flips_on_who_signed_it_and_nothing_else`, `an_entirely_refused_list_is_not_the_same_value_as_an_empty_one`, `declared_tor_reachability_admits_a_strangers_onion_relay`, `the_two_axes_do_not_grant_each_others_addresses`, `holding_one_keys_signer_never_widens_another_keys_routes`, `an_explicitly_named_identity_is_own_even_when_a_different_key_is_active`, `a_detached_signer_stops_being_one_of_our_identities`, `an_app_relay_on_localhost_is_heeded_by_the_socket_with_no_allowlist`, `an_explicit_write_route_is_heeded_by_the_socket`, `a_pinned_read_source_is_heeded_by_the_socket` and `signed_out_leaves_only_the_operator_tier`. Eight were run RED by reverting exactly two lines — deciding the source as `SomeoneElse` regardless of identity, and discarding the refused set — with `an_entirely_refused_list_is_not_the_same_value_as_an_empty_one` failing on two identical `AuthorRoutes { outbound: {}, inbound: {}, refused: {} }` values, the defect verbatim. `crates/nmp-transport/src/pool/connect.rs`'s `our_own_declaration_reaches_a_local_relay_with_no_allowlist` proves at the dial that the same loopback listener is refused as a stranger's and connected as our own with an empty allowlist, and `crates/nmp/tests/relay_admission_provenance_end_to_end.rs`'s `an_app_declared_loopback_relay_is_reached_with_an_empty_allowlist` proves the two halves are actually wired together: a live engine whose only operator input is one loopback `app_relays` entry, with `allowed_local_relay_hosts` empty, publishes over a real websocket and gets an ACK. Run RED by making the socket re-derive admission from the address, it reported `Routed { relays: {ws://127.0.0.1:51065}, complete: false }` followed by `AwaitingRelay` until timeout — the app told it has a destination it can never reach, which is the two-owner symptom verbatim. `crates/nmp/src/core/query_tests.rs`'s `a_relay_hint_naming_a_lan_address_is_skipped_even_when_we_use_that_relay` proves a forged hint inherits nothing by naming a relay the app declares and dials. `crates/nmp-network-policy/src/lib.rs` pins the pure table: `one_address_gets_opposite_answers_from_the_two_declarers`, `onion_is_governed_by_reachability_not_by_the_local_allowlist`, `tor_reachability_grants_nothing_to_local_addresses` and `our_own_public_looking_host_may_resolve_onto_our_own_network`. `crates/nmp-nip65/src/lib.rs`'s `the_parser_reports_local_rows_instead_of_deciding_about_them` pins that parsing decides nothing. `features/routing/relay-admission-provenance.feature` carries all ten governed scenarios as `built`.
- **Superseded path removed:** `RelayAdmissionPolicy::admits_discovered` and `EngineCore::admits_discovered_route` are deleted, as is `parse_relay_list`'s `admits: impl FnMut(&RelayUrl) -> bool` parameter and the same closure on `Nip65Coordinator::observe`/`observe_current_delta`. The single-argument `DestinationPolicy::new` and `admit_host` are gone. `.onion` is deleted from `HostClass::Local` and from `allowed_local_relay_hosts`'s meaning: an app that listed a hidden service there is now refused unless it declares `tor_reachable`, which is the correction, not a regression to smooth over. No alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or bool-returning convenience overload remains at any of these sites.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1251 is the implementation authority and PR #1254 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-04 — Replace NIP-29's closed discovery predicate with the general query language, and deliver "all groups this relay hosts" ([issue #1252](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1252), [issue #1233](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1233), [PR #1255](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1255))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1252 records that `GroupPredicate` was a closed enum with no spelling for a directory: a browse of a relay's advertised rooms asks no membership question, and the ids it wants are the answer rather than the input, so 29er-next's channel sidebar kept a hand-built demand (`NMPFilter(kinds: [39_000], limit: 250)`, pinned, strict) and the last surviving raw-tag parser in the app (`ios/Sources/GroupDirectory.swift`) — the one reader #1246 would otherwise have deleted. Two further defects were in the same type. `AnyOf(BTreeSet<String>)` took a literal id set where every sibling leaf took a `Binding`, so "watch the groups named in my own kind:10009 simple-groups list" could not be phrased at all: the app had to fetch its own 10009, extract the ids by hand, open a second observation, and redo all of it whenever the list changed — exactly the hand-rolled shape #1246 existed to end, surviving one layer up. And each leaf hardcoded the tag it constrained (`#p` for the two list leaves, `#d` for `AnyOf`), so a question the named leaves did not spell — "groups whose metadata record carries this topic row" — was unaskable even though `nmp-grammar`'s `Filter`/`Binding` already expressed it.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::nip29` replaces the closed `GroupPredicate` enum with two opaque values. `GroupPredicate` answers *which groups* — `nip29::all()` for every group the host advertises, or a `GroupIds` converted in through `From<GroupIds>` — and `GroupIds` answers *where the ids come from*: `nip29::groups_whose_record_matches(Filter) -> Result<GroupIds, GroupPredicateError>` is the general spelling, with `member_list_includes(Binding)` and `admin_list_includes(Binding)` as shorthands exactly equal to it and `any_of(Binding)` binding `#d` verbatim. `union`/`intersect`/`minus` move from `GroupPredicate` to `GroupIds`. A new `GroupPredicateError { NoKindSelected, NotAGroupRecordKind { kind } }` refuses a general selection naming no kind or a kind outside NIP-29's three relay-signed records. `RelayScope::observe` gains a fourth `limit: Option<usize>` argument and takes `impl Into<GroupPredicate>`. `nmp-nip29` adds `groups_whose_record_matches_at(host, selection) -> Binding` and changes `group_records_at(host, records, group_ids: Option<Binding>, limit: Option<usize>)` — `None` group ids build a branch with no `d` row at all. FFI splits `FfiGroupPredicate` (constructors `all`, `naming`) from the new `FfiGroupIds` object (`union`/`intersect`/`minus`), adds the `groups_whose_record_matches` free function, changes `any_of` to take an `FfiBinding` and return `Result<Arc<FfiGroupIds>, FfiError>`, adds `limit: Option<u32>` to `FfiRelayScope::observe_records`, and adds two `FfiError` variants (`GroupIdSelectionNamesNoKind`, `GroupIdSelectionNotAGroupRecordKind { kind }`). Swift adds `NMPGroupIds` with `whoseRecordMatches`/`memberListIncludes`/`adminListIncludes`/`anyOf` and the three combinators, reduces `NMPGroupPredicate` to `.all` and `.naming(_:)`, and adds `limit: UInt32? = nil` to `observeRecords(engine:matching:records:limit:)`. Kotlin mirrors all of it with `NMPGroupIds`, `NMPGroupPredicate.all()`/`naming(...)`, and `limit: UInt? = null`. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes. The branches this mints are the same per-host `Pinned({host})`, `AccessContext::Public`, `CacheMode::Strict` demands `nmp-nip29` already minted; the only wire-shape changes are that a branch may now carry no `d` row and may carry a NIP-01 `limit`.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. The new `limit` is the ordinary per-branch NIP-01 `Filter::limit` and is deliberately never promoted to `LiveQuery::aggregate_result_limit`, which stays `None` for every NIP-29 read: two hosts asked for 250 groups each were asked for 250 of their own, and reporting a per-branch bound as a bound on the merged union would make the declaration a second owner of row membership.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `features/groups/relay-scope-and-discovery.feature` governs four new scenarios, all `built` with running evidence. GROUPS-DISCOVERY-007 (`nmp::an_unconstrained_directory_asks_every_host_with_no_group_id_row`, `nmp::all_binds_no_group_id_row_whatsoever`, `nmp_nip29::an_unconstrained_listing_carries_no_join_key_row_at_all`) refuses an `all()` that lowers to a branch still carrying any `d` row — the relay would then answer about that id set alone, so a directory would show only rooms the app already knew, which on screen is indistinguishable from a relay advertising no groups; the same scenario refuses a door that treats a predicate naming no constraint as a fall-back to the app's own id list. GROUPS-DISCOVERY-008 (`nmp::a_named_shorthand_is_exactly_the_general_spelling`, `nmp::the_general_spelling_constrains_a_field_no_shorthand_names`) asserts the shorthand equalities as value equality rather than prose, so a leaf that quietly grows its own lowering path fails, and proves a `#t`-constrained metadata question no named leaf spells. GROUPS-DISCOVERY-009 (`nmp::a_derived_id_source_keeps_its_own_authority_and_stays_reactive`) proves an app's own kind:10009 lookup drives the observation while keeping `SourceAuthority::AuthorOutboxes` — never repinned to the group's hosts. GROUPS-DISCOVERY-010 (`nmp::a_kind_the_group_host_is_not_authoritative_for_is_refused`) proves the general spelling refuses a kind the group host does not own, which matters because that selection is evaluated with NIP-29's own pin and would otherwise be asked of the wrong relay and silently under-resolve. GROUPS-DISCOVERY-006's evidence is renamed with the test it names. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/nip29_group_publication.rs` adds `an_unconstrained_directory_is_phrasable_at_the_boundary` and `a_selection_naming_a_foreign_kind_is_refused_at_the_boundary`; both SDK test suites add their own directory and refusal cases. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-nip29 -p nmp-grammar -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-parity -p nmp-store -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, the Swift package build and tests, the Kotlin compile and test tasks, and the out-of-workspace `tools/nip29-consumer-swift` build all pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** `GroupPredicate::{MemberListIncludes, AdminListIncludes, AnyOf, Combined}` and their public variant access, the old `any_of(impl IntoIterator<Item = impl Into<String>>)`, `GroupPredicate::{union, intersect, minus}`, the three-argument `RelayScope::observe`, the three-argument `nmp_nip29::group_records_at`, `FfiGroupPredicate::{union, intersect, minus}`, the old `Vec<String>`-taking FFI `any_of`, and both SDKs' predicate-leaf constructors on `NMPGroupPredicate` are deleted in this change. No alias, deprecation, forwarding wrapper, or second construction door remains. `Combined` is not renamed into `GroupIds`: the surviving set-algebra variant is over id SOURCES, is host-open, and cannot admit `all()` — which is the point, because `all().minus(...)` is now unspellable rather than refused at runtime. That refusal is deliberate and is the one thing this change does not build: Nostr filters have no negation, so "everything except X" cannot narrow a wire request, and honouring it would mean asking the relay for everything and hiding rows after delivery — the same spelling as every other `minus` with none of the wire effect. The alternative, lowering `all()` to a derived query over kind:39000 so the algebra closes, is exactly the defect GROUPS-DISCOVERY-007 refuses: `all()` would then always carry an unbounded `#d`. An app hiding muted rooms drops them from the `Vec<GroupSnapshot>` it renders, where the cost is visible.
- **Human signoff:** issues #1252 and #1233 are the implementation authority and PR #1255 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-08-03 — Rename the durable-delivery subsystem to PublishQueue and replace the write vocabulary with the custody model ([issue #1026](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1026), [issue #1237](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1237), [PR #1249](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1249))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1026 records that NMP used one word, `outbox`, for two unrelated responsibilities — NIP-65 destination selection, and the durable execution of an already-selected write — and that the execution half was then renamed to `durable delivery`, a name the repository owner rejected outright ("`durable delivery` is a terrible name but it's this very same thing; this should just be `publishing queue` or some shit like that"). The subsystem therefore carried two names for one concept while `outbox` still meant something else entirely. Separately, `crates/nmp-store/src/redb_store/schema.rs` carried four coexisting per-table version conventions inside one epoch — 12 `_v6`, 6 `_v8`, 16 `_v1` and 6 unsuffixed — while its own doc comment already argued the opposite ("one epoch, not seven independently-versioned ones") and `SCHEMA_VERSION` was the only value any code ever read or compared. No store has ever existed that a per-table marker could discriminate between.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** **(#1237 vocabulary)** `WriteStatus`'s 21 flat variants are DELETED and replaced by `WriteFact`, which separates facts about the whole write from facts about one relay: `Signing(SigningState)`, `Relay { relay, state: RelayState }`, `Destinations { relays, complete }`, `Outcome(WriteOutcome)`. `SigningState` is `AwaitingSigner{pubkey} | Signed{event_id} | Refused{reason}`; `RelayState` is `Waiting(RelayWaiting) | Sent | Published | Rejected{reason} | AuthFailed{pubkey,source,reason} | GaveUp`; `RelayWaiting` is `NotConnected | NeedsAuth | BackingOff{attempt,eligible_at,cause,detail} | PersistenceStalled{detail}`; `WriteOutcome` is `Settled | NoDestination | NotSent(NotSentReason) | Refused(RefuseReason)`. `AuthFailed` is deliberately NOT folded into `Rejected` — `AuthDenialSource` keeps an app's own decision not to authenticate from being reported to a user as a relay refusing them — and `BackingOff` keeps #1032's `cause`/`detail`, without which that closed fix would be silently reverted. Exactly one `Outcome` ends every stream, so an app can distinguish a finished write from a dropped subscription. `publish()` gains typed refusals (`PublishError::{PersistenceFailed, NoActiveAccount, SignatureInvalid, IdentityContradictsSignedAuthor, ReservedKind, EmptyExplicitRoute}`, surfacing as `EngineError::PublishRefused` / `FfiError::PublishRefused` / `NMPError.publishRefused`), and `Engine::publish_queue()` / `remove_publish_queue_entry()` are new on every surface (#1039). `WriteIntent` loses exactly one field, `durability`; `payload`, `routing`, `identity` and `correlation` are unchanged. `EngineConfig::max_publish_attempts` is new (#1031). Swift and Kotlin mirror all of it, and `swift build` plus `./gradlew compileKotlin` were both run green on this head. **(#1026 rename)** the `nmp::delivery` module becomes `nmp::publish_queue`, and every `Delivery*` type, `DELIVERY_*` constant, `delivery_*` function and `delivery_*` module in `nmp`, `nmp-store`, `nmp-ffi` and `nmp-bdd` takes the `PublishQueue*` spelling — `PublishQueueLane`, `PublishQueueLaneKey`, `PublishQueueLaneState`, `PublishQueueAttempt`, `PublishQueueAttemptOutcome`, `PublishQueueTransientCause`, `PublishQueueDeadline`, `PublishQueueReceipt`, `PublishQueueIntent`, `PublishQueueCodecError`, `recover_publish_queue`, `recover_publish_queue_lanes`, `bootstrap_publish_queue_lanes`, `due_publish_queue_deadlines`. `outbox` keeps meaning destination selection and nothing else, and the unrelated English sense of delivery — a row or receipt fact delivered to an observer, an event delivered to a relay — is untouched, including `ReceiptDelivery`, `RowDeliveryMode`, `AsyncDelivery` and the `redelivery` test vocabulary. No FFI declaration, Swift type or Kotlin type changes: the entire governed UniFFI delta is one docstring on `NmpReceiptStream`, so both SDK projections are unaffected by this half.
- **Persistence impact:** **(#1237)** `ReceiptState` loses `Abandoned` and gains `Refused(RefuseReason)` and `NoDestination`, both terminal and both retained so a reattaching app is told WHY a write ended. `RefuseReason` gains `Serialize`/`Deserialize` and a codec discriminant carrying `ReplaceableBaseChanged`'s two event ids verbatim across restart — those ids are what let an app fetch what is actually at the coordinate, reapply the user's change and resubmit without troubling them. `WriteDurability` and the intent record's `durability` field are deleted, as are `PublishQueueAttemptOutcome::OutcomeUnknown`, the `pending_ephemeral_receipts` meta key and the boot reconciliation it gated (a reopen is now a pure read). `EventStore` gains `accept_refused`, `close_unroutable_intent`, `enumerate_publish_queue_receipts` and `remove_publish_queue_entry`, and loses `accept_ephemeral`, `cancel_ephemeral_receipt` and `mark_ephemeral_signed`. All of this rides the SAME `SCHEMA_VERSION` 12→13 bump as the rename half, which is why the two land as one PR. **(#1026)** Every durable table is renamed. The 16 queue tables move from `delivery_*_v1` to `publish_queue_*`, and all 40 production tables lose their `_v1`/`_v6`/`_v8` suffix, so `events_v6` becomes `events` and `postings_run_meta_v8` becomes `postings_run_meta`. `SCHEMA_VERSION` goes 12 to 13 and every existing store is refused at `RedbStore::open` before any byte is written. The wipe is accepted and unmitigated per the owner's ruling on #1026: no migration, no reader, no drain, no operator messaging beyond `docs/internals/conventions/schema-epoch-discard.md`. Recorded consequence, not designed around: the three provisional kind:5 claim tables are discarded with everything else, so content a user deleted while offline and unpublished reappears.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes; the stalled-write projection reads the same reducer state under the renamed types.
- **Updated falsifiers:** **(#1237)** `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `every_write_fact_maps_without_terminal_rollup` pins all nineteen arms of the vocabulary across the boundary, including `AuthFailed` staying distinct from `Rejected` and `ReplaceableBaseChanged` keeping both hex ids. `crates/nmp-store/tests/publish_queue_contract.rs` gains `a_refused_write_is_taken_into_custody_as_one_permanently_failed_receipt` (custody survives restart with both ids) and `removing_a_queue_entry_forgets_it_and_refuses_while_work_is_open`. `crates/nmp/src/core/write_tests.rs`'s `a_zero_destination_answer_separates_still_looking_from_finished_looking` pins the #1236 discriminator. `crates/nmp/src/error.rs`'s `every_publish_refusal_reaches_the_boundary_with_its_own_sentence` proves each rule-2 refusal keeps its own repair. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled, and its direct/FFI oracle grew from 6 to 13 cases. `-p nmp-store`, `-p nmp`, `-p nmp-ffi` and `-p nmp-bdd` (186 scenarios, 1548 steps) all pass on this head. **(#1026)** `crates/nmp-store/tests/publish_queue_schema_contract.rs` replaces a 16-name whitelist with the full 40-name durable inventory compared by equality, so an added, removed or misnamed table fails by name. Its `no_durable_table_name_carries_a_version_suffix` is the direct falsifier for the suffix strip and was run red by restoring exactly one _v1 suffix onto the lanes table, failing with "durable tables carry a per-table version suffix, but SCHEMA_VERSION is the one epoch authority" and naming that exact table, and green after restoring the unsuffixed name. `a_fresh_store_creates_exactly_the_named_publish_queue_inventory` was run red by restoring the retired delivery_ spelling onto the receipts table, failing with the exact two-set inventory diff naming it, and green after. `a_previous_epoch_publish_queue_database_is_refused_and_left_untouched` proves a database written by the previous epoch — a delivery_intents_v1 table and a schema_meta_v6 marker holding 12 — is refused as `UnsupportedSchema` and left byte-identical, so the accepted wipe can never become a partial adopt. `cargo test -p nmp-store`, `-p nmp`, `-p nmp-ffi`, `-p nmp-parity` and `-p nmp-bdd` all pass on this head, with 188 BDD scenarios and 1562 steps green.
- **Superseded path removed:** **(#1237)** `Durability` and all three modes, `WriteStatus` and all 21 variants, `OutcomeUnknown`, `HandoffAmbiguous`, `Accepted`, `AwaitingRoute`/`Routed`, `CancelEphemeralOutcome`, `ReceiptState::Abandoned` with its meta key and boot pass, and the pre-acceptance upper-half receipt-id namespace (with `PublishError`/`EngineError`/`FfiError::ReceiptCorrelationIdExhausted`, which the custody model leaves unconstructible) are DELETED, not aliased. No compatibility shim, no deprecated variant, no dual spelling. **(#1026)** The `delivery` spelling is deleted, not aliased — no `pub use` forwarding module, no type alias, no deprecated constant, and no dual-name table lookup. `crates/nmp/src/delivery/`, `crates/nmp-store/src/redb_store/delivery{,_ops,_codec}.rs` and the three `delivery_*_contract.rs` test files are moved rather than copied. The per-table version suffix is removed from every table including the five bench-only comparison shapes, and the schema contract now fails if one returns.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1026 and its 2026-08-03 naming ruling, issue #1237's 2026-08-03 settled design comment, and the owner's 2026-08-04 cold-start ruling (*"if the app has no app relay and no indexer then the user's own relay list has no where to go -- so yes, it should also fail"*) are the implementation authority; PR #1249 is the exact review record. This entry now covers BOTH halves of PR #1249 under the single `SCHEMA_VERSION` 12→13 bump the one-PR ruling requires. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Extract NIP-73 external content ids into their own crate ([issue #1258](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1258), [PR #1259](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1259))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1258 records that `crates/nmp-nip22/src/target.rs` (192 lines) described NIP-73's external content ids while living inside the NIP-22 comment crate. NIP-22 is one consumer of them and not even the only spec-mandated one — NIP-25's kind:17 external reactions "MUST include NIP-73 external content `k` + `i` tags" — so any other NIP that needed an external content id had to depend on the comment crate, dragging 1133 lines of kind:1111 decoder along, or re-derive the `i`/`k` canonicalisation itself. That is the schema-ownership split NMP exists to prevent, and it also blocks the unified tagging door recorded on #1243 (§8), whose `RootScope` seam needs an external content id to implement a `nmp-grammar` trait: `nmp-grammar` is `generic-value` and may reach neither `nmp-nip22` nor `nmp-nip73`, so the protocol crate must implement the grammar's trait downward — impossible while the type is imprisoned in a comment crate. Separately, `General`'s "both cells non-empty" validation was the only thing standing in for NIP-73's `web` row, whose own table requires "URL, normalized, no fragment": four spellings of one page (`#fragment`, uppercase host, explicit `:443`, bare) produced four different `i` values and therefore four separate threads for one article.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** new `crates/nmp-nip73`, whose role resolves to `pure-protocol` through the `nmp-nip` family rule in `scripts/dependency-direction-policy.json` (`pure-protocol` may reach `pure-protocol`), with `nmp-nip22` as a consumer that re-exports it. `nmp_nip22::Nip73Target` becomes `nmp_nip73::Nip73` and `Nip73TargetError` becomes `Nip73Error`; the variant `PodcastEpisodeGuid` becomes `PodcastEpisode` and its constructor `podcast_episode_guid()` becomes `podcast_episode()`; the wire `k` value still renders `podcast:item:guid`. NIP crates and the facade spell types with the NIP number (`Nip65Operations`, `Nip65Coordinator`, `Nip11Decision`) while `nmp-grammar` uses protocol-neutral nouns (`NostrEntity`, not `Nip19Entity`), so the number is correct at this layer. A new `Nip73::Url` variant carries an already-normalised URL and its constructor `Nip73::url` performs NIP-73's required normalisation (parse as an absolute URL — lowercasing scheme and host, applying IDNA, dropping a default port — then drop the fragment), with a new `Nip73Error::MalformedUrl` typed refusal for anything that is not an absolute URL; `k` is `web`. `parse_podcast_episode_guid_i_value` widens `pub(crate)` to `pub`, the one real API change, because its caller is now across a crate boundary. `nmp::nip22` re-exports `Nip73`/`Nip73Error`. FFI renames `FfiNip73Target` to `FfiNip73` with variants `PodcastEpisode`/`Url`/`General`, and `FfiError::InvalidNip73Target` to `FfiError::InvalidNip73` with a new message ("invalid NIP-73 external content id"). Swift renames `Nip73Target` to `Nip73` with `.podcastEpisode`/`.url`/`.general` and `NMPError.invalidNip73Target` to `.invalidNip73`; Kotlin mirrors with `Nip73.PodcastEpisode`/`Url`/`General` and `NMPError.InvalidNip73`. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added. The governed RUST projection is nevertheless unchanged, which is why this entry's projection set is `ffi,kotlin,swift`: `nmp::nip22` sits behind the non-default `nip22` feature, so `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` never carried `Nip73Target` and never carries `Nip73` either. Every renamed Rust symbol above is real and is a hard break for a direct-Rust consumer enabling that feature; it simply is not part of the default-feature surface the snapshot governs.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route or coverage key changes. `Nip73` is a value type composed and decoded in memory; the only bytes it decides are the `i`/`k` cells of an event a caller publishes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip73` carries three new ones and three carried over verbatim under the new names. `url_normalises_and_drops_the_fragment` requires `https://example.com/post/2#intro`, `HTTPS://Example.COM/post/2`, `https://example.com:443/post/2` and `https://example.com/post/2#` to produce ONE value — it was run red by replacing the constructor body with `General`'s non-empty check, failing on the first spelling, and green after restoring the normalisation. `url_keeps_server_significant_parts_distinct` requires `/Post` to stay distinct from `/post` and `?page=2` to survive, so "helpful" query-stripping or case folding fails; it was run red by adding `parsed.set_query(None)` and green after removing it. `url_refuses_anything_it_cannot_normalise` is the Reachability Gate construction site for `Nip73Error::MalformedUrl`. `podcast_episode_refuses_empty_and_round_trips_i_and_k`, `parse_podcast_episode_guid_i_value_requires_the_prefix` and `general_id_refuses_either_empty_cell_and_round_trips` prove exactly what they proved before the move. `crates/nmp-nip22`'s own 30 tests are unchanged in substance and still pass, which is the move's own proof: the decoder, the composer, the demand and the intent all behave identically over the relocated type. `cargo test --workspace` (green, no failures), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-grammar -p nmp-nip22 -p nmp-nip73 -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-parity -p nmp-store` re-run after merging current master, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `swift test` (165 tests, 0 failures) and `./gradlew test` (BUILD SUCCESSFUL) all pass on this head. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled.
- **Superseded path removed:** `Nip73Target`, `Nip73TargetError`, `Nip73Target::PodcastEpisodeGuid`, `podcast_episode_guid()`, `crates/nmp-nip22/src/target.rs`, `FfiNip73Target`, `FfiError::InvalidNip73Target`, Swift `Nip73Target`/`.podcastEpisodeGuid`/`.invalidNip73Target` and Kotlin `Nip73Target`/`PodcastEpisodeGuid`/`InvalidNip73Target` are DELETED in this change — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. `General` is deliberately NOT deleted: `crates/nmp-nip22/src/decode.rs` builds it for any namespace with no variant here, and without it NMP could not decode valid comments other clients publish. Decode also yields `General` for `web` rows rather than re-running `Nip73::url`, and that asymmetry is deliberate: reading is not composing, so a `web` row another client wrote IS the thread's identity whether or not it is canonical, and re-canonicalising on read would either rewrite that identity (splitting NMP off from every other reader) or refuse a valid event NMP has no business refusing.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1258 is the implementation authority and PR #1259 is the exact review record; the unified entity-tagging design recorded on #1243 (comment 5188465168, §8) sequences this extraction first and is the higher-level why. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Replace hand-built reference rows with one entity tagging door ([issue #1243](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1243), [PR #1262](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1262))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1243 reported that `nmp-nipc7`'s kind:9 chat composer was unreachable from Swift, so 29er-next hand-built the reply row (`ios/Sources/RoomTimelineSending.swift`: `tags.append(["q", reply.eventID, relay, reply.author.pubkey])`). The design exploration recorded at issue #1243 comment 5188465168 established that this was a symptom. Every hand-built reference row (`e`/`a`/`q`/`p`/`E`/`A`/`P`/`K`) across nmp, mosaico and 29er-next was enumerated: 33 rows, 32 production sites. The relay hint was filled in ONE (`crates/nmp-nipc7/src/lib.rs:47`, in a crate no app could reach); the author cell was filled in that same one; a NIP-10 marker appeared in one, and that one was Swift. `crates/nmp-nip22/src/build.rs` emitted `["E", <id>]` — two cells — where NIP-22 defines `["E", <id>, <relay>, <pubkey>]`, six times in one file. So hints were not sometimes wrong: nothing in the tree emitted one. The row #1243 named was wrong twice over: `q` is NIP-18's QUOTE marker whose stated purpose is that "the `q` tag ensures quote reposts are not pulled and included as replies in threads", so a conformant C7 client places the message outside the thread it replies to; and it was emitted with nothing in the content quoting it, so the half-formed quote could not render either. Prior art was read from source and shipped two dated corrections of its own: amethyst#629 marked a direct reply-to-root `"reply"` instead of `"root"` and broke reconstruction five hops deep, NDK `31f7e3bc` reverted marker parameters on NIP-22's uppercase rows, quartz shipped a NIP-22 reply missing the parent-author `p` entirely, and NDK's two reply paths still disagree with each other about the same operation.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp-grammar` gains the tagging door. `EventBuilder::tag` now takes `impl TagRows` — a raw `Tag` (the unchanged exact escape hatch), a target implementing the new `RootScope` trait, or a target carrying modifiers — and `EventBuilder::content` takes `impl Into<InterpolatedContent>`, so a plain `&str`/`String` is unchanged while a `text!` value also carries the rows its inline references need. New public grammar items: `reply_to`, `text!`, `InterpolatedContent`, `Mention`, `At`, `Modifiers` (the additive vocabulary `uppercase`/`without_carried_mentions`/`without_author`/`without_self`/`from_relay`), `RootScope`, `TagOptions`, `Tagged`, `TagRows`, `Pointer`, `ThreadPosition`, `entity_rows`, `event_parent_rows`, `event_root_rows`, `COMMENT_KIND`, `TEXT_NOTE_KIND`. The `nmp` facade re-exports the APP half documented (`reply_to`, `text!`, `InterpolatedContent`, `Mention`, `At`, `Modifiers`, `COMMENT_KIND`, `TEXT_NOTE_KIND`) and the IMPLEMENTOR half `#[doc(hidden)]` (`RootScope`, `TagOptions`, `Tagged`, `TagRows`, `Pointer`, `ThreadPosition`, `entity_rows`), the same treatment the runtime FIFO family already gets: protocol crates take those from `nmp-grammar` directly, and the governed facade snapshot describes what an app calls. `impl RootScope` is added for `nostr::Event` (grammar), `nmp::Row` (facade — it adds only the verified relay hint and delegates the rest), `nmp_nip73::Nip73` and `nmp_nip22::CommentRoot`. `nmp-nip22` folds composition onto the door: `compose_top_level_comment`/`compose_comment_reply` become one `compose_comment(target, content)`, and `comment_intent(target, content, correlation)` loses its separate `CommentParent` argument. `nmp-nipc7` replaces `compose_chat_reply(content, ChatReply)` with `chat_reply(target)` emitting `e` rather than `q`. New `crates/nmp-nip18` owns reposting with both of NIP-18's kinds internally (kind:1 → 6, anything else → 16 plus a `k` row). FFI adds a `tagging` module exporting `reply_to`, `chat_reply` and `repost`, each taking an `FfiRow` and returning the `FfiEventBuilder` the publish door already takes, and replaces `comment_intent(root, parent, ...)` with `comment_intent(target: FfiCommentTarget, ...)`. Swift adds `Tagging.swift` (`replyTo`, `chatReply(to:)`, `repost`, `WritePayload.withContent`), `CommentTarget`, and an `NMPGroup.publish(engine:authorPubkeyHex:payload:)` overload; Kotlin mirrors all of it. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added. All four projections move. `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` grows by the documented app half of the door, and the doc-hidden implementor half above is what keeps it inside its 30000-line cap: an earlier regeneration failed at 30151 lines, and the same regeneration now lands at 28975.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route or coverage key changes, and no `SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. Everything here composes tag rows in memory before a write is ever accepted; the only bytes it decides are the tag rows of an event a caller publishes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes, and no change to acquisition evidence, branch identity or coverage.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `features/writes/entity-tagging.feature` governs eight new scenarios, all `built` with running evidence. Every one of the design's five named falsifiers was RUN RED and then green. `a_root_is_tagged_with_a_single_root_marked_row` was run red by marking a direct reply-to-root `"reply"` (amethyst#629), failing with `"reply"` and `"root"` side by side. `every_wire_reply_shape_reads_to_the_same_thread_position` was run red by deleting the no-`"root"`-marker reading case; that probe first passed, exposing a real hole in the test — with a single `e` row that case and NIP-10's positional fallback agree — so the falsifier now also reads a plain mention row beside a `"reply"`-marked row, where the two rules disagree, and the probe then failed by naming the mention as the thread root. `nip22_root_scope_is_uppercase_with_no_marker_slot` was run red by putting a `"root"` marker on the `E` row (NDK `31f7e3bc`), failing while printing the exact offending row. `every_pointer_emits_its_author_row_unless_declined` was run red by dropping the companion `p` row (quartz's silent missed notification). `carry_forward_and_dedup_behave_identically_on_every_path` was run red by letting one internal path stop deduplicating while the other kept doing so (NDK's split-path caution), failing with 4 `p` rows where 3 were expected. `same_target_yields_same_rows_regardless_of_caller` and `modifiers_compose_in_any_order` pin the remaining two properties. `crates/nmp-nip18` adds `reposting_a_reply_names_the_reply_and_never_its_root`. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/tagging.rs` and both SDK suites add their own boundary cases. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`'s new C7 tombstone was itself verified red by planting `pub struct ChatReply {}` (exit 1) and green after removing it. `cargo test --workspace`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `swift test` (169 tests, 0 failures) and `./gradlew test` (115 tests, 0 failures, re-run 8 times including 3 under saturating CPU load) all pass on this head. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled, and its NIP-22 oracle was rewritten so both surfaces read the root scope off one real parent event's own rows instead of either side restating it.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp_nip22::compose_top_level_comment`, `compose_comment_reply`, `crates/nmp-nip22/src/build.rs`, the `CommentParent` argument to `comment_intent`, `nmp_nipc7::compose_chat_reply`, `nmp_nipc7::ChatReply`, the FFI `parent_from_ffi` conversion, and the SDK `commentIntent(root:parent:...)` spellings are DELETED in this change — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. `FfiCommentParent` survives as a DECODE-ONLY value: it used to be half a composition input, and that was the defect, since a caller who states the parent separately from the root can state a pair that never existed together on the wire. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` was CORRECTED with the code rather than kept green: it required the literal `Tag::parse(["q"` in the C7 crate and a falsifier named `reply_uses_q_and_no_e_p_h_or_previous_rows`, so the gate was pinning the defect; it now requires the `e`-reply verb and its falsifier and tombstones both retired spellings. Two deliberate deviations from the recorded design are stated in PR #1262's body rather than hidden: the modifier vocabulary is spelled as methods on the target (`.tag(target.uppercase())`) because Rust has no variadic functions and `.tag(target)` and `.tag(target, uppercase())` cannot be one method, with the five names and the additive order-independent semantics unchanged; and a repost uses `entity_rows` rather than the threading dialect, because a text note that is itself a reply threads as two `e` rows and a NIP-18 reader takes the first `e` as the reposted event, so threading a repost would repost the conversation's root instead of the note the user chose.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1243 and its settled design comment 5188465168 are the implementation authority, and PR #1262 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Tell a signature in flight apart from a signer nobody has ([issue #1261](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1261), [PR #1270](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1270))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1261 records that `EngineCore::publish_queue_entries` mapped every receipt state that is not `Signed`/`Compensated` onto `SigningState::AwaitingSigner`, so `ReceiptState::Accepted` — the ordinary state of every healthy write between acceptance and signature promotion — was reported as the one signing state whose own docstring says *"No clock ever ends this … the app removing the entry is the only other exit."* The store already held the distinction (`IntentSigState::AwaitingSigner` versus `Pending`, whose doc comment describes itself as the finer granularity the app does not get), so this was a projection dropping information, not a fact NMP lacked. mosaico (`pablof7z/mosaico#765`) proved it by building the consumer: `mosaico doctor`'s first draft classified `AwaitingSigner` as stuck and its real-engine test reported `stuck: [StuckWrite { reason: "no signer is attached for 8587c252…" }]` for a write published milliseconds earlier with its signer attached and working. The consumer dropped the feature #1039's enumeration door was partly built for, because the genuinely parked write — the one whose only termination path is the app's own removal — could not be picked out from every healthy one.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::SigningState` gains a fourth variant, `InFlight { pubkey: PublicKey }`: a signer for that exact frozen key HAS the request and has not answered yet. It is additive — `AwaitingSigner`, `Signed` and `Refused` keep their exact shapes and meanings, and `AwaitingSigner` narrows to what its docstring always claimed. `FfiSigningState` mirrors it as `InFlight { pubkey: String }` (64-char hex, the same `to_hex` projection every other pubkey on this enum uses), Swift adds `case inFlight(pubkey: String)` to `SigningState` and Kotlin adds `data class InFlight(val pubkey: String)`, both mapped in their existing `init(_ ffi:)` / `from(ffi:)` converters. No function signature, record field, or error variant changes anywhere. A foreign `switch`/`when` over `SigningState` gains one arm, which is the intended break: an app that must decide whether to alarm a user now has to say which of the two unsigned states it is talking about. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. The new variant is derived from the in-memory obligation's outstanding sign request (`PendingWrite::sign_request_in_flight`), which is already rebuilt deterministically on boot: recovery restores it as false, and attaching or activating the frozen key re-arms it through the existing `on_signer_attached` path. Nothing about the durable `IntentSigState` encoding changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound or ordering changes; `EngineCore::stalled_write_stage` and the stalled-write projection read the same reducer state.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/publish_queue_signing_inflight.rs`'s `a_signature_in_flight_is_not_reported_as_a_write_parked_on_a_missing_signer` is the whole proof, written against a real `EngineThread` the way mosaico's test was — one signer that takes the request and holds it, one key no signer answers for, one enumeration. It was run RED on the unfixed tree and failed with the defect verbatim: *"a healthy write whose attached signer is mid-round-trip was reported as AwaitingSigner { pubkey: … } -- indistinguishable from the parked entry above."* `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `every_write_fact_maps_without_terminal_rollup` grows the `InFlight` case, and `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` grows a fourteenth direct/FFI case plus a `NormStatus::SigningInFlight` arm distinct from `AwaitingSigner`, so a boundary that folded either onto the other fails the oracle; `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled. `features/writes/parked-writes.feature` carries the governed scenario `WRITES-PARKED-015` as `built`. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-store -p nmp-parity`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` all pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** the `_ => SigningState::AwaitingSigner { .. }` catch-all in `publish_queue_entries` is deleted, as is the unconditional `AwaitingSigner` in `reattach_receipt_page`'s `AwaitingCapability` replay; both now go through one `EngineCore::signing_park` helper so the two read-back doors cannot disagree about the same write. No alias, no second spelling, and no bool on `AwaitingSigner` retained alongside the variant — a field saying "…but actually a signer has it" would have left the variant's own docstring false at every site that reads it.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1261 is the implementation authority and PR #1270 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Make the NIP-29 write door mint the intent it owns ([issue #1242](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1242), [issue #1244](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1244), [PR #1274](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1274))

- **Failure evidence:** two issues, one defect: the NIP-29 write door only published, and what it handed back was too thin to use. #1242 — `Group::intent` was private and `publish`/`publish_signed` published immediately, so an app whose write path mints intents in one stage and submits them in another could not use the door at all. mosaico says so in its own source: `mosaico/src/nmp_host/write/compose.rs:98-107` reads *"Routing and identity are assembled by the caller only because `nmp::nip29::Group` exposes no mint-without-publish door … See NMP #1242; this is a visible gap, not a deliberate bypass of the group."* — and its `group_intent` then chooses `WriteRouting::Explicit(vec![relay])` and `Identity::Active` by hand, both decisions the door already makes correctly. #1244 — `Group::publish` took no correlation and returned no receipt id, so `reattach_by_correlation` could not find a group write; 29er-next's `pablof7z/29er-next#155` is an entire PR about keeping group-send receipts across restart that could not use the gate. The stated reason ("every `NMPGroup` write reaches the engine's untracked publish door, never `publish_tracked`") had been false since the PublishQueue rewrite: `Engine::publish` IS `publish_tracked(intent).map(|r| r.statuses)`, so the id was allocated on every group write and discarded on the way out.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `Group::intent(author, builder) -> Result<WriteIntent, GroupPublishError>` and `Group::signed_intent(event) -> Result<WriteIntent, GroupPublishError>` — the write door, which now PRODUCES the ordinary opaque intent and publishes nothing. `publish`/`publish_signed` become exactly one of those calls plus `Engine::publish_tracked`, so every group publish verb's return type changes from `GroupReceipts` to `nmp::ReceiptStream` (store-issued `ReceiptId` included). FFI adds `FfiGroup::intent`/`FfiGroup::signed_intent` returning the existing `FfiWriteIntent` record, and every `FfiGroup` publish verb now returns the ordinary `Arc<NmpReceiptStream>`. Swift adds `NMPGroup.intent(authorPubkeyHex:kind:…)`, `intent(authorPubkeyHex:payload:)` and `signedIntent(_:)` returning `WriteIntent`, and every publish verb returns `Receipt`; Kotlin mirrors all four. No correlation parameter is added to any publish verb, deliberately: the token is caller-minted and caller-persisted before the write exists (#591), so the only honest value the door can put on the intent is the absence, and the app stamps its own on the minted intent before handing it to the one publish door. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs` gains `write_intent_to_ffi`, total and infallible, reusing the existing `event_builder_to_ffi`/`write_routing_to_ffi`/`identity_to_ffi` projections. `nmp-facade.txt` is 28978 lines, under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt or route key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. A group write already took `publish_tracked`'s acceptance path through `Engine::publish`; the only change is that the id that transaction already allocated now reaches the caller.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. One strictly-truthful improvement falls out of deleting the group-specific stream: a lagged group receipt now reports `FactStreamLagged { receipt_id: Some(id) }` instead of the `None` the group stream had no id to fill in.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs` adds `the_mint_door_hands_back_a_fully_decided_intent_and_publishes_nothing`, `a_caller_supplied_context_is_refused_before_any_intent_exists`, `the_signed_mint_door_refuses_every_ill_scoped_event_including_a_second_h_row`, `a_group_write_is_reattachable_by_the_apps_own_correlation_token` (publishes through the one door with an app-minted token and requires `reattach_by_correlation` to resolve to that exact `ReceiptId`), and `the_inline_door_hands_back_the_store_issued_receipt_id` (matches the returned id against a real `publish_queue()` entry). `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs` adds `the_mint_door_projects_an_intent_the_general_publish_door_takes` and `the_signed_mint_door_refuses_an_event_claiming_two_groups`. Swift adds `testTheMintDoorHandsBackAnIntentTheGeneralPublishDoorTakes` and `testAGroupWriteCarriesTheStoreIssuedReceiptID`; Kotlin adds `theMintDoorHandsBackAnIntentTheGeneralPublishDoorTakes` and its `groupWriteFactStreamDeliversOrdinaryWriteFacts` now asserts the receipt id. `features/groups/the-write-door-mints-an-intent.feature` carries four governed scenarios, all `built`. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` was CORRECTED rather than kept green: it required the literal `engine.publish(intent)` — the receipt-id-discarding spelling — so the gate was pinning the defect; it now requires `publish_tracked(intent)` and BANS `engine.publish(` in the group door outright, so the id cannot be dropped again by an edit that looks harmless. `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh` excludes `intent`/`signed_intent`/`mint` alongside `publish`/`publish_signed`, the same kind-blind escape hatch for the same reason. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN, not merely compiled — this is an FFI shape change), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin compileTestKotlin` and `./gradlew test` all pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp::nip29::GroupReceipts`, `crates/nmp-ffi`'s `NmpGroupReceiptStream`, Swift's `NMPGroupWriteFacts` and Kotlin's `NMPGroupWriteFacts` are DELETED in this change — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. A group write returns the same receipt noun every other write returns, on every surface, which is what makes the group door strictly the general door rather than a parallel one. The private `Group::intent(payload, identity)` is renamed `mint` and the public `intent` takes its name, so there is no second spelling of contextualize-and-route. Two doc claims that had become false are corrected with the code rather than left standing: `NMPGroupWriteFacts`'s "reaches the engine's UNTRACKED publish door (never `publish_tracked`)" described a door that no longer exists, and `RelayScope::group`'s unqualified "neither is readable back out" now states the deliberate cost — an app holding a minted intent CAN read the route and, from the payload's `h` row, the group id. That widening is stated in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`'s module doc rather than hidden; the alternative, a group-shaped intent noun only a group-shaped publish door accepts, is a second write lifecycle, which is the thing that module exists not to have.
- **Human signoff:** issues #1242 and #1244 are the implementation authority and PR #1274 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Give an app-owned signer a door that crosses FFI ([issue #1238](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1238), [PR #1275](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1275))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1238 records that a Swift or Kotlin app could register no signer at all. `nmp::Engine::add_signer` is generic over `SigningCapability`, whose `sign` returns a `SignerOp` poll-thunk; neither a generic parameter nor a thunk crosses UniFFI, and no other door registered a signing capability. Verified still true on `origin/master` at 564b11612: the only `#[uniffi::export(callback_interface)]` in the crate remains the AUTH policy bridge (`crates/nmp-ffi/src/auth.rs:256`), and both `add_signer` call sites in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/facade.rs` sit below `#[cfg(test)]`. The `nmp-nip46` crate is now fully deleted (`docs/surface/components/nmp-nip46/component.toml`, `state = "retired"`, `retired_by_pr = 1171`), so `nmp-local-signer::LocalKeySigner` is the only shipping `SigningCapability` impl. The single identity path an app had was `add_account(secret_key)`, which is why 29er-next persists a plaintext `nsec` to its sandbox and ships two paragraphs of apology in its identity sheet. Two surfaces already asserted a resolution nothing could cause: `set_active_account`'s doc states a write parks "until a signer for THAT key attaches", and `docs/known-gaps.md:151-152` states that "an app may still supply any remote signer through the ordinary `SigningCapability` door" — neither was reachable from Swift or Kotlin, and `features/identity/awaiting-signer.feature` specifies a signer attaching after the park when the only transport that could attach one had been deleted.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** new `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs` adds `SignerMailbox`, `SignatureRequest`, `SignatureSettleError` and `MailboxSigner` to the `nmp` facade, plus `Engine::add_signer_mailbox(PublicKey) -> (SignerRegistration, SignerMailbox)`. The door takes no secret and no callable — only the public key the app can sign for — and returns an engine-owned mailbox of immutable requests the app drains on its own executor. It is deliberately NOT a `callback_interface`: #783's falsifier 1 names `capability.sign` as a call that must not exist, and a signer is the capability where that matters most, since answering honestly takes as long as a person takes to approve something on a hardware device. `MailboxSigner` registers through the one existing signer registry, so replace-by-key, the exact-instance `SignerRegistration` removal proof, and the promotion boundary's verification of the returned event against the frozen template all apply unchanged. New `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs` adds `FfiUnsignedEvent`, `FfiSignerRejection`, `FfiSignatureSettleError`, the `NmpSignatureRequest` object (`unsigned_event`/`resolve`/`reject`) and the `NmpSignerMailbox` object (`public_key`/`next`/`cancel`), with `NmpEngine::add_signer_mailbox` and `NmpEngine::remove_signer_mailbox` on the facade. The mailbox object IS the registration proof; no separate registration record is minted, because nothing a second object could prove is not already proven by holding this one. Swift adds `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/SignerMailbox.swift` (`NMPSignerMailbox`, `NMPSignatureRequest`, `NMPSignatureRequestBody`, `NMPSignerRejection`, `NMPSignatureSettleError`, an `AsyncSequence` over the requests, and `NMPEngine.addSigner`/`removeSigner`) plus a public memberwise `NMPSignedEvent` initializer so an app can construct the answer at all; Kotlin mirrors all of it in `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/SignerMailbox.kt` with a `Flow`. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added. Two spellings were decided by the compiler rather than by review: the signed-event parameter is `event` and not `signed`, because UniFFI writes parameter names straight into the generated C header where `RustBuffer signed` is not valid C and the entire Swift module failed to build; and the stream-ending verb is `cancel` and not `close`, because UniFFI generates a `close()` on every Kotlin object for its lifecycle, making an exported `close` a conflicting overload inside the generated file itself. `cancel` is also what every other pull handle on this surface already calls it. One stale doc claim is corrected in passing: `add_account`'s doc called `add_signer` "`unstable-mechanism`-gated", which it is not and never was.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A mailbox signer is a live capability registration exactly like every other one; it is not persisted, and a write parked awaiting one uses the existing durable `AwaitingCapability` state unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes. A mailbox signer appears in `AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot::signer_bound` exactly as any other registered capability does, because it IS one.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs` carries six. `an_undrained_mailbox_refuses_past_its_bound_and_still_works_afterwards` is #783's falsifier 2 made executable: it fills the mailbox to its bound without draining, requires the next request to be refused as the ordinary retryable `SignerError::Unavailable`, requires the queued backlog to still be delivered afterwards, and requires settling one to make room for one more. It was run red by removing the slot claim in `MailboxSigner::sign` and letting the underlying FIFO handle overflow, which latches `FifoState::Lagged` and permanently kills the mailbox; green after restoring the claim. `an_abandoned_request_answers_unavailable_and_releases_its_slot` proves a request the app takes and discards answers the write instead of stranding it and does not leak its slot; `settling_an_unawaited_request_is_reported` is the Reachability Gate construction site for `SignatureSettleError::NoLongerAwaited`; `a_rejection_carries_the_apps_own_terminal_reason` proves a decline arrives as terminal; `a_closed_mailbox_parks_writes_instead_of_stranding_them` proves cancelling parks rather than fails; `the_capability_reports_the_key_it_was_registered_for` pins the registry key. `crates/nmp/tests/app_supplied_signer.rs` carries four engine-level proofs including the headline `an_app_supplied_signer_signs_through_its_mailbox` (a key NMP holds no secret for produces a real verified signature) and `a_mailbox_the_app_never_reads_does_not_wedge_the_engine`, which bounds the never-drained case with a real timeout. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs` carries six FFI-tier proofs including `ffi_an_app_supplied_signer_signs_through_its_mailbox`, `ffi_a_malformed_answer_does_not_spend_the_request` (a correctable answer does not burn the request) and `ffi_a_request_settles_exactly_once`. Ten governed scenarios land in `features/identity/app-supplied-signer.feature` (`IDENTITY-APP-SIGNER-001`…`010`), all `nmp:status=built`. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (15 green — the direct/FFI oracle, run because this change adds FFI record/enum/object shapes), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` (all targets green, including the byte-stable `auth.rs` door tests, run because this is a signer-registration door change), `cargo test -p nmp --test app_supplied_signer` and `--lib signer_mailbox`, `swift build` (Build complete), `./gradlew compileKotlin` under JDK 17 (BUILD SUCCESSFUL), `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` (OK), and the behavior-traceability `check` against explicit base and head trees (exit 0). `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` was re-run; the resulting diff is purely additive with zero deletions.
- **Superseded path removed:** none, and deliberately so. This change introduces no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper and no second spelling of anything, because there was no FFI signer door to replace — the surface it adds to was empty. `Engine::add_signer<Sig>` is NOT deleted: an in-process Rust trait implementation is a different door with real callers (`nmp-bdd`'s entire signer world, `crates/nmp/tests/signer_surface.rs`'s consumer proof, and `add_account`'s own internal path), and collapsing every foreign capability onto the pull boundary is the whole subject of #783 rather than of this unit. Within this change's own vocabulary the no-compatibility rule is followed exactly: `close` and `signed` were replaced by `cancel` and `event` in the same commit that introduced them, with no aliased spelling left behind.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1238 is the implementation authority and PR #1275 is the exact review record; issue #783's required contract ("registration installs one engine-owned, bounded pull mailbox … NMP must not invoke app code") is the higher-level why that decided the pull shape over a `callback_interface` mirroring `add_auth_policy`, and #1169's removal of NIP-46 is what left the external-signer path empty. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-08-05 — Make a write parked on a signer nobody has removable ([issue #1269](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1269), [PR #1279](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1279))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1269 records that `EngineCore::remove_publish_queue_entry` refused on `self.pending.contains_key(&id)` — every accepted, non-terminal obligation — and a write parked on a missing signer is in that map forever by design (`on_signer_unavailable` leaves the row: the obligation is still owned and a signer may still arrive). So the door refused, as `StillActive`, precisely the entry #1039 said removal exists for: *"a write parked on a missing signer, and a permanently-failed entry, end **ONLY** by the app removing them, so removal is a termination path rather than housekeeping."* `SigningState::AwaitingSigner`'s own docstring makes the same promise — *"No clock ever ends this … the app removing the entry is the only other exit."* Both were false for the case they name: nothing ended a signer-parked write, not a clock (by design), not a signer (that is the premise), and not removal. Observed against a real `EngineThread` while fixing #1261: `a signer-parked entry is removable: StillActive { receipt_id: ReceiptId(2) }`. #1270 made the parked entry findable; this makes it removable.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::NotSentReason` gains a third variant, `Removed`: the app removed the queue entry while nothing was moving the write. It is additive — `Cancelled` and `Superseded` keep their exact shapes and meanings. The two differ in what SURVIVES, which is the whole difference between the cancellation and removal doors: a cancelled receipt is retained and stays reattachable, a removed one no longer exists, so an app told `Cancelled` for a removal would go looking for a receipt that is not there. `FfiNotSentReason` mirrors it as `Removed`, Swift adds `case removed` to `NotSentReason` and Kotlin adds `Removed`, both mapped in their existing `init(_ ffi:)` / `from(ffi:)` converters. No function signature, record field, or error variant changes anywhere; the only other governed movement is docstrings on `FfiRemoveQueueEntryError::StillActive` and `NmpEngine::remove_publish_queue_entry`, which now state the removability rule in terms of what is in motion rather than the old half-truth "the write still owns open delivery work." A foreign `switch`/`when` over `NotSentReason` gains one arm, which is the intended break. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. `EventStore` gains no door and loses none: releasing a parked obligation runs the existing atomic `EventStore::cancel_write` compensation, and the receipt is then forgotten through the existing `EventStore::remove_publish_queue_entry`, whose `RemoveQueueEntryOutcome::StillOpen` guard is unchanged and remains authoritative for anything the reducer does not hold in memory. Between those two durable steps the receipt is a cancelled one — the honest thing for a crash to leave behind, since the obligation really was released and what remains is terminal and removable, so the app's next attempt finishes the job.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound or ordering changes; `EngineCore::stalled_write_stage` and the stalled-write projection read the same reducer state, and a removed obligation simply leaves `pending` exactly as a cancelled one does.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/publish_queue_removal_parked.rs`'s `a_write_parked_on_a_missing_signer_is_removable_and_one_being_signed_is_not` is the whole proof, written against a real `EngineThread` with one signer holding a request open, one key no signer answers for, and a live query over the parked author. It was run RED on the unfixed tree and failed with `left: Err(StillActive { receipt_id: ReceiptId(1) }) / right: Ok(())`, then RED twice more as mutations against the FINAL test — dropping `sign_request_in_flight` from the refusal fails on *"a signer HAS this request and its answer is already on the way"*, and suppressing the outcome fact fails on *"the receipt stream ends on a fact naming what the app did"* — before passing. `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/publish_queue_doors.rs` gains `a_write_parked_on_a_signer_nobody_has_is_removable_across_the_boundary`, and its existing `removing_an_entry_with_open_delivery_work_is_still_active` (signed, live lane) stays green unchanged. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `every_write_fact_maps_without_terminal_rollup` and `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` both grow the `Removed` case, so a boundary folding it onto `Cancelled` fails the oracle; `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled. `features/writes/parked-writes.feature` carries `WRITES-PARKED-007` as `built` (it was `specified` with `nmp:gap=evidence`, so nothing previously claimed the path worked) and adds `WRITES-PARKED-016` for the refusal that makes the removal safe. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-store -p nmp-parity`, `cargo test -p nmp-bdd` (183 scenarios, 1522 steps), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` all pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** the unconditional `if self.pending.contains_key(&id)` refusal is deleted, replaced by `EngineCore::release_removable_obligation`, which asks what is in motion. No alias, no second removal spelling, and no "force" parameter retained beside the guarded one — a flag saying "remove it anyway" would have put the decision that protects a write about to succeed in the caller's hands, where it cannot be checked.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1269 is the implementation authority and PR #1279 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Name what a group's hosts prove about a subject's listing ([issue #1234](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1234), [PR #1280](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1280))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1234 records that NIP-29 moderation is two-phase — a client-signed 900x request, then the relay's own signed 39000/39001/39002 that every other participant actually reads — and that `Group::add_user`/`remove_user` returned a receipt for phase one only. `WriteStatus::Acked` proves a host took the request event; it never proves the host applied it. NMP modelled the request and nothing in NMP modelled the result, so an app that needed "the grant actually took" had one option: publish, poll, and guess how long to wait. The consumer audit counted **8 hand-rolled poll loops with 4 different backoff policies in one app** (`mosaico/src/fabric/provider/{membership_confirmation,group_management,group_topology}.rs` and three more), several of them REPUBLISHING the same moderation event every round — not because they believed the send failed, but because they had no other lever while waiting. The issue's own counter-argument — that convergence is inherently a timeout heuristic and therefore app territory — no longer needs resolving: after #1246 `HostRecords` already carries both what a host signed and how far its acquisition got, so "every host is `Ready` and none of their 39002s names this pubkey" is a settled fact with no timer in it. What was missing is that NMP never said it, leaving every app to walk `per_host`, check each `availability` and scan `subjects` — and absence during `Acquiring` reads identically to absence at `Ready` unless you check.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `nmp::nip29::Listing` — `Named(Vec<ListedSubject>)` / `Absent` / `Unestablished` — plus `GroupSnapshot::member_listing(subject)` and `admin_listing(subject)`. Two methods rather than one taking a `GroupRecord`, because `GroupRecord::Metadata` is not a list and a door accepting it would have to answer something untrue about it. `Listing::of(named, settled)` is the single statement of the rule. `GroupSnapshot` gains `selected: BTreeSet<GroupRecord>` — the records the observation asked for — threaded from `RelayScope::observe`/`Group::observe` through `records::observe` into the projection. FFI adds `FfiListing` and the free exported functions `member_listing(snapshot, subject)`/`admin_listing(snapshot, subject)`, the same idiom `member_list_includes`/`any_of` already use in that module: `FfiGroupSnapshot` is a plain UniFFI RECORD with no Rust value on the far side to hang a method on, so the boundary supplies the two facts and delegates the DECISION to `Listing::of` rather than restating it. `FfiGroupSnapshot` gains `selected` for the same reason the Rust snapshot does. Swift adds `NMPListing` and `NMPGroupSnapshot.memberListing(_:)`/`adminListing(_:)`; Kotlin adds `NMPListing` and the same two methods. Both retain the raw FFI record privately so the native tiers ask Rust the question instead of re-deriving the answer in two more languages — the exact reconstruction this issue counted eight times. `subject` is a decoded `PublicKey` in Rust and 64-char hex at every boundary; a malformed one is a typed refusal, never a silent no-match that would read to an app as a settled absence.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route or coverage key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. Everything here is computed from a delivered frame's already-projected snapshot.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound or ordering changes. `GroupAvailability` is read, never written, and its ladder is unchanged.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/nip29/records.rs` adds `absence_is_claimed_only_once_every_host_has_settled` (the IDENTICAL empty union answers `Unestablished` under `Acquiring`/`CachedOnly`/`SourceUnavailable` and `Absent` only under `Ready` — the whole point, and the thing no receipt can express), `a_record_the_observation_never_asked_for_is_never_reported_absent`, `inclusion_is_evidence_before_anything_has_settled`, `two_hosts_disagreeing_about_a_role_are_both_reported`, and `a_populated_list_that_omits_the_subject_is_a_settled_absence`. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs` mirrors four of them at the boundary. `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` adds `direct_and_ffi_listings_agree_on_every_settlement_and_selection_case`, exhaustive over all **36** availability x selection x union combinations — the oracle that keeps `Listing::of` the one implementation, so a boundary that grew its own opinion about the negative diverges in exactly one case. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled: this is an FFI shape change and `cargo check` compiles that oracle without ever executing it. Swift and Kotlin each add four mirrored tests. `features/groups/a-receipt-is-not-a-roster-change.feature` carries four governed scenarios, all `built`. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-parity -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `swift build`, `swift test`, the `tools/nip29-consumer-swift` harness build, `./gradlew :test`, the behavior-traceability `check`, and all eleven repo gate scripts pass on this head.
- **Superseded path removed:** nothing is replaced, so nothing is deleted — this names a fact NMP already held and never surfaced. Two things were deliberately NOT built, and both are recorded so they are not re-proposed. There is no new return type on `add_user`/`remove_user`: the two-phase shape is true of EVERY named operation (9007 and 9002 are answered by a 39000, 9000/9001 by a 39001/39002, 9021/9022 by a 39002), so a noun covering two of eleven verbs would mislead about the other nine, and one covering all eleven is `GroupReceipts` under a new name one PR after #1274 deleted it; `WriteStatus::Acked` has always meant "the relay said OK" for every kind, and that the ACK coincides with truth for a kind:9 chat message is a property of that kind rather than of the receipt. What #1234's title still wanted is a doc line, and `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`'s module doc now carries it for all the 900x verbs rather than two. And there is no wait, poll, backoff or deadline anywhere in this change: `Absent` rests on `GroupAvailability::Ready`, a statement about knowledge exhaustion, never about elapsed time.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1234 is the implementation authority and PR #1280 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Correct the signer mailbox's settle state, its cancellation contract, and the reason on record ([issue #1284](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1284), [PR #1286](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1286))

- **Failure evidence:** an adversarial review of the merged #1275 mailbox UPHELD the design and found three defects inside it plus one false statement of record. (1) `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs` declared `settled: bool` and read it inside `Drop` — the literal tell architecture review gate 3 exists to catch, and one the FFI side had already avoided (`crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs` uses `Option::take` and cites the gate by name). Its own doc comment was the confession: *"Cleared by `settle`, so `Drop` can tell an answered request from an abandoned one without a separate bool"* — `settle` SET it to `true`, and the field WAS the separate bool. (2) The Swift and Kotlin drains did not bridge cancellation, and on Swift the consequence is worse than a hang: UniFFI 0.29's generated Swift parks on `withUnsafeContinuation` (`uniffi_bindgen-0.29.5/src/bindings/swift/templates/Async.swift`), which task cancellation does not resume, so the Rust future is never freed and keeps `AsyncFifoReceiver`'s single-reader claim forever — **every later `next()` on that mailbox is `ConcurrentNext`, so the app has silently stopped being able to sign**. 29er-next drains ~14 NMP pull handles in a `withTaskGroup` under `.task(id: model.engineGeneration)`; bumping the generation hits exactly this. Kotlin's generated bindings use `suspendCancellableCoroutine` and DO free the future, so Kotlin recovers — but nothing said so and nothing tested it. (3) `NmpSignerMailbox::next` mapped `Err(_) => FfiError::ConcurrentNext` by wildcard; correct today because `nmp::ConcurrentNext` has one variant, silently wrong the day it has two. (4) Five places justified the pull shape with human latency — that a capability NMP calls into would have to hold NMP for as long as a person takes to approve something. That is checkably false: `crates/nmp/src/auth.rs` already states *"`evaluate` must return a ready-or-pending operation without blocking … Prompt UX remains app-owned — resolve the pending sender whenever the user answers, from any thread"*, and `SigningCapability::sign` has the identical ready-or-pending shape. A false reason invites a future change to "fix" the mailbox back into a callback once someone notices the stated reason does not hold.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
