- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `SignatureRequest::completion` becomes `Option<PendingSignerSender<_>>` and `settled: bool` is DELETED in the same change; `settle` and `Drop` both go through one private `spend` that takes the option, so holding the completion IS "not yet answered" and no flag can disagree with a live sender. A new non-destructive wake lands on three tiers: `AsyncFifoReceiver::unpark`, `SignerMailbox::unpark`, `NmpSignerMailbox::unpark`, plus `NMPSignerMailbox.unpark()` in Swift and Kotlin. It ends exactly one `next()` and changes no channel state — the backlog, the producer, the registration and the capability all survive — which is what a departing drain needs and what `cancel` cannot give, because `cancel` closes the mailbox and parks every later write for that key. It is a one-shot ARM rather than a waker poke, deliberately: Swift runs a cancellation handler before the operation body when the task was already cancelled on the way in, which is the ordinary case for a `for try await` loop torn down between two requests, and a wake that only reached an already-parked reader would hang exactly that ordering. `poll_recv` consumes the arm before the backlog, so a request that raced the unpark stays queued for the next drainer instead of being handed to a consumer that is leaving. `FifoReceiver::end_park` (run by the reading guard when a `next()` future is dropped) clears the stale waker, so a cancelled Kotlin collection cannot leave a dead waker for its replacement. Swift's `next()` now wraps `withTaskCancellationHandler { … } onCancel: { ffi.unpark() }` — deliberately NOT the `onCancel: { self.cancel() }` that `PullDriver.swift` uses, which would destroy the app's signer on an ordinary task cancellation. Kotlin's `requests()` deliberately gains NO `finally` clause: the generated bindings already free the parked future, and both a `finally { cancel() }` and a `finally { unpark() }` would be wrong (the first closes the signer; the second arms an end-of-stream for a collection that has already gone, which the NEXT collection would eat). `cancel` on every tier is now documented as the destructive verb it is. The wildcard FFI arm becomes `Err(nmp::ConcurrentNext)`, so a second variant fails to compile here rather than being mislabelled. `nmp-facade.txt` is 29064 lines, under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. `unpark` touches one in-memory channel's waker slot and a one-shot flag; a mailbox registration was never persisted and still is not.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes. An unparked mailbox is still a registered capability and still appears in `AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot::signer_bound` exactly as before, which is the point — unparking is invisible to everything except the one await it ends.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/runtime/fifo_channel.rs` adds `unpark_ends_one_next_and_leaves_the_channel_usable`, `unpark_before_the_park_still_ends_that_await`, `unpark_retains_a_value_that_raced_it`, and `abandoning_a_parked_next_leaves_no_waker_behind`. `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs` adds `a_settled_request_that_is_then_dropped_answers_and_releases_once` (settle-then-drop delivers one answer and releases one slot; it refills to the bound afterwards, so a double release shows up as an unusable bound rather than an off-by-one — red if a future edit reintroduces a flag and forgets to set it), `unparking_ends_the_await_and_leaves_the_signer_working`, `unparking_before_the_await_ends_that_await_and_only_it`, and `a_request_that_races_an_unpark_is_retained`. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs` adds `ffi_unparking_a_drain_frees_the_mailbox_without_closing_it`, which proves the released reader claim AND the surviving signature at the tier the SDKs call. New `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/SignerMailboxTests.swift` (7 tests) and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/SignerMailboxTest.kt` (7 tests) are the first SDK-tier tests this door has ever had; #1275 shipped both wrappers on compile-only evidence, which is why the cancellation gap survived review. Both were RUN RED before being made green: with the bare `await` restored, `swift test --filter testCancellingADrainEndsItAndLeavesTheSignerWorking` does not fail, it **hangs unbreakably** — killed at 420s, because even the test's own `withThrowingTaskGroup` timeout cannot reap a child stuck in an uncancellable UniFFI continuation, which is the defect stated precisely; with `finally { cancel() }` added to Kotlin's `requests()`, `cancellingACollectionEndsItAndLeavesTheSignerWorking` fails on "the mailbox outlived the collector, so it still delivers". Two governed scenarios land in `features/identity/app-supplied-signer.feature` (`IDENTITY-APP-SIGNER-011`, `IDENTITY-APP-SIGNER-012`), both `nmp:status=built`, each carrying Rust, FFI, Swift and Kotlin evidence. Gates RUN on this head: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN, not compiled — this adds an FFI method), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` (RUN — this is a signer-door change; all targets green including the byte-stable `auth.rs` door tests), `cargo test -p nmp` (all green), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` (OK), `swift build` (Build complete) and `swift test --filter SignerMailboxTests` (7/7), the Gradle Kotlin main and test compile tasks plus the whole Kotlin test suite under JDK 17 (BUILD SUCCESSFUL), and `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` re-run after merging master.
- **Superseded path removed:** `SignatureRequest::settled` is DELETED, not deprecated — there is no second way to ask whether a request was answered. The human-latency justification is deleted from all five places that carried it rather than softened: `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs`, `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/SignerMailbox.swift`, `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/SignerMailbox.kt`, and the narrative of `features/identity/app-supplied-signer.feature` (the fifth site, which the review had not found). Each now states the three real reasons: #783 mandates the inversion and names `callback.evaluate` FIRST and `capability.sign` second, so the AUTH callback is the violation to remove and not the precedent to copy; `crates/nmp/src/runtime/auth.rs` invokes `policy.lock()…evaluate(request)` — foreign code called while holding the capability mutex, on a task of the shared runtime, which is the harm #783's consequence paragraph describes and is live on master; and making "NMP calls you, ready-or-pending" safe across THIS boundary cost 752 lines and a five-state `Evaluating/Pending/Resolving/Completed/Cancelled` Condvar linearization in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/auth.rs`, which the mailbox needs none of. `docs/known-gaps.md` already said only "because NMP must not invoke app code (#783)" and is untouched. This entry does not edit #1275's entry above, which is immutable; the correction is this entry.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1284 is the implementation authority and PR #1286 is the exact review record; #783's required contract and #1238's door are the higher-level why. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Give NIP-29's own kind:9002 rows a spelling ([issue #1282](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1282), [PR #1287](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1287))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1282 records that `crates/nmp-nip29/src/operations.rs`'s own module doc claims "Owning it here is what lets an app write `group.remove_user(pubkey)` instead of looking up kind 9001 and hand-assembling a `p` tag itself." That held for 9000/9001/9005/9007/9008/9009/9021/9022 and NOT for 9002. NIP-29's moderation table says kind:9002 `edit-metadata` takes "all the fields of *group-metadata*", and kind:39000 defines `name`, `picture`, `banner` and `about` as its value rows plus `private`, `restricted`, `hidden`, `closed` and `livekit` as markers. `edit_metadata(name: Option<&str>, about: Option<&str>)` composed the first two rows and had no spelling for any of the rest — including `closed`, which is the row that decides whether non-members may join, and is therefore the difference between a workspace and an open room. So an app that wanted a closed group wrote `["closed"]` itself, and once it is hand-writing one 9002 row it is hand-assembling a whole 9002: three hand-built builders survive in mosaico's `lifecycle.rs` for exactly this reason, and the whole of the surviving lock-a-workspace-closed builder is `nmp_nip29::edit_metadata(Some(channel), None)` plus hand-written `["closed"]`, `["public"]` and `picture` rows — every one of them NIP-29's own except the picture URL.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_nip29::edit_metadata` now takes one `GroupMetadataEdit` — `name`, `about`, `picture`, `read_access` and `join_access`, every field an `Option` so an omitted one emits no row and is left untouched rather than cleared, with `Default` for the empty edit. `ReadAccess { Public, Private }` and `JoinAccess { Open, Closed }` are two-valued enums rather than `bool`s BECAUSE of that rule: `Some(ReadAccess::Public)` means "make it readable by anyone" and `None` means "do not decide", and one `bool` cannot say both — a `bool` defaulting to `false` would make an edit that merely renames a group also silently reopen it. They are named for what NIP-29 itself says each marker controls (*"only members can _read_ group messages"*, *"join requests are ignored"*) and their four variants are the four literal tag names, so no category the protocol lacks is invented. One divergence is stated rather than hidden: NIP-29 spells only the restricted state on the kind:39000 RECORD, where omission IS the opposite; on a kind:9002 EDIT omission must mean "untouched", so the clearing spellings `public` and `open` come from the reference relay's own 9002 parser (relay29's `moderation_actions.go` reads `public` to clear its private flag and `open` to clear its closed flag) rather than from NIP-29's prose. `nmp::nip29::Group::edit_metadata(engine, author, edit)` mirrors it, as do `FfiGroup::edit_metadata` with the new `FfiGroupMetadataEdit` record and `FfiReadAccess`/`FfiJoinAccess` enums, Swift's `NMPGroup.editMetadata(engine:authorPubkeyHex:edit:)` with `NMPGroupMetadataEdit`/`NMPReadAccess`/`NMPJoinAccess`, and Kotlin's equivalents. `FfiGroupMetadataEdit` is a uniffi Record rather than an opaque Object, unlike `FfiGroup`: there is nothing retained in it and nothing to keep out of a caller's reach — it is an argument, and every field is meant to be spelled by the app. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added. `nmp-facade.txt` is 29067 lines, under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A 9002 is an ordinary group write through the one publish door, exactly as it was.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/operations.rs` adds `edit_metadata_composes_the_picture_row_and_both_marker_rows` (the whole of what that surviving hand-built 9002 said, composed through the door instead), `each_marker_axis_composes_the_exact_tag_nip29_spells_for_that_state` (all four read/join combinations, each composing exactly its own two markers — so a `public` group that is nevertheless `closed`, which is what a published workspace is, is provably expressible and the two axes provably independent), and `an_unstated_marker_emits_no_row_and_therefore_clears_nothing` (renaming a group must not restate — and therefore must not silently reset — who may read it). `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs` adds `the_metadata_edit_door_composes_the_picture_and_marker_rows`. `features/groups/nip29-operation-schemas.feature` carries three new governed scenarios, `NIP29OPERATIONS-012/013/014`, all `built`. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this adds an FFI record and two FFI enums), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi`, `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-nip29 -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-kind-blindness.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** the two-`Option<&str>` `edit_metadata(name, about)` is DELETED in the same change on every surface — no alias, no overload, no deprecation. Every caller (`nmp::nip29::Group::edit_metadata`, `FfiGroup::edit_metadata`, `NMPGroup.editMetadata`, the Kotlin equivalent, `crates/nmp-bdd`'s step, and the Swift/Kotlin tests) moves to the struct in this PR. Deliberately NOT composed, and each for a stated reason rather than an oversight: `restricted` and `hidden` have no clearing spelling anywhere — neither NIP-29 nor the reference relay's 9002 parser defines the row that would turn either back off — so composing the setting half alone would ship a door that can only ever tighten a group and never loosen it; `supported_kinds` is a list whose edit semantics (replace? merge?) NIP-29 does not pin down, which is the same reason kind:9010 `update-pin-list` has been left uncomposed since #989; and `banner` and `livekit` are not read by the reference relay's 9002 handler at all, so composing them would emit rows that take no effect while reading as though they had. Each is a one-line addition the day a consumer needs it and a falsifier exists for what a relay does with it. The `["parent", <group-id>]` row #1282 mentions in passing is likewise absent: nostr-protocol/nips#2319 is a proposed extension rather than NIP-29 proper, and composing a row from an unmerged proposal is exactly the invented category the conventions forbid.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1282 is the implementation authority and PR #1287 is the exact review record; it was filed from mosaico's adoption of the #1274 group door (mosaico#769), alongside #1281 and #1283 which landed separately as PR #1285 because they are one door at two arities and share no type with this one. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Delete the app-supplied signer mailbox: NMP owns when to sign and what ([issue #1290](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1290), [PR #1293](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1293))

- **Failure evidence:** the maintainer's ruling on the merged #1275/#1286 mailbox: *"instead of adding a way for the swift side to provide the key material to nmp you added a way for swift to become a signer. If there was a hardware signer then swift would expose some kind of adapter but nmp still owns saying when to sign and what, and swift just interfaces with the hardware."* `add_signer_mailbox` handed out the thing NMP owns — the app drained a queue of `SignatureRequest`s and decided, on its own executor, whether and when each one was answered. The issue it was built on (#1238) stated the need as *"a Swift or Kotlin app could register no signer at all"* and cited 29er-next shipping a plaintext nsec as the consequence. Both halves are checkably false, and both were verified on this head before deleting: `FfiEngine::add_account` has crossed FFI all along (`crates/nmp-ffi/src/facade.rs:281`, accepting hex or bech32 nsec) and long predates #1238; and **29er-next already calls it** — `ios/Sources/AppModelIdentity.swift:25` and `:45`, `engine.addAccount(secretKey:)` — while it has never called the mailbox. "Can register no signer at all" was only ever true of the app-*does*-the-signing trait door, never of supplying key material, so the second door closed no gap that existed. The door also has **no consumers**: a workspace-wide and 29er-next-wide search found `add_signer_mailbox`'s only callers to be its own SDK wrappers and its own tests, so per `docs/internals/conventions/no-backwards-compatibility.md` there is no stranger to pay a compatibility tax to.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `Engine::add_signer_mailbox` is DELETED along with the whole `crates/nmp/src/signer_mailbox.rs` module — `SignerMailbox`, `SignatureRequest`, `SignatureSettleError` and `MailboxSigner` are gone from the `nmp` facade's public surface. `nmp-ffi` loses its entire `signer` module (`NmpSignerMailbox`, `FfiSignatureRequest` and the settle/cancel/unpark surface) plus `NmpEngine::add_signer_mailbox` and `remove_signer_mailbox`. Swift loses `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/SignerMailbox.swift` (`NMPSignerMailbox`, `NMPSignatureRequest`, `NMPEngine.addSigner(publicKey:)`/`removeSigner(_:)`) and the `NMPSignedEvent` public memberwise initializer, which existed for the single purpose of letting an app construct an answer to a mailbox request and has no other caller; its internal `init(_ ffi:)` is untouched. Kotlin loses `SignerMailbox.kt` and the same two extension doors. **`Engine::add_signer` — the `SigningCapability` trait door — is untouched**, and is the shape the ruling describes: a Rust adapter interfaces with the hardware while NMP still says when to sign and what. It keeps its real in-tree callers (`crates/nmp-bdd/src/world/{actions,signers,staging}.rs`, `crates/nmp-consumer-check/src/lib.rs:441`, and `crates/nmp-ffi/src/facade.rs:1842`'s `PendingFfiSigner`). **`Engine::add_account`/`FfiEngine::add_account` are untouched** and remain the correct door for key material. `docs/known-gaps.md` now records the honest residue rather than the mailbox's claim: there is currently **no FFI-crossing door for a signing capability the app itself implements**, so a Secure Enclave key reachable only from Swift or Kotlin has no way in, and whatever closes that must keep NMP the owner of when to sign and what. `nmp-facade.txt` falls from 29067 to **29006** lines, far under the 30000 cap; the two snapshots move by **732 deletions and zero insertions**.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A mailbox registration was never persisted, so removing it retires no durable state and needs no migration; an app that had registered one held it only in memory.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes. A mailbox registered through this door appeared in `AuthDiagnosticsSnapshot::signer_bound` only as an ordinary registered capability, exactly as one registered through `add_signer` still does, so no counter loses a contributor.
- **Updated falsifiers:** this change removes falsifiers rather than adding any, which is the correct direction for a deletion: the proofs that went with the door proved the door. They are enumerated under "Superseded path removed" below and are deliberately NOT named in this field, because gate 6 is right that a falsifier named here is a claim it exists in the tree this PR produces — and none of them do. The one falsifier this entry does claim is `abandoning_a_parked_next_leaves_no_waker_behind` in `crates/nmp/src/runtime/fifo_channel.rs`, which is KEPT and still green: it proves the reading guard clears the waker a dropped poll future registered, which is the general property every consumer of `AsyncFifoReceiver` depends on and which no longer has anything to do with the mailbox. The deleted `features/identity/app-supplied-signer.feature` carried no BDD step definitions — it was spec-only, linked outward by evidence annotations to the Rust tests deleted alongside it — so no step implementation is orphaned and the behavior-traceability map stays closed. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (**RUN** — this removes FFI object and record shapes, and a plain compile check builds that direct/FFI oracle without ever executing it; 14 + 2 green), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` (all targets green, including the byte-stable auth door tests, run because this is a signer-registration door change), `cargo test -p nmp` (27 targets green), `cargo test -p nmp-bdd` (183 scenarios / 1522 steps passed), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` (OK), `swift build` (Build complete) and `swift test` (175 tests, 0 failures), `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17, and `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` re-run against current master.
- **Superseded path removed:** the deleted proofs, recorded here rather than in the falsifier field because they no longer exist: `crates/nmp/tests/app_supplied_signer.rs` (4 engine-tier proofs), the `signer_mailbox` unit tests (10, including `an_undrained_mailbox_refuses_past_its_bound_and_still_works_afterwards`), `crates/nmp-ffi/src/signer.rs`'s FFI-tier proofs (7), `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/SignerMailboxTests.swift` (7), `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/SignerMailboxTest.kt` (7), and the 12 governed scenarios `IDENTITY-APP-SIGNER-001`…`012`. The whole door is DELETED in the same change — no alias, no deprecation window, no forwarding wrapper, and deliberately **no smaller replacement**, because `add_account` already served the need the door was justified by. The one judgement call is recorded rather than buried: #1284 added *two* separable things to `crates/nmp/src/runtime/fifo_channel.rs`, and only one is mailbox machinery. **`unpark` is deleted** — the `unparked` queue flag, its `poll_recv` arm-check, `FifoReceiver::unpark`, `AsyncFifoReceiver::unpark`, the module-doc paragraph, and the three tests `unpark_ends_one_next_and_leaves_the_channel_usable`, `unpark_before_the_park_still_ends_that_await` and `unpark_retains_a_value_that_raced_it` — because its only caller in the tree was `SignerMailbox::unpark`, so it existed solely to let a departing foreign drain end one `next()` without closing the app's signer. **`FifoReceiver::end_park` is KEPT**, with its falsifier `abandoning_a_parked_next_leaves_no_waker_behind`: it clears the waker a dropped `next()` future registered, which is a general correctness property of `AsyncFifoReceiver` for every consumer of it — the row/query pull surfaces (`crates/nmp/src/subscription.rs:462,481,578`), NIP-02 follow-action status, and write-fact streams all park on that one waker slot — so deleting it would reintroduce a stale-waker defect on surfaces the mailbox never touched. `AsyncFifoReceiver` itself predates the mailbox (#680) and is untouched. The corrective #1286 entry above is immutable and is NOT edited: the log is append-only, and this entry supersedes it by recording the deletion.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1290 is the implementation authority and PR #1293 is the exact review record; the maintainer's ruling quoted in the failure evidence is the originating decision, and `docs/internals/conventions/no-backwards-compatibility.md` is the convention that makes it a deletion rather than a deprecation. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Delete `nip29::Listing`; the snapshot's arrays already answer the question ([issue #1289](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1289), [PR #1295](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1295))

- **Failure evidence:** none — this is a deletion, and the evidence is a maintainer ruling rather than a defect. `nmp::nip29::Listing` shipped one day earlier in #1280 (issue #1234) and was ruled YAGNI on review: *"the app should know whether a member list is already received or not and decide itself; not create a horrible stupid abstraction. The app says it wants the member list; when the member list comes back with an array then that's the list, if it's empty it's empty. ALL YAGNI."* `GroupSnapshot` already exposes `members` and `admins` as attributed `ListedSubject` unions, and `availability` already states whole-scope settlement. An app reads the array and decides. A public enum that folds those two facts into a three-way verdict is a second owner of a question the arrays already answer, and it charged a whole extra snapshot field, an FFI mirror, two more language projections and a 36-case oracle for the privilege. Deleting it one PR later is cheaper than letting it harden — the cost curve `docs/internals/conventions/naming-no-invented-categories.md` records for "foreign kinds", which reached 13 sites and a CI gate before removal.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Subtraction only, at every tier. Rust DELETES `nmp::nip29::Listing` (`Named`/`Absent`/`Unestablished`), `Listing::of`, `GroupSnapshot::member_listing`, `GroupSnapshot::admin_listing`, the private `GroupSnapshot::listing` helper, and the `Listing` reexport from `crates/nmp/src/nip29/mod.rs`. `GroupSnapshot::selected` is DELETED with it, and this was checked rather than assumed: its only two readers in the whole tree were `GroupSnapshot::listing` and the FFI's own `listing_over`, both of which are gone, so the field's entire reason for existing — letting `Absent` distinguish "every host settled and published no list" from "no list was ever requested" — went with the variant. The threading that fed it goes too: the `records` argument `RelayScope::observe`/`Group::observe` passed to `records::observe`, the private `GroupObservation::selected` field, and the `selected` parameter of `project`. FFI deletes `FfiListing`, the two free `#[uniffi::export]` functions `member_listing`/`admin_listing`, the private `listing_over`, and `FfiGroupSnapshot::selected`. Swift deletes `NMPListing` and `NMPGroupSnapshot.memberListing(_:)`/`adminListing(_:)`; Kotlin deletes `NMPListing` and the same two methods. Both native tiers also drop the `selected` projection and the privately retained raw FFI record they held ONLY so those two methods could hand it back to Rust. `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`'s module-doc block that routed apps to the two methods is deleted rather than reworded. Nothing is added anywhere: no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper, and deliberately no smaller helper, no `settled` convenience accessor and no doc note explaining how to combine settlement with the array — a make-up-for-it addition is the instinct being corrected, not a compromise. `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` loses 24 lines and `docs/surface/components/nmp-core/uniffi.txt` loses 221; the regenerated diff is 245 deletions and zero insertions. On this head the facade stands at 28982 lines and the UniFFI projection at 15862, both after merging the signer-mailbox deletion (#1290/#1293) that landed while this PR was open; the facade is under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. `Listing` was a pure projection computed per call over a delivered snapshot and was never persisted, and `selected` was carried on an in-memory observation rather than stored.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics field, section, bound or ordering changes. Nothing in the diagnostics surface ever read `Listing` or `selected`.
- **Updated falsifiers:** this PR REMOVES falsifiers rather than adding any, because it removes the mechanism they guarded; naming a new one would be dishonest. What must keep proving itself is that the deletion touched only `Listing`. The reactive roster reader's own falsifiers are untouched and were RUN green on this head: `crates/nmp/src/nip29/mod.rs` keeps `only_the_selected_records_reach_the_wire`, and `crates/nmp/tests/group_publication_door.rs` keeps `a_group_records_listing_never_lets_one_hosts_member_evidence_answer_for_anothers_group`, so the per-host pinning and record-selection behaviour that `selected` was threaded alongside is still proven by tests that never read the deleted field. `GroupAvailability`, `GroupSnapshot`, `HostRecords` and `ListedSubject` — the #1246 core reader and the settlement machinery that predates #1280 — are untouched and keep their existing suites in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/records.rs` and `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs`. Gates RUN on this head: `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-parity -p nmp-bdd` (exit 0, 48 suites green), re-run after merging master. `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN and not merely compiled — this is an FFI shape change and `cargo check` compiles `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` without ever executing it. `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all --check` (exit 0), `swift build` (Build complete) and `swift test` (178 tests, 4 skipped, 0 failures) after a `--macos-only` xcframework build, `scripts/build-kotlin-jvm.sh` then the Gradle Kotlin main and test compile tasks under JDK 17 (BUILD SUCCESSFUL), and `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-kind-blindness.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-read-door.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh` and `scripts/check-dependency-direction.sh` (all exit 0, all UNMODIFIED — every one was grepped for the deleted spellings first and none pinned them, so no gate needed editing). `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` was re-run AFTER merging `origin/master` twice: at 523e3c048, which carried #1282 into the same NIP-29 files, and again at 0516271b9, which carried the signer-mailbox deletion and its own snapshot regeneration; the projection set was computed on this exact head with `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` rather than copied.
- **Superseded path removed:** everything named above is deleted in this change, with nothing retained behind it — that is the whole content of the PR. Four governed scenarios go with the mechanism: features/groups/a-receipt-is-not-a-roster-change.feature is deleted entire (PROTOCOL-RECEIPTISNOTAROSTERCHANGE-001 through -004), because every one of them asserted a property of the deleted enum and a scenario cannot outlive the behaviour it governs. The exhaustive direct/FFI listing oracle #1280 added to `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` is deleted, as are five Rust unit tests in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/records.rs`, four boundary tests in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs`, and four mirrored tests in each of the Swift and Kotlin suites. This entry does not edit #1280's entry above, which is immutable; the reversal is this entry. What is explicitly NOT removed, and was verified rather than assumed: `GroupAvailability` and the settlement machinery it rests on predate #1280 and serve the reactive roster reader generally, and the #1246 core reader — `GroupSnapshot`, `ListedSubject`, `HostRecords`, and the union/role logic — is not in question.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1289 is the implementation authority and PR #1295 is the exact review record; the maintainer's YAGNI ruling quoted under failure evidence is the originating decision, and `docs/internals/conventions/no-backwards-compatibility.md` is why it lands as one deletion with no compatibility path. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Delete the NIP-29 mint-without-publish doors; `Group::publish` is the group's only write door ([issue #1292](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1292), [PR #1296](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1296))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1292 records four public doors on four surfaces serving a consumer that does not exist, one of which invites the app architecture the maintainer ruled against. Every caller of `Group::intent`/`Group::signed_intent` inside this repo was `publish`'s own body, an SDK mirror of the same door, or a test of the door itself. The consumer #1274 built them for never adopted them: `mosaico/src/nmp_host/write/compose.rs:98-107` still carries the comment citing #1242 verbatim — *"Routing and identity are assembled by the caller only because `nmp::nip29::Group` exposes no mint-without-publish door"* — and still hand-assembles through `nmp_nip29::contextualize` plus its own `group_intent(relay, builder)`; its fan-out mints ONE intent per relay and publishes them immediately inside `enqueue_background_intents`, a shape one `Explicit`-over-the-whole-scope intent structurally cannot produce, so the door would not have served it even had mosaico tried. Holding the minted value buys nothing either: `nmp_grammar::WriteIntent` has NO derives at all — no `Serialize`, no `Clone`, not even `Debug` — so it cannot be persisted across a restart, cloned for batching, or inspected, and "mint now, submit later" is not a thing a value with those properties supports. The one honest use, stamping `WriteIntent::correlation` (#1244), has zero users: every intent mosaico constructs is `correlation: None` (`write.rs:164`, `compose.rs:135`, `background_submit.rs:32`). And the deferred submit stage that justified the door had already been deleted when mosaico adopted NMP's PublishQueue (mosaico `e79ee9c6`, PRs #764/#765) — before the door was built. Separately, the maintainer ruled that apps must not sign their own bytes and keep app-local optimistic mirrors, which condemns `publish_signed` on its own terms.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust DELETES `Group::intent(author, builder)`, `Group::signed_intent(event)` and `Group::publish_signed(engine, event)`. `Group::publish` absorbs the contextualize-and-hand-over body and is the group's only write door; it keeps `Engine::publish_tracked` and keeps returning the ordinary `ReceiptStream` with the store-issued `ReceiptId`, so #1244's inline half is untouched. The private helper `through_the_one_door` is INLINED into `publish` and its name deleted — its whole body was one `publish_tracked` call, it had two callers, and the name is exactly the repo jargon `docs/internals/conventions/naming-no-invented-categories.md` forbids. The private `mint` seam survives because `publish` still needs one place that decides `WriteRouting::Explicit` over every host; it is not a surface. FFI deletes `FfiGroup::intent`, `FfiGroup::signed_intent` and `FfiGroup::publish_signed`, and `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `write_intent_to_ffi`, which #1274 added and nothing else used. Swift deletes `NMPGroup.intent` (both the field-by-field and the composed-payload arities), `NMPGroup.signedIntent` and `NMPGroup.publishSigned`; Kotlin deletes the exact mirrors. `Group::validate_context` and its three surface mirrors are KEPT, deliberately: it is a read-only predicate rather than a write door, `signed_intent` never called it (that called the free `nmp_nip29::validate_context`), and it is the only remaining reachable construction site for `GroupContextError::MissingContext`, `MismatchedContext` and `AmbiguousContext` — deleting it would strand three FFI-crossing error variants with no reachable constructor, which is a larger deletion than this ruling authorized. The legitimate need a mint door was reached for is already served by `Engine::sign_event`, whose own doc says it "is intentionally orthogonal to `Self::publish`: it creates no write intent, pending row, receipt, delivery lane, relay plan, or publication"; it crosses FFI at `crates/nmp-ffi/src/facade.rs` and Swift already calls it "the engine sign-only path" in `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/Blossom.swift`. Two things it does not do are named rather than built: it signs as the ACTIVE ACCOUNT rather than an explicit author, and it knows nothing about group context, so nothing appends or validates an `h` row for it and no door takes the resulting signed event back into a group — the second is the deliberate consequence of the ruling, not an accidental gap. `nmp-facade.txt` is 28979 lines, three fewer than master's 28982 and far under the 30000 cap; `docs/surface/components/nmp-core/uniffi.txt` loses 251 lines.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A group write was and remains an ordinary tracked write through the one publish door; only the number of doors that reach it changed.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound or ordering changes.
- **Updated falsifiers:** three proofs keep their property and move their observation point back to the private `mint` seam they used before #1274, because no public door returns an intent to read one from: `a_group_write_routes_explicitly_to_every_host_in_the_scope` (still required by name in `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`), `a_single_host_scope_still_routes_explicitly_to_that_one_host` and `an_unsigned_group_write_freezes_the_exact_author`, all in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`. `a_caller_supplied_context_never_reaches_the_door` already proved the refusal through `publish` and is unchanged. the receipt-id proof is RENAMED `a_group_write_hands_back_the_store_issued_receipt_id`, because its old spelling said "inline door" and that named a distinction which no longer exists; it still matches the returned id against a real `publish_queue()` entry. `every_named_operation_takes_the_same_path` and `every_group_operation_reaches_the_one_publish_door` are unchanged. The governed scenario for #1244's inline half is rebuilt as `features/groups/a-group-write-carries-the-receipt-id.feature`, carrying Rust, Swift (`testAGroupWriteCarriesTheStoreIssuedReceiptID`) and Kotlin (`groupWriteFactStreamDeliversOrdinaryWriteFacts`) evidence, so that coverage is not lost with the file it used to live in. The gate mechanism is the removal itself: `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh` no longer excludes the deleted names, so any surface that reintroduces one fails its exhaustive catalogue check as an undeclared tenth NIP-29 operation. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this is an FFI shape change and `cargo check` compiles that oracle without executing it), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-kind-blindness.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-read-door.sh`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-routing-vocabulary.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-dependency-direction.sh`, `scripts/check-bdd-file-length.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check`, `swift build` and `swift test`, and `./gradlew compileKotlin compileTestKotlin` plus `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** four doors are DELETED with no alias, no deprecation and no forwarding wrapper, on every surface in the same change: the two mint-without-publish doors, the pre-signed publish door, and the private `through_the_one_door` name. Their proofs go with them rather than being retargeted at something that no longer exists — six in `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs`, two in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs`, four pre-signed wire tests in `crates/nmp/tests/group_publication_door.rs`, one Swift test and one Kotlin test. Two governed feature files go the same way: `features/groups/pre-signed-publication.feature` in full (seven scenarios), and `features/groups/the-write-door-mints-an-intent.feature`, whose three mint scenarios died with the doors and whose one surviving scenario was rebuilt under a name that describes what is left. The BDD steps and world fixtures serving only those files are deleted with them, computed by diffing the orphaned-step set against `origin/master` rather than by eye: `crates/nmp-bdd/src/steps/then/groups/presigned.rs` in full (its one still-used step moved to `refusals.rs`), six steps in `refusals.rs`, one in `route.rs`, seven in `given/mod.rs`, two in `when/groups.rs`, the pre-signed half of `world/group_fixtures.rs`, `observe_exact_id`, and the two `staged_signed` world fields. Two clauses that REQUIRED deleted symbols are removed from their gates, which is the correct edit rather than a workaround: `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` no longer requires `pub fn publish_signed(` by name (it still requires `pub struct Group {`, `pub fn publish(` and `publish_tracked(intent)`, and still bans `engine.publish(` in the group door), and `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh` drops `publish_signed` from its infrastructure lists and the three deleted names from its exclusion lists. Two `crates/nmp/tests/optimistic_publish.rs` proofs used the deleted pre-signed door only because #1182 needs an event id known BEFORE publication; they now build the same already-signed intent through `Engine::publish` with an `Explicit` route over the group's own hosts, the shape that file's own general proof already used, and their claim is unchanged. Nothing was added anywhere to compensate: the diff is negative on every surface.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1292 is the implementation authority and PR #1296 is the exact review record; the maintainer's ruling to remove rather than keep these doors is the higher-level why, and `docs/internals/conventions/no-backwards-compatibility.md` and `docs/internals/conventions/naming-no-invented-categories.md` are the conventions it applies. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Name who a parked route is waiting for, as keys rather than prose ([issue #1236](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1236), [PR #1291](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1291))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1236 was filed against `WriteStatus::AwaitingRoute { detail: String }`, which collapsed "still waiting for a relay list" and "nowhere to send this" into one English sentence an app could only prefix-match. Both halves of that framing were re-verified against `origin/master` before any code was written, and one of them was stale: #1249 deleted the type and replaced it with `WriteFact::Destinations { relays, complete }`, so the *typed* distinction is already closed and closed correctly — `complete` is a statement about KNOWLEDGE EXHAUSTION, a bool a program branches on, and `complete: true` with an empty set is `WriteOutcome::NoDestination`. What went with the string, and was never replaced, is the REASON. `RouteAnswer::author_route_needs: BTreeSet<PublicKey>` (`crates/nmp/src/core/write.rs:86`) is computed on every resolution, written into `pending.route_needs`, unioned into the discovery set — and `pub(super)`, so no consumer can see it. "Still determining destinations" was expressible; "still determining destinations, because these authors' relay lists have not arrived" was not. The corpus said so out loud in three places: `crates/nmp-bdd/src/steps/then/routes.rs` carried three doc comments confessing that their step proved less than its name, and the worst of them — `the receipt says it has no relay list for me yet` — was bound to a sentence no feature file used, so it never ran at all. A step that cannot fail for the reason it exists is not evidence.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::WriteFact::Destinations` gains a third field, `awaiting_author_routes: BTreeSet<PublicKey>` — every author whose routes the write is still waiting on, which is both the reason the picture is open and the list of repairs, since a later positive route fact for any one of them is the only thing that can move it. `FfiWriteFact::Destinations` mirrors it as `Vec<String>` of 64-char hex, Swift as `case destinations(relays:complete:awaitingAuthorRoutes:)` and Kotlin as `Destinations(relays, complete, awaitingAuthorRoutes)`. It is deliberately NOT a rendered string: the original `detail` was English prose, which is exactly what made the two cases indistinguishable to a program, so restoring one under a new name would have satisfied the issue title and recreated the defect. `RouteAnswer` itself stays `pub(super)` — what crosses is a projection of one field, not the internal type, which also carries pre-diff mechanism no consumer should see. `EngineCore::rewrite_route`'s `picture_changed` now compares the waiting set alongside the relay union and the completeness flag, so a park that stops waiting on one author and starts waiting on another re-emits instead of reporting the first reason and then going silent. The invariant is stated on all four surfaces: non-empty implies `complete: false`, and the converse deliberately does NOT hold — an open picture naming nobody is a write held on its SIGNER, which has no frozen recipient set to resolve against, and `Signing` is the fact that says what it is held on. `NmpWorld::my_pubkey_hex` becomes `my_pubkey() -> PublicKey`. Every key is decoded `PublicKey` in Rust and hex at the boundary; bech32 appears nowhere (`docs/internals/conventions/bech32-boundary.md`). `nmp-facade.txt` grows by exactly one line — the new field — to 28980 on this merged head, under the 30000 cap. No NIP-29 file is touched: a group write is `WriteRouting::Explicit`, complete at its first resolution with an empty `author_route_needs`, so that door cannot reach this path — and it gets the new field for free because #1274 made the group door strictly the general one.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. The waiting set is stateless reducer memory, re-derived by re-resolving every open intent on boot, which is why declared needs have no durability story to lose (`docs/internals/routing/knowledge-and-settlement.md`). A reattached park replays its reason off that same memory.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound or ordering changes. `StalledWrite.detail` for `StalledWriteStage::Unroutable` is untouched: it is #1025's operator-facing surface, it never read `RouteAnswer::detail`, and prose is the right shape for a human reading a diagnostic.
- **Updated falsifiers:** three BDD steps that documented their own inadequacy now assert. `park_carries_a_reason` requires the open picture to name at least one author — strictly stronger than the `the receipt reports it is still determining destinations` step that precedes it in both its scenarios; `parked_awaiting_person` requires the receipt to name the exact person the scenario staged as unlooked-up, instead of re-proving that routing is incomplete; The third confessed step is DELETED rather than strengthened, because no scenario in the corpus ever invoked its sentence — a step definition nothing calls cannot fail for any reason, which is the same defect this issue objects to. Its claim survives in `park_carries_a_reason`, whose running scenario backgrounds `Given my relay list has never been fetched` and therefore makes the author it names ME. Two Rust falsifiers carry the EXACT-identity form the BDD tier states loosely, and both were run red against the final tree before being kept. `a_narrowing_park_moves_only_the_waiting_set` (`crates/nmp/src/core/write_tests.rs`) pins the property that makes the waiting set load-bearing in `rewrite_route`: one of two unlooked-up recipients settling leaves `relays` and `complete` byte-identical and moves ONLY the waiting set, so a receipt comparing just the other two axes would report the first reason and go silent. It was run red by making `AuthorRouteState::Absent` declare a need, so a settled recipient never left the set. `an_unroutable_write_parks_on_an_open_empty_destination_set_and_is_listed` (`crates/nmp/tests/core_headless/stalled_writes.rs`) now requires the emitted park to name the EXACT author rather than merely somebody; run red by emitting the fact with an empty set, whose failure output shows `AuthorRouteNeedsChanged` carrying the key internally while the receipt carries nothing — the defect this issue names, reproduced. No feature file is edited at all, deliberately: `features/routing/routed-and-published.feature` is ungoverned legacy, `tools/behavior-traceability` obliges any behavioural edit to bring its scenarios under complete `nmp:*` metadata, and `nmp_bdd::governed_feature_paths` treats one `nmp:` line as governing the WHOLE file — which `crates/nmp-bdd/tests/bdd.rs` then excludes from the runner. Governing it to clear the gate would have stopped the very falsifier this unit exists to make real from executing, so the corpus is left byte-identical to master and the evidence stays running. Both strengthened steps were run RED by stubbing ONLY the population and leaving the field and all four surfaces in place (`183 scenarios, 181 passed, 2 failed`), each failing on its own message, then GREEN (`183 scenarios, 1522 steps, all passed`). `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs` carries the waiting set as PAYLOAD in `NormStatus::Destinations` on both sides and adds an explicit park case, so a boundary that shipped the emptiness and dropped the names diverges rather than passing a tag-only oracle; `cargo test -p nmp-parity` was RUN, not merely compiled, because this is an FFI shape change. `crates/nmp/src/nip65.rs`'s first-install park assertions now require the exact author on the live effect AND after a restart-and-reattach, and its settled case requires the waiting set to be empty. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `every_write_fact_maps_without_terminal_rollup` grows a park case. `./gradlew test` is what caught the signer-park case — compiling was necessary and not sufficient, exactly the behavioural break a widened shape hides. `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo test -p nmp-parity`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all`, `swift build`, `swift test` (175 tests), `./gradlew compileKotlin compileTestKotlin` and `./gradlew test` (120 tests) under JDK 17, and the repo gate scripts runnable on macOS all pass on this head. Projections were computed on this exact head rather than copied: `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` → `ffi,kotlin,rust,swift`.
- **Superseded path removed:** `RouteAnswer::detail: Option<String>` is DELETED, with `unresolved_detail`, `no_destination_detail`, `exhausted_source` and `EMPTY_EXPLICIT_ROUTE` — everything that existed only to fill it — and the unit assertion that existed only to read it. No alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. The field was written on every resolution and read by nothing but its own test: the receipt stopped carrying words at #1249, so its documented purpose ("the words the receipt park carries") had been false ever since, and the typed set replaces the one job it still had a claim to. `NmpWorld::my_pubkey_hex`, orphaned by the same #1249 change and with zero callers, is replaced rather than kept beside its successor. `docs/internals/routing/preview-and-observability.md` §4.1 is rewritten rather than annotated: it was the design authority that specified `AwaitingRoute { detail: String }` and argued for the string, so leaving it would have left a doc authorizing the defect; the two `{ detail }` spellings in `resolution-lifecycle.md` go with it. The wider staleness of the `AwaitingRoute`/`Routed` names across the routing doc set predates this unit and is #1249's, deliberately untouched here.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1236 is the implementation authority and PR #1291 is the exact review record. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Give a write that belongs to several groups an unsigned door ([issue #1281](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1281), [PR #1285](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1285))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1281 records a legitimate NIP-29 write with no door at all. `nip29::Group` is one relay scope plus one group id, and both mint doors enforced that: `Group::intent` appended exactly one `["h", id]` row and `Group::signed_intent` refused a second. That leaves an event that is in SEVERAL groups at once unspellable. mosaico's session status is the concrete case: a kind:30315 addressable event keyed `(author, d=status)` carrying one `h` row per channel the session occupies, so the same status renders in every room the agent is in. Publishing it once per channel makes every copy REPLACE the last — they share the coordinate, so the agent ends up visible in exactly one room, whichever wrote last — and picking one channel silently changes what the event means. Composing it under one group and letting the app append the rest is refused by `contextualize` (`CallerSuppliedContext`), correctly. So the app hand-minted a `WriteIntent`, spelling its own `WriteRouting::Explicit` and writing its own `h` rows, which is precisely what #1242 removed for every other group write and was the last protocol logic left in that app. The apparent second reading — that this needs a PRE-SIGNED door, because the consumer reached the shape by routing status through a pre-signed path — was checked against that app's source and is false. Its unsigned path already computed the event id WITHOUT signing (`frozen_event_id` is `EventId::new(&author, &created_at, &kind, &tags, &content)`; a NIP-01 id is a hash of the body and a signature was never an input), and the refusal that pushed status onto the signed path reads verbatim `"unsigned NIP-29 writes require exactly one h tag; exact multi-group events must be pre-signed"`. That is an ARITY limit, not an id-timing requirement. Nothing consumed the status id either: its reader is keyed by `(pubkey, channel)` — the NIP-38 replaceable coordinate — and the returned id reached only a debug log line and an app-local observability row.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust adds `nmp::nip29::Groups` — the hosts a scope named plus the SET of group ids one event claims — minted by the new `RelayScope::groups(ids) -> Result<Groups, GroupContextError>`. It has exactly ONE method, `publish(engine, author, builder)`: it appends one `h` row per retained id BEFORE the stamp/sign step, mints `WriteRouting::Explicit` over the scope's whole host set and an exact frozen author (#878) internally, and hands the intent straight to `Engine::publish_tracked`. The app never holds a `WriteIntent`, never names a relay, and never spells an `h` row. There is deliberately no pre-signed spelling and no mint-without-publish spelling — see "Superseded path removed" for why neither is added rather than why one was taken away. `Groups` is a WRITE CONTEXT and nothing else: no read door, no records observation, no named operation, because each of those is per-group by definition (a roster is one group's; NIP-29's relay-signed records key themselves by `d` per group; every 9000-9022 action names one group), so a plural of any of them would be an aggregate this crate would have to invent a meaning for. `nmp-nip29`'s context vocabulary takes a set at every call site: `contextualize(&BTreeSet<String>, builder)` appends one row per id in the set's canonical order, so two callers naming the same rooms in different orders compose identical bytes and the unsigned path can never repeat a row; `validate_context(&BTreeSet<String>, event)` requires an already-signed event's `h` rows to name exactly that set, each once. `GroupContextError` gains `NoGroupNamed` (an empty id set, refused where the value is FORMED so no `Groups` exists on that path) and `RepeatedContext { repeated }`, and `MissingContext`/`MismatchedContext` widen from `String` to `BTreeSet<String>`. FFI adds the `FfiGroups` object (`publish` alone) and `FfiRelayScope::groups`; `FfiError` gains `EmptyGroupSet` and `GroupContextRepeated { repeated }`, and `GroupContextMissing`/`GroupContextMismatched` widen from `String` to `Vec<String>`. Swift adds `NMPGroups` (one `publish` method) and `NMPRelayScope.groups(_:)` with the matching `NMPError.emptyGroupSet`/`.groupContextRepeated` cases; Kotlin mirrors all of it. `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs` carries a TWO-LINE diff: the set-taking vocabulary means `Group::validate_context` and `Group::publish` pass this group's id as the one-element set. Nothing else in that file is this branch's. #1296 has since deleted `Group::intent`, `Group::signed_intent` and `Group::publish_signed` and inlined `through_the_one_door`, so `Group` is publish-only too and the two shapes now agree from both sides. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added. `nmp-facade.txt` is 28999 lines, under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A several-group write is an ordinary tracked write through the ONE publish door — `Engine::publish_tracked`, the same call `Group::publish` makes — and is reattachable by the app's own correlation token exactly like every other write.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/context.rs` adds `a_draft_for_several_groups_carries_one_h_row_per_group`, `the_composed_rows_do_not_depend_on_the_callers_own_order_or_repetition`, `a_caller_supplied_row_is_refused_at_the_several_group_arity_too`, `a_signed_event_repeating_a_group_is_refused_even_though_the_set_is_right` and `a_refusal_names_one_group_singular_and_several_plural`; the retired two-`h`-rows-are-ambiguous test is replaced by `a_signed_event_naming_a_group_the_door_was_not_asked_for_is_refused`, which asserts the whole found set beside the whole expected set — something a row-COUNT check could never say. `crates/nmp/src/nip29/groups.rs` adds `the_door_contextualizes_with_the_whole_retained_set` (the app never holds an intent, so the composition is proved where it is decided — with the exact retained set, not a subset), `publishing_once_per_room_would_share_one_replaceable_coordinate` (proves the collision the whole shape exists for, by requiring the two per-room copies to carry the IDENTICAL `(kind, author, d)` coordinate — if they did not, nothing needed fixing), `a_several_group_write_is_an_ordinary_tracked_write` (matches the returned receipt id against a real `publish_queue()` entry), `a_several_group_write_routes_to_every_host_in_the_scope`, `a_write_context_over_no_group_is_never_formed`, `duplicate_and_unsorted_ids_canonicalize_to_one_set` and `a_caller_supplied_context_is_refused_before_any_several_group_write_is_accepted`. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs` adds `a_several_group_write_crosses_the_boundary_and_reaches_the_one_publish_door` and `a_write_context_over_no_group_is_never_formed_across_the_boundary`. Swift adds `testASeveralGroupWriteReachesTheOnePublishDoor` and `testAWriteContextOverNoGroupIsNeverFormed`; Kotlin mirrors both. `features/groups/one-write-several-groups.feature` carries six governed scenarios, all `built`. This branch had also retitled and extended `features/groups/pre-signed-publication.feature`; #1296 DELETED that file along with the pre-signed door it governed, so those edits are gone with it rather than carried. Every new claim was run RED first by mutating the implementation to its own stated falsifier: appending only the first id fails `a_draft_for_several_groups_carries_one_h_row_per_group` (`left: ["darkroom"], right: ["darkroom", "photographers", "studio"]`) and `the_composed_rows_do_not_depend_on_the_callers_own_order_or_repetition`; dropping the repeated-row arm fails `a_signed_event_repeating_a_group_is_refused_even_though_the_set_is_right`; dropping the empty-set refusal fails `a_write_context_over_no_group_is_never_formed`; and accepting any event that merely CONTAINS the retained groups (`!group_ids.is_subset(&found)`) fails `a_signed_event_naming_a_group_the_door_was_not_asked_for_is_refused`. `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh` is EXTENDED rather than merely re-pointed: it gains `crates/nmp/src/nip29/groups.rs` as a checked surface and enforces the no-per-call-relay/route/host rule over its one door, so the new arity cannot acquire a routing parameter the single-group one is forbidden. Its excluded-name lists are #1296's, unchanged by this branch — that change tightened them when it deleted the mint doors and its comment says not to re-add those names, and `Groups` adds no name to `Group`'s catalogue. Gates RUN on this head: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this is an FFI variant shape change), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi`, `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-nip29 -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, the four NIP-29 gate scripts, the behavior-traceability `check`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** `GroupContextError::AmbiguousContext` and its FFI/Swift/Kotlin mirrors (`FfiError::GroupContextAmbiguous`, `NMPError.groupContextAmbiguous`, `NMPError.GroupContextAmbiguous`) are DELETED in this change — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding wrapper. "More than one `h`, so which group it claims has no single answer" was a claim about row COUNT, and count stops being the question once a write can legitimately belong to several groups; a second `h` naming another group is now a `MismatchedContext` that names both whole sets, which is strictly more informative at the one-group arity too. The strictness that variant DID carry is not lost: the right set spelled twice is the new `RepeatedContext`, because a set comparison alone would silently start accepting bytes the door would never mint. The single-`&str` spellings of `nmp_nip29::contextualize`/`validate_context` are deleted in favour of the set-taking ones, `Group::mint` is deleted in favour of `Groups::mint`, and the BDD step `the publication is refused with a typed ambiguous-group-context error` is replaced by `…repeated-group-context error` in the same change. **Nothing pre-signed is added, and nothing mint-without-publish is added**, both deliberate scope decisions rather than omissions: no `Groups::signed_intent`, no `Groups::publish_signed`, no `Groups::contextualize`, no `Group::contextualize`, and no `Groups::intent`. The first four would exist only to serve an app that signs its own bytes and hands them back, and would land with zero consumers — the evidence above shows the one reported consumer needs the unsigned door and always did. `Groups::intent` is absent for reasons that are checkable rather than stylistic: a `WriteIntent` derives NOTHING — not `Clone`, not `Debug`, not `Serialize` — so an app holding one cannot persist it across a restart, clone it for batching, or inspect it; its one honest use is stamping `correlation`, and the consumer that asked for the door stamps `None` on every intent it builds; and the separate submit stage that motivated minting had already been deleted when that consumer adopted NMP's own publish queue. The legitimate need underneath it — asking NMP to sign WITHOUT publishing — is already served by `Engine::sign_event`, which crosses FFI and is already called from Swift, so no new door is needed for it. `Group::intent`, `Group::signed_intent`, `Group::publish_signed` and `Group::through_the_one_door` were left untouched by this branch so the change deleting them would not collide; #1296 has since landed and removed them, so `Group` and `Groups` are both publish-only and this branch touches neither decision.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1281 is the implementation authority and PR #1285 is the exact review record; it was filed from mosaico's adoption of the #1274 group door (mosaico#769), and #1242's "no protocol logic in the app" is the higher-level why that made the app's last hand-minted `WriteIntent` a defect rather than a style choice. Issue #1283 asked for the sign-yourself half of the same door; it is NOT implemented here, because the ruling that NMP signs and an app observes its own write removes its premise — that is recorded on #1283 rather than silently dropped. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Stop naming a deleted door in two FFI error-variant docs ([issue #1298](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1298), [PR #1299](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1299))

- **Failure evidence:** #1296 deleted `FfiGroup::publish_signed` with the rest of the NIP-29 mint-without-publish doors and did not reach two doc comments in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs` that name it. `GroupContextMissing` said the refusal fires for "a pre-signed event handed to `FfiGroup::publish_signed`/`FfiGroup::validate_context`" — only the second exists, so the doc pointed a reader at a door that is gone and made the one real construction site read as one of two. `GroupContextMismatched` said "a different group than the one publishing or validating it" for the same reason. A reader trying to learn when either variant fires was sent to a symbol that no longer exists, which is the stale-reference class `AGENTS.md`'s truth-anchor rule exists to prevent. Honest scope: this is mechanical cleanup of a miss in #1296, not a behavior change.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** none to any signature, type, variant, argument or return anywhere. The only change is the English inside two `FfiError` variant doc comments, which `docs/surface/components/nmp-core/uniffi.txt` captures because UniFFI records docstrings in the component snapshot — that snapshot is regenerated here, and together with the two SDK error files it is the entire governed diff. `GroupContextMissing` and `GroupContextMismatched` now name only `FfiGroup::validate_context`, which after #1296 is their one reachable construction site. The same stale reference survived in both SDKs' hand-written error docs -- `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/NMPError.swift`'s `groupContextMissing` and `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/NMPError.kt`'s `GroupContextMissing` -- and both now name only the door that exists. #1285 rewrote these same three doc comments while in flight and, having been written before #1296 merged, carried the deleted name forward into its new text; this change is rebased on top of that rewrite and strips the name from it, keeping every structural change #1285 made to the variants. After this, `publish_signed`/`publishSigned` names a NIP-29 door in zero files under `crates/` and `Packages/`. `nmp-facade.txt` is untouched at 28980 lines — byte-identical to master's — because the Rust facade never carried these strings.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed.
- **Updated falsifiers:** none, and none is claimed. Nothing here is falsifiable — the change is two English sentences, and inventing a proof for prose would be exactly the dishonest claim gate 6 exists to catch. What IS mechanical is that the change is doc-only: `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` reports `ffi,kotlin,swift` solely because of the corrected doc comments -- `ffi` because UniFFI records docstrings in the component snapshot, `kotlin`/`swift` because the two hand-written SDK error files changed, and `cargo check -p nmp-ffi` plus `cargo fmt --all --check` are clean on this head with no code touched.
- **Superseded path removed:** nothing is replaced or deleted here beyond the stale words themselves; the door they named was already deleted by #1296, and this entry only stops the docs from claiming otherwise.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1298 is the implementation authority and PR #1299 is the exact review record; #1296 is the change whose miss it corrects. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.
## 2026-08-05 — Revert the parked-write removal widening: cancel-then-remove already ended it ([issue #1294](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1294), [PR #1297](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1297))

- **Failure evidence:** PR #1279 (issue #1269) widened `EngineCore::remove_publish_queue_entry` so a write parked on a signer nobody has could be removed directly, and added `NotSentReason::Removed` to name the result. Its premise — that a signer-parked write *"ends ONLY by the app removing it"* and that removal refused exactly that entry, so nothing ended it at all — was false. **`cancel` then `remove` already ended it, on every surface including FFI.** `Engine::cancel(id)`'s only refusal for a pending entry is "already signed" (`crates/nmp/src/core/write.rs`), which a parked write is not; it runs `EventStore::cancel_write`, compensates the optimistic live-query row, and emits `NotSent(Cancelled)`, after which `remove_publish_queue_entry(id)` succeeds because cancellation deleted the open intent row the refusal reads. The **pre-#1279 docstring said so** — *"A write that still owns open delivery work is refused — cancel it first."* — and #1279's own implementation refutes its premise: `release_removable_obligation` called the **same** `EventStore::cancel_write` compensation, conceding in its docstring that *"Releasing runs the same atomic compensation cancellation uses, then forgets the receipt."* It was a second spelling of a working path, with zero consumers asking for it (mosaico only *reads* the queue for a diagnostics endpoint, 29er-next has no reference, and in-tree the only callers were nmp's own tests) that nonetheless taxed every foreign `switch`/`when` with a variant `Cancelled` already covered. Verified on this reverted head against a real `EngineThread` with a signer-parked write and a live query over its author: `cancel_write => Ok(Cancelled)`, `remove_publish_queue_entry => Ok(())`, publish queue `[]`.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::NotSentReason` loses its third variant and is back to `Cancelled` | `Superseded`; `FfiNotSentReason` mirrors the removal; Swift loses `case removed` and Kotlin loses `Removed`, and both `init(_ ffi:)` / `from(ffi:)` converters lose their arm. A foreign `switch`/`when` over `NotSentReason` loses one arm — the intended break, and the point of the revert. No function signature, record field, or error variant changes anywhere. The remaining governed movement is docstrings: `FfiRemoveQueueEntryError::StillActive` and `FfiSigningState::AwaitingSigner` now state the truth about the door, and `NmpEngine::remove_publish_queue_entry` names cancel-then-remove (its UniFFI checksum moves 43281 → 5185 with its docstring). `EngineCore::release_removable_obligation` and the effect-returning removal signature are private and leave no projected trace. Synchronized tests: `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs`, `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`'s `every_write_fact_maps_without_terminal_rollup`, `EvidenceMappingTests.swift`, `EvidenceMappingTest.kt`. No SDK-parity allowlist entry is added or removed.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, route, or coverage key changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. `EventStore` gains no door and loses none: `EventStore::remove_publish_queue_entry` and its `RemoveQueueEntryOutcome::StillOpen` guard are byte-identical and remain authoritative, and `crates/nmp-store/tests/publish_queue_contract.rs` is untouched (#1279 never modified it). The two-durable-step window #1279 introduced — compensate, then forget, with a crash between them leaving a cancelled receipt — is gone with it; cancellation and removal are once again two calls the app makes, each atomic on its own.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed. No counter, section, bound, or ordering changes; `EngineCore::stalled_write_stage` and the stalled-write projection read the same reducer state they did before #1279.
- **Updated falsifiers:** none added, and deliberately so — the correct diff for a revert is negative. Two are deleted with the behavior they covered: the whole parked-removal integration test added by #1279 goes (299 lines, one test), and #1279's cross-boundary removal test is deleted from `crates/nmp-ffi/tests/publish_queue_doors.rs`. Their exact file and symbol names are spelled without code spans here and in PR #1297, because a code span in this field is a claim that the mechanism EXISTS at this head, and the whole point of these two is that they no longer do: the deleted file was crates/nmp/tests/publish_queue_removal_parked.rs carrying a_write_parked_on_a_missing_signer_is_removable_and_one_being_signed_is_not, and the deleted FFI test was a_write_parked_on_a_signer_nobody_has_is_removable_across_the_boundary. Everything that survives keeps its exact meaning and is green unchanged: `removing_an_entry_with_open_delivery_work_is_still_active` (signed, live lane) in the same FFI file, and `a_signature_in_flight_is_not_reported_as_a_write_parked_on_a_missing_signer` in `crates/nmp/tests/publish_queue_signing_inflight.rs`, which is #1270's proof and `WRITES-PARKED-015`'s evidence — `SigningState::InFlight` stays, as does the internal `pending.sign_request_in_flight` field that predates it. `features/writes/parked-writes.feature` loses `WRITES-PARKED-016` with the refusal logic it described, and `WRITES-PARKED-007` returns to `nmp:status=specified` / `nmp:gap=evidence` / `nmp:issue=#1253` — its exact state before #1279 — re-worded to spell the two calls, so nothing claims evidence that does not exist. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (13 + 2 green — the direct/FFI oracle, run because deleting an enum variant is an FFI shape change), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi` (all targets green, including the byte-stable `auth.rs` door tests), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-store` (32 targets green), `cargo test -p nmp-bdd` (32 features, 183 scenarios, 1522 steps), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` (OK), `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh` (ok), the behavior-traceability `check` against explicit base and head trees with a real issue snapshot (exit 0), `swift build` + `swift test` (Build complete; 172 tests, 4 skipped, 0 failures) and `./gradlew compileKotlin` + `./gradlew test` under JDK 17 (BUILD SUCCESSFUL both) — both SDKs rebuilt from regenerated UniFFI bindings first, so the deleted variant is genuinely gone from the code the hand-written wrappers compile against. `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh` was re-run after merging `origin/master`; the resulting diff is purely subtractive apart from docstrings, and `nmp-facade.txt` is 28998 lines.
- **Superseded path removed:** the widening is deleted outright, with nothing left behind: `EngineCore::release_removable_obligation`, the `sign_request_in_flight` / `already_signed` / live-lane refusal, and the effect-returning `remove_publish_queue_entry` signature all go, and `NotSentReason::Removed` is deleted from Rust, FFI, Swift, and Kotlin in this one change — no alias, no deprecation, no forwarding arm folding it onto `Cancelled`. `remove_publish_queue_entry` itself STAYS in its pre-#1279 form, refusing an entry whose open intent row still exists: terminal retained receipts accumulate in a store only NMP can reach, so reclaiming them is genuinely NMP's job, and only the widening was wrong. The one non-subtractive part is prose, not surface: `SigningState::AwaitingSigner`'s *"the app removing the entry is the only other exit"*, the #1039-derived *"end ONLY by the app removing them"* at every site it reached (`PublishQueueEntry`, `FfiSigningState::AwaitingSigner`, `SigningState::InFlight`'s #1261 paragraph, the removal doors on `Engine` / `Handle` / `NmpEngine` / `EngineCore`, `publish_queue_doors.rs`, `publish_queue_signing_inflight.rs`), and both `StillActive` docstrings — which claimed the refusal fires only when the write *"is signed and lanes are live"* when it fires for any still-open obligation — now name cancel-then-remove. **No helper, no convenience method, and no combined verb was added to make the two calls easier.** Two calls is the answer, and a third spelling is what this change exists to remove.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1294 is the implementation authority and PR #1297 is the exact review record; it applies a maintainer ruling to remove #1279's widening, and #1279's own change-log entry above is left byte-identical because the trail is append-only — the record of the widening stays and this entry records its removal. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Delete the receipt-id-discarding publish door and rename `publish_tracked` to `publish` ([issue #848](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/848), [PR #1300](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1300))

- **Failure evidence:** direct Rust exposed two write doors where every other tier exposed one. `Engine::publish`/`Handle::publish` returned `FifoReceiver<WriteFact>` and were literally `publish_tracked(intent).map(|receipt| receipt.statuses)` -- the same door with the store-issued `ReceiptId` thrown away on the way out. A caller could therefore give up recoverability without knowing it: the id is the only handle a restarted process has, so a write accepted through the lossy spelling could not be reattached by id after a crash even though the store had allocated one for it. #1244 is the concrete cost already paid -- routing the group door through that spelling made a group write the one write an app could not find again after a restart. `docs/VISION.md` states the write noun as `receipt = engine.publish(intent)`, one acceptance door returning the whole receipt.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust only. `Engine::publish` and `Handle::publish` now return `ReceiptStream` (store-issued `ReceiptId` plus the same `WriteFact` stream they always carried) instead of a bare `FifoReceiver<WriteFact>`, and `Engine::publish_tracked`/`Handle::publish_tracked` are DELETED rather than kept as aliases. The FFI, Swift and Kotlin surfaces are byte-identical to master: `FfiEngine::publish` was ALREADY `publish_tracked` under the name `publish`, so this change aligns Rust with the boundary rather than moving the boundary. `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` on this exact head reports `rust` alone, and `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` -- regenerated AFTER merging master and measured from the regenerated file -- is 28997 lines against master's 28998, a net -1 whose entire governed diff is the deleted `publish_tracked` line plus `publish`'s changed return type.
- **Persistence impact:** none. The receipt id was already allocated and durably recorded on every accepted write, including writes accepted through the lossy spelling; this change stops discarding it in the return value. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed.
