- **Updated falsifiers:** none is added and none is claimed -- this is a deletion, and inventing a new proof for removing a lossy alias would be exactly the dishonest claim gate 6 exists to catch. What the change is held to is EXISTING evidence that already distinguishes the two doors, now covering the only door: `handle_surface_is_closed_and_receipt_reattachment_is_explicit` in `crates/nmp/tests/runtime_integration.rs` enumerates `Handle`'s public methods against an exact expected list and now requires exactly one `publish` where it previously required two entries; `a_group_write_hands_back_the_store_issued_receipt_id` and `a_several_group_write_is_an_ordinary_tracked_write` match the returned id against a real `publish_queue()` entry, so a door that stopped returning the id fails them. `features/groups/a-group-write-carries-the-receipt-id.feature` carried a falsifier naming "routing the group door back through `Engine::publish` (the receipt-id-discarding spelling it used before)"; that mutation is unspellable once the spelling is deleted, so the line is re-expressed as making `Group::publish` return only the `ReceiptStream`'s statuses -- the same observable loss, still performable, and it names only symbols that exist. Gates RUN on this head: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN, not merely compiled -- it is the direct/FFI oracle and both facades' publish doors are on it), `cargo test -p nmp-ffi`, `cargo test -p nmp`, `cargo test -p nmp-bdd` (32 features, 183/183 scenarios, 1522 steps), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-routing-vocabulary.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-read-door.sh`, `swift build`, `swift test --package-path Packages/NMP` (172 tests, 0 failures), and `./gradlew compileKotlin` plus `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** `Engine::publish_tracked` and `Handle::publish_tracked` are DELETED in this change -- no alias, no deprecation window, no forwarding wrapper from either spelling to the other (`docs/internals/conventions/no-backwards-compatibility.md`). The caller census was verified before deleting and then confirmed by the compiler after: every caller of the lossy form was the door's own body, a unit test inside `crates/nmp/src/engine.rs`, an integration test under `crates/nmp/tests/`, a `crates/nmp-bdd/src/world/` helper, `crates/nmp-parity/tests/parity.rs`, or `crates/nmp-consumer-check/src/lib.rs` inside `#[cfg(test)]` -- with exactly ONE non-test exception, `crates/nmp-demo/src/main.rs`, a demo binary that drains statuses and never wanted the id, migrated with `.statuses` here. That exception is recorded rather than smoothed over, because "zero production callers" was the premise this deletion rested on and it was very slightly false. `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh` is corrected rather than merely re-pointed: #1244 had required `publish_tracked(intent)` in the group binding AND banned `engine\.publish\(` there as "the receipt-id-discarding publish spelling". After the rename both clauses invert -- the requirement names a deleted method, and the ban names the CORRECT door. The requirement is re-expressed as `.publish(intent)`; the ban is REMOVED, because #848 deletes its referent. Removing it does not gut the property: keeping it would have, since `engine` and `.publish(intent)` sit on separate lines in `group.rs` and the regex would have gone on passing while policing nothing. There is now no spelling of `Engine::publish` that discards the id and `ReceiptStream` is the only thing acceptance can return, so the property #1244 policed by grep is carried by the type instead -- the type-over-convention rule in `docs/bug-class-ledger.md:3-5`. `skills/nmp/references/current-surface.md` and `skills/nmp/references/platforms.md` drop `publish_tracked` from the consumer-facing surface in the same change; `publish_tracked` appears nowhere under `crates/` or `tools/` afterwards.
- **Human signoff:** issue #848 is the implementation authority -- it states the required target (`Engine::publish` returns `ReceiptStream`, `publish_tracked` deleted, no aliases) and its own falsifier "public/source audits find one `publish` operation and no `publish_tracked`" -- and PR #1300 is the exact review record. #1244 is the incident that priced the defect. PR #1297 had right of way over the shared files and landed mid-flight; `origin/master` is merged in at `043191354` and every gate above was re-run after that merge, its deletion of `crates/nmp/tests/publish_queue_removal_parked.rs` resolved by accepting the upstream deletion. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Give kind:9007 a spelling for a subgroup's parent, and keep it off kind:9002 ([issue #1301](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1301), [PR #1302](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1302))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1301 records the asymmetry: `crates/nmp-nip29/src/records.rs` hands back a kind:39000's complete row list verbatim precisely so that an app reading a subgroup's `parent` row needs no hand-parser ("typed-fields-only would force them to keep a hand-parser alive for it, which is the very thing this module exists to delete"), while the write side had no equivalent — `create_group()` took no arguments and composed no rows, so an app creating a subgroup appended the tag itself. mosaico `b4f31476`, `src/fabric/nip29/lifecycle.rs`, is a module whose own doc says it survives for exactly this and nothing else: *"Exactly ONE draft is left that NMP cannot express: **`parent`.** … `parent` is filed as pablof7z/nmp#1301; when it lands, this module is deleted rather than shrunk."* Its `group_create_subgroup` is the whole of `nmp_nip29::create_group().tag(tag(&["parent", parent_h])?)` — NMP's own builder with one protocol row stapled on by the app.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp_nip29::create_group` now takes `parent: Option<&str>` — the parent's group id, NIP-29's own `d` value, a relay-scoped string that is never an `naddr` and never a key. `Some(id)` composes `["parent", "<id>"]`; `None` composes no row at all, never an empty one, which is exactly how NIP-29 spells a root group (*"A group without a `parent` tag is a root group"*). It follows the same idiom `join_request(invite_code)` has had since #989 rather than introducing a struct for one field. `nmp::nip29::Group::create_group(engine, author, parent)` mirrors it, as do `FfiGroup::create_group` (taking `Option<String>`), Swift's `NMPGroup.createGroup(engine:authorPubkeyHex:parent:)` and Kotlin's `createGroup(engine, authorPubkeyHex, parent)`. No new type, no new enum, no SDK-parity allowlist entry. `nmp-facade.txt` is 28997 lines, measured from the regenerated file, under the 30000 cap.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store schema, `SCHEMA_VERSION`, epoch, journal encoding, retained row, receipt, attempt, route or coverage key changes. A 9007 remains an ordinary group write through the one publish door.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none added or removed.
- **Two protocol facts this entry exists to correct and record.** First: **nostr-protocol/nips#2319 is MERGED** — `merged_at 2026-07-16T00:10:48Z`, three weeks before #1301 was filed — and NIP-29 now carries a `Subgroups` section defining `parent` and `child`. `parent` is therefore protocol, not a proposal and not reference-relay jargon, so naming it invents no category. The #1287 entry above states *"nostr-protocol/nips#2319 is a proposed extension rather than NIP-29 proper"*; that was already false when written. Those bytes stand — this log is append-only and corrections are new entries — and this is the correction. Second: **the merged spec and the only implementation disagree about which kind carries the row.** NIP-29's prose says *"Parenting and promotion to root are triggered by a `kind:9002` (`edit-metadata`) event … with the desired `parent` value"* and never mentions kind:9007. Probed live against `wss://nip29.f7z.io` on 2026-08-05, the relay both consumer applications use does the opposite: a kind:9007 carrying `["parent", P]` is honoured (the new group's relay-authored kind:39000 comes back carrying it and P's own kind:39000 gains `["child", C]`) and validated there (`restricted: parent group 'X' doesn't exist`; `restricted: must be an admin of the parent group`), while a kind:9002 carrying `["parent", P2]` beside a `name` row is accepted with the parent row IGNORED, and a kind:9002 carrying `parent` alone is refused with `invalid moderation action: missing metadata tags` — the reference relay's own error for a 9002 in which it recognised no field. relay29's `moderation_actions.go` reads `name`, `picture`, `about`, `public`/`private` and `open`/`closed`, and contains no `parent` or `child` code at all. A 9002 that omits `parent` also does NOT detach the group, contrary to the spec's *"Absence of the `parent` tag makes the group a root."* The divergence is recorded in the code on both `create_group` and `GroupMetadataEdit` rather than absorbed silently, exactly as #1287 recorded that `public`/`open` come from the reference relay's parser rather than NIP-29's prose.
- **Deliberately NOT composed, with the reason:** `GroupMetadataEdit` gains **no** `parent` field. Composing one would emit a row that takes no effect while reading as though it had — the same ground on which #1287 excluded `banner` and `livekit` — and worse here, because an app would believe it had moved a group under a new parent when the relay had left it exactly where it was. On the one relay that implements subgroups a parent cannot be restated after creation at all, which is what makes it identity rather than mutable metadata. `child` is likewise not composed: NIP-29 requires a parent's kind:39000 to carry its children as an ordered list and requires a 9002 to restate all of them, which is a list whose edit semantics no implementation honours yet — the same reason `supported_kinds` and kind:9010 stay uncomposed. Each is a one-line addition the day a relay honours it and a falsifier exists.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip29/src/operations.rs` adds `create_group_under_a_parent_carries_the_parent_row_on_the_create`, `create_group_at_the_root_is_kind_9007_with_no_tag_at_all` (a root states its rootness by carrying no row, never an empty one — which the relay refuses outright), `the_parent_row_carries_the_group_id_verbatim`, and `a_metadata_edit_never_composes_a_parent_row` (the guard on the door that must NOT grow the field). `crates/nmp-ffi/src/nip29.rs` adds `the_create_door_composes_the_parent_row_and_omits_it_for_a_root`. `crates/nmp/src/nip29/group.rs` extends its one-publish-door proof to a create under a parent. `features/groups/nip29-operation-schemas.feature` carries two new governed scenarios, `NIP29OPERATIONS-015/016`, both `built`, and its `NIP29OPERATIONS-011` evidence follows the renamed create proof. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this changes an FFI method signature), `cargo test -p nmp-nip29 -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-nip29-operation-catalogue.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-kind-blindness.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-read-door.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-bdd-file-length.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check` under the pinned `nightly-2026-07-07`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** the zero-argument `create_group()` is DELETED in the same change on every surface — no alias, no overload, no forwarding wrapper. Every caller moves in this PR: `nmp::nip29::Group::create_group`, `FfiGroup::create_group`, `NMPGroup.createGroup`, the Kotlin equivalent, and the Rust, Swift and Kotlin tests. Swift and Kotlin spell `parent` as a defaulted optional argument because that is those languages' ordinary spelling for an optional parameter and the SDK-parity gate treats it as one; the Rust and FFI signatures require it explicitly, so there is no surface on which the old zero-argument shape survives.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1301 is the implementation authority and PR #1302 is the exact review record; it was filed from mosaico's migration onto the #1292/#1281 group doors and is the last hand-written NIP-29 row that migration found. A distinct exact-head reviewer must still approve before merge; this entry is not reviewer approval or merge authorization.

## 2026-08-05 — Project C7's top-level chat and interpolated content to the native boundary ([issue #964](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/964), [PR #1303](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1303))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #964's 2026-08-05 consumer report names the exact two things an app still hand-writes after #1243/#1262 projected `chat_reply`. First, a message that is not a reply: `nmp_nipc7::compose_chat` never crossed the FFI, so `ChatDraft` in `29er-next` states `kind: 9` itself for every ordinary message, and no app-side test can catch that, because a kind the app chose is a kind the app agrees with. Second, the notification `p` rows: `nmp-grammar`'s interpolated content writes a NIP-27 `nostr:npub…` token and its `p` row from one statement precisely so the two cannot diverge, and it did not cross either — so an app that lets somebody @-mention a person appends `["p", hex]` by hand and hopes it matches the token it separately put in the content. That app carries `mentionRows` and `namedPubkeys` for exactly this, both recorded as excused crossings in its own boundary gate.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** Rust renames `nmp_nipc7::compose_chat(String)` to `nmp_nipc7::chat()` — content belongs to `EventBuilder::content`, which is also where an inline mention's or quote's rows come from, and the old signature made a caller with either pass an empty string and then restate the content, which is what that crate's own quote test was doing. `nmp-nipc7` is not reexported by the `nmp` facade, so the governed Rust projection is unchanged and the computed projection set is exactly the three above. FFI adds `chat()` and `with_content(draft, content)` in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/tagging.rs` plus the `FfiContentPart` enum in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/types.rs`; Rust spells interpolation as a variadic macro over a format string, which no FFI boundary has, and a list of parts is the projection and is strictly stronger, because a `{}` placeholder can outnumber its arguments and a part cannot. Swift adds `chat()`, `ContentPart` and `WritePayload.withContent([ContentPart])` in `Packages/NMP/Sources/NMP/Tagging.swift`; Kotlin adds the mirrors in `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/main/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/Tagging.kt`. Bech32 stays at the user boundary: every input is the decoded form (64-char hex, or the `Row` the app already holds) and the `nostr:npub1…`/`nostr:nevent1…` token is produced from it.
- **Persistence impact:** none; these are pure schema composers that return an `EventBuilder`. They read no clock, name no author, and touch no store, receipt, attempt, or journal record.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; nothing here reaches routing, the publish queue, or any diagnostics record.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-ffi/src/tagging.rs` adds `a_native_top_level_chat_is_kind_9_and_carries_no_rows`, `naming_a_person_writes_the_token_and_the_p_row_together`, `a_stated_relay_reaches_the_rendered_pointer_and_the_row_together`, `quoting_an_event_renders_it_and_emits_its_q_row_from_the_same_part`, `interpolated_rows_never_disturb_the_rows_a_composer_stated` and `a_malformed_named_key_refuses_rather_than_composing_half_a_message`. `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/TaggingTests.swift` adds `testChatIsKindNineAndCarriesNoRows`, `testNamingAPersonWritesTheTokenAndThePRowTogether`, `testAStatedRelayReachesTheTokenAndTheRowTogether`, `testQuotingAnEventRendersItAndEmitsItsQRow` and `testAMalformedNamedKeyRefuses`. `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/TaggingTest.kt` adds `chatIsKindNineAndCarriesNoRows`, `namingAPersonWritesTheTokenAndThePRowTogether`, `aStatedRelayReachesTheTokenAndTheRowTogether`, `quotingAnEventRendersItAndEmitsItsQRow` and `aMalformedNamedKeyRefuses`. `features/writes/entity-tagging.feature` carries two new governed scenarios, `WRITES-TAGGING-009` and `WRITES-TAGGING-010`, both `built`, each with Rust, Swift and Kotlin evidence. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this changes the FFI shape), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-bdd -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-nipc7 -p nmp-grammar -p nmp-nip18`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-bdd-file-length.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check` under the pinned `nightly-2026-07-07`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp_nipc7::compose_chat` is DELETED, not aliased. Swift's `WritePayload.withContent(_ content: String)` and Kotlin's `WritePayload.withContent(content: String)` are DELETED and replaced by the parts-valued spelling; no string-valued overload survives beside it, because a finished string is exactly the shape that lets a token and its row be written apart.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1303 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the run gates recorded on it and the merge record are the approval trail.

## 2026-08-05 — Give NIP-25 reactions a door, so no app writes kind 7 by hand ([issue #155](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/155), [PR #1304](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1304))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #155 records that NMP has no NIP-25 reaction door at all, and both consuming apps therefore hand-write one: kind 7, an `e` row, a `p` row and a content string. `29er-next`'s `ios/Sources/RoomTimelineReacting.swift` says so in its own comment — *"NMP has no dedicated reaction-intent helper yet"* — and hand-builds `tags: [["e", message.id], ["p", message.author], …]`; mosaico does the same. What that spelling loses is not the kind: it is the relay hint NMP actually observed, the author in the reference row's own slot, and the `k` row naming what was reacted to, all three simply absent and invisible, because nothing the composing app can see is missing. It also loses the thing that is actively wrong rather than merely thin — NIP-25 says *"There MUST be always an `e` tag set to the `id` of the event that is being reacted to"*, so a reaction names an ENTITY; an app reaching for its threading helper emits the conversation's ROOT `e` row first, and a client tallying reactions by the first `e` credits the root with a reaction nobody gave it.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,swift
- **Why there is no `rust` in that set, despite a new Rust crate:** the door DOES reach the `nmp` facade — `crates/nmp/src/nip25.rs` behind the non-default `nip25` cargo feature, mirroring `nip22` exactly — and the governed Rust snapshot is the DEFAULT-feature surface, which no feature-gated module enters. #1259 declared `ffi,kotlin,swift` for the same reason earlier today. The set was recomputed on this exact head with `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` after merging master and regenerating, not carried from an earlier run.
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** new crate `crates/nmp-nip25`, the kind:7 owner, exporting `REACTION_KIND`, `Reaction`, `ReactionError` and `react`, re-exported by the facade as `nmp::nip25` behind the optional `nip25` feature. That wiring is deliberate: #1239 records four protocol families `nmp-ffi` reaches and the `nmp` facade does not, so a direct-Rust app needs a second Cargo dependency for something a Swift app gets by linking one staticlib — a new family is wired through the facade at birth rather than added to that list. FFI adds `react_to(target, reaction)` in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/tagging.rs`, the `FfiReaction` enum in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/types.rs`, and one new error variant `FfiError::InvalidReaction` in `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs`. Swift adds `Reaction`, `react(to:with:)` and `NMPError.invalidReaction`; Kotlin adds `Reaction`, `react(target, reaction)` and `NMPError.InvalidReaction`. `Reaction` is a closed vocabulary rather than a content string because NIP-25 assigns `+`, `-` and the empty string fixed meanings, so an app writing content by hand can spell "like" three ways and can spell it by accident when an emoji picker returns nothing.
- **Deliberately NOT built, with the reason:** NIP-25's kind:17 external reaction is NIP-25's too and belongs in this crate when it can be reached — no `nmp_nip73::Nip73` value crosses the native boundary today, so a kind:17 arm would be a variant no caller could construct (Reachability Gate). NIP-30 custom emoji is also not written: a `:shortcode:` needs a companion `emoji` row, which is a token-and-its-row pairing that wants the one-statement treatment `nmp_grammar::text!` gives, not a `String` a caller fills in — so `Reaction::emoji` REFUSES a shortcode rather than emitting content no reader can render.
- **Persistence impact:** none; `react` is a pure schema composer returning an `EventBuilder`. It reads no clock, names no author, and touches no store, receipt, attempt, or journal record.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; nothing here reaches routing, the publish queue, or any diagnostics record.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-nip25/src/lib.rs` adds `the_three_readings_nip25_defines_render_as_plus_minus_and_the_emoji`, `an_empty_emoji_refuses_rather_than_silently_becoming_a_like`, `a_custom_emoji_shortcode_refuses_because_its_companion_row_is_not_written`, `reacting_to_a_reply_names_the_reply_and_never_its_root`, `a_reaction_carries_the_hint_the_author_slot_the_p_row_and_the_k_row`, `a_reaction_notifies_the_author_and_nobody_the_target_mentioned` and `a_reaction_invents_no_author_and_no_timestamp`. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/tagging.rs` adds `a_native_reaction_is_kind_7_and_carries_what_the_one_door_fills`, `the_native_reaction_vocabulary_is_nip25s_three_readings`, `a_native_reaction_to_a_reply_names_the_reply_and_never_its_root` and `an_emoji_that_would_say_something_else_refuses_before_a_builder_exists`. `Packages/NMP/Tests/NMPTests/TaggingTests.swift` adds `testReactionIsKindSevenAndCarriesWhatTheOneDoorFills`, `testTheReactionVocabularyIsNip25sThreeReadings`, `testReactingToAReplyNamesTheReplyAndNeverItsRoot` and `testAnEmojiThatWouldSaySomethingElseRefuses`. `Packages/NMPKotlin/src/test/kotlin/com/nmp/sdk/TaggingTest.kt` adds `reactionIsKindSevenAndCarriesWhatTheOneDoorFills`, `theReactionVocabularyIsNip25sThreeReadings`, `reactingToAReplyNamesTheReplyAndNeverItsRoot` and `anEmojiThatWouldSaySomethingElseRefuses`. `features/writes/reactions.feature` is a new governed file carrying `WRITES-REACTIONS-001` through `WRITES-REACTIONS-005`, all `built`. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — this adds an FFI method and an error variant), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-bdd -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-nip25`, `cargo test --workspace --all-features` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-features --all-targets -- -D warnings` (the `nip25` feature is non-default, so this is the job that actually builds `crates/nmp/src/nip25.rs`), `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-bdd-file-length.sh`, `scripts/check-dependency-direction.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check` under the pinned `nightly-2026-07-07`, `swift build`, `swift test`, `./gradlew compileKotlin` and `./gradlew test` under JDK 17.
- **Superseded path removed:** none in NMP — there was no reaction spelling here to replace; this is the first one. What it supersedes is app-side: the hand-written kind:7 in `29er-next`'s `RoomTimelineReacting.swift` and mosaico's equivalent, both of which are deleted in those repositories against this door rather than kept beside it.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1304 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the run gates recorded on PR #1304 and its merge record are the approval trail.

## 2026-08-06 — Give direct Rust the protocol and content families only Swift could reach ([issue #1239](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1239), [PR #1306](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1306))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1239 records protocol and content families that are direct dependencies of `nmp-ffi` and reachable through no `nmp` module, so the two supported consumer tiers have different protocol surfaces in the direction that penalises the tier NMP calls primary — a Swift app links one staticlib and gets all of them, a direct-Rust app adds a Cargo line per family, which is the exact shape `nmp-consumer-check` exists to forbid. The issue's table named four and a later comment named six; established from the manifests on this head it is **seven**, because `nmp-asset` behaves identically and had been counted by nobody. The consumer is not hypothetical: mosaico's `Cargo.toml` names `nmp-asset` and `nmp-blossom` as two extra git-pinned dependencies beside the facade and reaches `nmp_asset::Sha256Hash` on eight sites, one of which (`attachment_receive.rs`) contacts no Blossom server at all. For content it could not even do that — #1239 records the `find("nostr:")` scanner it hand-rolled instead, which silently discards every `note1`/`nevent1`/`naddr1` in a message body and never notices an `nsec1` pasted into one.
- **Changed projections:** ffi
- **Why `rust` is ABSENT and `ffi` is PRESENT, which is the reverse of what this change sounds like:** both halves were measured on this exact head, not carried. `rust` is absent LEGITIMATELY: the governed Rust snapshot is the DEFAULT-feature surface, and every module this PR adds is behind a non-default cargo feature, so none of them enters it — `nip22` and `nip25` already have that property, #1304 flagged it as a trap, and `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` regenerates BYTE-IDENTICAL to master at 28997 lines to prove it here. `ffi` is present for a reason that has nothing to do with a shape change: the governed UniFFI snapshot captures each exported item's DOCSTRING verbatim and derives its UniFFI checksum from metadata that includes it, so rewriting `nmp_blossom::BlossomClient` to `nmp::blossom::BlossomClient` in a doc comment moves the file. `docs/surface/components/nmp-core/uniffi.txt` is 16198 lines before and after; 38 lines change in place, 30 of them docstrings and 8 of them the checksums those docstrings feed. No function, method, record, enum, variant, field, argument or error is added, removed, renamed or retyped — which is why `cargo test -p nmp-parity` passes untouched. The doc rewrite is not cosmetic drift: leaving `nmp_blossom::` in a doc comment would name a crate `nmp-ffi` no longer depends on at all.
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** the `nmp` facade gains six modules behind six new non-default cargo features, each following the `nip22`/`nip25` shape: `nmp::nip18` (`repost`, `REPOST_KIND`, `GENERIC_REPOST_KIND`), `nmp::nipc7` (`chat`, `chat_reply`, `CHAT_KIND`), `nmp::nip51` (`active_account_demand`, both tolerant parsers, `SimpleGroupEntry`, `SimpleGroupsList`), `nmp::content` (`parse_content`, `MAX_CONTENT_BYTES` and the whole `ContentDocument` vocabulary), `nmp::asset` (`Sha256Hash`, `Sha256HexError`, `VerifiedAsset`) and `nmp::blossom` (the BUD-02/04/11/12 authorization drafts, descriptor codec and blocking client). `asset` is its own feature rather than folded into `blossom` because hashing and verifying received bytes is worth doing without speaking to a Blossom server, which is what mosaico does; `blossom` turns it on because `BlobDescriptor`/`VerifiedUpload` name `Sha256Hash` in their public signatures and a door whose own return types the caller cannot name is still a second Cargo dependency. `nmp-ffi`'s direct edges to all six are DELETED and it reaches each by enabling the facade feature — one owner of the values rather than two aligned by convention, which is what #851 established for `nmp-nip22` and what these edges had quietly re-opened. The dead `nmp-nip29` edge goes with them: it was in the manifest and `nmp-ffi` bound nothing from it, already reaching NIP-29 through `nmp::nip29`. No Swift or Kotlin source changes: every name those SDKs bind is unchanged.
- **Deliberately NOT built, with the reason:** `nmp-nip02` is the seventh family and is NOT wired, because no feature list can wire it. `nmp-nip02` depends on `nmp` — it is a `protocol-service` in `scripts/dependency-direction-policy.json`, a role defined as one that "may stop at the public facade but not reach mechanisms directly", so it sits ABOVE the facade rather than below it. The edge does not resolve: `cargo tree` answers `error: cyclic package dependency: package nmp v0.1.0 depends on itself`, verified by adding it and reading the refusal rather than assumed. `nmp-ffi` therefore keeps that one direct edge, now the only family edge left in its manifest, and `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh` documents why it is exempt instead of silently tolerating it. Reaching the follow vocabulary through the facade means splitting the engine-free schema/demand/composition half out of the observing service, or deciding the service belongs above the facade permanently; that is a different unit of work, tracked as #1307.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Every module added here is a re-export of an engine-free crate. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, coverage key, receipt row, attempt fact, or correlation representation changes, and no store door is added, widened or called.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. Nothing here reaches routing, the publish queue, the resolver, or any diagnostics record; the composers return `EventBuilder` values and the parsers return plain data.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp-consumer-check/src/lib.rs` adds `compose_every_retrofitted_family`, which composes a door from every wired family in ONE function — deliberately one and not six, because the claim is not "each family compiles" but "an app reaching all of them still names `nmp` alone", and only a single manifest with no second crate in it can say that. Every door composes and returns rather than being imported and dropped, so removing any one re-export breaks that crate. `crates/nmp/tests/nip51_headless.rs` now proves the kind:10009 demand against a real `EngineCore` through `nmp::nip51` — the path an app has — and `nmp`'s `nmp-nip51` dev-dependency is deleted rather than kept beside it. `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh` grows from three hardcoded mechanism crates to a table of every facade-owned family and checks three facts per family: the direct edge stays closed, the facade feature stays enabled, and the facade module that projects the vocabulary is present; it reads the `nmp = ...` dependency entry whole, so an inline and a multi-line table are the same fact to it. All three checks were negative-tested by hand — re-adding `nmp-content` to `[dependencies]`, dropping `"content"` from the feature list, and adding one `nmp_content::` path to a source file each fail with their own message. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — the FFI crate's every import moved, so the direct/FFI oracle is the thing that proves no shape did), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-consumer-check`, `cargo test --workspace --all-features` and `cargo clippy --workspace --all-features --all-targets -- -D warnings` (the six new features are non-default, so the all-features job is the one that builds them at all), `cargo fmt --all --check`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-nip51-no-derived-authority.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, `scripts/check-routing-vocabulary.sh`, `scripts/check-content-parser-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-dependency-direction.sh` and `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh`.
- **Superseded path removed:** `nmp-ffi`'s direct `nmp-nip51`, `nmp-nipc7`, `nmp-nip18`, `nmp-nip25`, `nmp-content`, `nmp-asset`, `nmp-blossom` and `nmp-nip29` dependencies are deleted outright, not deprecated and not kept beside the facade features — a second edge alongside a facade that now projects the same values is exactly the two-owner state #851 closed. `nmp`'s `nmp-nip51` dev-dependency is deleted for the same reason: the crate's own integration proof reaches NIP-51 the way an app does or the proof is about a path no app has. The `nmp_blossom::`/`nmp_nip51::`/`nmp_nip25::` paths in `nmp-ffi`'s doc comments are rewritten to their facade spellings rather than left as references to crates the manifest no longer names, which is the whole content of this entry's `ffi` projection change.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1306 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the gates recorded on PR #1306 and its merge record are the approval trail. The per-family judgement — six wired on their own evidence, `nmp-nip02` refused with the cargo error that refuses it and re-filed as #1307 — is stated in the PR body for review rather than left implicit in the diff.

## 2026-08-06 — Hand back the event id acceptance froze ([issue #1314](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1314), [PR #1315](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1315))

- **Failure evidence:** issue #1314 records that the only door reporting a write's frozen event id was `Engine::publish_queue()`, which materializes EVERY retained receipt and issues a lane read per open one, so an app asking what it just wrote paid the size of a set nothing bounds yet (#46). Mosaico's `src/nmp_host/write.rs::frozen_id` is that scan, on its write path. Reproduced here by the `#[ignore]`d `measure_frozen_id_read_cost` against a real `RedbStore`, release, M-series laptop: one lookup costs 0.24 ms at 250 retained receipts, 0.71 ms at 1,000 and 3.82 ms at 4,000, so 4,000 writes spend 15.28 s scanning — 16× the writes for 255× the scanning, quadratic in what the app has ever published. One correction to the issue, because it changes what the number proves: the ~140 s it reports is NOT mostly the scan. Acceptance in the same run costs 168.86 s, and a publish-only control with the scan removed measures 123.25 s for the same 4,000 writes — fsync-durable acceptance is a fixed per-write price the app pays once, unaffected by this change. The scan is the term with no ceiling above it, and that is the whole argument. This is the evidence #1288 was closed pending — *"a hot path where the scan is measurably too slow"* — rather than the ergonomic argument that closing already refused.
- **Changed projections:** rust
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::ReceiptStream` gains one field, `event_id: EventId` — the event id acceptance froze, alongside the receipt id it already carried. That is the entire projection diff: `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` regenerates at 28998 lines, one more than master's 28997, and the added line is `pub nmp::ReceiptStream::event_id: nostr::event::id::EventId`. No new type and no new door (Gate 1): `ReceiptStream` already is what acceptance answered, and `Engine::publish` was collapsed to one door in #848/#1300, so every accepted write — every `nmp::nip29::Groups::publish` included — already comes back holding it. Internally `core::Effect::WriteAccepted` carries `(ReceiptId, EventId)` and `core::ReceiptReplayPage` carries the `frozen_id` it already read from the retained record, so neither the ordinary nor the correlation-replay path performs an extra store read. Nothing crosses `nmp-ffi`, Swift or Kotlin, which is why `ffi`, `swift` and `kotlin` are absent from the projection set — computed on this exact head with `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections` after merging master and regenerating, not carried from an earlier run.
- **Deliberately NOT built, with the reason:** no by-`ReceiptId` lookup door, which is the other shape #1314 named. It would be strictly larger — a new `Engine` method, a new not-found/no-id-yet result shape, and an extra engine round trip for a value the first call already held — and it would be the wrong shape: an index that makes an unbounded store cheap to poke at is what lets the store stay unbounded. This deletes a reader of the retained set instead of optimising one. `ReceiptReattachment::Attached` does NOT gain the id either, and nothing crosses to `nmp-ffi`: the consumer standing in the gap is direct-Rust, and at the FFI the one `NmpReceiptStream` object is built from both doors, so projecting it there would need either an optional-by-which-door accessor or a widened reattachment — a decision that wants a Swift or Kotlin consumer in front of it. This does not close #46; retained receipts still grow without bound, and #753 still owns their retention.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, receipt row, attempt fact or correlation representation changes, and no store door is added, widened or called. `PublishQueueReceipt.frozen_id` was already the durable record of this value; the change is that acceptance stops discarding it on the way out.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. `Engine::publish_queue()` is unchanged and still returns every retained entry, because enumerating everything is the actual question a queue summary asks; its doc now says it is not the route to one write's identity.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/frozen_id_at_acceptance.rs` is a new file carrying `acceptance_answers_the_same_event_id_the_queue_reports`, `a_restamped_replaceable_edit_reports_its_post_restamp_id`, `the_frozen_id_is_the_id_the_signature_lands_on`, `a_correlation_replay_answers_with_the_retained_obligation_id`, `the_answer_does_not_grow_with_the_retained_receipt_set` and the `#[ignore]`d `measure_frozen_id_read_cost` that produced the table above. The restamp one is the behavioural point #1288 required be proved rather than caveated: it seeds a local winner stamped at 4,000,000,000, publishes a `WritePayload::ReplaceableEdit` that states no `created_at`, and asserts the returned id equals an `EventId::new` over `WINNER_STAMP + 1` computed independently in the test — with an `assert_ne!` against the wall-clock id so the fixture cannot pass vacuously. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN — no FFI shape changed, but the direct/FFI oracle is what would catch it if one had), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-store -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets`, `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh`, `scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh` and `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`.
- **Superseded path removed:** none in NMP — nothing here replaces an NMP spelling; `publish_queue()` keeps its own question. What it supersedes is app-side: mosaico's `frozen_id` helper and its per-write `publish_queue()` call are deleted against this pin in that repository, reading `ReceiptStream::event_id` where the caller already stands.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1315 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the gates recorded on PR #1315 and its merge record are the approval trail. The judgement the issue explicitly left to NMP — field on the receipt versus a separate lookup door, and whether the retained-set growth is the defect being fixed — is argued in the PR body for review rather than left implicit in the diff.
## 2026-08-06 — Give apps a typed "recreate the store" signal distinct from a broken disk ([issue #920](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/920), [PR #1318](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1318))

- **Failure evidence:** the maintainer's own 1.05 GB store refused to open today with *"predates the schema marker and is not the one supported epoch 13"*, and there was no typed way to know whether that meant "wipe it, the epoch moved" or "your disk is failing". Establishing the file was safe to delete took a read-only forensic investigation — table-by-table page accounting, a lane census over 274,054 lanes, a check for undelivered writes. The forensics also explain the wording: the marker sat at the address a superseded epoch owned (table `schema_meta_v6`, key `version` = 10), so the current probe of `store_meta`/`schema_version` found nothing and said so — literally true from its viewpoint, and indistinguishable from an unreadable file to a caller holding only the string. `RedbStore::open` has returned a named `RedbStoreOpenError::UnsupportedSchema { path, expected, found }` since #867/#917, but `nmp` does not re-export `RedbStoreOpenError` and `Engine::new` matched only `StoreAlreadyOpen`, funneling the rest through `error => EngineError::StoreOpenFailed { reason: error.to_string() }`. The distinction was real, reachable in Rust, and died at the boundary — the same message-grepping failure #895 removed inside the store and #915 removed for ownership.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** one variant. `nmp::EngineError` gains `StoreUnsupportedSchema { path: String, expected: u64, found: Option<u64> }`, mirrored as `FfiError::StoreUnsupportedSchema`, Swift `.storeUnsupportedSchema(path:expected:found:)`, and Kotlin `NMPError.StoreUnsupportedSchema(path, expected, found)`. `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` regenerates at 29002 lines, four more than master's 28998 — the variant and its three fields — measured from the regenerated file, under the 30000 cap. The projection set was computed on this exact head with `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections`, not carried. `found: None` means "not this epoch", never "no data": a marker at a superseded epoch's address is unreadable to this build, which is the incident's own case and the one a boolean would have flattened. `Engine::new`'s match over `RedbStoreOpenError` is now exhaustive, so the next store-open refusal fails the build until someone places it.
- **Deliberately NOT built, with the reason:** no `recreate()` door, no "should I wipe" predicate, and no severity enum — NMP states the fact, the app owns the discard through the destructive-reset door that already exists (`docs/internals/conventions/schema-epoch-discard.md`). The rest of the open family stays `StoreOpenFailed` on purpose: `PathResolutionFailed`, `LockFileOpenFailed`, `LockFailed`, `TargetChanged`, and `Database(_)` — an unresolvable path, a permissions failure, a locked file, a truncated or corrupt one — share the one app-visible fact that matters, that this store must not be discarded in response. Five more variants would be five doors with no consumer that cannot already act. `StoreOpenFailed` is not left a residue either: its doc in Rust, UniFFI, Swift, and Kotlin is now the positive claim that discarding the store is not the recovery for it, which is the sentence an app reads when deciding.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No table, schema marker, journal encoding, receipt row, attempt fact, or store door changes, and `SCHEMA_VERSION` is untouched. This projects an existing open-time refusal outward; it does not alter what open inspects or what it refuses. The refusal still deletes nothing — destruction remains the separate deliberate consumer action.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none; no diagnostics schema or value changes. The refusal's rendered text at this boundary reproduces #1017's operator contract — it names both the reacquirable relay-backed read cache and the permanently lost publish-queue state (accepted but unpublished writes, receipts, correlation tokens, route revisions, attempt evidence) — because the facade is where an app's operator reads it, and a typed branch does not excuse a refusal that understates what discarding costs.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/src/engine.rs::superseded_epoch_and_damaged_bytes_are_different_typed_open_refusals` builds the incident-shaped fixture (`schema_meta_v6`/`version` = 10) and asserts the new variant with `found: None`, a nonzero `expected`, the discard-cost text intact, and that a garbage file still yields `StoreOpenFailed` whose text does NOT say "discard and recreate". It then runs the exact sequence a consumer runs after branching — `reset_persistent_store` on the refused path, then a fresh `Engine::new` — because a variant telling an app the discard is correct is worth nothing if the refused open still owns the file. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/facade.rs::ffi_superseded_epoch_store_is_its_own_refusal_and_damaged_bytes_are_not` drives both fixtures end to end through `NmpEngine::new`; `ffi_epoch_refusal_carries_both_found_shapes` pins both `found` shapes across the projection, deliberately at the projection rather than end to end because reaching `found: Some(_)` would mean writing the current epoch's marker address from a crate that does not own it. Swift `EvidenceMappingTests`/`NMPErrorLocalizedDescriptionTests` and Kotlin `EvidenceMappingTest` each branch on the case and read `path` and `found` without touching the message. Gates RUN on this head: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN, not merely compiled — the FFI shape moved), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-store -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh`, `scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections`, plus `swift build`/`swift test` (182 tests) and `./gradlew compileKotlin test` under `JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@17` — the Kotlin branch test caught a defect that compiled cleanly (`assertTrue(x is T)` does not smart-cast), which is the class of miss a compile-only check waves through on a widened enum.
- **Superseded path removed:** the catch-all arm in `Engine::new`'s store-open mapping is deleted, not retained beside the new one; `StoreOpenFailed` keeps its name but no longer means "everything else". No alias, no forwarding variant, and no compatibility shim for the old collapsed shape.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1318 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the gates recorded on PR #1318, and its merge record are the approval trail. The judgement the issue left open — whether the epochs are useful to an app, and whether the rest of the open family needed typing too — is argued in the PR body for review rather than left implicit in the diff.
## 2026-08-07 — Give a source a way to say it finished answering ([issue #1235](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1235), [PR #1330](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1330))

- **Failure evidence:** `features/coverage/absence-settlement.feature` records the owner's ruling that settlement is EOSE "and nothing else -- not a timeout, not a retry budget, not a heuristic". The engine computes exactly that: `crates/nmp/src/core/auth_transport.rs`'s `EndOfStoredEvents` arm consumes the request's attribution FIFO and `emit_request_settled` does `active_request_evidence.remove(&send.revision())`. Removal is not a readable fact -- absence from that map means outstanding, or never sent, or finished, or refused, or dropped -- so the per-source read surface could not carry it. `SourceStatus` had six members and none meant "this source finished its request"; `Requesting`'s own doc said why it could not be read that way. The near-miss, `reconciled_through`, is a different claim that disagrees in both directions: `Some` from a prior window while a fresh request is still streaming, and `None` after a request that finished, because `AttributionState::record_send` poisons any request with `filter.limit.is_some()` as `CoveragePoison::LimitedRequest`, so its EOSE takes the `retire_request_evidence` arm and records no interval at all -- which is every bounded read. Mosaico wrote the resulting wall clock twice and the copies have already drifted: `src/nmp_host/read.rs:13` and `tests/common/nmp_client/read.rs:10` both carry a 500 ms `SNAPSHOT_QUIET_PERIOD`, and production's `empty_result_usable` (`:169`) requires ALL sources reconciled or ALL requesting while the test client's `usable_empty` (`:112`) accepts either PER SOURCE. Mixed-state evidence is rejected by one and accepted by the other. The divergence has a cause: production strips the caller's limit (`:100`), so its watermark can arrive; the test client passes `limit` through (`:62`), so its requests are poisoned, no watermark is ever recorded, and the quiet period is the only thing that ends a read.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** one enum variant. `nmp::SourceStatus` gains `FinishedStoredEvents`, mirrored as `FfiSourceStatus::FinishedStoredEvents`, Swift `.finishedStoredEvents`, and Kotlin `SourceStatus.FinishedStoredEvents`, with the matching `NMPRelayRuntimePresentation` case on both UI layers. `docs/surface/nmp-facade.txt` regenerates at 29003 lines, one more than master's 29002 -- measured with `wc -l` on the regenerated file, not carried -- and that one line is `pub nmp::SourceStatus::FinishedStoredEvents`; under the 30000 cap. The projection set was computed on this exact head with `SURFACE_BASE_REF=origin/master scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections`. It goes ON `SourceStatus` rather than beside it because the states are mutually exclusive -- a `finished: bool` next to `status` makes `Disconnected + finished` representable, and that is not a state -- and because it belongs to the axis it joins: `SourceEvidence`'s own doc keeps `reconciled_through` (durable past) and `status` (right now) from collapsing into one enum, and finishing is a CURRENT fact that dies with the socket while proving is durable. Internally the retained bit lives on `LiveWireRequest`, already documented as the wire owner that OUTLIVES the terminal, as `stored_events: StoredEvents { Streaming { request_revision }, Finished }` -- an enum, not a bool, because a replacement REQ reuses the `(session, sub_id)` key and a straggler terminal for the request it displaced must not mark the live one finished. `retire_request_evidence` reaches it too, deliberately: the wire frame ended the request whether or not its coverage claim survived, and that split is what makes a bounded read finishable. `ObservationFact::RequestSettled` is untouched and stays withheld from a poisoned completion, so nothing here lets a protocol consumer derive absence from a locally incomplete view. `GroupAvailability::Ready` and `FollowAvailability::Ready` now accept either connected-and-live state; both were approximating this fact with `reconciled_through.is_some() && all Requesting`, which is why neither was the answer.
- **Deliberately NOT built, with the reason:** no `is_complete`/`is_settled` on any type on any tier -- `crates/nmp/src/core/evidence.rs:36-42` forbids it and #12 found that collapse lying firsthand. The new fact is computed per source with the same all-or-nothing scoping `reconciled_through` uses, and `merge_acquisition_evidence` folds a source appearing in several scopes back to `Requesting` if any scope's request is still streaming, so one scope's completion can never answer for a scope nothing has answered. `Requesting` is not renamed: its doc is tightened to mean "still streaming stored events", and moving every call site and both SDKs would buy no fact. CLOSED still finishes nothing (#1078) -- a relay that refuses has not answered, and `close_requests_for_sub` still removes the wire owner and emits `RelayClosed`. No falsifier is claimed for the straggler-revision guard: staging a replacement REQ AND a delayed terminal for the request it displaced is not reachable with `ScriptedRelay`, which EOSEs immediately, so the guard is a type mechanism with no executable proof and this says so rather than naming a test that does not exercise it. `features/coverage/empty-vs-unknown.feature` is left ungoverned and untouched -- it is unchanged legacy, its first scenario still EXECUTES in the `nmp-bdd` runner, and governing the file would have silently stopped that from running.
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, journal encoding, coverage key, receipt row, attempt fact or correlation representation changes, and no store door is added, widened or called. The retained phase is memory-only and dies with the connection by design; the durable half of this pair is still the coverage row, unchanged.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. No diagnostics record or value changes. `ObservationEvidence` still renders `request_settled` exactly as before; the point of this change is that a causal trace on a separate channel was never a door an app could key a bounded read on, not that the trace was wrong.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `crates/nmp/tests/finished_stored_events.rs` is a new file carrying three proofs against real in-process relays over real sockets: `a_relay_that_finished_its_request_is_distinguishable_from_one_still_answering` (one relay EOSEs having sent nothing, one never confirms end of stored events, and before this both read `Requesting` with `reconciled_through: None`), `finishing_a_bounded_request_proves_nothing_and_says_so` (the limit-poisoned case -- finished AND no watermark, the pair that keeps #49's deletion deleted), and `an_empty_result_is_never_proven_by_a_source_that_has_not_answered` (the negative half: neither an unreachable relay nor a refusing one has finished). Both were RUN RED before green, and each experiment fails exactly the tests whose fact it destroys: making `finish_stored_events` return without writing fails the first two, each burning the full 20 s bound waiting for a fact that never arrives, while the negative test stays green; dropping only the `retire_request_evidence` call fails the bounded one alone and leaves the other two green -- the discrimination the change is about. `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs::acquisition_evidence_projects_every_fact_without_a_rollup` gains the new state deliberately paired with `reconciled_through: None`, so the projection proves the two facts stay independent across the boundary. Swift `RelayViewsTests.testEveryProjectedSourceStatusMapsWithoutInventingGlobalHealth` and Kotlin `RelayViewsTest.everyProjectedSourceStatusMapsWithoutInventingGlobalHealth` each enumerate the widened vocabulary. Three governed scenarios land in the new `features/coverage/finished-stored-events.feature` (`COVERAGE-FINISHED-001`...`003`), all `nmp:status=built`, each with one `rust:nmp::` locator and one falsifier. Gates RUN on this head, not merely compiled: `cargo test -p nmp-parity` (RUN -- this adds an FFI enum variant, so the direct/FFI oracle is what proves the projection did not drift), `cargo test -p nmp -p nmp-store -p nmp-ffi -p nmp-bdd -p nmp-parity -p nmp-nip02 -p nmp-router` (70 targets, exit 0), `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` (exit 0), `cargo fmt --all`, `scripts/check-sdk-parity.sh`, `scripts/check-no-compatibility-surface.sh`, `scripts/check-request-coverage-authority.sh` (the gate that owns this exact code), `scripts/check-routing-vocabulary.sh`, `scripts/check-bdd-file-length.sh`, `scripts/check-ffi-facade-boundary.sh`, `scripts/check-nip29-ownership.sh`, the behavior-traceability `check` against explicit base and head trees with a real issue snapshot (exit 0), `scripts/regenerate-surface-snapshots.sh`, `scripts/check-surface-governance.sh --print-projections`, plus `swift build`/`swift test` and `./gradlew compileKotlin test` under `JAVA_HOME=/opt/homebrew/opt/openjdk@17`, both rebuilt from regenerated UniFFI bindings first so the new variant is genuinely in the code the hand-written wrappers compile against.
- **Superseded path removed:** none in NMP -- nothing here replaces an NMP spelling. `SourceStatus`'s ratified-names doc on the FFI mirror is rewritten rather than left contradicting the diff: what those names protect is that every state is one source's own fact and that no query-level aggregate may ever appear, and the seventh per-source state is recorded there with the reason the six were not enough. What this supersedes is app-side: mosaico pins `nmp` by git rev, so both copies of `SNAPSHOT_QUIET_PERIOD` and the `acquisition_active`/`usable_empty` predicates built on them are deleted against this pin in that repository, in a separate PR there.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1330 is approved under the repository owner's delegated orchestration authority in this session; the PR review, the gates recorded on PR #1330 and its merge record are the approval trail. The judgement the issue explicitly left open -- whether the fact belongs on `SourceStatus` or beside it, and whether an existing door already answered it -- is argued in the PR body for review rather than left implicit in the diff.

## 2026-08-09 — Distinguish a locally unplaced request from a cache-satisfied source ([issues #849](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/849) and [#1350](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1350), [PR #1346](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1346))

- **Failure evidence:** a connected source with a planned REQ was reported as `Requesting` before `Pool::send` had accepted any exact attempt, and the same lie survived a local refusal while an engine-owned retry waited. The opposite no-wire case was also collapsed: a fresh `MaxAge` scope retained a shadow plan solely as its coverage witness, but global placement lookup could report `Connecting`, `Error`, or borrow an identical Live sibling's `Requesting` state even though that scope intentionally needed no request. These are different current facts and neither can be represented truthfully by the prior seven-state `SourceStatus` enum.
- **Changed projections:** ffi,kotlin,rust,swift
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `nmp::SourceStatus` gains the fieldless variants `AwaitingRequest` and `CoverageSatisfied`, mirrored as `FfiSourceStatus::{AwaitingRequest,CoverageSatisfied}`, Swift `.awaitingRequest` / `.coverageSatisfied`, and Kotlin `SourceStatus.AwaitingRequest` / `SourceStatus.CoverageSatisfied`; both native UI projections render them as “Awaiting request placement” and “Satisfied from cache”. `AwaitingRequest` is the connected-and-ready state before the exact local handoff succeeds and during owned retry backoff; `Requesting` now requires an accepted live request. `CoverageSatisfied` belongs only to a scope whose freshness decision suppressed wire work; a mixed source with any Live scope still reports the Live placement phase. The regenerated Rust facade is 29,005 lines, exactly two lines above the prior 29,003-line snapshot, and the UniFFI component diff is the two fieldless variants (twelve metadata lines).
- **Persistence impact:** none. No store table, schema marker, coverage key, row, receipt, route, attempt journal, or durable encoding changes; `CoverageSatisfied` reads the existing per-source durable coverage fact, while `AwaitingRequest` is volatile attempt/retry state.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. These are per-source `AcquisitionEvidence` states delivered with query rows, not relay diagnostics counters or a new query-level completion aggregate.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `core::request_attempt_tests::repeated_local_refusals_keep_one_goal_increase_backoff_and_become_requesting_only_on_accept` proves the source stays `AwaitingRequest` through bounded local retry and becomes `Requesting` only on exact acceptance; `core::request_attempt_tests::dynamic_full_recompile_publishes_awaiting_request_before_wire_dispatch` proves a full recompile publishes that pre-handoff truth before its `Effect::Wire`; `empty_neg_completion_projects_finished_status_through_the_plan_request`, `refused_neg_open_publishes_awaiting_before_its_fallback_request`, and `missing_id_backfill_publishes_awaiting_before_its_request` prove NIP-77 candidate, reconciliation, fallback, and missing-id child roles retain their plan-centric public status and publish Awaiting before request dispatch; `core::evidence::tests::coverage_satisfied_scope_never_borrows_request_placement_or_link_state` and `fresh_max_age_is_coverage_satisfied_alone_and_never_borrows_live_placement` separate fresh MaxAge from an identical Live sibling; `crates/nmp-ffi/src/convert.rs::acquisition_evidence_projects_every_fact_without_a_rollup`, Swift `EvidenceMappingTests.testEveryAcquisitionEvidenceVariantMapsWithoutARollup`, and Kotlin `EvidenceMappingTest.everyAcquisitionEvidenceVariantMapsWithoutARollup` enumerate both new cases without a rollup. Focused gates run on this working head: the two core ordering/freshness falsifiers, all 15 `freshness` integration tests, the exact FFI conversion test, and the `nmp-parity` target (13 passing plus 2 intentional ignores). The committed facade and UniFFI snapshots were inspected for the exact two-variant additive diff, but the repository-wide snapshot generator was not rerun locally because the machine had only 15 GiB free; CI remains the clean-room regeneration. Native Swift/Kotlin execution is not claimed here: this worktree does not contain the generated `NMP.xcframework` or generated Kotlin `uniffi` sources, so direct native test commands fail before compiling the changed wrappers.
- **Superseded path removed:** the plan-present-equals-`Requesting` inference and the shadow-plan placement lookup are replaced at their owning evidence reducer; no boolean beside `SourceStatus`, compatibility alias, fallback spelling, or synthetic request is retained. The enum remains one mutually exclusive current acquisition state per source.
- **Human signoff:** PR #1346 remains unmerged and is the review record for this provisional surface. This append records the exact generated shape and focused evidence; it is not reviewer approval or merge authorization, and exact-head review plus the repository's required surface gates remain mandatory.

## 2026-08-10 — Make opening-time freshness candidate-local ([issue #1348](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/issues/1348), [PR #1346](https://github.com/pablof7z/nmp/pull/1346))

- **Failure evidence:** opening one `MaxAge` observation compiled the new query together with every already-active demand, scanned the resulting whole plan to find the new scope, and retained that whole plan as the opening witness. A 207-incumbent falsifier retained 208 source entries for one fresh query; the Mosaico stress matrix stalled in its mixed-MaxAge phase at 4,096 observations while one core stayed busy. The cost grew with unrelated app work rather than with the query being opened.
- **Changed projections:** none. The Rust, FFI, Swift, and Kotlin query/evidence shapes are unchanged.
- **Rust / FFI / Swift / Kotlin impact:** `MaxAge` now uses a read-only Router admission preview for only the opening handle's non-CacheOnly scopes. The preview probes exact incumbent indexes, compiles only unresolved candidate work, applies the same remaining relay and per-session subscription budgets as live admission, and does not mutate wire ownership. A coverage-satisfied scope retains only its compact `AcquisitionEvidence` source facts and watermarks, never a speculative `RelayPlan` or synthetic subscription ids. `Live` and `CacheOnly` do no freshness planning.
- **Persistence impact:** none. Coverage rows, keys, schema epoch, journal records, and store calls are unchanged; each opening coverage row is still read once.
- **Diagnostics impact:** none. Candidate-local work counters are a doc-hidden bench/test surface, not application diagnostics.
- **Updated falsifiers:** `max_age_opening_retains_only_its_scoped_candidate_plan` proves one fresh opening beside 207 unrelated requests examines one candidate, visits zero unrelated demand/request entries, retains one source, and tears down to zero; `one_preview_never_visits_ten_thousand_unrelated_incumbent_demand_edges` structurally seeds 10,000 unrelated incumbent edges and requires the same zero incumbent visits and zero merge pairs; the preview budget tests prove fresh coverage cannot borrow a full global relay cap or per-session subscription budget while exact running coverage remains eligible; `live_and_cache_only_openings_do_zero_freshness_planning` proves both other modes keep every freshness counter at zero. `fresh_max_age_reads_each_coverage_row_once` and the full public `freshness` target remain the store-read and lifecycle controls.
- **Superseded path removed:** the whole-demand shadow compile, whole-plan scan, and retained opening `RelayPlan` are removed outright. There is no compatibility path or fallback to the old planner.
- **Human signoff:** issue #1348 owns the defect and PR #1346 is the review record. This entry records the implementation and focused evidence; it is not exact-head reviewer approval or merge authorization.
